coherence is an instance, and the drift of the weakly excited directions under a persistent disturbance (Anderson), of
which the $H^{-1}$ response is (\cite{S57}). New, in the transport-leak setting: the interpolation
$(h_j + \eta)/(h_j + \eta + \sigma_0)$ of the floor in the prior's mobility, the driving matrix
$Q = \langle(\dot{\hat J} + a\hat J)^\T(\dot{\hat J} + a\hat J)\rangle$ --- the sensitivity-filtered measurement the
law sees, built on \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}'s derivative Gram --- and their combination with the
coherence of the $K_0$-atom identification and its noise threshold at the lift. Checked:
\code{tests/test_measurement_noise.py} (the closed form against the Lyapunov solution, the trace identity at
$\eta = \infty$ and the monotonicity in $\eta$; the exact periodic floors of the linearised loop at $k_e = 100$ for
$\eta = 0$, $\sigma_0$ and $\infty$; a lift $m = 6$ under a noise path staying exactly replicated and following the
$K_0$-atom loop driven by the same path; a separation of the weakest class decaying at the noise std 0.01 and growing
at 0.05 --- a sign test, the rates within $10^{-4}$ of the lift's prediction ---; the separations' drift against an
independent quadrature of its two moments, and along the second-order transient the mean parameters moved by the
averaged force and the mean error carrying the tracking error of the developing deviation; the OU noise's stationary
variance and correlation, and the law seeing the measured error at the measured point; the responses
$(H + \sigma_0I)^{-1}$ and $H^{-1}$ to a constant force, the quasi-static force against the full second-order mean's
noise part to $2\cdot10^{-3}$, the parameters' part below $10^{-4}$ of the bias; the exact periodic mean against the
filtered-Gram response under both priors, the fixed prior's branch and its leak in the bias; and the mean function's
curvature term against an independent contraction).}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.30 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/measurement_noise.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_measurement_noise.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/measurement_noise.md}). The
three-bump instance ($\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$, $x_m = 2\sin t$, $k_e = 25$) at the lift of the exact
representation of the matching weights $q = 1/3$, the prior at its atoms. (A) The linear theory: $H$'s nine eigenvalues
span $3.06\cdot10^{-4}$--$0.195$; $\tr Q = 8.784$, of which 8.105 is the derivative Gram
$\langle\dot{\hat J}^\T\dot{\hat J}\rangle$ and 0.679 the slope term $\langle a^2\hat J^\T\hat J\rangle$ (the cross
term vanishes, as on every one-dimensional reference: an exact differential); $Q_{jj}/h_j$ ranges from 104 (mode 1) to
5.5 (mode 6). The parameters' floor per unit $N_0$ is 0.1597 under the fixed prior, 0.1627 at $\eta = \sigma_0$,
0.1690 at $10\sigma_0$ and $0.1757 = \tr Q/(2k_e)$ at $\eta = \infty$ --- the adaptive prior costs 10\% here, the
strong modes carrying the floor --- while the weakest mode's parameter variance grows from 0.0865 to 2.09,
$\times24.1$ in the averaged system ($\times28.0$ in the exact linearised loop at $k_e = 25$, Part B). The fast parts
are $\tr H^2 = 0.0657$ and $\langle\abs{\hat J}^4\rangle = 0.299$. The second-order force of the noise's own moments
(OU noise, $B = 100$) has the averaged function-level stationary bias 491 (fixed prior) and 2397 (adaptive,
$\times4.88$) per unit $(\E\nu^2)^2$, equal to the floor of the parameters at the noise std 0.0026 and 0.0012
respectively and dominant above; the full second-order mean (the periodic covariance contracted with the true field's
second derivatives) reproduces the force to $1.7\cdot10^{-4}$, the parameters' fluctuation changing the bias by less
than $10^{-5}$ ($2397.66\to2397.68$; the mean function's 2397.65), and the exact periodic second-order mean of the
linearised loop gives 504 and 2653 ($\times5.27$). The averaged bias grows in $k_e$ with the local exponent 1.93--1.99
for $B\ge20k_e$ but 1.69 and 1.47 at $B = 8k_e$ and $4k_e$, and saturates beyond. At the lift the separations'
second-order drift has $\lambda_{\max}(S_k) = 0.0148$, $0.0101$ and $0.298$ per unit $\E\nu^2$ (real time) in the
three classes: there the third class's separations stop decaying at the noise std 0.031 (1.5\% of the reference's
amplitude), and at Part C's stds 0.003 and 0.01 the margin $\sigma_0/k_e$ is used to 0.9\% and 10.5\%. (B) The exact
periodic stationary covariance of the linearised loop (the monodromy and a discrete Lyapunov equation) against the
prediction, at $\eta = 0$, $\sigma_0$, $\infty$ and $k_e = 25$--$800$: the function-level floor's ratio 0.976--0.980
at $k_e = 25$, 0.994--0.995 at 50, 0.9986--0.9988 at 100, 0.9997--0.9998 at 200, and above 1 at 800
($+1.2\cdot10^{-5}$ at $\eta = 0$, $+1.3\cdot10^{-5}$ at $\eta = \infty$); the deviation's successive ratios per
doubling are 3.87, 4.38, 5.26, 11.4 and a change of sign ($\eta = \infty$) --- a $k_e^{-2}$ term dominating to
$k_e = 200$ over a first-order one of the opposite sign --- while with the noise input $-k_e\nu$ (the slope removed,
the control) they are 3.67, 3.90, 3.98, 3.99, 4.00: without the slope the expansion is numerically even (to
$k_e = 800$; not proved). The weakest mode's variance is 0.845 (fixed prior) and 0.980 (adaptive) of its
slow-plus-fast prediction at $k_e = 25$, tending to 1. The exact stationary second-order mean of the linearised loop
lies 10.7\%, 2.65\%, 0.66\%, 0.17\% above the averaged bias under the adaptive prior and 2.58\%, 0.64\%, 0.16\%,
0.04\% under the fixed one at $k_e = 25$, $50$, $100$, $200$ --- the weak modes' stiffness being the filtered Gram
$H - k_e^{-2}\langle\dot{\hat J}^\T\dot{\hat J}\rangle$ of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}(c). (C) The
real loop (RK4, $\mathrm{d}t = 10^{-3}$, an OU noise exact at the steps, 12 noise paths of 300 time units common to the
four cases) against the transient covariance of the linearised loop plus the second-order mean deviation: at the noise
std 0.003 within one standard error at every record ($z$ from $-0.99$ to $0.99$), the mean part 42\% of the
prediction at $t = 300$; at std 0.01 within 1.75 standard errors ($z$ from $-0.3$ to $1.75$, the records of a run
correlated), the mean part 89\% at $t = 300$ and the covariance part alone 8.8--8.9 times below the Monte Carlo ---
the second-order force is the dominant effect of the noise there. The adaptive and the fixed prior drift apart, by
6.0\% and 6.4\% at $t = 300$ (predicted 5.3\% and 6.1\%): the weak modes, where the two responses part, equilibrate
over $k_e/h_1\approx8\cdot10^4$ time units, so over 300 time units the transient checks the force (the weak modes
still grow linearly), and the stationary response is checked against the exact linearised loop (B). The lift
$m = 15$ (classes of five) driven by the same noise path as the $K_0$-atom run gives the same $\fhat$ to rounding
(identical in the stored float32, $5.6\cdot10^{-16}$ apart in float64): the noise is coherent, as (i) states. (D) But
invariance is not attraction: from a separation of size $10^{-6}$ along $S_3$'s top eigenvector, on eight antithetic
pairs of noise paths of 300 time units per std ($\nu$ and $-\nu$, whose pair means cancel the first-order part of the
log-growth), the lift's third class separates at $-2.50\cdot10^{-4}\pm3.8\cdot10^{-7}$ per time unit at the std
0.01, $+2.74\cdot10^{-6}\pm3.3\cdot10^{-6}$ at 0.03 and $+4.53\cdot10^{-4}\pm8.4\cdot10^{-6}$ at 0.05. The
noise-induced part $\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}} := (\mathrm{rate} + \sigma_0/k_e)/\E\nu^2$ is $0.3271\pm0.0038$,
$0.3175\pm0.0037$ and $0.2943\pm0.0034$, against the lift's drift $\lambda_{\max}(S_3) = 0.2981$ and the drift along
the second-order transient from the lift, 0.3264, 0.3270 and 0.3281 (the same estimator applied to
$\dot u = (\E\nu^2S_3(t) - \sigma_0/k_e)u$). Along the transient the mean error's rms per unit $\E\nu^2$ grows from
0.121 to 0.602 over the six 50-unit windows to $t = 300$ and $\lambda_{\max}(S_3(t))$ from 0.307 to 0.349: the
tracking error of the developing errors-in-variables deviation. The weighted fit
$\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}} = c_0 + c_1\E\nu^2$ has the small-noise limit $c_0 = 0.3291\pm0.0033$ --- 10.4\% above the
lift's drift, within its standard error of the transient's 0.3264 --- and the next order $c_1 = -13.8\pm2.0$; over
this window the measured rate vanishes at the std 0.0299, against 0.0308 by the lift's drift and 0.0294 by the
transient's. Above it the replicated configuration repels, its within-class spread staying exactly 0.0 from a
replicated start only because each particle's update is the same arithmetic.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.31 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-22 version, as committed at
% 29f9394; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/eiv_remedy.md). The references [S49], [S53], [S54],
% [S57] and [S58] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) are the bibitems S49, S53, S54, S57 and S58 of refs.tex.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.31}[the adaptive prior is a washout leak; the desired regressor makes the noise's
persistent force a least-squares force]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:31}%
Let the leak be \hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}'s toward an adaptive prior,
$\dd z_j/\dd s = -\eta(z_j - \hat T_j)$ with $0 < \eta < \infty$ (the nearest-atom form of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}(ii) with the class barycentres likewise), in the averaged loop or in
the real loop \textup{(CL)} of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26} ($s = t/k_e$); at an exact
representation of representable weights $w$ let $H$, $\hat J$, $a$ and the mean $\langle\cdot\rangle$ over a period be
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}'s, $\norm{g}^2_\rho := \langle g^2\rangle$ for a function $g$ along the
orbit, and $P$ the $L^2_\rho$-projection onto the span of $\hat J$'s components.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the washout)} Along every solution and every path of the plan with $\hat T_j$ of bounded variation,
    $\hat T_j(s) - z_j(s) = \int_0^se^{-\eta(s-\tau)}\dd\hat T_j(\tau) + e^{-\eta s}(\hat T_j(0) - z_j(0))$ --- and for
    every measurable $\hat T_j$ (a chattering plan included) in the integrated form
    $z_j(s) = e^{-\eta s}z_j(0) + \eta\int_0^se^{-\eta(s-\tau)}\hat T_j(\tau)\dd\tau$: the prior lags the barycentre by
    the barycentre's exponentially forgotten increments. For integral plans (the nearest-atom cells, the OT plan at a
    commensurate $m$) the leak on a particle, $-\sigma_0(\vartheta_i - z)$, is therefore the full
    $\sigma$-modification of its offset from the barycentre plus, on the barycentre, the $\sigma$-modification passed
    through the washout filter $(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}s)(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}s + \eta)^{-1}$ --- zero at zero
    frequency, the full leak above $\eta$; with split rows (an incommensurate $m$) the barycentres also feel
    $-\sigma_0(I - M)z$, $M_{jl} := (m/q_j)\sum_i\gamma_{ij}\gamma_{il}$, which is not washed out. Linearised at the
    exact representation, mode $j$ of the collective block has at the slow frequency $\omega$ the stiffness
    $h_j + \sigma_0i\omega/(i\omega + \eta)$: the fixed prior's $h_j + \sigma_0$ for $\omega\gg\eta$, the bare $h_j$ at
    $\omega = 0$. This is \hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}(iii)'s bias removal --- feedback through a
    washout filter leaves the equilibria where they are (\cite{S54}), here the replicated ones --- and its price: no
    leak opposes a persistent force on the class barycentres (the separations keep the full leak).
  \item \emph{(the stationary identity)} Let the prior be an affine time-invariant filter of the barycentre and of a
    fixed anchor $z_a$ --- linearised, $\zeta = \mathcal{K}[y] + (I - \mathcal{K})[\delta]$ in the whitened weighted
    coordinates, $\delta$ the anchor's misplacement from the representation; for example
    $\dd z/\dd s = -\eta(z - \hat T) - \kappa(z - z_a)$, $\kappa\ge0$, with $\mathcal{K}(0) = \eta/(\eta + \kappa)$
    ($\kappa = 0$ the adaptive prior, $\kappa = \infty$ the fixed prior at $z_a$) --- and
    $S_0 := \sigma_0(I - \mathcal{K}(0))$ the leak's zero-frequency gain (the anchor entering through
    $I - \mathcal{K}$: the filter returns the anchor when the barycentre sits there). A persistent force $F$ on the
    collective coordinates and the misplacement $\delta$ give the stationary deviation $y = RF + B\delta$ with
    $R = (H + S_0)^{-1}$ and $B = (H + S_0)^{-1}S_0$, so
    \[
      RH + B = I,\qquad\text{i.e.}\qquad R = (I - B)H^{-1}:
    \]
    the response is the unregularised one times the share not taken by the anchor ($HR + B = I$ as well where $S_0$
    commutes with $H$, as for the family; not for a prior fixed on some coordinates or a projected prior with an
    active face). On every mode, whatever $S_0$, $h_jr_{jj} + b_{jj} = 1$ ($r_{jj} := v_j^\T Rv_j$,
    $b_{jj} := v_j^\T Bv_j$): a vanishing bias forces the response $1/h_j$. So no such prior both removes the leak's
    bias and keeps its bound on the response to persistent forces --- the trade binding on the modes with
    $h_j < \sigma_0$, where $1/h_j$ exceeds the fixed prior's $1/\sigma_0$ --- and a remedy for the robustness loss of
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(iii) has to change the force.
  \item \emph{(least-squares forces)} For $H\succ0$ and every $S$ with $S + S^\T\succeq0$,
    $F^\T(H + S)^{-\T}H(H + S)^{-1}F\le F^\T H^{-1}F = \min\{\norm{g}^2_\rho : \langle\hat J^\T g\rangle = F\}$, with
    equality at $S = 0$: the function-level stationary response to a persistent force is at most the squared norm of
    the smallest target perturbation that exerts it ($\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$ is the learning force of the target
    $f + g$), and the adaptive prior attains the bound. A least-squares force $\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$ therefore
    moves the learned function by at most $\norm{g}_\rho$ under every prior --- under the adaptive prior by exactly
    $Pg$, so that the stationary point's projected residual $P(g - \hat Jy)$ vanishes.
  \item \emph{(the desired regressor)} Let the adaptive law evaluate its regressor at the reference,
    $\dot\vartheta_i = G\nabla_\vartheta((y - x_m)^\T\tilde\Phi(x_m;\vartheta_i))
    - (\sigma_0/k_e)(\vartheta_i - z_{\pi(i)})$ (DR), with the compensation $\fhat_\Theta(y)$ as before or also at
    the reference, $u = \dot x_m - k_e(y - x_m) - \fhat_\Theta(x_m)$ (DR*, the desired compensation adaptation
    law~\cite{S53}). Then (a) without noise the lift $\Gamma_w$ is a solution and the linearisation at it is
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}'s --- for DR exactly (the regressor's variation multiplies
    $e\equiv0$), for DR* with $-(k_e - a(t))$ in place of $-k_e$ in the error channel --- and the averaged loop is
    unchanged at leading order in $1/k_e$ (the regressor moves by $x - x_m = O(1/k_e)$); (b) to first order in $\nu$,
    DR is \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(ii)'s linearised loop driven by the same noise, path by
    path; DR* has the noise input $-k_e\nu$ in place of $-(k_e + a)\nu$, and the same floor up to a relative
    $O(1/k_e)$; (c) to second order the part of the force carried by the noise's own second moments (at fixed
    parameters) is the least-squares force $\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$ (slow time) of $g := k_e\E[e]$, the rescaled
    mean tracking error at the lift; for an Ornstein--Uhlenbeck noise of rate $B$, quasi-statically along the orbit,
    $g = -\frac12\partial^2_x\fhat\cdot\E\nu^2(B - k_e - 2a)/(k_e + B)$ for DR and
    $g = \frac12\partial^2_xf\cdot\E\nu^2k_e^3/((k_e - a)^2(k_e - a + B))$ for DR* ($k_e > \max a$), so
    $\abs{g}\le\frac12\abs{\partial^2_xf}\E\nu^2(1 + O(\abs{a}/k_e))$ for all $k_e$ and $B$. By (iii) its
    function-level response is at most
    $\norm{g}^2_\rho\le\frac14\norm{\partial^2_xf}^2_\rho(\E\nu^2)^2(1 + O(1/k_e))$ under every prior --- uniformly in
    $k_e$, in $B$ and in the spectrum of $H$ --- and under the adaptive prior it leaves no projected mean tracking
    error. The parameters' first-order fluctuation adds a second-order force of the same order in $N_0$: in the error
    equation the compensation's curvature $\frac12D^2_\Theta\fhat[\Sigma_{\Theta\Theta}]$ and, for MR and DR, its
    slope--parameter term (DR*'s compensation, at $x_m$, has none) --- a least-squares force again, of a perturbation
    set by the parameters' covariance; the curvature also shifts the mean function, and the loop removes its tangent
    part ---, in the learning law the correlation $\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi\E[\varepsilon_m\delta\vartheta]$ of
    the regressor's parameter dependence with the measured error --- an errors-in-variables term in the parameters,
    which the reference in the regressor does not remove and which is not the learning force of a mean tracking
    error, so no bound in the noise's moments applies to it. Its size is set by the parameters' stationary covariance
    (mode $j$'s variance $(N_0/2k_e)Q_{jj}(h_j + \eta)/(h_j(h_j + \eta + \sigma_0))$, unbounded as $h_j\to0$ at
    $\eta > 0$), so the bound $\norm{g}^2_\rho$ is the noise part's, not the total's. With the measured regressor the
    noise part is $F_1 + \langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$, $F_1 := k_e\langle\hat J'^\T\E[\varepsilon_m^2]\rangle$ --- the
    regressor's errors in variables proper, the learning force of no mean tracking error --- and the adaptive prior's
    stationary point keeps the projected mean tracking error $P\E[e] = -\hat JH^{-1}F_1/k_e$: what grows like $k_e^2$
    while $k_e\ll B$ is $k_e$ times it, of squared norm $F_1^\T H^{-1}F_1$, the function-level bias; the mean tracking
    error itself, of squared norm $F_1^\T H^{-1}F_1/k_e^2\propto(B/(k_e + B))^2$, stays bounded.
\end{enumerate}
Hence with the desired regressor the first-order floor still falls like $1/k_e$ while the noise part of the
errors-in-variables bias stays bounded, and on the instance the parameters' part's own response falls like $k_e^{-2}$
--- a one-dimensional reference, where the law's leading term, the period average of a function of $x_m$ times
$\dot x_m$ (a line integral $\oint M\partial_x\hat J\dd x$ along the orbit), vanishes; on a curve in $\R^n$, $n > 1$,
it need not: the trade-off in $k_e$ of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30} disappears, and what is left is
the plant's state jitter $\E[e^2] = \E\nu^2(k_e + a)^2/(k_e(k_e + B))$ (DR; DR*:
$k_e^2\E\nu^2/((k_e - a)(k_e - a + B))$) --- the classical price of a high gain under measurement noise.
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) The integrated form is the variation of constants for $\dd z_j/\dd s = -\eta(z_j - \hat T_j)$; for $\hat T_j$ of
bounded variation (it jumps where the plan does, finitely often on bounded intervals), $u := \hat T_j - z_j$ satisfies
$\dd u = \dd\hat T_j - \eta u\dd s$, whence $u(s) = e^{-\eta s}u(0) + \int_0^se^{-\eta(s-\tau)}\dd\hat T_j(\tau)$; and
$\vartheta_i - z = (\vartheta_i - \hat T) + u$ for an integral plan (for split rows the targets are
$m\sum_j\gamma_{ij}z_j$, and averaging the leak over a class gives $-\sigma_0(\hat T - Mz)$). Linearised,
$\zeta = \eta(i\omega + \eta)^{-1}y$ on each mode (the prior and the leak are scalars in the whitened weighted
coordinates, so they commute with $H$), and $\sigma_0(y - \zeta) = \sigma_0i\omega(i\omega + \eta)^{-1}y$. (ii) At
zero frequency $\zeta = \mathcal{K}(0)y + (I - \mathcal{K}(0))\delta$, so the leak on $y$ is
$-\sigma_0(y - \zeta) = -S_0(y - \delta)$, and the stationary equation $Hy + S_0(y - \delta) = F$ gives
$y = (H + S_0)^{-1}(F + S_0\delta)$; then $RH + B = (H + S_0)^{-1}(H + S_0) = I$, and on the diagonal in $H$'s
eigenbasis $h_jr_{jj} + b_{jj} = 1$. For the family, the prior's stationary equation
$\eta(y - \zeta) = \kappa(\zeta - \delta)$ gives $\mathcal{K}(0) = \eta/(\eta + \kappa)$. (iii) By congruence with
$H + S$, $(H + S)^{-\T}H(H + S)^{-1}\le H^{-1}\Leftrightarrow H\le(H + S)^\T H^{-1}(H + S)
= H + S + S^\T + S^\T H^{-1}S$, true for $S + S^\T\succeq0$. The map $g\mapsto\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$ has the
adjoint $F\mapsto\hat JF$, so the smallest $g$ with $\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle = F$ is $\hat JH^{-1}F$, of squared norm
$F^\T H^{-1}F$; for $F = \langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$, $F^\T H^{-1}F = \norm{Pg}^2_\rho\le\norm{g}^2_\rho$, and at
$S = 0$, $\hat Jy = \hat JH^{-1}\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle = Pg$. (iv) (a) At $e = 0$ both laws vanish, and their
variations, $\delta e\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x;\vartheta) + e(\cdots)$, coincide at $x = x_m$; for DR*,
$f(x) - \fhat(x_m) = a\delta e - \hat Jy$ to first order. Off the lift $e$ is slaved,
$e = r(x_m)/k_e + O(k_e^{-2})$, so the two regressors differ by $O(1/k_e)$. (b) The regressor's dependence on $y$
enters the law multiplied by $\varepsilon_m$, at second order; for DR* the compensation no longer carries $\nu$. (c) At
fixed parameters the mean of $\varepsilon_m\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x_m;\vartheta)$ is
$\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x_m)\E[e]$, since $\E\nu = 0$; the parameters' fluctuation adds
$\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x_m)\E[\varepsilon_m\delta\vartheta]$ in the law and the compensation's curvature and
slope--parameter terms in the error equation (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}'s proof). For DR,
$\E[e]$ is \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}'s (the same compensation); for DR*,
$\dot e = (a - k_e)e + \frac12\partial^2_xf\,e^2 - k_e\nu + O(3)$, whose linear part has
$\operatorname{Var}(e) = k_e^2\E\nu^2/((k_e - a)(k_e - a + B))$ for the OU noise, and the mean balance is
$(k_e - a)\E[e] = \frac12\partial^2_xf\operatorname{Var}(e)$. The bounds:
$\abs{B - k_e - 2a}\le(k_e + B)(1 + 2\abs{a}/(k_e + B))$ and
$k_e^3/((k_e - a)^2(k_e - a + B))\le(k_e/(k_e - a))^3$. With the measured regressor, at the adaptive prior's
stationary point $\langle\hat J^\T(g - \hat Jy)\rangle + F_1 = 0$ and the mean of the error equation gives
$k_e\E[e] = g - \hat Jy$ at second order, so $k_eP\E[e] = -\hat JH^{-1}F_1$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved, exactly (every solution of the real or the averaged loop; the differential form for $\hat T$ of
bounded variation, the integrated one always; the leak's decomposition for integral plans); (ii) proved as
$RH + B = I$ and per mode --- linear algebra on the averaged linearised block; $HR + B = I$ where $S_0$ commutes with
$H$; (iii) proved ($S + S^\T\succeq0$, $H\succ0$); (iv)(a) proved at the lift and for its linearisation, the
leading-order equality of the averaged loops by Theorem J's averaging argument (not rewritten); (iv)(b) the
first-order identity proved for the linearised equations, the floors themselves with
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(ii)'s status; (iv)(c) formal and split --- the noise part by
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(iii)'s quasi-static second-moment computation, its consequences exact
for the averaged system; the parameters' part computed numerically (the full second-order mean), not in closed form.
Classical: reference regressors and the desired compensation adaptation law (\cite{S53}; DR* without Sadegh and
Horowitz's nonlinear damping is a local statement), washout filters (\cite{S54}), the adaptive law kept near a
low-pass filtered copy of itself --- Yucelen and Haddad's low-frequency learning, which has the washout structure
(\cite{S58}) --- and elastic averaging's moving-average centre (\cite{S49}); the identity, per mode the Tikhonov
filter factor plus the residual factor (the Kalman-gain split of a regularised estimate); the bound (iii), the
minimum-norm solution with the contraction of the regularised fit; and the split of the noise's force into the
regressor's errors in variables proper and the output's, of which only the mean biases; and the parameters' own errors
in variables, the classical second-order bias of a constant-gain stochastic approximation from its own fluctuation ---
the mean field's curvature in the parameters contracted with their stationary covariance, plus the noise--parameter
correlation (Benveniste--M\'etivier--Priouret; for constant-step stochastic gradient descent Dieuleveut--Durmus--Bach,
who remove it by Richardson--Romberg extrapolation; the curvature bias of nonlinear least squares, Box's, the analogue
of $\frac12D^2_\Theta\fhat[\Sigma_{\Theta\Theta}]$: \cite{S57}). New are their transfer to the transport leaks --- the
washout reading of the adaptive prior in the mass-constrained, mean-field setting, uniform in $m$ --- and the
instance's quantification, with the observation that the desired regressor does not remove the parameters' part.
Checked: \code{tests/test_eiv_remedy.py} (the washout identity along a real-loop solution at finite $\eta$, the
barycentre's velocity integrated alongside, and on the averaged loop across a plan switch, the jump's share large; the
collective block's transfer function against the washout formula, $RH + B = I$ along the prior family and, $R$ and
$B$ from their dynamic blocks, for priors whose zero-frequency leak does not commute with $H$ --- fixed on the
amplitudes, one active face, a matrix anchor gain, a random one with $K + K^\T\succeq0$ --- where $HR + B = I$ fails;
the response bound under random leaks $S\ge0$, the smallest equivalent perturbation and the projection; the lift exact
under DR and DR*, on a common noise path DR's run within the second order of MR's, and the regressor's point in the
law; DR's and DR*'s noise forces least-squares, their pointwise bounds on $g$, MR's $F_1$ three orders of magnitude
above; the quasi-static forces against the full second-order mean's noise part, the parameters' part above 1\% for DR
and DR* and below $10^{-5}$ for MR).}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.31 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/eiv_remedy.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_eiv_remedy.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/eiv_remedy.md}). The instance of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30} with an Ornstein--Uhlenbeck noise of rate $B$; MR the measured
regressor and compensation, DR the regressor at the reference, DR* both at the reference. (A) The averaged system's
biases per unit $(\E\nu^2)^2$. From the noise's own moments, the adaptive prior's function-level bias at $k_e = 25$,
$B = 100$ is 2397 (MR), 0.471 (DR) and 0.0526 (DR*); with the parameters' fluctuation (the full second-order mean)
2398, 0.486 ($+3.3\%$) and 0.0481 ($-8.7\%$), the mean function's 2398, 0.476 and 0.0511. Over $k_e = 25$--$200$ and
$B = 100$, $1000$ the parameters' part changes MR's bias by at most 0.03\%, DR's by $-0.75\%$ to $+3.3\%$ except at
$k_e = B = 100$ --- where DR's noise part nearly cancels ($4.5\cdot10^{-5}$) and the parameters' part raises it by
56\% --- and DR*'s by $-8.7\%$ to $-0.15\%$; its own response is $3.9\cdot10^{-4}$, $1.1\cdot10^{-4}$,
$2.9\cdot10^{-5}$ and $7.4\cdot10^{-6}$ at $k_e = 25$, $50$, $100$, $200$ ($B = 100$), like $k_e^{-2}$ --- the
cancellation of the law's leading term on the one-dimensional reference ---, while its share of DR's bias does not fall
monotonically. MR over DR (full) is $4.9\cdot10^3$ at $k_e = 25$, $B = 100$, and $3.0\cdot10^3$--$2.1\cdot10^8$ over
the grid. MR's bias grows like $(k_eB/(k_e + B))^2$ ($\times46.6$ from $k_e = 25$ to 200 at $B = 1000$, the law
$\times46.7$, $k_e^2$ $\times64$), while DR's and DR*'s stay at most 1.18, below
$\frac14\norm{\partial^2_xf}^2_\rho = 1.31$. DR's noise part attains its bound $\norm{g}^2_\rho$ to 0.01\% at
$k_e = 25$ --- $g$ lies almost in the tangent space, the second derivative of a Gaussian bump being in the span of its
amplitude and width derivatives. The rescaled projected mean tracking error $k_e^2\norm{P\E[e]}^2_\rho$ under the
adaptive prior is 2394 for MR (0 for DR and DR* by construction), the mean tracking error itself 3.83 at $k_e = 25$ and
0.66 at 200 ($B = 100$). The noise level at which the adaptive prior's full bias reaches its floor rises from the std
0.0012 (MR) to 0.085 (DR) and 0.27 (DR*) at $B = 100$ (stds above 0.1 lie outside the small-noise regime). (B) Along
the prior family at $\eta = \sigma_0$ ($\kappa/\eta = 0$, $0.1$, $1$, $10$ and the fixed prior; $S_0$ from 0 to
$\sigma_0$) the weakest mode's response falls from 3269 to 136 while its bias factor rises from 0 to 0.959, with
$h_1r_1 + b_1 = 1$ and $\max\abs{RH + B - I}\le1.1\cdot10^{-14}$; the trade binds on the 3 of 9 modes with
$h_j < \sigma_0$; MR's errors-in-variables bias falls only from 2397 to 491, and DR's from 0.471 to 0.211 --- MR over
DR $2.3\cdot10^3$--$5.1\cdot10^3$ along the family. For priors whose zero-frequency leak does not commute with $H$, $R$
and $B$ again from their dynamic blocks --- the prior fixed on the amplitudes or on the centres and adaptive at
$\eta = \sigma_0$ elsewhere, one active face of the projected adaptive prior ($\eta = \infty$), the anchor gain
$K = \eta P_{\mathrm{amp}}$, a random anchor gain with $K + K^\T\succeq0$ --- the block's $R$ and $B$ equal
$(H + S_0)^{-1}$ and $(H + S_0)^{-1}S_0$, $S_0$ by elimination of the prior, to $1.9\cdot10^{-15}$: $RH + B = I$ holds
to $1.4\cdot10^{-14}$ and per mode to $8.9\cdot10^{-15}$, while $\max\abs{HR + B - I}$ is 0.47--0.79. (C) The real
loop ($k_e = 25$, $B = 100$, std 0.01, $\eta = \infty$, RK4 $\mathrm{d}t = 10^{-3}$, 12 runs of 300 time units, the
three laws on common noise paths): at $t = 300$, $\E\norm{\fhat - f}^2_\rho$ is $2.43\cdot10^{-6}$ under MR against
$2.80\cdot10^{-7}$ under DR and $2.75\cdot10^{-7}$ under DR*, a ratio of 8.7 (predicted 9.2); DR and DR* sit at their
covariance part, their mean parts 0.07\% and 0.008\% of it. Against the prediction the $z$-scores run from $-2.04$ to
$-0.04$ (MR; \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}'s runs of the same loop, on other paths, lay 7\% above
it) and from $-1.8$ to $0.82$ (DR, DR*). The run mean of $\fhat_{\mathrm{MR}} - \fhat_{\mathrm{DR}}$, in which the
first-order responses cancel path by path, equals the predicted $\hat J(\bar y_{\mathrm{MR}} - \bar y_{\mathrm{DR}})$
to a relative $L^2_\rho$ error of 0.5--5.3\% at every record, 0.43--1.83 times the run mean's relative sampling error;
the averaged prediction carries its own $O(1/k_e)$ error, and the test checks $F_1$ (through MR $-$ DR), not DR's own
force, whose mean part is $7\cdot10^{-4}$ of its covariance part.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.32 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-23 version, as committed at
% 1ddff8c; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/cohesion_leak.md). The anchors res:thmDppp:32 and
% res:thmDppp:33 are \hyperref targets, as res:thmDppp:21 is; its (A) as committed at 9d122dd (the dead class's mass).
% Proposition D'''.34, named in the Status and in the numerics (K), is linked to res:thmDppp:34 (manuscript part 4g).
% The block cites no bibitem.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.32}[the cohesion leak: a leak on the unexcited subspace, without a
prior]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:32}%
Let $\psi : [0,\infty)\to[0,1]$ be $C^2$, non-increasing, $\psi(0) = 1$ --- of finite range, $\psi(z) = (1 - z)^3_+$
(the range $R$), or Gaussian, $\psi(z) = e^{-z/2}$ (the scale $R$) --- and let the particles attract each other
instead of leaking toward a prior:
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\dd\vartheta_i}{\dd s} = G\langle\nabla_\vartheta(r_\Theta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i))\rangle_\rho
  - \frac{\sigma_0}m\sum_j\psi(\norm{\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j}^2_{G^{-1}}/R^2)(\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j)
  \tag{$\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{coh}}$}
\end{equation}
(in the real loop \textup{(CL)}, $\sigma_0/k_e$ in place of $\sigma_0$ and the learning law in place of the averaged
one).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(an energy; the network's own modes)} \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{coh}}$)} is the $G$-gradient
    flow of $J^{\mathrm{coh}}_m(\Theta) := m\bar L(\mu_\Theta) + (\sigma_0m/2)\iint W\dd\mu_\Theta\dd\mu_\Theta$,
    $W(u) := R^2\omega(\norm{u}^2_{G^{-1}}/R^2)$ with $\omega' = \psi/2$ (so $\nabla^2W(0) = G^{-1}$), which
    decreases along every solution; its mean-field form is the Wasserstein gradient flow (in the metric $G^{-1}$) of
    $\bar L(\mu) + (\sigma_0/2)\iint W\dd\mu\dd\mu$ --- an aggregation equation in the external potential
    $-\langle f\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta)\rangle_\rho$ with two interactions, the fit's
    $\langle\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta)\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta')\rangle_\rho$ and the cohesion $W$. The leak
    force on $\vartheta_i$ is $\sigma_0\bar\psi_i(m_i - \vartheta_i)$, $\bar\psi_i := (1/m)\sum_j\psi_{ij}$ the
    kernel mass of the cloud at $\vartheta_i$ and $m_i := \sum_j\psi_{ij}\vartheta_j/\sum_j\psi_{ij}$ the
    kernel-weighted mean: the mean-shift step of the cloud's own kernel density, so the leak pulls the network toward
    the modes of its own parameter distribution --- no prior, no atom count, no plan, no cells. The self-interaction
    vanishes ($\psi(0)\cdot0$), so the empirical measure of \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{coh}}$)} solves the
    mean-field equation exactly, and Theorem D's finite-horizon propagation of chaos holds (the velocity field is
    Lipschitz in $\vartheta$ and in $\mu$ for $W_1$, Dobrushin's estimate). With the Gaussian kernel
    $J^{\mathrm{coh}}_m$ is real-analytic (the saturated network is), so every bounded solution converges to a single
    stationary point ({\L}ojasiewicz's theorem, as for Proposition D$'''$.20). The forces sum to zero: the leak is
    internal and gives no a-priori bound.
  \item \emph{(no bias at any clustered configuration)} Let $\Theta$ be replicated by $K$ classes with atoms
    $\vartheta_1,\dots,\vartheta_K$ and masses $w_k = n_k/m$. With the finite range and the atoms pairwise at least
    $R$ apart, the leak vanishes on $\Theta$, and $\Theta$ is stationary iff $(\vartheta_k)$ is a critical point of
    the unregularised weighted loss
    $L_w := \frac12\langle\abs{f - \sum_kw_k\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_k)}^2\rangle_\rho$ --- for every $K$ and every
    $w$, with no prior and no $K_0$ fixed in advance; in particular the lift of an exact representation
    $\vartheta^e(w)$ is stationary at every representable sample ($w\in W$,
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}), as under the adaptive prior
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}(iii)). With the Gaussian kernel the classes attract each other with
    the forces $-\sigma_0w_l\psi(d^2_{kl}/R^2)(\vartheta_k - \vartheta_l)$ ($d_{kl}$ the atoms' distance), and at a
    nondegenerate critical point ($\tilde A_w\succ0$, the whitened weighted Hessian of $L_w$) the stationary point
    continues to one at the whitened distance $\tilde A_w^{-1}b + O(\abs{b}^2)$ from it, $b$ the whitened inter-class
    force: a bias at most of the order
    $\sigma_0\norm{\tilde A_w^{-1}}\max_k\sum_lw_ld_{kl}e^{-d^2_{kl}/(2R^2)}$, exponentially small in
    $(\min d_{kl}/R)^2$. No bias is the finite range's property; the Gaussian kernel pays this small bias for the
    merging of (v).
  \item \emph{(the leak acts exactly on the unexcited subspace)} At a configuration as in (ii) (the finite range), in
    the whitened weighted coordinates, the linearisation of \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{coh}}$)} is block
    diagonal: on the separations of class $k$ ($\sum_{i\in k}u_i = 0$) it is $-(\sigma_0w_kI - R^G_k)$, with
    multiplicity $n_k - 1$,
    $R^G_k := G^{1/2}\langle r\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_k)\rangle_\rho G^{1/2}$ the residual
    curvature; on the class means it is $-\tilde A_w$, the linearised unregularised weighted $K$-atom flow, the leak
    absent. The separations are the directions in which the plant does not see the network ($D\fhat = 0$ on them),
    and where $H_L(w)\succ0$ they are all of them: the leak acts exactly on the unexcited subspace. At an exact
    representation the separations of class $k$ decay at $\sigma_0w_k$ and the collective modes at $h_j(w)$ (the
    eigenvalues of $H_L(w)$), for every $w\in W$; the class means' stationary response to a persistent force $F$ is
    $\tilde A_w^{-1}F$ --- the adaptive prior's (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}(i)--(iii)): no leak
    opposes it. The restoring force is linear only near the class: under the compact kernel the pair force
    $\psi(d^2/R^2)d$ is largest at $d = R/\sqrt7$ and vanishes at $R$, so a separation pushed beyond $R$ is not
    restored.
  \item \emph{(splitting, and no over-splitting)} The splitting margin of class $k$ is
    $\sigma_0w_k - \lambda_{\max}(R^G_k)$. For a scalar amplitude per kernel at the gauge transform
    $\vartheta^b(w) = G_w\vartheta^b(q)$ of a nondegenerate critical point $\vartheta^b(q)$
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:28]{Proposition D$'''$.28}) it is $\sigma_0w_k - (q_k/w_k)\lambda^+_k$, so a class can
    split iff $\sigma_0w_k^2 < q_k\lambda^+_k$ --- the leak's strength sets the resolution at which a class becomes
    unstable where the residual's curvature asks for a kernel, light classes first; at an exact representation
    $\lambda^+ = 0$ and no class splits, whatever $\sigma_0 > 0$: an exact representation is never over-split (as
    linear stability). The unstable separations of a class are $(n_k - 1)$-fold degenerate, so the linear theory does
    not choose which particles leave; the kernel's concavity ($\omega'' = \psi'/2 < 0$) makes shedding single
    particles the cheapest split (Numerics (G)).
  \item \emph{(twins and dead classes under the Gaussian kernel)} At a degenerate exact representation --- twins (two
    classes with the same centre and width) or a dead class ($a_k = 0$) --- the fit is neutral along a curve (the
    transfer of amplitude between the twins at fixed $w_ka_k + w_la_l$; the dead class's centre and width), and along
    it only the cohesion acts. For twins with no third class within the kernel's reach, the fit relaxed
    quasi-statically along the curve, the twins' amplitude difference $\Delta\alpha$ contracts at the rate
    \[
      \sigma_0\psi(\Delta\alpha^2/(G_\alpha R^2))\cdot\frac{(w_ka'_k + w_la'_l)^2}{w_ka'^2_k + w_la'^2_l},\qquad
      a' := \dd a/\dd\alpha,
    \]
    so they merge, for unsaturated twins on the time scale
    $G_\alpha R^2e^{\Delta\alpha^2/(2G_\alpha R^2)}/(\sigma_0(w_k + w_l)\Delta\alpha^2)$
    ($\Delta\alpha^2\gg G_\alpha R^2$). A third class within reach of one twin can win: twins need not merge, and a
    degenerate exact representation can be a saddle of $J^{\mathrm{coh}}_m$ that the flow leaves toward a biased
    configuration (Numerics (I)). A dead class moves under the attraction of the live ones, a Gaussian mixture in its
    coordinates whose weights carry the live classes' amplitude coordinates ($w_le^{-\alpha_l^2/(2G_\alpha R^2)}$);
    when the components are farther apart than a few $R$ its local minima lie near them, where it becomes a twin of a
    live class --- on the time scale $R^2e^{d^2/(2R^2)}/(\sigma_0wd^2)$, $d\gg R$ its distance from the nearest live
    class and $w$ that class's mass. With the finite range, dead and duplicated classes farther than $R$ from the
    others persist.
  \item \emph{(the real loop)} In \textup{(CL)} with the cohesion leak (finite range) the lift of an exact
    representation $\vartheta^e(w)$ by classes pairwise at least $R$ apart is a $T_0$-periodic solution with
    $e\equiv0$ at every representable sample; its Floquet exponents are $\sigma_0w_k/k_e$ exactly on the separations
    of class $k$ (multiplicity $(n_k - 1)p$) and, on the collapsed subspace, those of the unregularised weighted
    $K$-atom loop, which are \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}'s at $\eta = \infty$ ---
    $(1/k_e)[h_j(w) + c_j(w)/k_e^2 + O(k_e^{-4})]$ under the derivative condition
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:26a]{Lemma D$'''$.26a}). So perfect tracking is exponentially stable (locally, for each
    $m$) at the rate $(1/k_e)\min\{\sigma_0\min_kw_k,\ h_1(w) + c_1(w)/k_e^2 + \dots\}$. The replicated configuration
    is invariant under a measurement noise common to the particles, and
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(ii)--(iii) hold at $\eta = \infty$ (the floor
    $(N_0/2k_e)[\tr Q + \tr H^2]$, the errors-in-variables response $H^{-1}$), the separations' noise threshold
    scaled by $w_k$ in the variance ($\sqrt{w_k}$ in the standard deviation) --- the softest of the leaks; with the
    desired regressor the regressor's own term leaves their noise drift, whose threshold is then bounded below
    uniformly in $k_e$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:33]{Corollary D$'''$.33}). Under a dither $\tau$ (the averaged loop
    with $\sqrt{2\tau G}$ per particle) the separations of class $k$ are an Ornstein--Uhlenbeck process of stiffness
    $\sigma_0w_k$: the particles' stationary variance about the class mean is $(1 - 1/n_k)\tau/(\sigma_0w_k)$ per
    whitened coordinate, against $(1 - 1/n_k)\tau/\sigma_0$ under a leak toward a prior and the free diffusion
    $2\tau s$ without a leak.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) $W$ is even, so
\[
  \partial_{\vartheta_i}[(\sigma_0/(2m))\sum_{j,l}W(\vartheta_j - \vartheta_l)]
  = (\sigma_0/m)\sum_j\nabla W(\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j),
\]
and $G\nabla W(u) = R^2\omega'(z)\cdot2u/R^2 = \psi(z)u$ ($z = \norm{u}^2_{G^{-1}}/R^2$); with
$\partial_{\vartheta_i}(m\bar L) = -\langle r\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i)\rangle_\rho$ the
right-hand side of \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{coh}}$)} is $-G\nabla_{\vartheta_i}J^{\mathrm{coh}}_m$, and
$\dd J^{\mathrm{coh}}_m/\dd s = -\sum_i\norm{\nabla_{\vartheta_i}J^{\mathrm{coh}}_m}^2_G\le0$.
$\sum_j\psi_{ij}(\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j) = m\bar\psi_i(\vartheta_i - m_i)$, and
$\sum_i\sum_j\psi_{ij}(\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j) = 0$ by antisymmetry. The mean-field form replaces $(1/m)\sum_j$ by
the integral against $\mu$: $\bar L(\mu) = \frac12\langle f^2\rangle_\rho - \int\langle f\tilde\Phi\rangle_\rho\dd\mu
+ \frac12\iint\langle\tilde\Phi\tilde\Phi'\rangle_\rho\dd\mu\dd\mu$, the velocity is
$v[\mu](\vartheta) = G\nabla_\vartheta\langle r_\mu\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta)\rangle_\rho
- \sigma_0\int\psi(\norm{\vartheta - \vartheta'}^2_{G^{-1}}/R^2)(\vartheta - \vartheta')\dd\mu(\vartheta')
= -G\nabla_\vartheta(\delta\mathcal{F}/\delta\mu)$, $\mathcal{F} := \bar L + (\sigma_0/2)\iint W\dd\mu\dd\mu$, and on
$\mu_\Theta$ it is the right-hand side of \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{coh}}$)} at the particles, the term
$j = i$ vanishing. (ii) Inside a class $\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j = 0$; across classes $\psi = 0$ at distances
$\ge R$. The learning drift of a particle of class $k$ is
$G\langle r_w\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_k)\rangle_\rho = -Gw_k^{-1}\nabla_{\vartheta_k}L_w$, which
vanishes for all $k$ iff $(\vartheta_k)$ is critical for $L_w$. With the Gaussian kernel the class drift is
$-Gw_k^{-1}\nabla_{\vartheta_k}L_w + b_k$, $b_k := -\sigma_0\sum_lw_l\psi(d^2_{kl}/R^2)(\vartheta_k - \vartheta_l)$;
in the whitened weighted coordinates its linearisation at the critical point is $-\tilde A_w$ (plus the derivative of
$b$, of the order of $b/R$), and the implicit function theorem gives the continuation with the stated first-order
displacement and the bound. (iii) A separation $u$ ($\sum_{i\in k}u_i = 0$, the other classes fixed) leaves $\fhat$
unchanged at first order --- $D\fhat[u] = (w_k/n_k)\sum_{i\in k}\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_k)u_i = 0$
--- so the residual is unchanged and the learning drift varies by $GR_ku_i$; the leak's variation is
$-(\sigma_0/m)\sum_{j\in k}\psi(0)(u_i - u_j) = -\sigma_0w_ku_i$ ($\psi'$ terms vanish at coincident particles, and
across classes $\psi$ and $\psi'$ vanish at distances $\ge R$). A perturbation of the class means moves the leak not
at all (inside a class all particles move together; across classes $\psi = \psi' = 0$), and the learning drift's
variation is the unregularised $K$-atom flow's; the two subspaces do not mix (the learning drift's cross terms vanish
as in Proposition D$'''$.5(i)). Whitening, $y = \sqrt{w_k}G^{-1/2}\cdot$, gives the blocks; $D\fhat$ restricted to
the class means is the weighted Jacobian whose Gram matrix is $H_L(w)$, so where $H_L(w)\succ0$ the kernel of
$D\fhat$ is exactly the separations. At an exact representation $r = 0$, $R_k = 0$ and $\tilde A_w = H_L(w)$. The
stationary response follows from the linearisation;
$\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}d}[(1 - d^2/R^2)^3d] = (1 - d^2/R^2)^2(1 - 7d^2/R^2)$. (iv)
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:28]{Proposition D$'''$.28}(iii): $R_k(\vartheta^b(w)) = (q_k/w_k)R_k(\vartheta^b(q))$; the
separations of class $k$ are $u_i = c_iv$ with $\sum c_i = 0$ along each eigenvector $v$, all with the same linear
rate. (v) Twins: the network depends on their amplitudes only through $p = w_ka(\alpha_k) + w_la(\alpha_l)$, and their
other coordinates coincide; with $u := \langle r\varphi\rangle_\rho$ common to both,
$\dot\alpha_k = G_\alpha a'_ku - \sigma_0w_l\psi\Delta\alpha$ and
$\dot\alpha_l = G_\alpha a'_lu + \sigma_0w_k\psi\Delta\alpha$ ($\psi := \psi(\Delta\alpha^2/(G_\alpha R^2))$; no
third class within reach), so
$\dot p = G_\alpha u(w_ka'^2_k + w_la'^2_l) - \sigma_0\psi\Delta\alpha w_kw_l(a'_k - a'_l)$. The fit relaxes $p$ at
the rate $G_\alpha\langle\varphi^2\rangle_\rho(w_ka'^2_k + w_la'^2_l)$, fast against $\sigma_0\psi$, so to leading
order $\dot p = 0$ fixes $u$, and
$\dd(\Delta\alpha)/\dd s = G_\alpha u(a'_k - a'_l) - \sigma_0\psi(w_k + w_l)\Delta\alpha
= -\sigma_0\psi\Delta\alpha(w_ka'_k + w_la'_l)^2/(w_ka'^2_k + w_la'^2_l)$; for unsaturated twins
$z := \Delta\alpha^2/(2G_\alpha R^2)$ obeys $\dd z/\dd s = -2\sigma_0(w_k + w_l)ze^{-z}$, so the time from
$z_0\gg1$ is $e^{z_0}/(2\sigma_0(w_k + w_l)z_0)(1 + O(1/z_0))$. A third class at the distance $d$ from one twin adds
to that twin a force of the order $\sigma_0w\psi(d^2/R^2)d$, which wins when $d$ is below the twins' own distance. A
dead class: $L_w$ does not depend on its centre and width, so $J^{\mathrm{coh}}_m$ varies there only through its
attraction to the live classes, a Gaussian mixture in those coordinates (its amplitude coordinate fixed, the live
classes' entering the weights); a mixture's modes away from its components need overlapping components, so for
components farther apart than a few $R$ its local minima lie within $O(Re^{-d^2/(2R^2)})$ of the components, and
the descent toward the nearest has the rate $\sigma_0w\psi(d^2/R^2)$: $z := d^2/(2R^2)$ obeys
$\dd z/\dd s\approx-2\sigma_0wze^{-z}$, whence the time scale as for twins. (vi) As
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}: along the lift $e\equiv0$, the separations do not move $\fhat$ at
first order and the second-derivative term of the learning law vanishes with $e$, so a separation stays a separation
with $\delta\dot\vartheta_i = -(\sigma_0w_k/k_e)\delta\vartheta_i$; the collapsed subspace is invariant and on it the
leak vanishes, which is the $\eta = \infty$ adaptive prior's collapsed variational equation;
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:26a]{Lemma D$'''$.26a} applies to it. The noise:
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(i) needs only that every particle of a class sees the same signals,
and its (ii)--(iii) only the collapsed linearisation; the separations' drift there is opposed by $\sigma_0w_k$
instead of $\sigma_0$. The dither: the offsets from the class mean are forced by $(1 - 1/n_k)\cdot2\tau$ per whitened
coordinate.
\end{proof}
\status{(i)--(iii) proved, exactly; (iv) proved for the stationary points and their linearisation, the split's
pattern explained (the degeneracy and the kernel's concavity), its dynamics numerical; (v) the twins' contraction rate
proved to leading order in $\sigma_0\psi$ against the fit's relaxation (checked to 0.04\%, Numerics (I)), their merger
only without a third class within reach (a counterexample, Numerics (I)); the dead class's motion and time scale
stated, its merger not proved; (vi) the lift and its separation exponents exact, the collapsed exponents by
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26} and \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26a]{Lemma D$'''$.26a}, the noise statements
as in \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30} (formal where it is). Not proved: the transit from a spread
cloud --- which classes form and with which masses --- and with it any selection of the number of kernels: the count
is the transit's and the schedule's. Under an annealed Gaussian scale it ended at the target's count on the instance
and on well-separated targets whose transit masses were representable, not for bumps closer than the broad scale or
for unrepresentable transit masses, and at a fixed scale not at all (Numerics (C), (H), (J), (K)); the replicator
weight law of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34} removes the mass obstruction. Nor is the basin
uniform in $m$ proved. Checked: \code{tests/test_cohesion_leak.py} (the drift equal to $-G\nabla J^{\mathrm{coh}}_m$ by central differences and
$J^{\mathrm{coh}}_m$ decreasing along the flow, for both kernels; the forces summing to zero and equal to
$\sigma_0\times$ the kernel mass $\times$ the mean-shift vector; at the exact representation for the counts
$(3,2,2)$ and $(4,3,3)$ the drift zero and the Jacobian's spectrum the predicted one --- the separations at
$-\sigma_0w_k$, the class means the unregularised $K$-atom flow's --- to $10^{-9}$; at the best one-atom fit the top
separation eigenvalue $\lambda^+ - \sigma_0w$, stable at $\sigma_0$ and split at $\lambda^+/2$; twins contracting at
the predicted rate to 1\% under the Gaussian kernel and persisting beyond the compact range; the kernels' range and
scale (a pair at $0.99R$ attracted, at $1.01R$ not; the Gaussian force at $R$ $e^{-1/2}$ of its linear part); the
clusters of known configurations; the real loop's lift $T_0$-periodic and a separation contracting by
$e^{-\sigma_0w_kT_0/k_e}$ per period to $10^{-6}$).}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.32 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/cohesion_leak.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_cohesion_leak.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/cohesion_leak.md}). The three-bump
instance ($\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$), the averaged slow loop, the midpoint rule ($\mathrm{d}t = 0.25$),
single-linkage clusters at 0.25. (A) At each of the 15 count vectors of $m = 15$ in $W$ the lift of the exact
representation $\vartheta^e(w)$ is stationary --- the largest drift $2.8\cdot10^{-17}$ (compact kernel, $R = 1$) and
$6.2\cdot10^{-10}$ (Gaussian, $R = 1$) --- and the Jacobian's spectrum is (iii)'s to $2.8\cdot10^{-11}$ (the finite
differences' error) and to $4.6\cdot10^{-9}$ (the Gaussian kernel's cross-class terms, which (iii) omits): the
separations at $\sigma_0w_k$ ($1.41\cdot10^{-3}$--$3.77\cdot10^{-3}$), the collective modes the unregularised flow's
(the slowest $2.2\cdot10^{-4}$--$4.2\cdot10^{-4}$), the atoms at least 5.80 apart. The Gaussian kernel's bias (Newton
on the class drift, counts $(5,5,5)$) is $2.1\cdot10^{-8}$ (whitened; the fit error $6.6\cdot10^{-10}$) at $R = 1$,
$1.3\cdot10^{-3}$ ($3.9\cdot10^{-5}$) at $R = 1.5$ and 0.068 ($2.1\cdot10^{-3}$) at $R = 2$, the first order
$\tilde A_w^{-1}b$ to $6\cdot10^{-8}$, $9\cdot10^{-4}$ and 1.0\% --- and 56\% off at $R = 3$, where the classes
overlap. With the fourth bump ($\varepsilon = 0.3$; the three-atom approximant $\vartheta^b(q)$, the fit error 0.017,
$\lambda^+_k = 1.00\cdot10^{-4}$, $2.86\cdot10^{-4}$, $1.08\cdot10^{-4}$) the predicted margins
$\sigma_0w_k - (q_k/w_k)\lambda^+_k$ are the Jacobian's to $2\cdot10^{-11}$ (the finite differences' error) at three
count vectors. At $\sigma_0/8$ the counts $(6,4,5)$ have one negative margin, $-1.22\cdot10^{-4}$, and that class
splits, shedding one particle: its diameter grows from $2.2\cdot10^{-3}$ to 1.0 over $10^5$ slow units, the clusters
$3\to4$ (the masses 0.200 and 0.067), the fit error $0.017\to0.0148$. At $(5,5,5)$ the smallest margin,
$+8.7\cdot10^{-6}$, makes that class's diameter decay at $8.8\cdot10^{-6}$ ($6.9\cdot10^{-4}\to3.4\cdot10^{-4}$ from
$s = 2\cdot10^4$ to $10^5$); at $\sigma_0$ every class collapses to rounding. Twins (bump 3 carried by classes of
masses $3/15$ and $2/15$, the weighted amplitude shared $3:1$, 2.31 apart) merge under the Gaussian kernel ($R = 1$:
their distance $6.1\cdot10^{-3}$ at $s = 4000$, 0 by $s = 18000$, the fit error $4.5\cdot10^{-10}$), on (v)'s time
scale (${\sim}10^3$), and persist beyond the compact range (2.31 throughout). A dead class (amplitude 0, mass $2/15$,
at 5.19 from the nearest live class, of mass $4/15$) joins it under the Gaussian kernel at $R = 2$ (to rounding by
$s = 16000$; the fit error then $1.6\cdot10^{-3}$, the inter-class bias at that scale) and at $R = 1.5$ (at
$s\approx2.8\cdot10^4$, (v)'s time scale $1.8\cdot10^4$; the fit error $1.7\cdot10^{-5}$); at $R = 1$, where the live
classes do not overlap and the time scale is $1.4\cdot10^7$, it does not move in $4\cdot10^4$ slow units, nor under
the compact kernel. (I) The twins' contraction: along (A)'s twins at $R = 1$ over $s\in[200,2000]$,
$\ln(\Delta\alpha(200)/\Delta\alpha(2000)) = 1.2949$ against (v)'s rate integrated along the run, 1.2943 (0.04\%).
Twins far apart do not merge on the runs' time scale: with bump 3's amplitude shared $0.9 : 0.1$ (5.28 apart) and
$0.97 : 0.03$ (8.19) their distance does not change in $4\cdot10^4$ slow units ((v)'s rate at their distance
$1.3\cdot10^{-9}$ and $3\cdot10^{-18}$; $1.45\cdot10^{-4}$ for (A)'s twins). A third class within reach wins:
opposite-sign twins of bump 3 (counts $(5,3,5,2)$, amplitudes $+3.8$ and $-3.5$, 12.7 apart), the light twin at the
amplitude coordinate of the class of bump 2 (3.63 from it), jittered by $10^{-3}$, do not merge in $4\cdot10^5$ slow
units (their distance 11.5--12.9); the light twin is drawn toward the class of bump 2 (its centre $1.5\to0.88$) and
the fit error grows from $3.2\cdot10^{-4}$ to $4.5\cdot10^{-3}$, a biased four-class configuration; the annealed
schedule does the same (11.7 apart at the end, the fit error $5.0\cdot10^{-3}$). (B) From
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}'s warm clouds ($m = 15$, $60$, $240$ against $M = 510$, four seeds,
6000 slow units) both kernels remove the floor as the adaptive prior does, without a prior:
$\norm{\fhat_m - \fhat_M}^2$ at $s = 6000$ is $1.9\cdot10^{-10}$--$5.4\cdot10^{-8}$ at $m = 15$ over the three
representable samples, under both kernels and the adaptive prior alike (the fixed prior's floor
$3.0\cdot10^{-4}$--$2.4\cdot10^{-3}$), and $2.4\cdot10^{-10}$--$7.0\cdot10^{-10}$ and
$5.1\cdot10^{-14}$--$2.2\cdot10^{-9}$ at $m = 60$ and $240$ (the adaptive prior's
$2.4\cdot10^{-10}$--$6.2\cdot10^{-10}$, $7.2\cdot10^{-14}$--$2.2\cdot10^{-9}$; the fixed prior's floor
$1.1\cdot10^{-4}$--$9.9\cdot10^{-4}$, $4.9\cdot10^{-6}$--$8.2\cdot10^{-5}$); the class means lie within 0.012, 0.0010
and 0.0045 (whitened) of $\vartheta^e(w^m)$. The sample of seed 3 at $m = 15$, the counts $(8,4,3)$, lies on the
boundary of $W$ and is not representable: there every leak but the fixed prior ends at
$7.0\cdot10^{-5}$--$7.1\cdot10^{-5}$ (the fit error 0.013; the fixed prior $5.5\cdot10^{-3}$ and 0.119) --- no leak
repairs an unrepresentable sample ---, and the compact kernel's run ends with a fourth class (a class diameter of
0.81). (C) From broad starts ($m = 60$, six seeds; $m = 120$, three) the annealed Gaussian cohesion leak ($R = 2$ for
20000 slow units, then $R = 1$ for 20000) ends at three classes with the exact representation's centres and widths in
all nine runs, the fit error $1.8\cdot10^{-7}$--$2.3\cdot10^{-6}$, the masses the transit's (0.217--0.500). The
nearest-atom adaptive prior told the right count ($K = 3$) does as well ($4.2\cdot10^{-9}$--$2.1\cdot10^{-6}$); told
$K = 2$ it ends at three biased classes (0.131--0.191), told $K = 4$ at four, one bump split
($5.4\cdot10^{-4}$--$5.6\cdot10^{-3}$); the $\sigma$-modification ends at three biased classes (one run four; 0.24),
no leak at 40--48 classes ($8.2\cdot10^{-4}$--$1.6\cdot10^{-3}$), and the compact kernel at $R = 1$ without the broad
stage at 17--22 ($6.0\cdot10^{-4}$--$1.5\cdot10^{-3}$): the transit's fragments lie farther apart than $R$, where the
leak no longer acts. (J) The schedule, not the kernel, gives the count: at a fixed Gaussian scale the same broad starts
end at 4--6 classes ($R = 1$; the three bumps' classes and light fragments of 0.017--0.083, the fit error
$4.8\cdot10^{-4}$--$1.6\cdot10^{-3}$, six seeds) or at three biased classes ($R = 2$;
$7.8\cdot10^{-4}$--$1.4\cdot10^{-3}$, the order of (A)'s bias at that scale, three seeds); the annealed schedules
$R = 1.5\to1$, $2\to0.5$ and $3\to1$ (seed 0) end at three classes, $2.4\cdot10^{-8}$--$5.9\cdot10^{-6}$. (D) The real
loop ($k_e = 25$): the lift of $\vartheta^e(w)$, $w = (6,5,4)/15$, returns after a period to $7.8\cdot10^{-16}$, and
its 45 slow Floquet exponents --- 36 separations at $\sigma_0w_k/k_e = 7.55\cdot10^{-5}$, $9.43\cdot10^{-5}$,
$1.13\cdot10^{-4}$ and 9 collective --- are the predicted ones to $5.5\cdot10^{-6}$ (relative; median
$1.5\cdot10^{-7}$; the monodromy's finite differences' error). From the warm clouds (seed 0, 3000 time units) the rms
tracking error falls to $5.8\cdot10^{-4}$ ($m = 15$) and $2.4\cdot10^{-4}$ ($m = 60$) under the cohesion leak, against
$1.3\cdot10^{-3}$ and $3.9\cdot10^{-4}$ under the fixed prior. (E) Under the dither $\tau = 10^{-5}$ ($m = 60$ from the
lift of $\vartheta^e(q)$, three seeds, 20000 slow units) the within-class spread per whitened coordinate, averaged
over the second half, is 0.068 (compact) and 0.063 (Gaussian) against (vi)'s
$\sqrt{(1 - 1/n_k)\tau/(\sigma_0w_k)} = 0.0635$ ($n_k = 20$) --- the compact kernel softening at that spread,
$\psi = (1 - z)^3\approx1 - 3z$ ---, 0.036 under the fixed prior ($\sqrt{(1 - 1/n_k)\tau/\sigma_0} = 0.0366$) and 0.030
under the $\sigma$-modification, while without a leak it diffuses (0.44--0.56 at $s = 20000$); the class means'
whitened deviation is 0.020 (the fixed prior's 0.0085), the fit error $1.9\cdot10^{-3}$ ($1.5\cdot10^{-3}$). (F) On
the cogging servo of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:29]{Proposition D$'''$.29} ($\sigma_0 = 0.01$, $m = 64$, broad starts,
three seeds; the Gaussian kernel at $R = 0.7$, then 0.35), whose torque no finite mixture represents exactly, the
cohesion leak settles on seven or eight classes inside the saturation ($\abs{a}/a_{\max}\le0.84$) with the fit error
0.14\%--0.35\% after 80000 slow units --- below the four-kernel designs of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:29]{Proposition D$'''$.29} (1.46\% inside, 0.64\% at the saturation), above the best
equal-weight six- and eight-kernel fits ($9.4\cdot10^{-6}$ and $3.3\cdot10^{-7}$, both saturated or nearly): the count
is the transit's, not the best configuration of that count, and its smallest classes are single particles. (G) The
resolution (the fourth bump, $m = 30$ from the jittered lift of $\vartheta^b(q)$ with the counts $(10,10,10)$, the
compact kernel at $R = 1$, $\sigma$ halved every $10^5$ slow units from $\sigma_0$ to $\sigma_0/64$ with a jitter
$10^{-3}$ renewed at each stage, three seeds). At $w = q$, (iv) puts the first split in the middle class at
$\sigma = 3\lambda^+_2 = 0.121\sigma_0$: down to $\sigma_0/8$ the three classes stay, the fit error 0.0170, the middle
class's margin there $-2.0\cdot10^{-6}$ to $+9.3\cdot10^{-7}$ ($8.7\cdot10^{-6}$ without the jitter, (A)); at
$\sigma_0/16$ it is $-1.24\cdot10^{-4}$ to $-1.40\cdot10^{-4}$ and the class splits in every seed. Over the 21 stages
no class with a positive margin split, every class of two or more particles whose margin was below
$-5\cdot10^{-5}$ split within its stage (8 of 8), and of those between $-5\cdot10^{-5}$ and 0 --- where the jitter
grows by $e^{\abs{\mathrm{margin}}\cdot10^5} = 1.2$--$140$ over a stage --- 3 of 7; a class counts as split when a
piece passes the clustering threshold 0.25 from the jitter $10^{-3}$, a growth by 250 within $10^5$ slow units, i.e.\
a margin below $-\ln(250)/10^5 = -5.5\cdot10^{-5}$, which is where the tallies change. The split is not a halving: the
unstable class sheds one to three single particles --- (iv)'s degeneracy leaves the pattern to the nonlinear terms, and
the kernel's concavity makes shedding the cheapest: for a class of ten split by `$k$ particles at $+d$, $10 - k$ at
$-kd/(10 - k)$' at a fixed $\sum\norm{u_i}^2 = 0.5$ the cohesion energy is 0.43, 0.62, 0.70, 0.74 of its quadratic
part for $k = 1$, $2$, $3$, $5$ (compact; Gaussian 0.87, 0.93, 0.94, 0.95), the same for all $k$ at $10^{-4}$. A
particle of mass $1/30$ carries at most $a_{\max}/30 = 0.13$ of amplitude, less than the fourth bump's 0.3, so the
singletons do not represent it; the middle and third classes move toward it (centres 0.19--0.22 and 1.07--1.16 at
$\sigma_0/64$) and the singletons fill the flanks they leave (centres $-0.29$ to 0.02 and 1.69--1.76,
$\abs{a}/a_{\max}$ up to 0.91). The fit error falls --- 0.0151--0.0152 at $\sigma_0/16$, 0.0141 at $\sigma_0/32$,
0.0028--0.0031 at $\sigma_0/64$ --- but the count overshoots: 8--10 classes at $\sigma_0/64$, five to seven of them
single particles, for a target of four bumps. $\sigma_0$ sets the resolution as (iv) says; which classes form, and
with which masses, the shedding decides --- the transit, which is not proved. (H) Other targets (the annealed Gaussian
schedule of (C), broad starts, $m = 60$, three seeds each): two bumps ($\sum_k\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} = 0.45$) end at two
classes with the fit error $8.9\cdot10^{-9}$--$1.6\cdot10^{-8}$ in all three runs; the four-bump $f_\varepsilon$ of
(A) and (G) at three classes with the fit error 0.0174 in all three, near the three-atom approximant's 0.0170 --- the
fourth bump lies below the resolution $\sigma_0$, and its width is the bound $s_{\min} = 0.3$, so $f_\varepsilon$ has
no exact representation; five bumps ($s_{\min} = 0.2$; $\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} = 0.225$, $0.15$, $0.175$, $0.125$, $0.2$,
$\Sigma = 0.875$) at 6--9 classes with the fit error 0.045--0.118. An exact representation needs
$w_k > \abs{A_k}/a_{\max}$ for every bump, which leaves 0.125 of the mass free, and the transit's masses miss it: in
every run two or three of the three inner bumps get less mass than they need (0.067--0.167 against 0.125--0.175),
their classes saturated ($\abs{a}/a_{\max}$ 0.96--1.00), and the outer bumps are carried by pairs of classes (one pair
in seed 0, two in seeds 1 and 2). (K) Where the count fails and where it holds (the same schedule): three equal bumps
--- three classes in 3 of 3, the fit error $9.4\cdot10^{-5}$--$1.8\cdot10^{-4}$, the Gaussian kernel's bias between
same-sign classes 1.5 apart in the centre: (L) it stays at $9.39\cdot10^{-5}$ for $2\cdot10^5$ more slow units and
falls to $2.5\cdot10^{-13}$ under the compact kernel; four alternating bumps ($\sum_k\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} = 0.46$) ---
four classes in 3 of 3, $9.6\cdot10^{-3}$--$1.1\cdot10^{-2}$, a slow transient, not a bias: (L) the fit error falls
geometrically, to $6.1\cdot10^{-4}$ after $2\cdot10^5$ more slow units ($3.4\cdot10^{-4}$ after the compact stage), the
classes' weighted amplitudes the bumps' to $10^{-4}$; three heavy bumps ($\sum_k\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} = 0.95$, 0.05 of
the mass free) --- 3--5 classes, 0.058--0.20, the masses short again; two bumps closer than the broad scale ($\pm0.45$,
the widths 0.4) --- the broad stage merges them into one class of width 0.67--0.72 that the fine stage keeps (its
margin positive, $6.5\cdot10^{-3}$--$7.1\cdot10^{-3}$: a stable biased configuration), with one or two stray
particles, 1--3 classes, 0.098--0.13. So the count is the target's when the bumps are farther apart than the broad
scale and the transit's masses are representable, and the approximant's below the resolution; the scale and the
masses, the memory of the transit, decide the rest --- the masses are \hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition
D$'''$.34}'s remedy, the scale is not.
\end{sloppypar}

% Corollary D'''.33 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-23 version, as committed at
% 1ddff8c; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/eiv_remedy.md, Part D). The references [S53] and [S61]
% (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) are the bibitems S53 and S61 of refs.tex.
\begin{thm}{Corollary}{D$'''$.33}[the desired regressor keeps the replicated configuration attracting under
measurement noise]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:33}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30} let the adaptive law's regressor be evaluated at
the reference --- the desired regressor (DR) or the desired compensation adaptation law (DR*) of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}(iv) --- and let $s_k$ be the stiffness of class $k$'s separations:
$\sigma_0/k_e$ under the fixed and the adaptive prior, $\sigma_0w_k/k_e$ under the cohesion leak
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}(vi)). Write
$M_k := G^{1/2}\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x_m;\vartheta_k)G^{1/2}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the drift)} With the regressor at $x_m$ the law's Jacobian on a separation of class $k$ is
    $\varepsilon_mG^{1/2}\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x_m;\vartheta_k(t))G^{1/2}$: the regressor's own term
    $\langle\E[\varepsilon_m^2]G^{1/2}\partial_x\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi G^{1/2}\rangle$ of
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(i), which does not decrease with $k_e$ and carries the measured
    regressor's drift, is absent. To second order in $\nu$ the separation's mean drift is $S_k + C_k$:
    $S_k = \langle\E[e]M_k\rangle$ (at the lift $\E[e]$ is MR's, the regressor entering only the parameters'
    equation) and the cross term
    $C_k = \langle G^{1/2}\sum_l\E[\varepsilon_m\delta\vartheta_{kl}]\partial_{\vartheta_l}\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi G^{1/2}\rangle$,
    zero at the lift, which builds up within a period as the class atom fluctuates --- negligible against the
    measured regressor's drift, of the order of DR's and DR*'s. A separation's exponent along a unit $\theta$ adds the
    nonnegative variance term $\E\norm{P_\perp v_1(t)}^2/t$, $v_1(t) = \int_0^t\varepsilon_mM_k\theta$ and
    $P_\perp = I - \theta\theta^\T$ (to second order
    $\E\log\norm{u(t)}/\norm{u(0)} = -s_kt + \int_0^t\theta^\T(S_k + C_k)\theta + \E\norm{P_\perp v_1(t)}^2$,
    $\varepsilon_mM_k$ being symmetric).
  \item \emph{(a threshold bounded uniformly in $k_e$)} At the lift $k_e\E[e] = g$,
    $\abs{g}\le\frac12\abs{\partial^2_xf}\E\nu^2(1 + O(\abs{a}/k_e))$
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}(iv)(c)), so
    $\norm{S_k}\le(\E\nu^2/(2k_e))\norm{\partial^2_xf}_\infty\langle\norm{M_k}\rangle(1 + O(1/k_e))$: the drift and the
    stiffness are both $O(1/k_e)$, and the noise level up to which the replicated configuration attracts at the lift
    is bounded below uniformly in $k_e$ ---
    $\E\nu^2\ge2\sigma_0/(\norm{\partial^2_xf}_\infty\max_k\langle\norm{M_k}\rangle)$ up to $(1 + O(1/k_e))$ ---, and
    constant when the noise's bandwidth is scaled with the gain ($B/k_e$ fixed, where $k_eS_k$ depends on $k_e$ only
    through $a/k_e$). For a sensor of fixed bandwidth the factor $(B - k_e - 2a)/(k_e + B)$ of (iv) changes sign at
    $k_e\approx B$, and DR's drift with it; the thresholds then fall toward the bound's level. Under the measured
    regressor the threshold falls like $1/k_e$ in the variance while $k_e\ll B$, and levels off for $k_e\gg B$, where
    $\E[\varepsilon_m^2]\to\E\nu^2B/k_e$.
  \item \emph{(the stationary point)} At the adaptive prior's stationary point the slow force balance carries the
    parameters' part of the second-order force (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}(iv)(c)):
    $\langle\hat J^\T(g + g_{\mathrm{par}} - \hat J\bar y)\rangle + k_e\langle f_{y,\mathrm{par}}\rangle = 0$,
    $g_{\mathrm{par}}$ and $f_{y,\mathrm{par}}$ the parameters' parts of the error equation's and of the learning
    law's forcing, so (averaged)
    \[
      k_e\E[e] = (I - P)(g + g_{\mathrm{par}}) - \hat JH^{-1}k_e\langle f_{y,\mathrm{par}}\rangle,
    \]
    $P$ the projection onto the regressor's span: the residual of the noise's own target outside the tangent space,
    plus the parameters' part, whose learning-law term lies in the tangent space and is not removed. The drift there
    is $\langle\E[e]M_k\rangle + C_k$ with the stationary covariance's cross term. Both parameters' terms are of the
    order of the noise's residual and need not be smaller: $\E[e]$ does not vanish at the stationary point when $g$
    lies in the tangent space.
  \item \emph{(the sign under DR)} With the compensation $\fhat(y)$ the mean error at the lift is the compensation's
    rectification of the noise, $\E[e] = -(\partial^2_xf/(2k_e))\E\nu^2(B - k_e - 2a)/(k_e + B)$. A Gaussian kernel
    depends on $x$ and its centre $c$ through $x - c$, so $\partial^2_c\tilde\Phi = \partial^2_x\tilde\Phi$, and the
    drift's entry along the centre coordinate $\gamma$, $c = c(\gamma)$, with the metric weight
    $G_c := G_\gamma c'(\gamma)^2$, is
    $-(\E\nu^2G_c/(2k_e))\langle\partial^2_xf\cdot\partial^2_x\tilde\Phi_k\cdot(B - k_e - 2a)/(k_e + B)\rangle
    - c''(\gamma)G_\gamma\langle\E[e]\partial_x\tilde\Phi_k\rangle$; for $B\gg k_e$ its first term is
    $-(\E\nu^2G_c/(2k_e))[w_k\langle(\partial^2_x\tilde\Phi_k)^2\rangle_\rho
    + \sum_{l\ne k}w_l\langle\partial^2_x\tilde\Phi_l\partial^2_x\tilde\Phi_k\rangle_\rho]$
    ($f = \sum_lw_l\tilde\Phi_l$ at the exact representation, $\tilde\Phi_l := \tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_l)$),
    negative when the kernels are separated, while the saturation's term, zero at $c = 0$, has no sign. So at the
    lift a noise fast against the loop ($B > k_e + 2\max\abs{a}$) stiffens the centres of separated kernels where
    that term is dominated, and a slower one ($B < k_e - 2\max\abs{a}$) reverses the factor and the sign. The other
    directions have no sign: the whole matrix is negative definite for separated kernels of alternating sign well
    inside the orbit, as on the instance, and indefinite for same-sign, wide or edge kernels (their widths and
    amplitudes soften). Along the trajectory $\E[e]$ relaxes with the collective modes
    ($k_e\E[e] = g - \hat J\bar y(t)$), the fast modes first, so it passes through the part of $g$ carried by the slow
    modes, which has no sign relation to $\partial^2_xf$: the sign holds near the lift, not along the trajectory.
    With the compensation at the reference (DR*) the mean error is the plant's rectification of its state jitter,
    $+\frac12\partial^2_xf\cdot\operatorname{Var}(e)/(k_e - a)$, and the sign at the lift is reversed.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) With the regressor at $x_m$ the law is $G\nabla_\vartheta(\varepsilon_m\tilde\Phi(x_m;\vartheta_i))$ minus the
leak, and its Jacobian on a separation is $\varepsilon_mG^{1/2}\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x_m;\vartheta_k)G^{1/2}$:
the measurement enters only through $\varepsilon_m$, whose mean is $\E[e]$ ($\E\nu = 0$), and the class atom's
fluctuation through the Hessian, whose correlation with $\varepsilon_m$ is the cross term. A separation does not enter
$\fhat$ at first order, so neither the compensation nor the leak adds a noise term. The exponent: with
$A_1 = \varepsilon_m^{(1)}M_k$, $w = ue^{s_kt}$,
$\log\norm{w} = \ip{\theta}{w_1} + \ip{\theta}{w_2} + \frac12\norm{w_1}^2 - \ip{\theta}{w_1}^2 + O(\nu^3)$, and
$\E\ip{\theta}{w_2} = \int\theta^\T\E[A_2]\theta + \frac12\E\norm{w_1}^2$ for a symmetric $A_1$, whence the variance
term. (ii) The mean of the error equation at second order gives $k_e\E[e] = g - \hat J\bar y$
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}'s proof), at the lift $\bar y = 0$, and the bound on $g$ is
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}(iv)(c)'s; with $B = \beta k_e$ the factor of (iv) is
$(\beta - 1 - 2a/k_e)/(1 + \beta)$. For $k_e\gg B$,
$\E[\varepsilon_m^2] = \E\nu^2(a^2 + k_eB)/(k_e(k_e + B))\to\E\nu^2B/k_e$. (iii) The mean of the learning law adds
the parameters' part $k_e\langle f_{y,\mathrm{par}}\rangle$ (the law's
$\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi\E[\varepsilon_m\delta\vartheta]$) to the least-squares force, and the error equation's
mean forcing is $g + g_{\mathrm{par}}$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}(iv)(c)); stationarity gives
$H\bar y = \langle\hat J^\T(g + g_{\mathrm{par}})\rangle + k_e\langle f_{y,\mathrm{par}}\rangle$, and
$\hat J\bar y = P(g + g_{\mathrm{par}}) + \hat JH^{-1}k_e\langle f_{y,\mathrm{par}}\rangle$. (iv) $\tilde\Phi$ depends
on $x$ and $c$ through $x - c$, so $\partial_c\tilde\Phi = -\partial_x\tilde\Phi$ and
$\partial^2_c\tilde\Phi = \partial^2_x\tilde\Phi$; for $c = c(\gamma)$,
$\partial^2_\gamma\tilde\Phi = c'^2\partial^2_c\tilde\Phi + c''\partial_c\tilde\Phi$, and the quasi-static $\E[e]$ of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}'s proof with $\partial^2_x\fhat = \partial^2_xf$ gives the entry;
DR*'s $\E[e]$ is in \hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}'s proof.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) and (iv)'s formula exact consequences of the second-order expansion --- formal at the order of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(i)'s drift, with its caveats ---; (ii)'s bound proved given (i) and
D$'''$.31(iv)(c)'s bound on $g$; (iii)'s balance formal (averaged), its numbers from the exact periodic second-order
mean; (iv)'s sign proved for the centre direction of separated Gaussian kernels where the saturation's term is
dominated, at the lift; the whole matrix's sign numerical and specific to the target, and lost along the trajectory.
Classical: the desired compensation adaptation law's insensitivity to measurement noise (\cite{S53}); the attraction of
identical uncoupled systems driven by a common noise --- noise-induced synchronisation, decided by the transverse
Lyapunov exponent at second order in the noise, which strong noise can make positive (Teramae--Tanaka,
Goldobin--Pikovsky, Nakao--Arai--Kawamura, \cite{S61}) --- is the frame of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(i)'s threshold and of this corollary; the second-order drift of a
regressor without the measured state is the one-line consequence of a $y$-independent regressor. New: that consequence
for the $K_0$-atom identification --- the separations' drift $O(1/k_e)$ like their stiffness, a threshold bounded
uniformly in $k_e$, which makes the desired regressor the natural partner of the cohesion leak, whose separations are
the softest ($\sigma_0w_k$) --- and the instance's quantification: the sign at the lift, its loss along the
trajectory, the stationary point. Checked: \code{tests/test_eiv_remedy.py} (DR's drift MR's mean-error term alone at
the lift, negative definite in every class, MR's 0.30 in class 3; DR*'s $O(1/k_e)$, $k_e$ times its largest eigenvalue
the same at $k_e = 25$ and 50, and DR*'s drift against an independent quadrature to $10^{-4}$; at the adaptive prior's
stationary point the exact periodic mean's noise and parameters' parts summing to it, the parameters' part dominant and
largely in the tangent space, the drift indefinite; the period maps continuing \code{second_order_transient} to
$10^{-7}$ and DR's drift turning indefinite).}

% Numerics of Corollary D'''.33 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/eiv_remedy.md, Part D).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_eiv_remedy.py} Part D $\to$ \code{results/chaos/eiv_remedy.md}).
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}'s instance ($k_e = 25$, the OU noise $B = 100$), per unit
$\E\nu^2$, real time. (D1) At the lift the separations' drift has the extreme eigenvalues
$[-3.7\cdot10^{-1},+1.5\cdot10^{-2}]$; $[-2.6\cdot10^{-2},+1.0\cdot10^{-2}]$; $[-3.5\cdot10^{-2},+3.0\cdot10^{-1}]$ in
the three classes under MR, $[-1.1\cdot10^{-2},-5.0\cdot10^{-4}]$; $[-1.5\cdot10^{-2},-1.5\cdot10^{-4}]$;
$[-7.2\cdot10^{-3},-3.4\cdot10^{-4}]$ under DR and $[+1.6\cdot10^{-4},+3.8\cdot10^{-3}]$;
$[+5.1\cdot10^{-5},+5.0\cdot10^{-3}]$; $[+1.1\cdot10^{-4},+2.4\cdot10^{-3}]$ under DR*: DR's is negative definite,
DR*'s largest eigenvalue, $+4.98\cdot10^{-3}$, is 60 times below MR's 0.2982 --- the threshold std 0.238 under the
adaptive prior against MR's 0.031, and 0.138 against 0.018 under the cohesion leak's stiffness $\sigma_0w_k$
($w_k = 1/3$). (D2) With $B = 4k_e$ MR's largest eigenvalue is $0.2982\to0.3045$ over $k_e = 25\to800$, its threshold
std $0.031\to0.0054$ ($\propto k_e^{-1/2}$), while $k_e$ times DR's is $-0.0038$ and $k_e$ times DR*'s
0.1245--0.1242 at every $k_e$ (DR*'s threshold 0.238--0.239). At the fixed $B = 100$ DR's drift is indefinite at
$k_e = 100$ and positive definite from $k_e = 200$, its threshold std 0.185, 0.138, 0.121 at $k_e = 200$, $400$, $800$;
DR*'s falls $0.238\to0.113$ and MR's $0.031\to0.014$ (its variance by 4.6 over a factor 32 in $k_e$). $\norm{S_k}$ is
at most 0.37 of (ii)'s bound in these cases (DR 0.253, DR* 0.084 at $B = 4k_e$), which gives the threshold the floor
std $\ge0.069$ for DR and DR*. (D3) For other exactly represented targets at the lift DR's centre entries are negative
for all six; its whole drift is negative definite for the instance and for two opposite bumps, and indefinite for three
separated same-sign bumps (the largest eigenvalue $+2.9\cdot10^{-4}$), wide bumps ($S = 1$; $+5.6\cdot10^{-4}$), bumps
at the orbit's edge ($+2.7\cdot10^{-4}$) and two close same-sign bumps ($+5.0\cdot10^{-4}$). (D4) At the adaptive
prior's stationary point (the exact periodic second-order mean) the mean error's rms is $6.70\cdot10^{-4}$ under DR
and $6.44\cdot10^{-4}$ under DR* --- 2.4\% and 7.0\% of the lift's ---, its parameters' part $6.32\cdot10^{-4}$ and
$6.27\cdot10^{-4}$ and its noise part $2.11\cdot10^{-4}$ and $1.37\cdot10^{-4}$ (the round-22 averaged noise-only
balance $(I - P)g/k_e$ gave $2.34\cdot10^{-4}$ and $2.60\cdot10^{-4}$); its tangent part, $5.80\cdot10^{-4}$ and
$5.81\cdot10^{-4}$, is the parameters' part's. The drift there is indefinite in every class --- DR's
$[-4.35,+3.68]\cdot10^{-5}$; $[-0.13,+0.30]\cdot10^{-5}$; $[-2.82,+2.44]\cdot10^{-5}$ ---, the parameters' part of
$\E[e]$ contributing up to $+2.6\cdot10^{-4}$ and the cross term down to $-2.6\cdot10^{-4}$ in class 2, nearly
cancelling. Under the fixed prior the mean error keeps 41\% of the lift's (rms $1.13\cdot10^{-2}$, DR), DR's drift
stays negative definite (the largest eigenvalue $-3.8\cdot10^{-5}$) and DR*'s positive definite
($+1.50\cdot10^{-3}$, the threshold std 0.435). (D5) Along DR's exact second-order transient from the lift (82\,000
periods, $5.2\cdot10^5$ time units) the drift's largest eigenvalue, $-4.7\cdot10^{-4}$, $-1.6\cdot10^{-4}$,
$-3.1\cdot10^{-4}$ in the first period, turns positive at $t = 4103$, $2947$, $2714$ in classes 1, 2, 3 --- with the
noise part of $\E[e]$ alone at $t = 4072$, $2721$, $2689$: (iv)'s mechanism, not the parameters' terms --- and stays
positive, reaching $+1.05\cdot10^{-4}$ (class 1, $t\approx3.1\cdot10^4$), formally the threshold std 1.64, far
outside the expansion, before settling at the stationary point's (the period maps' limit within 0.3\% of the exact
periodic mean's). DR*'s largest eigenvalue is positive throughout ($3.6\cdot10^{-3}$ in the first period,
$3.4\cdot10^{-5}$ at the stationary point). Over the first 300 time units the cross term moves DR's class-2 window
drift by $-8\%$ ($[0,50)$: $-1.61\cdot10^{-4}$, without it $-1.49\cdot10^{-4}$) and DR*'s by $-17\%$
($+3.69\cdot10^{-3}$ against $+4.45\cdot10^{-3}$), so DR*'s threshold std over $[0,50)$ is 0.277 (0.238 at the lift's
quasi-static moments). (D6) The real loop (the lift $m = 15$, one class separated by $10^{-6}$ along a direction
$\theta$, four antithetic pairs per family on paths common to the laws, 300 time units, the rate fitted over
