$t\ge50$): along MR's top eigenvector $u$ of $S_3$ DR's separations decay at
$-2.9651\cdot10^{-4}\pm1.3\cdot10^{-7}$ (std 0.05) and $-3.3650\cdot10^{-4}\pm5.3\cdot10^{-7}$ (std 0.1), faster
than $\sigma_0/k_e = 2.8298\cdot10^{-4}$; their noise-induced part is $0.98\pm0.01$ and $0.97\pm0.01$ of the
transient's prediction --- the mean error's term along the transient, the cross term and the variance term --- (2.3
and 3.5 sampling errors), against 0.82 and 0.81 of the lift's. Along DR's own top eigenvectors, its weakest
directions, the separations decay faster than $\sigma_0/k_e$ by only 0.36\%, 0.14\%, 0.26\% (classes 1--3, std 0.05;
1.4\%, 0.54\%, 1.0\% at 0.1), at the transient's prediction to 0.9, 1.9 and 0.05 sampling errors (0.8, 1.8, 0.1);
along DR*'s top eigenvector of $S_2$ they decay slower than $\sigma_0/k_e$ by 3.2\% and 12.6\%, the noise-induced part
$0.97\pm0.10$ and $0.96\pm0.10$ of the transient's prediction (0.72 of the lift's). The pair means of DR decay faster
than $\sigma_0/k_e$ in every family; no DR or DR* run grows. MR's separations grow at
$+4.69\cdot10^{-4}\pm1.3\cdot10^{-5}$ and $+1.25\cdot10^{-3}\pm3.1\cdot10^{-5}$ (8 of 8 runs each), the latter less
than half the second-order prediction (the next order of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}). On the
same noise paths the measured regressor's replicas separate and the desired regressor's stay together.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.34 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-24 version, as committed at
% 9d122dd; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/mass_transfer.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:34 is a
% \hyperref target, as res:thmDppp:21 is. The reference [S62] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) is the bibitem S62 of
% refs.tex.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.34}[mass transfer: the replicator weight law removes the saturation trap of the
transit's masses]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:34}%
Let the particles of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32} carry weights $\beta_i > 0$ with
$\sum_i\beta_i = m$ --- the network $\fhat = (1/m)\sum_i\beta_i\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i)$, its measure
$\mu_{\Theta,\beta} = (1/m)\sum_i\beta_i\delta_{\vartheta_i}$, the class masses $W_k = \sum_{i\in k}\beta_i/m$ --- and
let the weights adapt by the replicator law
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\dd\beta_i}{\dd s} = \gamma\beta_i(u_i - \lambda),\qquad
  u_i := \langle r\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i)\rangle_\rho,\qquad
  \lambda := \sum_j\beta_ju_j\Big/\sum_j\beta_j
  \tag{$\mathfrak{F}$}
\end{equation}
beside
$\dd\vartheta_i/\dd s = \beta_iG\langle\nabla_\vartheta(r\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i))\rangle_\rho
- (\sigma_0/m)\sum_j\psi(\norm{\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j}^2_{G^{-1}}/R^2)(\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j)$
($r = f - \fhat$; in the real loop, on $\sum\beta = m$,
$\dot\beta_i = \gamma\beta_ie^\T(\tilde\Phi(x;\vartheta_i) - \fhat(x))$, the replicator dynamics with the fitness
$e^\T\tilde\Phi_i$ --- an exponentiated-gradient law).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(a gradient flow; the bound kept)} The pair is the gradient flow of
    $J(\Theta,\beta) := m\bar L(\mu_{\Theta,\beta}) + (\sigma_0m/2)\iint W\dd\mu_\Theta\dd\mu_\Theta$ (the cohesion
    energy of the unweighted cloud, so that the weights see the fit only) in the metric
    $\sum_i\norm{\delta\vartheta_i}^2_{G^{-1}} + \sum_i\delta\beta_i^2/(\gamma\beta_i)$ on $\{\sum_i\beta_i = m\}$ ---
    Euclidean on the positions, Fisher--Rao (Shahshahani) on the weights; with $\varrho_i := \sqrt{\beta_i}$,
    gradient descent on $(\varrho,\Theta)$, with the step $\gamma/4$ in $\varrho$, on the sphere
    $\sum_i\varrho_i^2 = m$, $\varrho_i^2/m$ the output weights of a two-layer network ---, so $J$ decreases;
    $\beta > 0$ and $\sum\beta$ are invariant (from every start), and on $\sum\beta = m$,
    $\abs{\fhat}\le(1/m)\sum\beta_i\abs{a_i}\le a_{\max}$ as for equal weights: no projection is needed. With
    $\beta\equiv1$ and $\gamma = 0$ it is the loop of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}. (A
    Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao metric would weigh the positions by their masses,
    $\sum_i\beta_i\norm{\delta\vartheta_i}^2_{G^{-1}}$: its drift lacks the factor $\beta_i$ on the fit and divides
    the cohesion force by $\beta_i$; and $J$ is not a function of $\mu_{\Theta,\beta}$ alone, its cohesion being the
    unweighted cloud's.)
  \item \begin{sloppypar}\emph{(the real loop at fixed positions)} With the positions at atoms where
    $f(x) = (1/m)\sum_i\beta^*_i\tilde\Phi(x;\vartheta_i)$ at every state the loop visits (for $n = 1$ and analytic
    features, on the orbit's interval), $\sum\beta^* = m$, the real loop with \textup{($\mathfrak{F}$)} has
    $\dot V = -k_e\abs{e}^2$ for
    $V := \frac12\abs{e}^2 + (1/(\gamma m))\sum_i\beta^*_i\log(\beta^*_i/\beta_i)$: the relative entropy of the
    weights takes the place of the quadratic parameter error --- the negative-entropy instance of the mirror-descent
    adaptation laws with Bregman Lyapunov functions (\cite{S62}). The identity needs only $\sum\beta^* = m$ and the
    pointwise representation; $V\ge0$ needs $\sum\beta = m$, which the law keeps (it does not restore it).
    $\beta\to\beta^*$ is not claimed: it needs the persistent excitation of $(\tilde\Phi(x_m(t);\vartheta_i))_i$.\end{sloppypar}
  \item \emph{(the stationary points; the saturated transients)} $(\Theta,\beta)$ is stationary iff
    $\beta_iG\langle r\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i)\rangle_\rho
    = (\sigma_0/m)\sum_j\psi_{ij}(\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j)$ for every $i$ and $u_i = \lambda$ on the support: the
    fit's force carries the factor $\beta_i$, the cohesion does not. At a clustered configuration under the finite
    range (the classes $R$ apart) the cohesion vanishes, the condition is
    $\langle r\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_k)\rangle = 0$, whose amplitude component
    $(1 - a_k^2/a_{\max}^2)\langle r\varphi_k\rangle = 0$ forces $\langle r\varphi_k\rangle = 0$, so
    $u_k = a_k\langle r\varphi_k\rangle = 0 = \lambda$: there the stationary points are
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}'s, for every vector of masses. Under the Gaussian kernel the
    inter-class forces do not vanish and the fit's factor $\beta_i$ weighs against them, so $u_k = \lambda$ fixes the
    masses: the replicator selects the masses of each class configuration and moves the Gaussian kernel's bias
    (Numerics (G)); and a class whose particles carry unequal weights is not exactly replicated at a biased
    stationary point. The difference is the saturated transient: when a class is short of mass
    ($\abs{A_k} > W_ka_{\max}$) the equal-weight loop at fixed positions stays biased (it has no critical point)
    while $\abs{a_k}\to a_{\max}$ with $\langle r\varphi_k\rangle\not\to0$, its amplitude gradient exponentially
    small in $\alpha_k$ (heuristically $\alpha_k$ grows like $(a_{\max}/2)\log s$); under \textup{($\mathfrak{F}$)}
    the starved class's mass grows at the rate $\gamma(u_k - \lambda)$,
    $u_k\approx a_{\max}\abs{\langle r\varphi_k\rangle}$ --- $\dd W_k/\dd s = \gamma W_k(1 - W_k)u_k > 0$ exactly
    when the class's particles coincide and $u_l = 0$ for the other classes --- and the others take up the gauge
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:28]{Proposition D$'''$.28}: their $p_l = W_la_l$ kept by $\abs{a_l}$ increasing).
  \item \emph{(fixed positions: representability)} With one particle at each of $K$ distinct atoms whose centres and
    widths are fixed and whose features $\varphi_k$ are linearly independent in $L^2_\rho$, and
    $f = \sum_kA_k\varphi_k$ in their span (otherwise $A$ is the least-squares coefficient vector), the flow of
    $(W,\alpha)$ is a gradient flow of the least-squares loss in $p_k := W_ka(\alpha_k)$, convex in $p$, on the open
    simplex times $\R^K$; its critical points are the exact fits $p = A$, which exist iff
    $\sum_k\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} < 1$ (the representable set of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24} is not
    empty); every trajectory bounded in $(\log W,\alpha)$ converges to one ($\bar L$ is real-analytic there:
    {\L}ojasiewicz), so bounded trajectories reach the exact fit iff the target is representable (boundedness is not
    proved); when $\sum_k\abs{A_k}/a_{\max}\ge1$ every trajectory leaves every compact set, each class saturating or
    losing its mass, and the fit error stays above the least-squares error over the $\ell^1$ ball
    $\sum_k\abs{p_k}\le a_{\max}$. The transit's masses no longer decide whether the target is fitted.
  \item \emph{(the linearisation at an exact representation)} At the lift of an exact representation
    ($\beta\equiv1$) the weights add, on $\{\sum\beta = m\}$, $\sum_k(n_k - 1)$ neutral directions (the
    redistribution of mass inside a class, which $\fhat$ does not see) and $K - 1$ gauge directions (mass moved
    between classes against their amplitudes, $W_ka_k$ fixed), and no unstable one --- in the coordinates
    $(\Theta,\log\beta)$, the constraint not imposed, the common rescaling of the weights against the amplitudes is a
    $K$-th gauge; the separations' block is \hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}'s ($-\sigma_0w_k$,
    $w_k$ the particle fraction), the collective block changes by the replicator's metric on the masses. In the real
    loop the lift is a periodic orbit in a manifold of periodic orbits (the gauge and the redistributions), neutral
    along it.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) $\partial_{\vartheta_i}(m\bar L) = -\beta_i\langle r\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i)\rangle_\rho$
and $\partial_{\beta_i}(m\bar L) = -u_i$ ($\fhat$ is linear in $\beta$); the cohesion energy does not depend on
$\beta$; the Fisher--Rao gradient on $\{\sum\beta = \mathrm{const}\}$ is
$-\gamma\beta_i(\partial_{\beta_i}J - \lambda')$, $\lambda' = \sum_j\beta_j\partial_{\beta_j}J/\sum_j\beta_j$, which
is \textup{($\mathfrak{F}$)}, and
$\dd J/\dd s = -\sum_i\norm{\dd\vartheta_i/\dd s}^2_{G^{-1}} - \sum_i(\dd\beta_i/\dd s)^2/(\gamma\beta_i)\le0$;
$\dd\log\beta_i/\dd s$ is bounded on bounded sets, so $\beta$ stays positive, and
$\sum_i\dd\beta_i/\dd s = \gamma\sum_i\beta_i(u_i - \lambda) = 0$. With $\beta_i = \varrho_i^2$,
$\delta\beta_i^2/(\gamma\beta_i) = 4\delta\varrho_i^2/\gamma$. (ii) With $\sum\beta = m$, $\lambda(t) = e^\T\fhat(x)$;
$\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sum_i\beta^*_i\log(\beta^*_i/\beta_i)
= -\gamma e^\T\sum_i\beta^*_i(\tilde\Phi_i - \fhat) = -\gamma me^\T(f - \fhat)$
($f = (1/m)\sum\beta^*_i\tilde\Phi_i$, $\sum\beta^* = m$), and $\dot e = f - \fhat - k_ee$;
$\sum_i\beta^*_i\log(\beta^*_i/\beta_i) = m\cdot\KL(\beta^*/m\,\|\,\beta/m)\ge0$ when $\sum\beta = m$. (iii) The
stationarity of the positions and of the weights read off (i); at a clustered configuration under the finite range
$\psi = 0$ across classes and $\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j = 0$ inside them; the amplitude's derivative
$\dd a/\dd\alpha = 1 - a^2/a_{\max}^2 = 4e^{-2\abs{\alpha}/a_{\max}}(1 + o(1))$;
$\dd W_k/\dd s = (1/m)\sum_{i\in k}\gamma\beta_i(u_k - \lambda) = \gamma W_k(u_k - \lambda)$ and
$\lambda = \sum_lW_lu_l = W_ku_k$ when $u_l = 0$ for $l\ne k$. (iv) In the variables $(W,\alpha)$ the flow is the
restriction of (i)'s gradient flow; $\bar L$ depends on them through $p$,
$\partial\bar L/\partial p_k = -\langle r\varphi_k\rangle =: -g_k$, and at a critical point with $W$ in the open
simplex the amplitude component gives $g_k = 0$ for every $k$, i.e.\ $p$ is the least-squares solution $A$;
$\abs{a(\alpha)} < a_{\max}$ and $\sum W_k = 1$ make $p = A$ attainable iff $\sum_k\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} < 1$. The flow
is the gradient flow of a real-analytic function for an analytic metric in $(\log W,\alpha)$, so a bounded
trajectory has a single limit, a critical point ({\L}ojasiewicz's theorem). The dissipation
$\int\norm{\nabla J}^2\dd s\le J(0)$ makes every $\omega$-limit point critical, so without a critical point a
trajectory has no $\omega$-limit point and eventually leaves every compact set; and
$\sum_k\abs{p_k} = \sum_kW_k\abs{a_k} < a_{\max}$ always, which bounds the fit error below by the $\ell^1$ ball's.
(v) At $r = 0$ the Hessian of $m\bar L$ is the Gram matrix of the derivatives of $\fhat$, and $J$'s Hessian is the
linearisation's up to the metric: inside a class $\partial\fhat/\partial\beta_i = \tilde\Phi_k/m$ is the same for
every particle (the redistributions), and $\partial\fhat/\partial\beta_i = (a_k/a'_k)\partial\fhat/\partial\alpha_i$
at $\beta_i = 1$ (the gauge, one per class; $K - 1$ combinations of them keep $\sum\delta\beta = 0$); the cohesion
energy acts on the separations only and does not see the weights; $J$'s Hessian is positive semidefinite there (the
Gram matrix plus the cohesion's $\nabla^2W(0) = G^{-1}$ on the separations), so no direction is unstable; along the
lift $e\equiv0$, so $\dd\beta/\dd t = 0$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i), (ii), (iii)'s stationarity at clustered configurations under the finite range and (v)'s zero modes
proved; under the Gaussian kernel the masses' selection numerical (G); (iii)'s transient: the rate exact under its
conditions, the bias's duration heuristic; (iv) proved for bounded trajectories, boundedness not proved; the transit
with the cohesion leak numerical, and with it any selection of the number of kernels: the replicator does not act on
the separations at a critical point ($u_k = 0 = \lambda$ there), so the shedding of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}(iv) is untouched (Numerics (C)). Classical: Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao
(Hellinger--Kantorovich) distances and gradient flows of measures, conic particle gradient descent and the lift to
$(\sqrt\beta,\Theta)$ with its global convergence for sparse optimisation on measures, neuron birth--death, the
exponentiated-gradient law and EM's multiplicative update of mixture weights, mirror-descent adaptation laws with
Bregman Lyapunov functions --- of which (ii) is the negative-entropy instance ---, the replicator equation as the
Shahshahani gradient flow with its relative-entropy Lyapunov function (\cite{S62}). New: the replicator as the mass
transfer of the cohesion leak in mean-field adaptive control --- the stationary set unchanged at clustered
configurations under the finite range, the masses selected under the Gaussian kernel, the saturation trap of the
transit's masses removed ---, the representability criterion at fixed positions for bounded trajectories, and the
numerics.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.34 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/mass_transfer.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_mass_transfer.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/mass_transfer.md};
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}'s instances, the midpoint rule on $(\Theta,\log\beta)$). (A) Fixed
positions --- the five-bump target's atoms, one particle per class, the masses (0.15, 0.30, 0.20, 0.25, 0.10), two of
them short of $\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} = (0.225, 0.150, 0.175, 0.125, 0.200)$, the amplitudes and the weights adapted:
without the replicator the fit error stays at 0.388 from $s = 4000$ on --- the least fit error at those masses is
0.387 (G) ---, the two short classes at $\abs{a}/a_{\max} = 0.999$; with $\gamma = 10^{-3}$ it falls to
$2.9\cdot10^{-4}$ at $s = 40000$, with $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ to $3.0\cdot10^{-14}$ (geometrically, by about 6 per 2000
slow units), every mass above its $\abs{A_k}/a_{\max}$ at the end (0.234, 0.199, 0.191, 0.168, 0.208). With the
amplitudes scaled by 1.25 ($\sum_k\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} = 1.094$) the fit error falls to 0.071 at both rates and still
creeps down toward the $\ell^1$ ball's least error 0.0684 (G), every class at $\abs{a}/a_{\max}\ge0.992$, the masses
already the minimiser's (0.271, 0.164, 0.194, 0.132, 0.239 against 0.270, 0.164, 0.194, 0.132, 0.239): (iv)'s
dichotomy. (B) The five-bump target from broad starts ($m = 60$, the annealed Gaussian schedule): without the
replicator 6--9 classes, the fit error 0.045--0.118 (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}, Numerics
(H)); with $\gamma = 10^{-3}$ 5--7 classes, 0.0076--0.0139; with $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ five classes in 3 of 3 runs,
their centres the bumps' to 0.01 ($-1.600$, $-0.798$, $+0.004$, $+0.798$, $+1.597$ for seed 1), the fit error
$6.0\cdot10^{-4}$--$1.4\cdot10^{-3}$, falling at the end by 2--4\% per 1000 slow units. Continued (G, seed 1) it
levels off --- $5.99\cdot10^{-4}$, $3.62\cdot10^{-4}$, $3.01\cdot10^{-4}$, $2.87\cdot10^{-4}$, $2.82\cdot10^{-4}$,
$2.80\cdot10^{-4}$ at $s = 0, 2, \dots, 10\cdot10^4$ under the Gaussian kernel, near its class-level stationary point
($2.63\cdot10^{-4}$), and $2.85\cdot10^{-4}$ after $4\cdot10^4$ slow units under the compact kernel, whose
class-level stationary point is exact ($2.4\cdot10^{-16}$): the nearly saturated classes ($\abs{a}/a_{\max}$ up to
0.96) are slow. (D) The instance (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{D$'''$.32} (C)) with $\gamma = 10^{-2}$: three classes
in 3 of 3 runs, the fit error $2.4\cdot10^{-7}$--$9.7\cdot10^{-7}$ (without the replicator, the same seeds 0--2,
$1.9\cdot10^{-7}$--$9.0\cdot10^{-7}$). (C) The resolution's finest stage (the fourth bump, compact $R = 1$,
$\sigma_0/64$, from the jittered lift of $\vartheta^b(q)$, $10^5$ slow units): with $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ the same 8--10
classes as without, from the same splits at nearly the same times (3 of 51 records differ by one class; the fit error
0.0055--0.0075 against 0.0059--0.0078): at a critical point of $L_w$ the fitness vanishes
($u_k = a_k\langle r\varphi_k\rangle = 0 = \lambda$), so the replicator does not act on the separations'
instability, and the shedding of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{D$'''$.32} (iv) is untouched. (F) The targets where
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{D$'''$.32}'s count failed (its Numerics (K); the annealed schedule, $\gamma = 10^{-2}$):
three heavy bumps ($\sum_k\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} = 0.95$) end at three classes at the bumps in 3 of 3 runs, the fit error
$7.8\cdot10^{-8}$--$1.6\cdot10^{-7}$, every mass above its $\abs{A_k}/a_{\max}$ (0.411--0.423, 0.308--0.313,
0.265--0.280 against 0.400, 0.300, 0.250), where without the replicator 3--5 biased classes ended at 0.058--0.20;
two bumps closer than the broad scale ($\pm0.45$) end as without it, merged (1--3 classes, 0.068--0.13, one class of
mass 0.88--1.00 at the centre): the scale's obstruction is not the masses', and no mass transfer separates what the
broad stage merged. (G) The stationary points at the class level (a run's classes replicated with equal weights
inside each, the conditions of (iii) solved to rounding, $R = 1$): for B's seed 1 (the counts $(6,6,20,10,18)$)
under the Gaussian kernel the fit error is $3.85\cdot10^{-4}$ with the masses held and $2.63\cdot10^{-4}$ with
$u_k = \lambda$, the masses moving (0.1573, 0.1330, 0.2618, 0.2060, 0.2419 $\to$ 0.1579, 0.1318, 0.2661, 0.2076,
0.2366) and the classes of each sign then at a common $\abs{a}/a_{\max}$ (0.9578, 0.8464); under the compact kernel
both are exact ($2.4\cdot10^{-16}$--$2.5\cdot10^{-16}$), the masses free to $5\cdot10^{-4}$. For
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{D$'''$.32} (L)'s three equal bumps $(28,16,16)$ the Gaussian kernel's stationary point has
$9.39\cdot10^{-5}$ with the masses held and $9.17\cdot10^{-5}$ with them free, the masses moving from (0.467, 0.267,
0.267) toward (0.567, 0.217, 0.217) (the residual of the conditions there $1.7\cdot10^{-10}$: the outer classes
approach the saturation), the compact kernel's exact either way: (iii)'s difference between the kernels. (E) The real
loop at the instance's atoms ($m = 3$, $k_e = 25$, $\gamma = 2$, $\beta(0) = (1.6, 0.7, 0.7)$, 200 time units): $V$
from 0.0418 to $2.1\cdot10^{-7}$, monotone; $\dot V$ from the vector field equal to $-k_ee^2$ to $1.1\cdot10^{-16}$
of $\max k_ee^2$; $\sum\beta = m$ to $2.4\cdot10^{-14}$; $\beta$ at the end within $1.3\cdot10^{-3}$ of
$\beta^* = 1$. The averaged loop's linearisation in $(\Theta,\log\beta)$ at the lift of $\vartheta^e(w)$, counts
$(6,5,4)$, compact $R = 1$: with $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ 15 eigenvalues below $10^{-9}$ --- the 12 redistributions and the
3 gauges of (v) --- and the 45 others in $[-0.1834,-3.081\cdot10^{-4}]$; with $\gamma = 0$ the 45 are
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}'s predicted spectrum to $2.1\cdot10^{-11}$ (the finite differences'
error).
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.35 with its proof sketch, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-25 version, as committed
% at 2e5eb10; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/split_bifurcation.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:35 is a
% \hyperref target, as res:thmDppp:21 is. The reference [S63] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) is the bibitem S63 of
% refs.tex; Fukumizu--Amari and Rose are in S60.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.35}[the split of a class is an $S_n$-symmetric transcritical bifurcation: shedding, its
order, its cascade]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:35}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32} (the finite range), let class $k$ of $n\ge3$
particles sit at an atom $\vartheta_k$ of a nondegenerate critical point of $L_w$, the classes at least $R$ apart, and
let $\lambda^+ = \lambda_{\max}(R^G_k)$ be simple with the whitened unit eigenvector $v$. Write the separations along
$v$ as $u_i = \xi_iG^{1/2}v$ ($\sum_i\xi_i = 0$), $\mu := \lambda^+ - \sigma_0w_k$ (the negative splitting margin), and
\[
  c_3 := \partial^3_t\langle r\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_k + tG^{1/2}v)\rangle_\rho\big|_{t=0}
  \qquad(\text{$r$ the residual at the critical point, frozen}),
\]
the residual's third derivative along the splitting direction.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the reduced equation)} For $\abs{\mu}$ small against the rates of the other modes, the flow has a
    centre manifold over the separations along $v$ --- the standard representation of the symmetric group $S_n$,
    which permutes the particles --- and on it
    \[
      \frac{\dd\xi_i}{\dd s} = \mu\xi_i + \frac{c_3}2\Big(\xi_i^2 - \frac1n\sum_j\xi_j^2\Big)
      + O(\abs{\xi}^3 + \abs{\mu}\abs{\xi}^2):
    \]
    the quadratic coefficient is the residual's alone, since the cohesion energy is even in the differences
    ($W(-u) = W(u)$) and the mean term is common to the particles. The splitting margin of
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}(iv) is the linear coefficient; the quadratic one identifies the
    equivariant normal form's coefficient for this flow. At third order the cohesion's concavity, the residual's own
    cubic term and the slaving of the class mean all enter.
  \item \emph{(no small split is stable)} If $c_3\ne0$ the bifurcation at $\mu = 0$ is transcritical. Its equilibria
    near 0 off the $S_{n/2}\times S_{n/2}$ symmetry are the split states with isotropy $S_q\times S_{n-q}$ --- $q$
    particles at $(n - q)t^*$, $n - q$ at $-qt^*$, $t^* = -2\mu/(c_3(n - 2q))$, $1\le q < n/2$ --- and on the centre
    manifold each has the eigenvalues $-\mu n/(n - 2q)$ (multiplicity $q - 1$, inside the $q$-group),
    $+\mu n/(n - 2q)$ (multiplicity $n - q - 1$, inside the other group) and $-\mu$ (along the branch), all to leading
    order in $\mu$: every one is a saddle, for either sign of $\mu$. For even $n$ the quadratic term vanishes on the
    $S_{n/2}\times S_{n/2}$ states, which are cubic (at $\abs{\xi}\sim\sqrt{\abs{\mu}}$, on one side of the threshold
    only). Past the threshold ($\mu > 0$) the class leaves a fixed neighbourhood of the replicated state from almost
    every initial condition: the split is a jump, not a gradual separation.
  \item \emph{(who leaves first)} The particles keep their order along $v$ ($\operatorname{Fix}$ of a transposition
    is invariant under any $S_n$-equivariant flow, so two particles never cross), so the particle with the largest
    $\sgn(c_3)\xi_i$ is ahead on that side at all times; in the quadratic truncation its excursion
    $\zeta = \sgn(c_3)\xi$ obeys $\dd\zeta/\dd s = \mu\zeta + (\abs{c_3}/2)\zeta^2$ up to the common term, with the
    Riccati solution
    $s = (1/\mu)\ln[\zeta_1(\mu + \abs{c_3}\zeta_0/2)/(\zeta_0(\mu + \abs{c_3}\zeta_1/2))]$ --- the leading behaviour
    of the departure, which is measured against the class's median. The quadratic term decides once $\zeta$ exceeds
    $2\mu/\abs{c_3}$: the top-ranked particle then runs away along $\sgn(c_3)v$, alone when its lead is large, with
    its neighbour as a bound pair when their $\zeta_0$ are nearly tied (within about 11\% on the instance). A split
    sheds the extreme particle, alone or with a near-tied neighbour, because the quadratic term --- the residual's, not
    the kernel's --- makes it run away first.
  \item \emph{(after a departure)} After $k$ particles have left the kernel's range the class has $n - k$, and its
    margin is $\sigma_0(w_k - k/m) - \lambda^+(r',\vartheta')$, the curvature at the new residual and the remaining
    particles' raised amplitude (the gauge of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:28]{Proposition D$'''$.28}: by $n/(n - k)$ when the
    residual is unchanged): both the stiffness's drop and the curvature's gauge rise favour further departures, and
    the class stops shedding only if the residual's change lowers $\lambda^+$ by more than $\mu + k\sigma_0/m$ plus
    that rise. On the instance the residual's change dominates and the margin is positive by the end of each stage;
    the cascade resumes when $\sigma$ is lowered (Numerics (C), (D)).
  \item \emph{(the mass-free leak)} The mass-free leak $\sigma(m_i - \vartheta_i)$ --- the kernel-normalised mean
    shift, $\sigma/(\sigma_0\bar\psi_i)$ times the cohesion force --- vanishes at clustered configurations under the
    finite range as the cohesion leak does and has its blocks with $\sigma$ in place of $\sigma w_k$ on the
    separations (the class means free), so its thresholds are $\sigma = \lambda^+_k$, lower than the cohesion leak's
    by the factor $w_k$, and (i)--(iv) hold for it with $\mu = \lambda^+ - \sigma$. It is not a gradient flow, and its
    forces do not sum to zero. It does not stop the cascade: the curvature's change after a departure is the same.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(i) The centre manifold theorem for the $S_n$-equivariant flow; the expansion of the learning drift along $v$ at the
replicated state (the mean term $(S_2/2m)\langle\Phi''\Phi'\rangle$ is common to the particles and leaves with the
projection on $\sum\xi = 0$); $W$ even, so $\nabla^3W(0) = 0$. (ii) Substituting the isotropy ansatz gives
$\mu(n - q)t + (c_3/2)(n - q)(n - 2q)t^2 = 0$; the Jacobian $J_{ij} = (\mu + c_3\xi_i)\delta_{ij} - (c_3/n)\xi_j$ on
$\{\sum\xi = 0\}$ has the stated eigenvalues on the zero-sum perturbations inside each group and along the branch; on
$\operatorname{Fix}(S_{n/2}\times S_{n/2})$ $\sum\xi_i^2$ is constant across the groups' values and the quadratic term is
the common one. (iii) Equivariance under the transposition $(i\;j)$ makes $\{\xi_i = \xi_j\}$ invariant; the Riccati
solution. (iv) \hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}(iv) at the new configuration with
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:28]{Proposition D$'''$.28}'s gauge scaling of $R_k$. (v) Inside a class in range $m_i$ is the
class mean, so the force is $\sigma(\bar\vartheta_k - \vartheta_i)$, $-\sigma$ on the separations, and nothing on the
class mean; across classes $\psi = 0$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i)--(ii) proved to leading order in $\mu$ (the centre manifold, the $S_n$-equivariant normal form; checked:
the branches by Newton on the full drift, their eigenvalues converging to the predicted ones as $\mu\to0$); at
$\sigma_0/16$ three stable modes are slower than $\mu$, so there the reduced equation's agreement (C) reflects the weak
coupling of those modes, not the centre manifold; (iii) the order by equivariance, the escape time in the quadratic
truncation (checked); (iv) a statement of the margin, the cascade's extent numerical; (v) the blocks proved, its
cascade numerical. Classical: $S_n$-equivariant bifurcation theory --- the standard representation's quadratic
equivariant makes generic symmetry-breaking from the fully symmetric state transcritical, with no stable small branch
(Elmhirst; Golubitsky--Stewart; Arjevani--Field), the jump of sympatric speciation (Stewart--Elmhirst--Cohen,
Golubitsky--Stewart), the permutation-symmetric critical points of neural networks (Fukumizu--Amari, Brea et al.,
\c{S}im\c{s}ek et al., Arjevani--Field), a cluster's split at a critical parameter in deterministic annealing (Rose),
order preservation (equivariance) and the Riccati escape time (\cite{S63}, \cite{S60}). New: the identification for
the cohesion leak --- the quadratic coefficient the residual's third derivative along the splitting direction, the
cohesion and the mean term entering only at third order or in the common direction ---, the margin after a departure,
and the instance's quantification.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.35 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/split_bifurcation.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_split_bifurcation.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/split_bifurcation.md};
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}'s resolution instance: the lift of $\vartheta^b(q)$ with the counts
$(10,10,10)$, $m = 30$, the compact kernel at $R = 1$). (A) At the lift $\lambda^+ = 1.00\cdot10^{-4}$,
$2.86\cdot10^{-4}$, $1.08\cdot10^{-4}$ for the three classes, $v$ the centre against the width ($(0,+0.75,-0.66)$,
$(0,+0.72,-0.70)$, $(0,+0.78,-0.62)$, whitened), $c_3 = 1.42\cdot10^{-5}$, $-1.257\cdot10^{-3}$, $1.20\cdot10^{-4}$
(two difference steps agreeing to $2\cdot10^{-5}$ relative); the middle class splits first, at
$\sigma = \lambda^+/w_k = 0.1213\sigma_0$, and leaves on the side $-v$ (its centre lower, its width larger). (B) At
$\mu = 2\cdot10^{-6}$ and $5\cdot10^{-7}$ Newton on the full particle drift (90 coordinates, the drift to $10^{-17}$)
finds the four split states $q = 1,\dots,4$ from (ii)'s $t^*$: their positions within 1.3\%, 1.8\%, 3.5\%, 15\% of the
prediction at $\mu = 2\cdot10^{-6}$ and within 0.3\%, 0.4\%, 0.8\%, 3.0\% at $5\cdot10^{-7}$; on the separations along
$v$ the eigenvalues are $+1.2544\mu$ ($\times8$) and $-1.094\mu$ for $q = 1$ at $2\cdot10^{-6}$ and $+1.2511\mu$
($\times8$) and $-1.023\mu$ at $5\cdot10^{-7}$, against $+1.25\mu$ and $-\mu$, and likewise for $q = 2, 3, 4$ (the
unstable ones $+1.6708\mu$, $+2.5165\mu$, $+5.1444\mu$ at $5\cdot10^{-7}$ against $5\mu/3$, $5\mu/2$, $5\mu$), the
errors falling about fourfold with $\mu$, as the next order says; every split state is a saddle, with
$n - q - 1 = 8, 7, 6, 5$ unstable directions of the full Jacobian. (C) From the lift with a jitter $10^{-3}$ (six
seeds) at $\mu = 5\cdot10^{-5}$ and $1.39\cdot10^{-4}$ ($\sigma_0/16$, Part G's first split), an excursion being a
particle 0.1 from the class's median (far inside the range $R = 1$): the reduced quadratic equation integrated from the
measured $\xi(0)$ names the first excursion's particle in 12 of 12 runs and gives its time to 1.1\%
($\mu = 5\cdot10^{-5}$: $6.61\cdot10^4$--$8.84\cdot10^4$ slow units; $\sigma_0/16$: $2.68\cdot10^4$--$3.35\cdot10^4$);
the linear equation ($c_3 = 0$) names it in 6 of 12 and misses the time by up to 22.4\%, the equation with $c_3$'s sign
flipped names it in 2 of 12 --- the ranking, which every equivariant law preserves, does not name the particle by
itself; the quadratic term does. The first excursion is on the side $\sgn(c_3)v$ in 10 of 12 runs; the two on the
other side ($\sigma_0/16$, seeds 2 and 4) are the linear growth of a particle whose excursion stays below
$2\mu/\abs{c_3} = 0.22$, and neither leaves the range. After $10^5$ slow units the pieces beyond the kernel's range
are, at $\sigma_0/16$, one particle in 3 of 6 runs and a bound pair in 3 of 6 --- the top two of $\sgn(c_3)\xi(0)$
within 1.2\%, 4.4\% and 11\% of each other; where the lead was 22\% or more the leader went alone ---, at 1.5--1.9
(single) and 1.1--1.2 (pairs) from the main piece; at $\mu = 5\cdot10^{-5}$ one particle in 3 of 6 runs and none yet in
the other 3. The main piece at the clustering threshold (0.25) then has 8 or 9 particles and a positive margin
($7.5\cdot10^{-6}$--$1.6\cdot10^{-4}$): the cascade stops within the stage, and resumes at the next lower $\sigma$.
(D) Part G's schedule ($\sigma$ halved every $10^5$ slow units, the jitter renewed, seeds 0--2): the cohesion leak ends
at 8--10 classes at $\sigma_0/64$ (five to seven single particles, the fit error 0.0028--0.0031,
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32} (G)); with the replicator ($\gamma = 0.1$) at 6, 6 and 7, the first
escapee's mass growing to 0.105--0.106 (three particles' worth) and the later ones to 0.029--0.060, the fit error
0.0031--0.0032. (E) With the replicator at $\gamma = 1$ the same schedule ends at 5, 6 and 8 classes, the first
escapee's mass 0.134--0.136 (four particles' worth), the fit error 0.0029--0.0030: with the replicator the cascade ends
at 5--8 classes against 8--10 without, and the rate makes no difference in three seeds; the jitter's seeds are not
paired with Part G's. (F) The mass-free leak $\sigma(m_i - \vartheta_i)$ --- the separations' stiffness $\sigma$
instead of $\sigma w_k$ --- splits later, its threshold $\lambda^+ = 0.040\sigma_0$ instead of
$\lambda^+/w_k = 0.121\sigma_0$: at $\sigma_0/64$, where Part G's schedule stops, it has 4 classes in 3 of 3 runs (the
fit error 0.0149); continued to $\sigma_0/256$ it sheds as the cohesion leak does, to 9, 10, 9 classes at
$\sigma_0/128$ and 9, 8, 9 at $\sigma_0/256$ (four to six single particles, a pair in one run), the fit error 0.0028
--- the cohesion leak's end state: its 4 classes were where the schedule stopped, not a count the leak selects.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.36 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-26 version, as committed at
% c054367; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/gauge_dither.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:36 is a
% \hyperref target, as res:thmDppp:21 is. The reference [S64] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) is the bibitem S64 of refs.tex.
% The Brownian motion in the proof, PARADIGM's W, is written \mathcal W here, to keep it apart from the masses W.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.36}[the gauge under dither: the replicator's masses diffuse along the manifold of exact
fits; a leak on the masses pins them]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:36}%
Let the loop of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34} sit at a representable exact representation with one
particle per class --- $K$ classes, the masses $W$, the amplitude coordinates $\alpha_k$ with $W_ka(\alpha_k) = A_k$ ---,
and dither the positions by $\sqrt{2\tau G}$ per particle (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}(vi)).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the gauge manifold)} The exact fits through the configuration form a manifold
    $M = \{W_ka(\alpha_k) = A_k,\ \text{the centres and widths fixed}\}$ of dimension $K - 1$ inside the representable
    set, parametrised by the masses: $J$ is constant on $M$, and where $H_L\succ0$ the flow attracts $M$
    exponentially, its tangent $T$ spanned by the gauges $\delta\alpha_k = -A_k\delta W_k/(W_k^2a'_k)$,
    $\sum\delta W = 0$ ($a' = 1 - (a/a_{\max})^2$). With $n_k > 1$ particles in a class the redistributions of its
    mass among them are further zero modes (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34}'s count), $M$ has
    dimension $m - 1$, and $T_\alpha$ below carries the factor $n_k$: the statements here are for one particle per
    class.
  \item \emph{(the masses diffuse)} As $\tau\to0$, on the time scale $1/\tau$, the masses perform a diffusion on $M$
    (a Katzenberger limit) whose covariance per unit slow time is
    \begin{gather*}
      D(W) = (T^\T gT)^{-1}T^\T g\Sigma gT(T^\T gT)^{-1},\qquad
      \Sigma = 2\tau G\ \text{on the positions},\\
      g = \operatorname{diag}\big(G^{-1}\ \text{on the positions},\ (m/\gamma)/W_k\ \text{on the masses}\big),
    \end{gather*}
    the $g$-orthogonal projection of the noise onto $M$ (the linearised gradient flow's limit map), plus a drift of the
    same order in $\tau$ from the second derivative of that map (computed in
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}). For two classes $D_{11} = 2\tau T_\alpha/(T_\alpha + T_W)^2$,
    $T_\alpha := \sum_k(A_k/(W_k^2a'_k))^2/G_\alpha$, $T_W := (m/\gamma)\sum_k1/W_k$: there is no restoring force in
    $D$; $D\to0$ as $\gamma\to0$ (with equal weights the amplitudes are pinned by the fit) and $D\to2\tau/T_\alpha$
    as $\gamma\to\infty$; at the boundary of the representable set $a'_k\to0$, so the saturating class's row and
    column of $D$ vanish quadratically in $W_k - \abs{A_k}/a_{\max}$ (the other masses keep diffusing), while in the
    amplitude coordinate $\alpha_k\approx-(a_{\max}/2)\log(W_k - \abs{A_k}/a_{\max})$ the diffusion tends to
    $2\tau G_\alpha$ exactly: heuristically the class's excursion into the saturation is unbounded in $\alpha_k$ ---
    outside the theorem, which holds on compact parts of $M$, and subject to the drift of
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}. The function $\fhat$ does not move: the gauge's motion changes
    the representation's conditioning, not the fit.
  \item \emph{(a leak on the masses)} The entropic leak on the masses --- the energy
    $(\varepsilon/\gamma)\sum_i\beta_i\log\beta_i$ added to $J$, so that $\dd\log\beta_i/\dd s$ gains
    $-\varepsilon(\log\beta_i - \sum_j\beta_j\log\beta_j/\sum_j\beta_j)$ --- is a leak on the unexcited subspace: it
    does not change $\fhat$ along $M$, so at an exact representation with $W = w$ (equal weights) the configuration
    stays stationary --- no bias where $w$ is representable --- and the gauge becomes an Ornstein--Uhlenbeck
    direction: linearised at $W = w$ the masses' limit on $M$ is an Ornstein--Uhlenbeck process with the stiffness
    $\kappa = \varepsilon(T^\T gT)^{-1}T_W$ ($T_W$ the masses' share of the metric on $M$,
    $T^\T\operatorname{diag}(0,(m/\gamma)/W)T$), so their stationary covariance $C$ solves
    $\kappa C + C\kappa^\T = D$ --- for two classes $C = D/(2\kappa)$, $\kappa = \varepsilon T_W/(T_\alpha + T_W)$ ---
    and the spread falls as $\varepsilon^{-1/2}$, while the drift term of (ii) shifts the pinned state by
    $O(\tau/\varepsilon)$; where $w$ is not representable the leak pulls the masses back toward the saturation trap
    that the replicator removed (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34}(iii)), a bias of order
    $\varepsilon/\gamma$ --- the leak's energy $(\varepsilon/\gamma)\sum\beta\log\beta$ against the fit's ---, not
    $\varepsilon$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) On $M$ each amplitude is a function of its class's mass, $\alpha_k = a^{-1}(A_k/W_k)$, so $M$ is a graph over the
representable masses; the derivatives of $\fhat$ along the complement of $T$ are those of the fit at fixed masses,
whose Gram matrix $H_L$ is $\succ0$ by assumption. (ii) Near an attracting manifold of equilibria of a gradient flow
$\dot x = -g^{-1}\nabla J$, the limit map $\Phi$ (the point of $M$ the flow converges to) has derivative
$\partial\Phi$ = the $g$-orthogonal projection onto $T$ (the linearisation $g^{-1}\nabla^2J$ is $g$-self-adjoint with
kernel $T$); Katzenberger's theorem gives the limit
$\dd Y = \partial\Phi(Y)\sqrt{2\tau}B\dd\mathcal W + \tau\partial^2\Phi(Y)[BB^\T]\dd s$, $BB^\T = G$ on the positions
(so $2\tau BB^\T = \Sigma$); in the masses' coordinates, the coefficient of $\delta W$ along $T$ is
$(T^\T gT)^{-1}T^\T g\xi$. For $K = 2$ $T$ is one vector with $\norm{t}_g^2 = T_\alpha + T_W$ and
$T^\T g\xi = \sum_kt_{\alpha,k}\xi_k/G_\alpha$ of variance $2\tau T_\alpha$. Near the boundary
$a'_k\approx2(1 - \abs{a_k}/a_{\max})\propto W_k - \abs{A_k}/a_{\max}$. (iii) The leak's term is the Fisher--Rao
gradient of $(\varepsilon/\gamma)\sum\beta\log\beta$ on $\sum\beta = m$; at $\beta\equiv1$ it vanishes; along $M$
$\fhat$ is constant, so the leak's energy is the only one that varies there, its Hessian on $T$ being
$\operatorname{diag}(\varepsilon m/(\gamma W))$; the reduced flow on $M$ in the metric $T^\T gT$ is then
$-(T^\T gT)^{-1}H(W - w)$ to first order, and the linear SDE's stationary covariance is the Lyapunov equation's
solution.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved for one particle per class; (ii) the diffusion coefficient by Katzenberger's theorem on compact parts
of $M$ (the drift term in \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}), the vanishing of $D$ at the boundary
proved, the excursion in $\alpha_k$ heuristic; (iii) the stationarity, the restored stiffness and the linearised spread
proved, the $O(\tau/\varepsilon)$ shift and the $O(\varepsilon/\gamma)$ bias sketched. The time-scale separation is thin
on the instance: the slowest normal rate ($3.06\cdot10^{-4}$, the second bump's width near its lower bound) is only
about $3\tau$, and at $\varepsilon = 10^{-3}$ the leak's stiffness exceeds it; the reduction holds because that mode
barely couples to the masses. Classical: diffusions forced onto a manifold by a large drift (Katzenberger), the
noise-driven motion along manifolds of minima of overparameterised models and its implicit regularisation (Blanc et
al., Li--Wang--Arora) (\cite{S64}). New: the gauge of the amplitude--mass representation
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:28]{Proposition D$'''$.28}) as such a manifold for the replicator of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34}, the masses' diffusion coefficient computed in the flow's metric, the
excursion into the saturation, and the entropic leak on the masses as a leak on the unexcited subspace that pins the
gauge without bias where the particle fractions are representable.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.36 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/gauge_dither.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_gauge_dither.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/gauge_dither.md};
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition D$'''$.32}'s three-bump instance with one particle per class at the exact
representation $\vartheta^e(1/3)$, $m = 3$, $A = (1,-0.7,0.8)$, $a_{\max} = 4$ --- the representable masses
$W_k\ge\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} = 0.25$, $0.175$, $0.2$ ---, the replicator at $\gamma$, the positions dithered at
$\tau = 10^{-4}$, Euler--Maruyama at $\dd t = 0.25$). (A) At $W = 1/3$ the predicted diffusion of the masses along $M$
is $D_{kk} = 2.85\cdot10^{-8}$, $1.63\cdot10^{-8}$, $1.81\cdot10^{-8}$ per unit slow time at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$,
rising with $\gamma$ ($4.85\cdot10^{-10}$ for the first class at $\gamma = 10^{-3}$, $4.01\cdot10^{-7}$ at $\gamma = 1$,
$4.23\cdot10^{-7}$ at $\gamma = 10$: saturating as $\gamma\to\infty$, as for two classes). (B) Measured over 64 seeds at
$\gamma = 10^{-2}$ for $5\cdot10^4$ slow units, the variance of the masses' displacement (the mean square less the
drift's square) grows linearly ($1.5\cdot10^{-4}$ at $5\cdot10^3$, $1.4\cdot10^{-3}$ at $5\cdot10^4$ for the first
class), its slope over $[10^4,5\cdot10^4]$ $(2.69\pm0.58)\cdot10^{-8}$, $(2.21\pm0.47)\cdot10^{-8}$,
$(1.62\pm0.38)\cdot10^{-8}$ (bootstrap errors) against the predicted $2.85$, $1.63$, $1.81\cdot10^{-8}$: the ratios
0.95, 1.36, 0.90, within two standard errors ($D$'s variation along the path not included). (C) Over $2\cdot10^5$ slow
units (10 seeds) the masses spread to standard deviations 0.057, 0.053, 0.052 over the second half (the free
diffusion's $\sqrt{Ds}$ at $s = 1.5\cdot10^5$: 0.065, 0.049, 0.052, comparable within the 10 seeds' scatter), and the
amplitude coordinates run into the saturation: $\alpha_1$ up to 16.1 ($\abs{a}/a_{\max} = 0.9994$), against
$[3.58,4.43]$ and $\abs{a}/a_{\max}\le0.803$ at $\gamma = 0$ (4 seeds, the masses fixed); the fit error over the second
half is the dither's in both (0.0253 with the replicator, 0.0269 without): the gauge's motion costs no fit --- it
changes only the representation's conditioning. (D) The entropic leak at $\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$ and $10^{-3}$ (4 seeds
each, $2\cdot10^5$ slow units) pins the masses: their second-half standard deviations 0.0152, 0.0103, 0.0117 and
0.0044, 0.0032, 0.0034 against the Ornstein--Uhlenbeck prediction of (iii) at $W = 1/3$, 0.0139, 0.0099, 0.0105 and
0.0044, 0.0031, 0.0033; four seeds scatter by 12--23\%, so only the $\varepsilon = 10\tau$ agreement is informative, and
at $\varepsilon = \tau$ the linearisation is not expected to hold (the shift and the spread's variation along $M$ are
comparable) --- the 16-seed test is \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37} (G). The amplitudes stay inside
the range in these four seeds ($\abs{a}/a_{\max}\le0.89$ and 0.82, against 0.803 at $\gamma = 0$), the maximum growing
with the number of seeds; the means $(0.3252, 0.3397, 0.3351)$ and $(0.3329, 0.3338, 0.3333)$ are within 1.5 standard
errors of 1/3, too few seeds to resolve the $O(\tau/\varepsilon)$ shift (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition
D$'''$.37} (G)). The fit error is the dither's throughout (0.0264, 0.0265). The drift itself is visible in (B): after
$5\cdot10^4$ slow units the masses' means are $0.3212\pm0.0046$, $0.3445\pm0.0041$, $0.3342\pm0.0038$ (from 1/3), the
first two 2.6 and 2.7 standard errors away --- the drift \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37} computes,
which the diffusion's $\sqrt{Ds}$ (0.038 for the first class) dominates on this horizon.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.37 with its proof sketch, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-27 version, as committed
% at 5952611; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/gauge_drift.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:37 is a
% \hyperref target, as res:thmDppp:21 is. The references [S64], [S65] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) are the bibitems S64 and
% S65 of refs.tex. The chart, PARADIGM's gamma, is written \chi here, to keep it apart from the replicator's rate gamma.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.37}[the dither's drift along the gauge: in the slow-replicator limit the masses descend
the log-determinant of the positions' Hessian --- toward the saturation ---; a boundary layer of width $O(\sqrt\gamma)$
slows them but does not stop them]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:37}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36} (the replicator at rate $\gamma$, the positions
dithered by $\sqrt{2\tau G}$, the manifold $M$ of exact fits parametrised by the masses), write
$x = (\Theta,\log\beta)$, $F$ the drift, $A = \partial F$ on $M$ with the eigenpairs $(\lambda_i,e_i)$, completed on the
tangent $T$ by the chart's columns, and $P$ the spectral projection onto $T$ along $\operatorname{range}A$ (for this
gradient flow the $g$-orthogonal projection of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}(ii)).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the drift)} The limit map's second derivative at a point of $M$ is
    \[
      \partial^2\Phi[e_i,e_j] = -P\partial^2F[e_i,e_j]/(\lambda_i + \lambda_j)
    \]
    unless both $e_i$, $e_j$ lie in $T$, and $(I - P)\partial^2\chi[c_i,c_j]$ for two tangent vectors $e_i = \partial\chi c_i$,
    $e_j = \partial\chi c_j$ ($\chi$ the chart); on the time scale $1/\tau$ the masses of Katzenberger's limit move by
    \begin{gather*}
      \dd W = b(W)\dd s + D(W)^{1/2}\dd B,\\
      b = \partial W\cdot\tfrac12\partial^2\Phi[\Sigma] + \tfrac12\partial^2W[P\Sigma P^\T],\qquad
      D = \partial W\,P\Sigma P^\T\,\partial W^\T,
    \end{gather*}
    with $\Sigma = 2\tau\operatorname{diag}(G,\dots,G,0)$ the position noise: the drift is of the same order $\tau$ as
    the diffusion of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}(ii), so the masses do not merely diffuse.
  \item \emph{(the slow-replicator limit: an entropic free energy)} Let $\gamma$ be small against the positions'
    relaxation rates at the exact representation. At fixed masses the dithered positions are a reversible diffusion
    with the invariant law $\propto\exp(-J(\Theta;\beta)/\tau)$; averaged over it, the masses' fitness is
    $u_k = \tau\partial_{\beta_k}\log Z(\beta) = -(\tau/2)\partial_{\beta_k}\ell(\beta) + O(\tau^2)$,
    $\ell(\beta) := \log\det\nabla^2_\Theta J$ at the exact representation with the masses $\beta/m$ (Laplace), so that
    \[
      \frac{\dd W_k}{\dd s} = -\frac{\gamma\tau}2W_k\Big(\partial_{\beta_k}\ell - \sum_lW_l\partial_{\beta_l}\ell\Big)
      + O(\gamma^2\tau,\gamma\tau^2),\qquad D = O(\gamma^2\tau):
    \]
    the masses follow the replicator flow --- the Fisher--Rao gradient flow --- of the entropic free energy
    $(\tau/2)\ell$, deterministically on the time scale $1/(\gamma\tau)$ (their spread there $O(\sqrt\gamma)$), down
    the free energy: the dither favours the masses whose exact fit is flattest in the positions, not the target's
    particle fractions $w$. With one particle per class the amplitude's row is the only mass-dependent part of the
    Hessian, and $\ell$ has the closed form
    \[
      \ell(W) = 2\sum_k\log(W_ka'_k) + \text{const}
      = 2\sum_k\log\big(W_k - (\abs{A_k}/a_{\max})^2/W_k\big) + \text{const},
    \]
    which has no minimiser on the representable masses: it decreases to $-\infty$ at every saturation. Here the Gibbs
    law is the quasi-stationary law of the positions' local well around the exact representation (the global law
    $\exp(-J/\tau)$ also charges other representations; the escape from the well is exponentially slow in $1/\tau$).
    Within a class of coincident particles $\ell$ depends on the class's total mass only --- the fit's block is of
    rank $p$ along the class's $\beta$-weighted mean and its Schur complement is the cohesion's on the separations ---,
    so the slow limit selects the classes' masses and leaves their split among the particles free.
  \item \emph{(the saturation and its layer: no stop)} As a class saturates ($\abs{a_k}\to a_{\max}$,
    $W_k\to\abs{A_k}/a_{\max}$) its amplitude's curvature $\propto W_k^2a_k'^2\to0$, so $\ell\to-\infty$ there: the
    flattest exact fits are saturated, and the free energy's flow drives the masses toward the saturation that the
    replicator had removed as a trap (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34}(iii)). The limit (ii) needs
    $\gamma$ small against the saturating amplitude's relaxation rate
    $mG_\alpha\Gamma_{kk}W_k^2a_k'^2\approx4mG_\alpha\Gamma_{kk}(W_k - \abs{A_k}/a_{\max})^2$
    ($\Gamma_{kk} = \langle\varphi_k^2\rangle_\rho$), which falls below the mass channel's
    $\gamma a_k^2W_k\Gamma_{kk}\approx\gamma a_{\max}\abs{A_k}\Gamma_{kk}$ within
    $\delta^* := \frac12\sqrt{\gamma a_{\max}\abs{A_k}/(mG_\alpha)} = O(\sqrt\gamma)$ of the saturation. Inside that
    layer the residual relaxes through the masses, the amplitude diffuses freely along $M$ with the mass slaved to it
    ($W_k = A_k/a(\alpha_k)$), and the drift tends to the It\^o term of this convex map,
    \[
      b_k\to\tau G_\alpha\partial^2_\alpha\big(A_k/a(\alpha_k)\big) > 0\qquad
      (W_k - \abs{A_k}/a_{\max}\ll\delta^*):
    \]
    the drift of $W_k$ turns outward and has a zero at a fixed fraction of $\delta^*$, but it is only the It\^o term of
    a driftless diffusion of $\alpha_k$, with the diffusion $2\tau G_\alpha$: in the amplitude coordinate nothing
    restores, the zero-flux density in $W_k$ $\propto1/(W_k - \abs{A_k}/a_{\max})$ is not normalisable, and the
    excursions into the saturation grow (null recurrence in $\alpha_k$). The attracting zeros of the mean drift are
    therefore not states the masses settle in; without a leak the masses' law drifts into the saturations. Within
    about $0.1\delta^*$ of a saturation the transverse mass fluctuations are as large as the distance itself, and the
    description in the coordinates $W$ breaks down.
  \item \emph{(the leak's bias)} With the entropic leak of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}(iii) the
    limit SDE on $M$, $\dd W = f(W)\,\dd s + D^{1/2}\dd B$ ($f = b$ + the leak's projected force), has the stationary
    mean $E[W] = W_0 - J^{-1}\cdot\tfrac12\partial^2f[C] + \dots$, $W_0$ the zero of $f$ ($= w + \kappa^{-1}b(w)$ to
    first order), $J = \partial f(W_0)\approx-\kappa$, $JC + CJ^\T + D = 0$: the bias is $O(\tau/\varepsilon)$ --- the
    drift balance $\kappa^{-1}b(w)$, whose direction is the dither's drift at the target's fractions, and the
    fluctuations acting through the curvature of $f$, almost all of it the leak's projected force, whose stiffness
    varies on the scale of the distance to the saturation (of the same order on the instance,
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition D$'''$.38}(iv)). The expansion needs the spread $O(\sqrt{\tau/\varepsilon})$
    small against the scale on which $b$ and $D$ vary, and the leak's stiffness below the slowest normal rate
    ($3.06\cdot10^{-4}$ on the instance): $\tau\ll\varepsilon\ll3\cdot10^{-4}$, which the instance barely meets. In the amplitude coordinate the leak's force on a saturating class grows as
    $\dd W_k/\dd\alpha_k\to0$, so the leak does restore what the layer does not.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(i) Differentiate $\partial\Phi(x)F(x)\equiv0$ twice at a point of $M$ ($F = 0$ there):
$\partial^2\Phi[u,Av] + \partial^2\Phi[v,Au] = -P\partial^2F[u,v]$; in the eigenbasis this is the stated division by
$\lambda_i + \lambda_j\ne0$; for two tangent vectors $\Phi\circ\chi = \chi$ gives
$\partial^2\Phi[\partial\chi c,\partial\chi c'] = (I - P)\partial^2\chi[c,c']$. The limit is Katzenberger's
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}(ii)), its drift $\frac12\partial^2\Phi[\Sigma]$; It\^o's formula for
$W(x)$. (ii) The positions' drift is $-G\nabla_\Theta J$ and their noise $\sqrt{2\tau G}$, so at fixed $\beta$ they are
a reversible diffusion with the Gibbs law; the masses' law \textup{($\mathfrak{F}$)} has $u_k = -\partial_{\beta_k}J$;
for $\gamma$ small the averaging principle replaces $u_k$ by its Gibbs mean, which is $\tau\partial_{\beta_k}\log Z$;
Laplace's method at the exact representation ($J = 0$ there, the cohesion inactive between the classes) gives
$\log Z = -\frac12\ell + \text{const} + O(\tau)$. The masses' diffusion is the projection of the positions' noise
through the $\gamma$-small cross-mobility, $O(\gamma^2\tau)$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}(ii):
$D\to0$ as $\gamma\to0$, quadratically). Within a class: in the basis of the class's mean and its separations the fit's
block is $(\sum\beta)^2Q$ on the mean, $(\sum\beta)\beta_\perp^\T\otimes Q$ off it and
$\beta_\perp\beta_\perp^\T\otimes Q$ + cohesion on the separations, whose Schur complement is the cohesion's alone.
(iii) The amplitude's row of $\nabla^2_\Theta J$ is $\propto W_ka'_k$, so $\det\propto a_k'^2$ and
$a'_k\approx2(W_k - \abs{A_k}/a_{\max})/W_k$; the rates: $\partial^2J/\partial\alpha_k^2 = m\Gamma_{kk}W_k^2a_k'^2$
and, through the masses, $\dd e_k/\dd s = -\gamma W_ka_k^2\Gamma_{kk}e_k$ for the residual's coefficient
$e_k = W_ka_k - A_k$; inside the layer the limit map moves along $\alpha_k$, and $W = A/a(\alpha)$ is convex in
$\alpha$ ($a = a_{\max}\tanh(\alpha/a_{\max})$), so its It\^o term is positive. (iv) The zero $W_0$ of
$f = b(W) - \kappa(W)(W - w)$ expanded in $b = O(\tau)$ is the drift balance; stationarity, $E[f(W)] = 0$, expanded at
$W_0$ to second order in the spread gives $J(E[W] - W_0) + \tfrac12\partial^2f[C] = O(\abs{C}^{3/2})$, $C$ the
linearisation's stationary covariance ($JC + CJ^\T + D = 0$) --- the small-noise expansion of a stationary mean.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved (given Katzenberger's theorem; checked against the integrated limit map); (ii) the averaging and
Laplace steps sketched, the law checked to three digits at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$; (iii) the divergence proved, the layer's
scale by the crossing of the rates, the inside law sketched (checked to 0.6\%), the non-normalisability inside the
layer from the driftless $\alpha$-diffusion (the full loop's excursions growing, (H)); (iv) the drift balance and the second-order
stationary mean sketched (the small-noise expansion; its window $\tau\ll\varepsilon\ll3\cdot10^{-4}$ not quantified).
Classical: the second derivative of the limit map and its use for the noise-driven drift along a manifold of minima
(Katzenberger; Li--Wang--Arora, \cite{S64}), averaging and the free energy of fast variables (the potential of mean
force), Laplace's method, the preference of noisy gradient dynamics for flat minima (\cite{S65}); the
log-pseudo-determinant as the drift for isotropic noise is Li--Wang--Arora's (\cite{S64}), here met with noise on the
positions only and a slow mass channel. New: the replicator's gauge as the slow variable, its free energy in closed
form; the consequence that dither drives the masses into the saturation, the layer of width $O(\sqrt\gamma)$ that slows
but does not stop it; the leak's bias.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.37 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/gauge_drift.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_gauge_drift.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/gauge_drift.md};
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}'s instance: three bumps, one particle per class at an exact
representation, $m = 3$, $\tau = 10^{-4}$, the first class saturating at $W_1 = 0.25$; $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ unless
stated). (A) The second-order identities agree with the limit map computed by integration (LSODA to $1.2\cdot10^5$ slow
units, second differences at $\pm0.02$ along the nine noise coordinates) to $2.2\cdot10^{-5}$ at $W = 1/3$ and
$1.1\cdot10^{-4}$ at $(0.30, 0.35, 0.35)$. (B) The drift is the amplitudes' noise's: at $W = 1/3$ each class's
amplitude noise moves mass out of that class at 5.34, 3.47, $4.07\cdot10^{-7}$ per unit time (into the others), the
centres' and widths' noise contributing at most $2.2\cdot10^{-10}$ ($4.9\cdot10^{-10}$ at $(0.30, 0.35, 0.35)$); the
sum $(-2.06,+1.58,+0.48)\cdot10^{-7}$. (C) The slow-replicator limit: the drift over $\gamma\tau$ converges as $\gamma$
decreases and at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$ agrees with the log-determinant's replicator flow
$-\frac12W_k(\partial_k\ell - \sum_lW_l\partial_l\ell)$ to 0.3\% at the four points tested ($(-0.362,+0.242,+0.120)$
at $W = 1/3$ both); at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ it is 0.07--0.65 of the limit, component by component, at the interior points
and of the opposite sign at $W = (0.28, 0.36, 0.36)$, inside the first class's layer. (D) The layer: on $W_2 = W_3$ the
first class's drift is positive (out of the saturation) below a zero and negative above it, the zero at
$W_1^\circ - 0.25 = 0.038$, 0.019, 0.011, 0.0057, 0.0033 for $\gamma = 10^{-2}$, $3\cdot10^{-3}$, $10^{-3}$,
$3\cdot10^{-4}$, $10^{-4}$: 0.66, 0.61, 0.59, 0.57, 0.57 times $\delta^*$, tending to a fixed fraction ($\approx0.56$)
as $\gamma\to0$; deep inside the layer ($W_1 - 0.25 = 0.002$--$0.0028$ at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$) the drift is
$\tau G_\alpha\partial^2_\alpha W_1$ to 0.6\% (the ratios 1.006, 1.003, 0.999). (E) At $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ the drift
field on the representable masses has seven zeros: a repelling node at $(0.376, 0.299, 0.325)$; three saddles, each
with one class at its layer's edge ($(0.288, 0.343, 0.369)$, $(0.422, 0.209, 0.370)$, $(0.422, 0.343, 0.235)$); and
three attracting nodes with two classes at their layers' edges and the third holding the rest ---
$(0.284, 0.205, 0.511)$, $(0.284, 0.484, 0.232)$, $(0.562, 0.206, 0.232)$, their rates
$1.7$--$2.4\cdot10^{-5}$ --- the indices summing to $3 - 3 + 1 = 1$, the disc's: attractors of the mean drift, two of
the three amplitudes at $\abs{a}/a_{\max} = 0.85$--$0.88$ there, but not states the masses settle in (iii), as (H)
shows. (F) The ensemble mean of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}'s 64 seeds follows the mean path
$\dd W/\dd s = b(W)$ within its standard errors at $10^4$, $2\cdot10^4$, $3\cdot10^4$ and $5\cdot10^4$ slow units (at
$5\cdot10^4$: $0.3212\pm0.0046$, $0.3445\pm0.0041$, $0.3342\pm0.0038$ against 0.3223, 0.3419, 0.3358), while the spread
is the diffusion's (0.038 for the first class). (G) The entropic leak's pinned state (16 seeds, $4\cdot10^5$ slow units,
the second half): at $\varepsilon = 3\cdot10^{-4}$ and $10^{-3}$ the masses' mean shift is
$(-0.0013\pm0.0004, +0.0011\pm0.0002, +0.0002\pm0.0003)$ and $(-0.0003, +0.0003, 0.0000)\pm0.0001$ against
$\kappa^{-1}b(w) = (-0.0009, +0.0007, +0.0003)$ and $(-0.0003, +0.0002, +0.0001)$, within 1--2 standard errors, the
spreads within 5\% of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}(iii)'s; at $\varepsilon = \tau = 10^{-4}$ the
shift $(-0.0051\pm0.0014, +0.0040\pm0.0007, +0.0011\pm0.0011)$ is about 1.8 times $\kappa^{-1}b$ and the spreads
(0.0165, 0.0112, 0.0114) 9--19\% above the linear prediction --- (iv)'s drift balance alone is approximate for
$\varepsilon\gtrsim3\tau$ and fails at $\varepsilon = \tau$, where the masses' excursions reach where $b$ and $D$ vary;
the second component is above $\kappa^{-1}b$ at every $\varepsilon$ (by 2.8, 2.1 and 1.7 standard errors, the same
seeds for the three $\varepsilon$, so not independent checks) --- (iv)'s fluctuation term: with it 1.7, within 1.03
in every component (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition D$'''$.38}'s Part G at $\kappa_F = 0$) and 0.8 ---, and at $\varepsilon = 10^{-3}$ the leak's stiffness
exceeds the slowest normal rate: the window $\tau\ll\varepsilon\ll3\cdot10^{-4}$ of (iv) is barely met. (H) Without a
leak over $10^6$ slow units (12 seeds) the masses leave 1/3 and spread toward the saturations: the fraction of seeds
within 0.02 of a class's saturation grows to 0.50, 0.25, 0.33 at $10^6$ (0.17, 0, 0 at $10^5$), the amplitudes reaching
$\abs{a}/a_{\max} = 0.9999$, 0.9941, 1.0000; the excursions grow through the run, the seeds' mean of the largest
$\abs{\alpha_k}$ by quarters 6.6, 10.1, 10.9, 11.3 for the first class and 4.6, 6.2, 9.4, 11.5 for the third (the
fraction with $\abs{\alpha_k} > 10$ reaching 0.26 and 0.28), and in the last quarter the first and third masses are
beyond their saturation --- off the manifold of exact fits --- in 11\% and 12\% of the records: the layer does not stop
them. The limit SDE on $M$ (4000 paths on E's field) gives means within 1.6 standard errors of the 12 seeds' except the
third at $10^6$ (2.2), and its near-saturation fractions 0.30, 0.18, 0.26 and spreads 0.10--0.13 against 0.08--0.15;
confined to the grid ($W_k\ge\abs{A_k}/a_{\max} + 0.004$), it cannot test the saturation itself.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.38 with its proof sketch, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-28 version, as committed
% at 5d14f92; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/gauge_fixman.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:38 is a
% \hyperref target, as res:thmDppp:21 is. The references [S64], [S66] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) are the bibitems S64 and
% S66 of refs.tex. PARADIGM's potential Phi := kappa_F(tau/2)ell is written \Phi_c here, to keep it apart from the limit
% map Phi of Propositions D'''.36 and D'''.37 (PARADIGM's proof writes the limit map's derivative as dPhi in the same
% sentence); the Jacobian J = df(W_0) of (iv) is written J_f, to keep it apart from the energy J. Proposition D'''.40,
% which the text cites, is not stated in PARADIGM.md (in progress), so the citation is plain text.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.38}[a Fixman correction for the replicator under dither: the entropic drift cancelled,
or reversed toward the D-optimal exact fit]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:38}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37} (one particle per class, the replicator at rate
$\gamma$, the positions dithered at the level $\tau$, the manifold $M$ of exact fits with
$\ell = \log\det\nabla^2_\Theta J$ along it), add to the replicator's fitness the correction
\[
  c_k := \kappa_F\frac\tau2\,\partial_{\beta_k}\ell(\beta/m)
  = \kappa_F\frac\tau m\,\frac{1 + \hat W_k^2/W_k^2}{W_k - \hat W_k^2/W_k},\qquad
  W_k = \beta_k/m,\quad \hat W_k = \abs{\hat A_k}/a_{\max},
\]
with a gain $\kappa_F\ge0$ and $\hat A_k$ the classes' contributions to the fit (on $M$, $\hat A_k = W_ka_k = A_k$ and
$\hat W_k = W_{\min,k}$): \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}'s closed form
$\ell(W) = 2\sum_k\log(W_k - W_{\min,k}^2/W_k) + \text{const}$ differentiated in the masses --- the masses form. It
needs the dither's level, the saturation bound and the classes' contributions, vanishes without dither, acts on the
masses only ($\sum\beta = m$ is kept), and with $\hat A$ fixed is the replicator's fitness of the potential
$\kappa_F(\tau/2)\ell$, a function of the masses alone, as in Fixman's correction of stiff constraints (a potential on
the soft coordinates computed on the constraint manifold). The corrected loop's deterministic part is then the gradient
flow of $J - \kappa_F(\tau/2)\ell(W)$ in \hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34}'s metric: $\ell$, which tends
to $-\infty$ at every saturation, enters as a log-barrier on the representable masses (the implementation floors the
denominator at $0.01W_k$, see (iii)). The plug-in form, $\hat W_k = W_k\abs{a_k}/a_{\max}$ at the current amplitudes,
i.e.\ $\kappa_F(\tau/m)(1 + (a_k/a_{\max})^2)/(W_ka'_k)$, $a'_k = 1 - (a_k/a_{\max})^2$, equals it on $M$ and depends on
the amplitudes; it is $\kappa_F\tau g'_k(\alpha_k)$ with $g_k = a_k/(\beta_ka'_k)$. Since
$\partial_{\alpha_k}J = -u_k/g_k$ (the gauge), one integration by parts gives
$\langle u_k\rangle = -\tau\langle g'_k\rangle$ in the positions' quasi-stationary law in the exact fit's well at fixed
masses (Proposition D$'''$.40(i); the global Gibbs law has no such identity, $\langle g'_k\rangle$ diverging on the
plateau of saturated fits): under that law the plug-in form cancels the slow-limit fitness to every order in $\tau$,
the masses form to Laplace's order.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the cancellation)} On the slow time scale the correction --- an $O(\tau)$ perturbation $F_c$ of the
    flow --- adds $\partial W\cdot P\cdot F_c$ to the drift of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}(i),
    $P$ the projection onto the tangent of $M$ along the range of the linearisation, $F_c$ evaluated on $M$ (so
    (i)--(iv) hold for both forms). $F_c$ is the flow's gradient of $\Phi_c := \kappa_F(\tau/2)\ell$, a function of the
    masses, so exactly $P\cdot F_c = (T^\T gT)^{-1}\nabla_W\Phi_c$, $T$ the tangent of $M$ in the masses' chart and $g$
    the flow's metric. In the slow-replicator limit the masses' own coordinates dominate $T^\T gT$ and $P\cdot F_c$ is
    the replicator flow ascending $\Phi_c$, so the masses' drift is the replicator flow of the potential
    $(1 - \kappa_F)(\tau/2)\ell$ plus $O(\gamma^2\tau)$, on compact sets of the representable masses outside the layers
    of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}(iii): at $\kappa_F = 1$ the entropic drift is cancelled to
    leading order, and what remains, $O(\gamma^2\tau)$, is of the order of the diffusion $D = O(\gamma^2\tau)$, its
    direction not controlled by the correction. At finite $\gamma$ the amplitudes' share of $T^\T gT$ grows, and the
    dither's drift falls below its slow limit faster than the correction's (to 57\% and 74\% of them at
    $\gamma = 10^{-2}$, $W = 1/3$): at $\kappa_F = 1$ the correction over-compensates once $\gamma$ is a few
    $10^{-3}$ --- the net drift reversed from $\gamma\approx2\cdot10^{-3}$ on, at 31\% of the uncorrected drift's size
    at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$.
  \item \emph{(the gain: the D-optimal fit)} For $\kappa_F > 1$ the masses ascend $\ell$. Each term of $\ell$ is
    strictly concave in $W_k$ on the representable masses and tends to $-\infty$ at the saturation, so $\ell$ has a
    unique maximiser $W^\sharp$ on the simplex, interior --- the exact fit whose positions' Hessian has the largest
    determinant (D-optimal; the condition number along $M$ is nearly flat and minimised elsewhere), the repelling node
    of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}'s drift ---, and in the slow-replicator limit the masses
    converge to it, deterministically on the time scale $1/((\kappa_F - 1)\gamma\tau)$, their spread there
    $O(\sqrt{\gamma/(\kappa_F - 1)})$. With an estimate $\hat\tau$ of the dither's level in the correction the net
    potential is $(\kappa_F\hat\tau/\tau - 1)(\tau/2)\ell$: the selection needs only $\kappa_F\hat\tau > \tau$ --- and
    $\hat\tau$ can be read from the rms fit error by equipartition, $\hat\tau = m\langle F^2\rangle\langle e^2\rangle/p$
    with $p$ the position coordinates holding fit energy (each stores $\tau/2$ of $J = (m/2)\langle r^2\rangle$; the
    mean of $e$ is biased low by Jensen's inequality).
  \item \emph{(the saturation)} For the correction as stated (unfloored): inside the layer of
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}(iii), where the amplitude dominates the tangent of $M$ in the
    flow's metric, the projected correction moves the saturating amplitude at
    $\dd\alpha_k/\dd s = G_\alpha\partial_{\alpha_k}\Phi_c = -\kappa_FG_\alpha\tau(1 + (a_k/a_{\max})^2)/a_k
    \to-2\kappa_FG_\alpha\tau/a_{\max}$. With the amplitude's noise $2\tau G_\alpha$ the zero-flux density of
    Katzenberger's limit near the saturation behaves like $e^{-2\kappa_F\alpha_k/a_{\max}}$ in the amplitude coordinate
    and like $\delta^{\kappa_F - 1}$ in $\delta := W_k - W_{\min,k}$: normalisable for every $\kappa_F > 0$, flat at
    $\kappa_F = 1$ (excursions along $M$ into the layer are then unopposed),
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}'s $\delta^{-1}$ at $\kappa_F = 0$; the mean drift descends
    $(1 - \kappa_F)\ell$, so only for $\kappa_F > 1$ is the saturation repelling. The implementation floors the
    denominator at $0.01W_k$: within $\delta_{\mathrm{cap}} = W_{\min,k}(1/\sqrt{0.99} - 1)$ (0.00126 for the first
    class) of the saturation the correction stops growing, its contribution to the exponent falls from $\kappa_F$ to
    $\kappa_F(1 - W_{\min,k}^2/W_k^2)/0.01\approx200\kappa_F\delta/W_{\min,k}$, and the density goes back to
    $\delta^{-1}$ --- the mass near the saturation log-divergent in the $\tau\to0$ limit, as without the correction.
    Normalisability needs the unfloored correction, or a floor that shrinks with $\tau$ (at the finite-$\tau$
    threshold below, where the description in $W$ ends anyway). At finite $\tau$ the law needs
    $\delta\gg(\kappa_F\tau/(ma_{\max}^2\Gamma_{kk}))^{1/2}$ ($\Gamma_{kk} = \langle\varphi_k^2\rangle_\rho$;
    $0.003\sqrt{\kappa_F}$ for the first class on the instance): there the fit's overshoot, through which the masses
    relax, leaves $a'_k$ at its value on $M$ and the two forms agree.
  \item \emph{(with the leak)} With the entropic leak of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}(iii) the
    limit SDE on $M$, $\dd W = f(W)\dd s + D^{1/2}\dd B$ with $f = b_c + P\cdot F_{\mathrm{leak}}$, has the stationary
    mean
    \[
      \E[W] = W_0 - J_f^{-1}\cdot\tfrac12\partial^2f[C] + \dots,\qquad J_fC + CJ_f^\T + D = 0,
    \]
    $W_0$ the zero of $f$ ($= w + \kappa^{-1}b_c(w)$ to first order, $b_c$ the corrected drift, $\kappa$ the leak's
    stiffness along $M$), $J_f = \partial f(W_0)\approx-\kappa$, $C$ the masses' stationary covariance: the drift
    balance --- $O(\gamma\tau/\varepsilon)$ without the correction, $O(\gamma^2\tau/\varepsilon)$ at $\kappa_F = 1$,
    toward $W^\sharp$ at $\kappa_F > 1$ --- and the fluctuations acting through the curvature of $f$, almost all of it
    the leak's projected force, whose stiffness varies on the scale $\delta$ of the distance to the saturation through
    the finite-$\gamma$ weights of (i): $O(C/\delta) = O(\gamma^2\tau/(\varepsilon\delta))$, of the order of the drift
    balance at the instance (30--70\% of it without the correction, larger than it at $\kappa_F = 1$). The window of
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}(iv), $\tau\ll\varepsilon\ll{}$ the slowest normal rate, keeps the
    expansion valid.
  \item \emph{(off $M$: the forms differ)} Off $M$ the plug-in form reads the current amplitudes: an amplitude's
    excursion at fixed masses --- the positions' own escape from the exact fit's well toward approximate fits with
    saturated amplitudes, which the dither reaches at finite $\tau$ (Part F's two escaping seeds) --- is read as a mass
    at its saturation, and the class is fed mass up to the correction's cap: the masses leave the state the leak or the
    correction holds them in. The masses form does not see the excursion: the masses stay, and the escape remains the
    positions' own. A running average of the classes' contributions in place of $\hat A$ inherits the plug-in form's
    fault on the time scale of the average.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The gradient structure: $c_k = \partial_{\beta_k}\Phi_c$ with $\Phi_c = \kappa_F(\tau/2)\ell(\beta/m)$, independent of
$\Theta$, so the masses' fitness is $-\partial_\beta(J - \Phi_c)$ and the positions' drift
$-G\nabla_\Theta(J - \Phi_c)$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34}). (i) Katzenberger's limit with an
$O(\tau)$ perturbation of the drift adds $\partial\Phi\cdot F_c = P\cdot F_c$
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}(ii)); the linearisation of a gradient flow is self-adjoint in its
metric $g$, so $P$ is the $g$-orthogonal projection onto $T$, and with $gF_c = \nabla\Phi_c$,
$P\cdot F_c = T(T^\T gT)^{-1}T^\T\nabla\Phi_c = T(T^\T gT)^{-1}\nabla_W\Phi_c$; in the slow-replicator limit $g$'s
masses' block, of order $1/\gamma$, dominates $T^\T gT$, and
$(T^\T gT)^{-1}\nabla_W\Phi_c\to\gamma W_k(c_k - \sum_lW_lc_l)$, the replicator flow ascending $\Phi_c$, which adds to
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}(ii)'s descent of $(\tau/2)\ell$. The closed form:
$W_ka'_k = W_k - W_{\min,k}^2/W_k$ on $M$, and $\partial_{\beta_k} = m^{-1}\partial_{W_k}$. (ii) With
$f(W) = \log(W - c/W)$, $c = W_{\min}^2$, $f'' = -2(W^2 + c)/(W^2 - c)^2 + 1/W^2 < 0$ for $W^2 > c$ (equivalent to
$W^4 + 4cW^2 - c^2 > 0$); $f\to-\infty$ as $W\downarrow\sqrt c$; so $\ell$ restricted to the simplex is strictly
concave with an interior maximiser, characterised by equal partial derivatives; the replicator flow ascending a
strictly concave function converges to its maximiser (the Shahshahani gradient flow, with $\ell$ as its Lyapunov
function). (iii) Inside the layer $\abs{\partial\alpha_k/\partial W_k} = a_k/(W_ka'_k)\to\infty$, so the amplitude's
part of $T$ dominates $T^\T gT$ and $P\cdot F_c$ moves the configuration along $M$ at
$\dd\alpha_k/\dd s = G_\alpha\partial_{\alpha_k}\Phi_c$, $\Phi_c$ read as a function of $\alpha_k$ on $M$
($W_k = A_k/a_k$); there $\partial_{\alpha_k}\log(W_ka'_k) = a''_k/a'_k - a'_k/a_k = -(1 + (a_k/a_{\max})^2)/a_k$ for
$a = a_{\max}\tanh(\alpha/a_{\max})$, which gives the stated drift; a one-dimensional diffusion with constant drift
$-v$ and diffusion $2\tau G_\alpha$ has the density $e^{-v\alpha/(\tau G_\alpha)}$, and
$\delta\propto e^{-2\alpha_k/a_{\max}}$ near the saturation; with the floor, $c_k$ is capped at
$\kappa_F(\tau/m)(1 + W_{\min,k}^2/W_k^2)/(0.01W_k)$, the fraction $(1 - W_{\min,k}^2/W_k^2)/0.01$ of the unfloored
value; the finite-$\tau$ condition compares $a'_k(M)\approx2\delta/W_{\min,k}$ with the overshoot's change of $a'_k$,
$\approx2c_k/(W_ka_{\max}^2\Gamma_{kk})$, $c_k\approx\kappa_F\tau/(m\delta)$. (iv) Stationarity of $\E[W]$:
$\E[f(W)] = 0$, expanded at $W_0$ to second order in $W - W_0$. (v) The plug-in form's denominator $W_ka'_k$ vanishes
with $a'_k$ at fixed $W_k$; the masses form depends on the masses alone; a running average of $W_ka_k$ over an
excursion with $a_k$ near $\pm a_{\max}$ tends to $W_k(\pm a_{\max})$, so its $\hat W_k$ tends to $W_k$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) the slow-limit cancellation proved (given \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}(ii) and
Katzenberger's theorem), the finite-$\gamma$ formula exact, the $O(\gamma^2\tau)$ remainder numerical; (ii) the
concavity, the uniqueness and the slow-limit convergence proved; (iii) the projected drift proved on $M$ (given
Katzenberger's theorem), the exponents checked in Katzenberger's limit ($\tau\to0$) --- the finite-$\tau$ statement is
untested at the instance's $\tau$ --- and the floor's cap checked; (iv) the second-order expansion of the stationary
mean (standard), checked on paired seeds to within one standard error; (v) a mechanism, numerical. The correction is
the instance's: for one particle per class, through \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}'s closed form of
$\ell$; for other architectures its general form, $\kappa_F(\tau/2)$ times the derivative of
$\log\det\nabla^2_\Theta J$ along $M$ (through the implicit function $\Theta^*(\beta)$ of the exact fit), is the
statement and the closed form its evaluation. Classical (\cite{S66}): the Fixman correction --- the log-determinant of
the constraint metric added to the energy of a system with stiff constraints so that the flexible and the rigid
ensembles agree (Fixman), the Brownian dynamics of stiff bonds against rigid constraints (Hinch), the projection of
diffusions on submanifolds and the mean force (Ciccotti--Leli\`evre--Vanden-Eijnden); the log-determinant of the
confining Hessian as the stiff limit's zero-point term (Helfand; in the overdamped setting Waszkiewicz--Lisicki, the
``trimer paradox''); cancelling a free-energy drift along a slow coordinate online --- the adaptive biasing force
method (Darve--Pohorille; Leli\`evre--Rousset--Stoltz) --- and a gain on such a bias, well-tempered metadynamics' bias
factor (Barducci--Bussi--Parrinello); the log-barrier, its maximiser and the max-det (D-optimal) selection
(Sonnevend; Nesterov--Nemirovski; Vandenberghe--Boyd--Wu); noise-induced drifts of the replicator dynamics
(Fudenberg--Harris; Mertikopoulos--Viossat). New: the correction for the replicator's gauge --- online, in closed form
on the masses ---, the distinction between its masses form and its plug-in form and the plug-in form's failure off
$M$; the selection of the D-optimal fit and the confinement exponent $\delta^{\kappa_F - 1}$ are their applications.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.38 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/gauge_fixman.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_gauge_fixman.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/gauge_fixman.md};
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}'s instance: three bumps, one particle per class at an exact
representation, $m = 3$, $\tau = 10^{-4}$; the drifts from \code{gauge_drift.limit_drift} with the correction as the
perturbation $F_1$, evaluated on $M$, where the two forms agree). (A) At $\kappa_F = 1$ the corrected drift over
$\gamma\tau$ at $W = 1/3$ is $(+0.063, -0.034, -0.029)$, $(+0.020, -0.010, -0.010)$, $(-0.0007, +0.0006, +0.0001)$ and
$(-0.0002, +0.0002, +0.0001)$ at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$, $5\cdot10^{-3}$, $10^{-3}$ and $10^{-4}$, against the uncorrected
$(-0.206, +0.158, +0.048)$, $(-0.288, +0.203, +0.085)$, $(-0.350, +0.236, +0.115)$ and $(-0.361, +0.241, +0.120)$: the
ratio of the largest components 0.31, 0.07, $2.1\cdot10^{-3}$ and $6.8\cdot10^{-4}$, the remainder $O(\gamma^2\tau)$ at
small $\gamma$ (over $\gamma\tau$, $2.4\cdot10^{-4}$, $4.5\cdot10^{-4}$, $6.1\cdot10^{-4}$ at $\gamma = 1$, 2,
$3\cdot10^{-4}$), reversed from $\gamma = 2\cdot10^{-3}$ on ($+0.0017$ in the first component, against $-0.337$) --- the
correction's projected drift at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ is 74\% of its slow limit, the uncorrected one 57\% of its own (from
the table) ---; at $\gamma = 2\cdot10^{-2}$ the ratio is 1.6, and at $(0.40, 0.25, 0.35)$ and $(0.36, 0.30, 0.34)$ 1.3
