and 0.78, reversed as well. (B) $W^\sharp = (0.37652, 0.29876, 0.32472)$, $\abs{a}/a_{\max} = 0.66$, 0.59, 0.62 there,
the D-optimal exact fit --- not the best-conditioned: the condition number of $\nabla^2_\Theta J$ is 649.9 at
$W^\sharp$ and 648.6 at its minimiser along $M$ $(0.3546, 0.3277, 0.3177)$, nearly flat; at $\kappa_F = 2$ the
corrected field's zero lies within $1.8\cdot10^{-4}$, $4.4\cdot10^{-5}$ and $5.4\cdot10^{-6}$ of $W^\sharp$ at
$\gamma = 10^{-2}$, $10^{-3}$, $10^{-4}$, attracting, its eigenvalues $-(5.7$--$6.8)\gamma\tau$, the stationary spread
of the limit SDE linearised there 0.040, 0.037, 0.038 at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$, $\propto\sqrt\gamma$ (0.014 at $10^{-3}$,
0.0046 at $10^{-4}$); at $\kappa_F = 1$ the remainder's zero is attracting at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$
($(0.379, 0.297, 0.324)$, the eigenvalues $-4.5\cdot10^{-7}$, $-5.5\cdot10^{-7}$) but repelling at $10^{-3}$ and
$10^{-4}$ (near $(0.39, 0.29, 0.32)$, the eigenvalues $+16$--$19\gamma^2\tau$ and $+29$--$30\gamma^2\tau$): the neutral
gain leaves the remainder's direction to the higher orders. (C) In Katzenberger's limit ($\tau\to0$), along
$W_2 = W_3$, 0.002--0.004 from the first class's saturation (inside its layer at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$; at the instance's
$\tau$ these $\delta$ are below the finite-$\tau$ threshold of (iii)), the exponent $\dd\log p/\dd\log\delta$ of the
one-dimensional zero-flux density is $-0.99$, $-0.00$ and $+0.98$ for $\kappa_F = 0$, 1, 2, against $\kappa_F - 1$.
(H) Across the floor's cap ($\delta = 0.0015$--$0.0004$; $\delta_{\mathrm{cap}} = 0.00126$) the exponent at
$\kappa_F = 2$ falls from $+0.99$ to $-0.22$ ($+0.99$ to $+1.15$ without the floor), at $\kappa_F = 1$ from $+0.00$ to
$-0.53$: the unfloored exponent minus $\kappa_F(1 - (1 - W_{\min}^2/W^2)/0.01)$ to $10^{-3}$. (D) The full loop with
the plug-in form over $10^6$ slow units at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$, 12 seeds each, with the implemented floor, on the seeds
of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}'s Part H ($\kappa_F = 0$): the seeds' mean of the largest
$\abs{\alpha_1}$ by quarters is 6.6, 10.1, 10.9, 11.3 without the correction, 5.0, 5.7, 5.1, 4.5 at $\kappa_F = 1$ and
4.5, 4.7, 4.4, 4.1 at $\kappa_F = 2$ (the third class's $4.6\to11.5$, $3.7\to5.5$ and $3.5\to4.3$); the records beyond
a saturation in the last quarter 11\% and 12\% of the first and third classes' without it, none with it, though at
$\kappa_F = 1$ 1\% of the third class's last-quarter records have $\abs{\alpha_3} > 10$ (the largest over all the
records 12.5, against 26.9 without the correction and 7.9 at $\kappa_F = 2$) --- excursions along $M$ into its layer,
as (iii)'s flat law at $\kappa_F = 1$ allows ---; the masses' spread at $10^6$ 0.08--0.15 without, 0.05 at
$\kappa_F = 1$, 0.03--0.04 at $\kappa_F = 2$; the second half's mean ($0.3728\pm0.0110$, $0.3139\pm0.0122$,
$0.3133\pm0.0112$) at $\kappa_F = 1$, within 1.5 standard errors both of $W^\sharp$ and of the deterministic mean path
from 1/3 $(0.3563, 0.3196, 0.3241)$ --- the run is shorter than that zero's relaxation, so it does not tell them apart
---, and ($0.3777\pm0.0056$, $0.3041\pm0.0053$, $0.3182\pm0.0061$) at $\kappa_F = 2$, within 1.07 standard errors of
$W^\sharp$ and of the mean path, which has reached it. The rms fit error over the second half is 0.0267 and 0.0268 with
the correction and 0.0253 without, against equipartition's $\sqrt{p\tau/(m\langle F^2\rangle)} = 0.0267$, 0.0252,
0.0236 for $p = 9$, 8, 7 position coordinates holding fit energy:
$p_{\mathrm{eff}} = m\langle F^2\rangle\langle e^2\rangle/\tau = 8.96$ and 9.04 with the correction, 8.06 without --- a
saturated amplitude holds none ---, the dither's level read back as $\hat\tau = 0.996\cdot10^{-4}$ and
$1.00\cdot10^{-4}$ against $\tau = 10^{-4}$. (E) The masses form ($\hat A$ fixed at the initial exact fit, the seeds of
D): the seeds' mean of the largest $\abs{\alpha_1}$ by quarters 5.0, 5.7, 5.1, 4.5 at $\kappa_F = 1$ and 4.5, 4.7, 4.3,
4.1 at $\kappa_F = 2$, no record beyond a saturation; at $\kappa_F = 2$ the largest $\abs{\alpha}$ over all the records
is 8.1 and the second half's mean ($0.3784\pm0.0057$, $0.3030\pm0.0056$, $0.3186\pm0.0063$) is within 1.0 standard
error of $W^\sharp$ (and of the deterministic mean path from 1/3, which has reached $W^\sharp$), the spread at $10^6$
(0.032, 0.042, 0.037) against (B)'s 0.040, 0.037, 0.038; at $\kappa_F = 1$, where (iii)'s law is flat, the third
class's amplitude makes excursions along $M$ into its layer ($\abs{\alpha_3}$ up to 13.5, $\abs{a_3}/a_{\max} = 0.998$),
and the second half's mean ($0.3746\pm0.0115$, $0.3136\pm0.0127$, $0.3118\pm0.0116$) is within 1.6 standard errors
both of $W^\sharp$ and of the mean path $(0.3563, 0.3196, 0.3241)$ --- the run is shorter than the $\kappa_F = 1$
zero's relaxation time ($2\cdot10^6$ slow units), so it does not tell the two apart: the drift toward $W^\sharp$ there
is (i)'s finite-$\gamma$ over-compensation, not the cancellation. Equipartition with the rms fit error:
$p_{\mathrm{eff}} = m\langle F^2\rangle\langle e^2\rangle/\tau = 8.92$ and 9.03 with the correction, 8.06 without it
(one saturated amplitude holds no fit energy) --- the dither's level read back as $\hat\tau = 0.99\cdot10^{-4}$ and
$1.00\cdot10^{-4}$ against $\tau = 10^{-4}$; the correction pays the fit energy of the amplitude it keeps coupled.
(F) With the entropic leak ($\gamma = 3\cdot10^{-3}$, $\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$, 16 seeds, $4\cdot10^5$ slow units): the
same two seeds leave the exact fit's well --- some $\abs{\alpha_k} > 10$ from $1.13\cdot10^5$ and $1.27\cdot10^5$ slow
units on --- without the correction and in every form, the fit error there 0.043 against 0.027. The masses stay where
the leak holds them without the correction (0.314--0.348 over all the records) and with the masses form at
$\kappa_F = 2$ (0.313--0.349); the plug-in form reads the escaped amplitudes as masses at their saturation and moves
the masses over 0.21--0.46; the masses form with $\hat A$ tracked ($T_A = 10^5$) absorbs the escaped classes'
contributions ($\hat A = 1.38$, $-0.99$, 1.10 and 1.39, $-1.10$, 1.16 at $4\cdot10^5$ against 1, $-0.7$, 0.8) and
starts to move them ($W_1$ up to 0.366): $\hat A$ is to be taken from a fit, not from a running average. On the 14
seeds without an escape the leak's pinned state is shifted by ($-0.00072\pm0.00035$, $+0.00062\pm0.00024$,
$+0.00010\pm0.00034$) without the correction and by ($+0.00145\pm0.00032$, $-0.00083\pm0.00023$, $-0.00061\pm0.00033$)
with the masses form at $\kappa_F = 2$ (the plug-in form's alike), against the first order
$(-0.00106, +0.00072, +0.00035)$ and $(+0.00111, -0.00073, -0.00037)$ and the second order
$(-0.00116, +0.00077, +0.00039)$ and $(+0.00103, -0.00069, -0.00034)$: the reversal (iv) predicts, within 1.1 (first
order) and 1.3 (second order) standard errors, the fluctuation term inside the errors at this $\varepsilon$. (G) With
the leak inside (iv)'s window ($\gamma = 10^{-2}$, $\varepsilon = 3\cdot10^{-4}$; the 16 seeds of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}'s Part G, whose uncorrected runs this repeats bit for bit): the
pinned state's shift from 1/3 is ($-0.00129\pm0.00039$, $+0.00112\pm0.00023$, $+0.00017\pm0.00035$) without the
correction, ($+0.00001\pm0.00037$, $+0.00026\pm0.00022$, $-0.00027\pm0.00034$) at $\kappa_F = 1$ and
($+0.00120\pm0.00035$, $-0.00054\pm0.00021$, $-0.00066\pm0.00033$) at $\kappa_F = 2$, against the second order
$(-0.00134, +0.00089, +0.00045)$, $(-0.00006, +0.00004, +0.00001)$ and $(+0.00112, -0.00075, -0.00038)$, within 1.03
standard errors in every component (the first order $\kappa^{-1}b_c$, $(-0.00092, +0.00066, +0.00027)$,
$(+0.00028, -0.00015, -0.00014)$ and $(+0.00149, -0.00095, -0.00054)$, is 1.9--2.1 standard errors off in $W_2$ ---
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{D$'''$.37}'s excess); the fluctuation term, $(-0.00042, +0.00023, +0.00019)$ without the
correction and $(-0.00036, +0.00020, +0.00016)$ at $\kappa_F = 1$, comes from the leak's projected force ($b$'s part
$2\cdot10^{-5}$, the correction's below $10^{-5}$). So ``the bias gone at $\kappa_F = 1$'' is a cancellation between the
finite-$\gamma$ over-compensation, $\kappa^{-1}b_c = (+0.00028, -0.00015, -0.00014)$, and the fluctuation term, not
(i)'s cancellation. On the same seeds the correction's own shift, the difference from $\kappa_F = 0$, is measured to
$1$--$4\cdot10^{-5}$: $(+0.00129, -0.00086, -0.00044)$ and $(+0.00249, -0.00166, -0.00083)$, against the second
order's $(+0.00128, -0.00085, -0.00043)$ and $(+0.00246, -0.00164, -0.00082)$ --- within 0.93 standard errors ---
where the first order $(+0.00121, -0.00081, -0.00040)$ and $(+0.00241, -0.00161, -0.00080)$ is 1.2--4.8 standard
errors off and the nonlinear fixed point 2.4--3.8: the paired design resolves the fluctuation term. The second order
also accounts for \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{D$'''$.37}'s Part G at its other leaks: at $\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$ and
$10^{-3}$ the second component's excess over $\kappa^{-1}b$ falls from 2.8 and 1.7 to 1.7 and 0.8 standard errors.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.39 with its proof sketch, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-31 version, as committed
% at 5b747b9; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/gauge_plateau.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:39 is a
% \hyperref target, as res:thmDppp:21 is. The reference [S67] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) is the bibitem S67 of refs.tex.
% The unconstrained coordinates (alpha, gamma, kappa) are Theorem H's; gamma is also the replicator's rate in the
% numerics, as in PARADIGM. The Brownian motions, PARADIGM's B_i, are written \mathcal W_i here, as in Proposition
% D'''.36's proof, to keep them apart from the box B. Proposition D'''.40, which the text cites, is not stated in
% PARADIGM.md (in progress), so the citation is plain text.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.39}[dissipativity of the dithered gauge: without a prior the dithered loop has no
stationary law; the dither reaches saturated approximate fits; a dead zone restores the law, without bias for the exact
fits inside it]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:39}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36} --- the positions in the unconstrained coordinates
$\vartheta = (\alpha,\gamma,\kappa)$ of the saturating maps $a = a_{\max}\tanh(\alpha/a_{\max})$,
$c = c_{\max}\tanh(\gamma/c_{\max})$, $s = s_{\min} + (s_{\max} - s_{\min})\operatorname{sig}(\kappa)$, dithered at the
level $\tau$ ($\dd\vartheta_i = -G\partial_{\vartheta_i}J\dd s + \sqrt{2\tau G}\dd\mathcal W_i$), the cohesion leak, the
masses fixed or following the replicator ---:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(no stationary law)} $J$ is bounded on the unconstrained coordinates --- the physical coordinates are
    bounded and the cohesion energy $W\le R^2/8$ ---, so at fixed masses the Gibbs measure $e^{-J/\tau}\dd\Theta$,
    invariant for the positions' diffusion, has infinite mass for every $\tau > 0$: the positions have no invariant
    probability law, and the fraction of time they spend in any bounded set of the unconstrained coordinates tends to
    zero. With the prior leak of Theorems D$'$--J$'$, $(\sigma_0/2)\sum_i\norm{\vartheta_i - \vartheta^0}^2_{\Ginv}$,
    $J$ is coercive and the Gibbs law has Gaussian tails (Theorem J$'$); the cohesion leak, a function of the
    differences $\vartheta_i - \vartheta_j$, removed the prior's bias (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:32]{Proposition
    D$'''$.32}) and with it this confinement.
  \item \emph{(the plateau and the escape)} Where a coordinate saturates, $J$ tends to its value at the bound and that
    coordinate's force vanishes ($\partial J/\partial\alpha_k = a'_k\partial J/\partial a_k$, $a'_k\to0$): approximate
    fits with saturated coordinates form plateaus on which the saturated coordinates diffuse without drift. The exact
    fit's well holds the positions for a time $e^{\Delta J_D/\tau + o(1/\tau)}$ (Freidlin--Wentzell), $\Delta J_D$ the
    lowest energy above the exact fit on the boundary of a part $D$ of its basin that excludes the plateaus; after that
    the positions spend a growing fraction of the time on the plateaus, their fit error at the plateau's level and the
    saturated amplitudes' gain $a'_k\to0$. On the instance the way out is a monotone climb, without a saddle, of the
    widths and amplitudes to a plateau $\Delta J\approx7.7\tau$ ($\tau = 10^{-4}$) above the exact fit.
  \item \emph{(the dead zone)} Add to $J$ the dead zone
    $V(\Theta) = (\sigma_B/2)\sum_i\norm{\vartheta_i - \Pi_B(\vartheta_i)}^2_{\Ginv}$, $B = \prod_j[-L_j,L_j]$ a box in
    the unconstrained coordinates, $\sigma_B > 0$ (the Moreau--Yosida envelope of the box's indicator; in adaptive
    control a switching $\sigma$-modification) --- on the drift $-\sigma_B(\vartheta_i - \Pi_B(\vartheta_i))$, zero
    inside $B$. Then (a) $e^{-(J+V)/\tau}$ is integrable: at fixed masses the positions have a unique stationary law,
    and the excursions onto a plateau, still reached at the rate of (ii) where the plateau meets $B$, end; (b) inside
    $B$ the deterministic objects do not change --- the exact fits with their positions in $B$ are stationary without
    bias, and the manifold $M$, Katzenberger's limit on it and Propositions
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{D$'''$.36}--\hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{D$'''$.38} are unchanged there (a dithered
    trajectory is the unboxed one until a coordinate leaves $B$) ---, but the stationary law is not the well's: it gives
    the plateau, where it meets $B$, the weight of its Laplace order (on the instance 9--19\% of the records at
    $\tau = 2\cdot10^{-4}$, Numerics (D)), and there the box's force enters the masses' averaged fitness at the size of
    the noise's own selection (Proposition D$'''$.40(i)); (c) the representable masses become
    $W_k > W_{B,k} := \abs{A_k}/(a_{\max}\tanh(L_\alpha/a_{\max})) > W_{\min,k}$: the gauge's saturation is replaced by
    the box's wall, where $a'_k = \operatorname{sech}^2(L_\alpha/a_{\max}) > 0$, so $\ell$ stays finite on the
    representable masses, and the entropic drift of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37} ends at vertices
    of the polytope $P_B = \{W_k\ge W_{B,k},\ \sum W_k = 1\}$ --- the local minima of the strictly concave $\ell$ on it
    --- instead of running into the saturation; $V$ is $C^{1,1}$ only (its Hessian jumps by $\sigma_B$ on $B$'s faces),
    so Katzenberger's limit and the entropic drift, which use third derivatives, hold away from a boundary layer at
    $P_B$'s faces, and the vertex statement is at leading order (a $C^3$ penalty removes the layer); (d) with
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition D$'''$.38}'s correction at $\kappa_F > 1$ the masses go to $W^\sharp$,
    provided $W^\sharp\in P_B$, i.e.\ $L_\alpha > a_{\max}\operatorname{artanh}(W_{\min,k}/W^\sharp_k)$ for every $k$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(i) The image of the saturating maps is bounded and $\fhat$ is continuous in the physical coordinates, so the
residual's mean square is bounded, and $\omega\le1/8$ (compact kernel) or $\le1$ (Gaussian): $J$ is bounded,
$e^{-J/\tau}\ge e^{-\sup J/\tau} > 0$ and $\int e^{-J/\tau}\dd\Theta = \infty$. The generator
$\tau\nabla\cdot(G\nabla) - G\nabla J\cdot\nabla$ is symmetric in $L^2(e^{-J/\tau}\dd\Theta)$; the drift is bounded (the
saturating maps have bounded derivatives, $s\ge s_{\min}$, the cohesion force $\le\sigma_0R$), so the diffusion does
not explode, and it is elliptic. A positive-recurrent elliptic diffusion has a unique invariant measure up to a factor,
finite --- but $e^{-J/\tau}\dd\Theta$ is invariant and infinite; so the diffusion is null recurrent or transient, and
the occupation fraction of a bounded set tends to zero (the ratio limit theorem against ever larger sets, or a finite
occupation time). (ii) On a plateau the saturated coordinate's drift is $O(a'_k) = O(e^{-2\abs{\alpha_k}/a_{\max}})$.
For the gradient diffusion the quasi-potential is $J - J(\text{exact})$, and Freidlin--Wentzell's exit theorem gives
$\tau\log\E[\text{exit time from }D]\to\Delta J_D$ for a domain $D$ in the basin whose closure flows to the exact fit;
the path's monotone climb (B) makes the plateau's entrance the boundary's lowest part. (iii) (a)
$J + V\ge(\sigma_B/2)\operatorname{dist}_{\Ginv}(\Theta,B^m)^2 - \sup\abs{J}$, integrable against $e^{-\cdot/\tau}$;
$J + V$ is a Lyapunov function (its generator $\to-\infty$ outside a compact); uniqueness by ellipticity. (b) $V$ and
$\nabla V$ vanish on $B$. (c) An exact fit needs $\abs{a_k} = \abs{A_k}/W_k\le a_{\max}\tanh(L_\alpha/a_{\max})$; below
$W_{B,k}$ the fit in $B$ undershoots and the class's fitness $u_k > 0$ feeds it, so $P_B$ is invariant to leading
order; $\ell = 2\sum\log(W_k - W_{\min,k}^2/W_k)$ is finite and strictly concave on $P_B$, and a local minimum of a
strictly concave function on a polytope is a vertex. (d) \hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition D$'''$.38}(ii) on
$P_B$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved; (ii) the plateau, its level and the monotone path numerical, the exit time's exponential order
Freidlin--Wentzell's (classical), its slope measured at $(0.81\pm0.14)\Delta J$ at the instance's
$\Delta J/\tau = 2.6$--$5.1$, within 1.3 standard errors of Freidlin--Wentzell's limit; (iii)(a), (b) proved ((b) for
the deterministic objects and the dynamics before the first exit from $B$, not for the stationary averages), (c) the
polytope's invariance at leading order and the vertices away from the faces' boundary layer (sketched), (d) given
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition D$'''$.38}(ii). Classical: dissipativity and the existence of invariant laws for
Langevin dynamics (Mattingly--Stuart--Higham; Raginsky--Rakhlin--Telgarsky for learning), the exit problem and
metastability (Freidlin--Wentzell; Bovier--Eckhoff--Gayrard--Klein), parameter projection and its Lipschitz boundary
layer in adaptive control (Ioannou--Sun; Pomet--Praly), Langevin sampling on a convex set through the Moreau--Yosida
envelope of its indicator (MYULA: Durmus--Moulines--Pereyra; Brosse--Durmus--Moulines--Pereyra) (\cite{S67}). New:
that the cohesion leak's removal of the prior's bias also removed the dithered loop's stationary law; the plateau of
saturated approximate fits on the instance and the monotone climb to it; the dead zone's effect on the gauge --- the
saturation replaced by a non-degenerate wall at which the entropic drift ends --- and its compatibility with the Fixman
correction.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.39 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/gauge_plateau.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_gauge_plateau.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/gauge_plateau.md};
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}'s instance, energies over $\tau = 10^{-4}$; the box $L = (5, 3, 4)$ in
$(\alpha,\gamma,\kappa)$, $\sigma_B = 1$, so $\abs{a}\le3.39$ and the representable masses
$W_k\ge(0.2947, 0.2063, 0.2358)$ against the saturation's $(0.25, 0.175, 0.2)$). (A) The lowest configurations with one
amplitude fixed at $\pm20$ (200 starts each) lie $7.72\tau$ (the first or third class) and $7.76\tau$ (the second) above
the exact fit at $W = 1/3$, their fit error 0.035 --- approximate fits with the widths broadened (0.58--0.75 against
0.4--0.5) and two amplitudes up, or the same with the first and third particles exchanged --- and $7.64\tau$,
$7.71\tau$ at $W^\sharp$; on the escape set's boundary $\max\abs{\alpha_k} = 6$, $7.69\tau$ ($W = 1/3$) and $7.67\tau$
($W^\sharp$); at the box's edge $7.66\tau$ and $7.63\tau$: the box does not raise the plateau, it bounds it. (B) At
$W = 1/3$ the minimum-energy path to the plateau (40 images) climbs monotonically --- 0, 1.86, 5.33, 7.17, 7.57, 7.66,
$7.70\tau$ at every fourth image ---, the second width $0.40\to0.75$ and the fit error $0\to0.035$ along it, without a
saddle; at $W^\sharp$ the path to the lowest plateau (the outer amplitudes saturated, the middle width 1.17) crosses a
saddle at $8.21\tau$, the escape boundary's lowest point ($7.67\tau$) lying on the other family. (C) The escapes at
fixed masses $W = 1/3$ ($\gamma = 0$, 24 seeds each, up to $4\cdot10^5$ slow units; an escape when
$\max\abs{\alpha_k} > 6$): 10, 19 and 24 of the 24 at $\tau = 1.5$, 2 and $3\cdot10^{-4}$, the rates
$(1.33\pm0.42)\cdot10^{-6}$, $(4.06\pm0.93)\cdot10^{-6}$ and $(1.09\pm0.22)\cdot10^{-5}$ per slow unit, the fit error
at the escape 0.046--0.054; $\log(\text{rate})$ against $1/\tau$ has the slope $6.31\cdot10^{-4}$ (weighted by the
escape counts $(6.25\pm1.09)\cdot10^{-4}$) against the escape boundary's lowest $\Delta J = 7.69\cdot10^{-4}$ ---
$(0.81\pm0.14)\Delta J$, within 1.3 standard errors of Freidlin--Wentzell's limit at these barriers
($\Delta J/\tau = 2.6$--$5.1$; the rate times $e^{\Delta J/\tau}$ is 2.25, 1.90 and $1.42\cdot10^{-4}$). (D) The dead
zone at fixed masses ($\tau = 2\cdot10^{-4}$, 16 seeds, $4\cdot10^5$ slow units; a record on the plateau when the second
width exceeds 0.6): without the box every seed reaches the plateau and the occupation grows --- 0.18, 0.40, 0.54, 0.57
of the records by quarters, $\max\abs{\alpha} > 6$ in $0.09\to0.56$ of them, the largest $\abs{\alpha}$
$15.0\to27.6$, the fit error $0.040\to0.045$ ---; with the box every seed still reaches it, but the occupation stays
at 0.09--0.19, no record has $\max\abs{\alpha} > 6$ ($\abs{\alpha}\le5.0$, the box's edge), and the fit error stays at
0.039--0.040: the excursions onto the plateau end. (E) The full loop with the box ($\gamma = 10^{-2}$,
$\tau = 10^{-4}$, 12 seeds, $10^6$ slow units; $P_B$'s vertices $(0.558, 0.206, 0.236)$, $(0.295, 0.470, 0.236)$,
$(0.295, 0.206, 0.499)$): each run is the unboxed one record for record until a coordinate first leaves $B$ (without
the correction from $1.7\cdot10^4$ to $4.1\cdot10^5$ slow units; at $\kappa_F = 2$ from $2.4\cdot10^4$ on, one seed
never); without the correction the masses' spread at $10^6$ is 0.049, 0.075, 0.057 against 0.145, 0.154, 0.081
without the box, each seed's masses still 0.10--0.18 from the nearest vertex --- on their way, at the entropic drift's
slow rate; at $\kappa_F = 2$ the masses' mean at $10^6$ ($0.3803\pm0.0093$, $0.2961\pm0.0122$, $0.3236\pm0.0102$) is
within 0.41 standard errors of $W^\sharp$, every seed within 0.010--0.080 of it; $\abs{\alpha}\le5.03$, the fit error
0.026. (F) In \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}'s runs without a leak (H) every record with
$\abs{\alpha_k} > 10$ but one (3860 of 3861, on 8, 3 and 7 of the 12 seeds) has $W_k$ within 0.01 of the saturation,
and the exact fit's error (0.024): the gauge's channel; with the masses held near 1/3 by the leak and no correction
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition D$'''$.38} (F)) every such record has $W_k$ more than 0.05 above it and the fit
error 0.041--0.043 (516 and 13 records, on 2 and 1 of the 16 seeds): the positions' own channel, the escape of (ii).
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.41 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d as committed at da1a80e, with the last
% sentence of (iv)(e) and Numerics (G)'s gamma = 10^-5 sentence as revised after the round-31 rerun of Part G
% (a8074e0), a revision not yet committed when this was typeset; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/gauge_label.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:41 is a \hyperref target, as res:thmDppp:21 is. The references
% [S64], [S69] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) are the bibitems S64 and S69 of refs.tex. PARADIGM's mobility M is written
% \mathsf M here, to keep it apart from the manifold M of exact fits (PARADIGM's (i) uses both in one sentence: "On M
% every U_x is orthogonal to M in the flow's metric g = M^-1").
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.41}[measurement noise on the gauge: no selection in continuous time; per update, the
increment's second moment selects]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:41}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36} (one particle per class, the classes beyond the
cohesion's range, the replicator at rate $\gamma$, the manifold $M$ of exact fits), let the loop be driven by the
measured error: the residual $r$ replaced by $r + \xi$ in both laws --- with the desired regressor of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:33]{Corollary D$'''$.33} the measurement noise enters through the error alone ---, $\xi$ a noise
on the orbit's quadrature nodes, of intensity $v$ ($\langle\xi h\rangle_\rho$ has the variance $v\langle h^2\rangle_\rho$
per unit slow time), and no dither. Unless said otherwise $\xi$ is homoscedastic and independent over the nodes --- the
image of a measurement noise white on the orbit's resolution (correlation time $\tau_n\ll T/N$). In the coordinates
$z = (\Theta,\log\beta)$ the loop is $\dd z = \langle(r + \xi)U\rangle_\rho\dd s$ with
\[
  U_x := m\mathsf M\nabla_z\fhat(x) = \big(\beta_iG\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x;\vartheta_i),\
  \gamma(\tilde\Phi(x;\vartheta_i) - \fhat(x))\big)_i,
\]
$\mathsf M$ the flow's mobility ($G$ on the positions, $\gamma/\beta$ on $\log\beta$, projected on $\sum\beta = m$): the
noise enters along the descent's own vector fields. Write $F = \langle rU\rangle_\rho$ for the noiseless flow, $\Phi$
for its limit map, $A = \partial F$ at a point of $M$ (eigenvalues 0 on $TM$ and $-\lambda_i < 0$),
$\psi_x := \partial\Phi\cdot U_x$, and $\tilde\Phi_k = a_k\Phi_k$ ($\Phi_k$ the $k$-th bump's shape,
$a_k = a_{\max}\tanh(\alpha_k/a_{\max})$ its amplitude).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(no diffusion along $M$)} On $M$ every $U_x$ is orthogonal to $M$ in the flow's metric
    $g = \mathsf M^{-1}$ --- $\langle U_x,t\rangle_g = m\partial_t\fhat(x) = 0$ for $t$ tangent to $M$ ---, so the masses
    do not diffuse along $M$ at order $v$ (under dither they do, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}).
  \item \emph{(no drift in continuous time)} For white noise in Stratonovich's sense --- the limit of noise with smooth
    paths (Wong--Zakai) ---, and for a stationary noise with smooth paths and any correlation function in time, the
    drift along $M$ vanishes at order $v$: Katzenberger's It\^o drift $\tfrac12\partial^2\Phi[\Sigma]$,
    $\Sigma = v\langle UU^\T\rangle_\rho$, and the Wong--Zakai term $(v/2)\partial\Phi\langle(U\cdot\nabla)U\rangle_\rho$
    cancel exactly on $M$, and the correlation adds $\langle\nabla\psi\cdot K(A)U\rangle_\rho = 0$ for every function
    $K$. A loop adapting in continuous time does not select among its exact fits through its measurement noise. Beyond
    the hypothesis --- a variance profile over the nodes, a noise correlated across them (a measurement offset; a
    real-time noise correlated on the orbit's scale, $\tau_n\gtrsim T/N$), a noise that reaches the adaptation through
    the loop and arrives correlated over the orbit --- the node covariance $C$ weights the bilinear form below and its
    antisymmetry no longer removes the Stratonovich drift; on this gauge that drift is $O(\gamma)$ relative to its own
    It\^o drift: in the slow-replicator limit the masses' part of $\psi = \partial\Phi\cdot U$ vanishes to
    $O(\gamma^2)$ in a neighbourhood of $M$ --- the masses' noise is the amplitudes' carried along the gauge ---, a
    second mechanism, which several particles per class break (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:42]{Proposition D$'''$.42}(iv));
    the $O(\gamma)$ law is asymptotic ($\gamma\lesssim10^{-3}$ on the instance, $\gamma\lesssim10^{-4}$ for the strongly
    modulated profile $1 + 0.8\sin t$, whose $O(\gamma)$ coefficient is small: the local exponent 1.4 between $10^{-3}$
    and $10^{-4}$), and its constant grows like $1/a'$ toward a saturation.
  \item \emph{(the selection per update)} With noise drawn afresh at each update of an Euler step $h$ in $z$ (the It\^o
    interpretation: sampled-data adaptation, label-noise gradient descent), the drift along $M$ is
    $-(v/2)\partial\Phi\langle(U\cdot\nabla)U\rangle_\rho$ up to $O(h\lambda_{\max})$ ((iv) gives the whole factor). For
    a Euclidean metric this is $-(mv/4)\nabla_M\tr\nabla^2J$ --- label-noise SGD's flat-minimum selection. On the
    replicator's masses, in the slow-replicator limit, it is the replicator flow of the fitness
    \begin{gather*}
      \bar u_k = -(v/2)\beta_kG_\alpha\langle\Phi_k^2\rangle_\rho(a_k'^2 - a_ka''_k)
      = -(v/2)\beta_kG_\alpha\langle\Phi_k^2\rangle_\rho(1 - q_k^2) = -\partial_{\beta_k}\Psi_\ell,\\
      q_k = (a_k/a_{\max})^2,\qquad
      \Psi_\ell(W) = (v/4)\sum_kG_\alpha\langle\Phi_k^2\rangle_\rho m^2(W_k - W_{\min,k}^2/W_k)^2
    \end{gather*}
    (the amplitudes' part of $(mv/4)\tr(G\nabla^2J)$; the centres' and widths' parts are constant on $M$), strictly
    convex in $W$, with a unique minimiser $W_\ell$ on the simplex, interior to the representable masses
    ($W_{\ell,k} > W_{\min,k}$ for every $k$) --- the slow limit's target. The It\^o selection depends on the
    coordinates of the update (the Stratonovich zero of (ii) does not): an Euler step in $z' = \chi(z)$ adds
    $-(v/2)\partial\Phi\langle\partial^2\chi[U,U]\rangle_\rho$; in the amplitude $a$ itself instead of the network's
    parameter $\alpha$ the factor $a'^2 - aa''$ becomes $a'^2 - 2aa'' = (1 - q)(1 + 3q)$, the fitness of the strictly
    convex $(v/2)\sum_kG_\alpha\langle\Phi_k^2\rangle_\rho(\beta_k^2/2 + 2c_k\log\beta_k + 3c_k^2/(2\beta_k^2))$,
    $c_k = (mA_k/a_{\max})^2$, with an interior minimiser $W_\ell^a$. Either fitness vanishes linearly at the
    saturation ($q\to1$), where the dither's diverges (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}): fresh
    measurement noise draws the masses away from the saturation, but does not hold them off it against a dither of
    comparable level.
  \item \emph{(correlated draws: the increment's second moment selects)} Let the draws be stationary, with step
    correlations $\rho_d$ and per-step variance $\sigma_\xi^2$ (homoscedastic and independent over the nodes), and the
    step $h < 2/\lambda_{\max}$. At order $v$ the Euler scheme's drift along $M$ is Katzenberger's drift of the
    filtered covariance
    \begin{gather*}
      \Sigma_f = \sum_{il}f_{il}\Sigma_{il}e_ie_l^\T\\
      (\Sigma_{il}\ \text{the components of}\ \langle UU^\T\rangle_\rho\ \text{in the eigenbasis}\
      (e_i,-\lambda_i)\ \text{of}\ A\ \text{on the normal space}),\\
      f_{il} = 1 - h(\lambda_iR(a_i) + \lambda_lR(a_l))
      + h^2\lambda_i\lambda_l(1 + G(a_i) + G(a_l))/(1 - a_ia_l),\\
      a = 1 - h\lambda,\qquad G(a) = \sum_{d\ge1}\rho_da^d,\qquad R(a) = G(a)/a,
    \end{gather*}
    times the per-update variance rate $v_h := h\sigma_\xi^2/N$: the second moment of the whole increment
    $h(F + \langle U\xi\rangle_\rho)$ --- the normal deviation responding to the past draws --- over that of the noise.
    The intensity is $v = v_h\sum_d\rho_{\abs d}$ ($(1 + \rho)/(1 - \rho)\cdot v_h$ for AR(1) noise $\rho_d = \rho^d$).
    So: (a) if $h\lambda_{\max}\ll1 - \rho$ ($\lambda_{\max}\tau_c\ll1$, $\tau_c$ the correlation time) $f\approx1$ and
    the selection is $(1 - \rho)/(1 + \rho)$ times (iii)'s at a fixed intensity --- more for anti-correlated noise,
    $1/k$ of it for a draw kept $k$ steps ---; (b) for noise drawn afresh $f_{ii} = 2/(2 - h\lambda_i)$, and on a
    flat-metric gradient flow with label noise ($\Sigma\propto\nabla^2J$ on $M$) the selection descends
    $\tr\Psi(\nabla^2J)$, $\Psi' = f_{ii}$: here $\Psi(\lambda) = -(2/h)\log(1 - h\lambda/2)$, Damian--Ma--Lee's
    finite-step regulariser, convex in $\lambda$ (the stiff modes weigh more); (c) for an AR(1) noise --- a Markov noise,
    with white innovations --- of correlation time $\tau_c$ sampled finely ($h\ll\tau_c,1/\lambda_{\max}$),
    $f_{il}\to(1 + 2x_ix_l/(x_i + x_l))/((1 + x_i)(1 + x_l))$, $x = \lambda\tau_c$ (the active Ornstein--Uhlenbeck
    particle's filter $1/(1 + \lambda\tau_c)$ on the diagonal), and $\Psi(\lambda)\to\log(1 + \lambda\tau_c)/\tau_c$:
    the selected potential goes from the trace of the Hessian ($\tau_c\to0$) to
    $(1/\tau_c)\log\det(I + \tau_c\nabla^2J)$ --- the log-determinant for a slow noise, which the stiff modes absorb
    (the residual follows the noise) ---, so the noise's colour reweights the normal modes and turns the selection's
    direction, not only its size. The log-determinant is the Markov noise's: for any stationary noise sampled finely,
    of normalised spectral density $\hat s$,
    $f(\lambda) = \E[\dot n^2]/\sigma_\xi^2 = \int\hat s(\omega)\omega^2/(\lambda^2 + \omega^2)\dd\omega/2\pi$, the
    response's velocity over the noise --- decreasing in $\lambda$ for every coloured noise, constant only for white
    noise ---, so $\Psi = \int f$ is concave for every colour, and $\tr\Psi(\nabla^2J)$ is a concave function of the
    Hessian; a smooth noise (Gaussian step correlations, finite $\E[\dot\xi^2]$) has $f\approx(\lambda\tau_c)^{-2}$
    for $\lambda\tau_c\gg1$ and a bounded $\Psi$; (d) at a fixed physical noise the selection vanishes with $h$: (ii);
    Heun's scheme with the same draw in both stages integrates the ODE driven by the draws' piecewise-constant path,
    and its $O(v)$ selection is $O((h\lambda)^2)$ of the Euler scheme's, zero only as $h\to0$; (e) \emph{(a slow Markov
    measurement noise is a dither)} for label noise $\Sigma = -m\cdot A\mathsf M$ on $M$, and an AR(1) noise with
    $\lambda_{\min}\tau_c\to\infty$, the filter turns it into $\tau_c\Sigma_f\to X_h$, $X_h - BX_hB^\T = 2hS$
    ($B = I + hA$, $S$ the normal part of $\Sigma$: the pair $(i,l)$ weighted by
    $2/(\lambda_i + \lambda_l - h\lambda_i\lambda_l)$), and as $h\to0$ into
    $-A_D\Sigma Q^\T = m\cdot Q\mathsf MQ^\T$ ($A_D$ the group inverse, $Q = I - P$) --- the flow's own mobility on the
    normal space, a thermal noise in the flow's metric: the noise enters through the descent's fields ($\propto A$) and
    the slow filter divides by $A$ ---, so a slow Markov measurement noise selects as a dither of the flow's mobility
    does: on the replicator's masses, in the slow-replicator limit, as \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition
    D$'''$.37}'s dither of temperature $\tau_{\mathrm{eff}} = m\cdot v_h/(2\tau_c)$ --- the entropic selection toward
    the saturation, the opposite of the fresh noise's interior target, which \hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition
    D$'''$.38}'s correction at $\tau_{\mathrm{eff}}$ cancels. Its size is small:
    $\tau_{\mathrm{eff}}\approx mvh/(4\tau_c^2)$ at a fixed intensity. The limit needs $\lambda_{\min}\tau_c\gg1$, which
    fails near a saturation, where the softest normal rate vanishes. At a finite replicator rate the selection is
    multistable instead: from $\tau_c\approx100$ on three stable zeros, each with one class's mass large, and from
    $\tau_c = 10^3$ on an unstable node in the middle, next to $W^\sharp$ (Numerics (G)).
  \item \emph{(with dither)} The leading-order drifts of independent noises add (Katzenberger's drift is linear in the
    covariance, the Wong--Zakai terms of independent noises do not mix): with dither and measurement noise the masses'
    selection is the dither's (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}) plus (iii)'s, the latter zero in
    continuous time --- so \hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition D$'''$.38}'s correction needs only the dither's level
    --- and, by (iii), unable to offset the dither's drive into the saturation near it.
  \item \emph{(two exact examples)} For the scalar factorisation $f = uv$ fitted to $y$ with label noise, the rescaling
    symmetry's charge $Q = u^2 - v^2$ is conserved by the gradient flow and by the noise's field $(v,u)$, hence
    pathwise by the Stratonovich dynamics, while an Euler step multiplies it by exactly $1 - (\xi_n - r_n)^2h^2$
    whatever the noise sequence: $\E[Q]$ decays at $h\cdot\E[(\xi - r)^2]$ per unit time --- the per-step variance times
    $f(h\lambda)$, $\lambda = u^2 + v^2$ ((iv)); $\sigma^2$ for noise drawn afresh as $h\to0$, the It\^o SDE's
    $\dd Q = -\sigma^2Q\dd t$ exactly. For the diagonal linear network $w = u\odot u - v\odot v$ fitted to $n < d$
    samples the products $P_i = u_iv_i$ --- the effective initialisation scales --- are conserved likewise, and an Euler
    step multiplies each by exactly $1 - 4h^2(g_i - \tilde\xi_i)^2$ ($g = \nabla_wL$, $\tilde\xi = X^\T\xi/n$): the
    selection --- the descent of $\tr\nabla^2L = (4/n)\sum_ic_i\sqrt{w_i^2 + 4P_i^2}$ ($c_i = \sum_jX_{ji}^2$), a
    weighted $\ell^1$ norm $\sum_ic_i\abs{w_i}$ as $P\to0$ --- runs at $4h^2\E[(X_i^\T e)^2]/n^2$ per step
    ($e = r - \xi$), the per-step variance times $\sum_jf(h\kappa_j)(X_i^\T q_j)^2/c_i$ over the residual's modes
    $(\kappa_j,q_j)$ of $K = (4/n)X\operatorname{diag}(u^2 + v^2)X^\T$: a factor that differs from coordinate to
    coordinate when the noise is slow. Stratonovich noise does not move the interpolant. On a generic overparametrised
    network the sharpness $T = \tr(J^\T J)/n$ descends at $-(v_h/4)\nabla T\cdot P_T\nabla\tr\Psi(K)$
    ($K = J^\T J/n$, $P_T$ the orthogonal projection onto the manifold's tangent), $-(v/4)\abs{P_T\nabla T}^2$ for
    fresh noise at small $h$, not at all for Stratonovich noise (checked for $\rho\le0.9$; slower noise selects below
    the measurement's resolution there).
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) $\langle U_x,t\rangle_g = m(\mathsf M\nabla\fhat(x))^\T\mathsf M^{-1}t = m\nabla\fhat(x)\cdot t$, and $\fhat$ is
constant along $M$. (ii) $\Phi$ is the limit map of $F = \langle rU\rangle_\rho$ ($r$ vanishes on $M$),
$\psi_x = \partial\Phi\cdot U_x$ vanishes on $M$ by (i), $\partial\Phi$ being the $g$-orthogonal projection onto $TM$
there. The invariance $\partial\Phi\cdot F\equiv0$ reads $\langle r\psi\rangle_\rho\equiv0$ near $M$; differentiating
twice at a point of $M$, where $r$ and $\psi$ vanish, gives
$\langle\partial_vr\,\partial_w\psi + \partial_wr\,\partial_v\psi\rangle_\rho = 0$: the bilinear form
$B(v,w) := \langle\partial_vr\,\partial_w\psi\rangle_\rho$ is antisymmetric. In Stratonovich's form, It\^o's formula
gives $\dd\Phi(z) = \partial\Phi\cdot{\circ}\dd z$, whose drift at order $v$ is
$(v/2)\langle U\cdot\nabla\psi\rangle_\rho = -(mv/2)\sum_{ab}\mathsf M_{ab}B(e_b,e_a) = -(mv/2)\tr(\mathsf MB) = 0$,
$\mathsf M$ symmetric, $B$ antisymmetric; the same sum is Katzenberger's $\tfrac12\partial^2\Phi[\Sigma]$ plus the
Wong--Zakai term $(v/2)\partial\Phi\langle(U\cdot\nabla)U\rangle_\rho$ (the product rule). For a smooth stationary noise
of correlation $R$ the linear response $\delta z(s) = \int_0^\infty e^{uA}\langle U\xi(s - u)\rangle_\rho\dd u$ gives the
drift $\langle\nabla\psi\cdot K(A)U\rangle_\rho$, $K(A) = \int_0^\infty R(u)e^{uA}\dd u$, and on $M$,
$A = \langle U\nabla r^\T\rangle_\rho = -\mathsf M\nabla^2J$, so
$\langle\nabla\psi\cdot K(A)U\rangle_\rho = -m\tr(K(-\mathsf M\nabla^2J)\mathsf M\cdot B) = 0$,
$K(-\mathsf M\nabla^2J)\mathsf M = \mathsf MK(-\nabla^2J\mathsf M)$ being symmetric. A node covariance $C$ replaces $B$
by $B_C(v,w) = N^{-2}\sum_{xy}C_{xy}\partial_vr_x\partial_w\psi_y$, which the invariance does not make antisymmetric
unless $C\propto I$. The second mechanism: in the slow-replicator limit $\partial\Phi$ reads the log-masses' component
minus $(\gamma/\beta_k)(a_k/(a'_kG_\alpha))$ times the amplitude's (the $g$-orthogonal projection onto the gauge's
tangent, $\delta\alpha_k = -(a_k/a'_k)\delta\log\beta_k$), and the fields' two parts are proportional node by node ---
$U^\alpha_k = \beta_kG_\alpha a'_k\Phi_k$, $U^{\log\beta}_k = \gamma(a_k\Phi_k - \fhat)$ ---, and the limit map reads
them in that proportion at every point of the basin, not only on $M$: $J$ depends on $\beta_k$ and $\alpha_k$ through
$\beta_ka_k$ (one particle per class), so $u_k = -\partial_{\beta_k}J = -g_k\partial_{\alpha_k}J$ with
$g_k = a_k/(\beta_ka'_k)$, and along the noiseless flow at frozen masses $u_k = g_k\dot\alpha_k/G_\alpha$ --- the masses'
$O(\gamma)$ displacement until the positions reach the exact fit is exactly
$\gamma\Phi_{1,k} = (\gamma/G_\alpha)(\Gamma_k(\alpha_k^*(\beta)) - \Gamma_k(\alpha_k))$, $\Gamma'_k = g_k$,
$\alpha_k^*(\beta)$ the exact fit's amplitude at the current masses. Hence
$\psi^{\log\beta}_{x,k} = U^{\log\beta}_{x,k} + \gamma\partial\Phi_{1,k}\cdot U_x + O(\gamma^2)
= \gamma(a_k\Phi_k - \fhat)(x) - (\gamma/G_\alpha)g_kU^\alpha_{x,k} + O(\gamma^2) = -\gamma\fhat(x) + O(\gamma^2)$ near $M$
($\partial\alpha_k^*(\beta)$ enters through $U^{\log\beta} = O(\gamma)$), the common part removed by $\sum\beta = m$;
every $C$-weighted residual is carried by $\partial\psi$, so on the masses it is $O(\gamma^2)$ against the It\^o
drift's $O(\gamma)$. With several particles per class $\beta_ka_k$ is no longer what $J$ sees, and the residual stays
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:42]{Proposition D$'''$.42}(iv)). (iii) The It\^o drift is (ii)'s sum minus the Wong--Zakai term.
On the masses $\langle(U\cdot\nabla)U\rangle_\rho$ has the log-mass component
$\gamma\beta_k\langle\norm{\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi_k}^2_G\rangle_\rho$ and the amplitude component
$(\beta_k^2G_\alpha/2)\partial_{\alpha_k}\langle\norm{\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi_k}^2_G\rangle_\rho + O(\gamma)$, and with
$\langle\norm{\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi_k}^2_G\rangle_\rho = G_\alpha a_k'^2P_k + a_k^2Q_k$
($P_k = \langle\Phi_k^2\rangle_\rho$, $Q_k$ the centre's and width's part, independent of $\alpha_k$) the combination read
by $\partial\Phi$ is $\gamma\beta_kG_\alpha P_k(a_k'^2 - a_ka''_k) = \gamma\beta_kG_\alpha P_k(1 - q_k^2)$ for
$a = a_{\max}\tanh(\alpha/a_{\max})$ ($a' = 1 - q$, $aa'' = -2q(1 - q)$); $\bar u_k = -\partial_{\beta_k}\Psi_\ell$ since
$\partial_\beta(\beta a')^2 = 2\beta(1 - q^2)$ on $M$ ($\beta a' = \beta - m^2A_k^2/(a_{\max}^2\beta)$). Each term of
$\Psi_\ell$, $h(W) = (W - c/W)^2$, has $h'' = 2 + 6c^2/W^4 > 0$ and $h' = 0$ at $W = \sqrt c = W_{\min,k}$, increasing
beyond: the minimiser on the simplex has equal $P_kh'_k > 0$, so $W_{\ell,k} > W_{\min,k}$. The coordinates: an Euler
step in $z' = \chi(z)$ is $z' + D\chi\,\Delta z + \tfrac12\partial^2\chi[\Delta z,\Delta z] + \dots$, so the scheme's
It\^o drift in $z'$ carries $\tfrac12v\langle\partial^2\chi[U,U]\rangle_\rho$ besides the pushed-forward one; for
$\chi\colon\alpha\mapsto a$ the amplitude component gains
$a''\langle(U^\alpha_k)^2\rangle_\rho = \beta_k^2G_\alpha^2a_k'^2a''_kP_k$, which the projection's amplitude weight
$(\gamma/\beta_k)(a_k/(a_k'^2G_\alpha))$ turns into $-\gamma\beta_kG_\alpha P_ka_ka''_k$: the factor
$a'^2 - 2aa'' = (1 - q)(1 + 3q) = 1 + 2q - 3q^2$; with $q = c/\beta^2$ it is $-(2/(vG_\alpha P))\partial_\beta$ of
$\beta^2/2 + 2c\log\beta + 3c^2/(2\beta^2)$, whose second derivative $1 - 2q + 9q^2$ is positive. At the saturation
both factors vanish like $2(1 - q)$ and $4(1 - q)$, the dither's fitness like $1/(1 - q)$. (iv) For the Euler step
$z_{n+1} = z_n + h(F(z_n) + \langle U(z_n)\xi_n\rangle)$, with $\partial\Phi\cdot F\equiv0$,
\[
  \Phi(z_{n+1}) - \Phi(z_n) = h\langle\psi(z_n)\xi_n\rangle
  + (h^2/2)\partial^2\Phi[F + \langle U\xi_n\rangle,F + \langle U\xi_n\rangle] + O(h^3),
\]
and at linear response the normal deviation is $n_k = h\sum_{j\ge1}(I + hA)^{j-1}\langle U\xi_{k-j}\rangle$
($F = An + O(n^2)$; the quadratic parts of $F$ and $U$, the mean shift of $n$ and $\partial^2\Phi$'s dependence on the
point enter the mean through third moments only). The first term's mean is
$h^2\sigma_\xi^2N^{-1}\langle\nabla\psi\cdot K_hU\rangle_\rho$, $K_h = \sum_{d\ge1}\rho_d(I + hA)^{d-1}$, zero by (ii)'s
identity ($K_h\mathsf M$ symmetric). In the second (the draws' third moments entering at $O(v^{3/2})$, Gaussian or
not), the cross term $h^2\partial^2\Phi[An,\langle U\xi\rangle]$ --- $O(h^2\abs F\abs\xi)$, whose mean vanishes only for
independent draws --- and the relaxation term $(h^2/2)\partial^2\Phi[An,An]$ give, in $A$'s eigenbasis,
$\E[n^i\xi] = h\sigma_\xi^2R(a_i)U^i/N$ and $\E[n^in^l] = h^2\sigma_\xi^2Q_{il}\langle U^iU^l\rangle/N$ with
$Q_{il} = \sum_{j,j'\ge1}a_i^{j-1}a_l^{j'-1}\rho_{\abs{j-j'}} = (1 + G(a_i) + G(a_l))/(1 - a_ia_l)$: the pair $(i,l)$
carries $f_{il}$, and the mean per unit time is $\tfrac12\partial^2\Phi[v_h\Sigma_f]$. (a)
$f_{il} = 1 + O(h\lambda/(1 - \rho))$. (b) With $\rho_d = 0$, $f_{ii} = 1 + h^2\lambda^2/(1 - a^2) = 2/(2 - h\lambda)$.
For a flat metric and $\Sigma\propto\nabla^2J =: H$ on $M$ (label noise: $\langle\nabla\fhat\nabla\fhat^\T\rangle_\rho$
is the Gauss--Newton matrix), $\Sigma$ is diagonal in $H$'s eigenbasis, $\Sigma_f = \varphi(H)H = HX + XH$ with
$X = \tfrac12\varphi(H)$ on the normal space, $\varphi(\lambda) = f_{ii}$; differentiating $\partial\Phi\cdot F\equiv0$
twice at a point of $M$ gives $\partial^2\Phi[AX + XA^\T] = -\partial\Phi\cdot\partial^2F[X]$, and
$\partial^2F[X] = -\nabla\tr(X\nabla^2J)|_X = -\tfrac12\nabla\tr\Psi(H)$
($\dd\tr\Psi(H) = \tr(\varphi(H)\dd H)$; for the Gauss--Newton form $H = JJ^\T$ the kernel block does not contribute,
$J^\T P_{\ker} = 0$): the drift is $-(v_h/4)P_T\nabla\tr\Psi(H)$. (c) $hR(a)\to\tau_c/(1 + \lambda\tau_c)$ and
$h^2Q_{il}\to\tau_c^2(\lambda_i + \lambda_l + 2/\tau_c)/((\lambda_i + \lambda_l)(1 + \lambda_i\tau_c)(1 + \lambda_l\tau_c))$
as $h\to0$ at fixed $\tau_c = -h/\log\rho$, and $f_{il}$ follows; $f_{ii}\to1/(1 + \lambda\tau_c)$,
$\Psi\to\log(1 + \lambda\tau_c)/\tau_c$. For a general finely sampled stationary noise the mode obeys
$\dot n = -\lambda n + \xi$ at linear response, and the increment per step is $h(\xi - \lambda n) = h\dot n$, so
$f = \E[\dot n^2]/\E[\xi^2] = \int S(\omega)\omega^2/(\lambda^2 + \omega^2)\dd\omega/\int S(\omega)\dd\omega$ ($S$ the
spectral density): each $\omega^2/(\lambda^2 + \omega^2)$ decreases in $\lambda$, so $f$ does, and
$\Psi'' = f'\le0$; trace functions of concave functions are concave on the symmetric matrices. For
$S\propto(1 + \omega^2\tau_c^2)^{-1}$ this is $1/(1 + \lambda\tau_c)$; when $\E[\dot\xi^2] < \infty$,
$f\approx\E[\dot\xi^2]/(\lambda^2\sigma_\xi^2)$ for large $\lambda$, and
$\Psi(\infty) = \int_0^\infty f = (\pi/2)\int S(\omega)\abs\omega\dd\omega/\int S(\omega)\dd\omega
= (\pi/2)\langle\abs\omega\rangle$ is finite ($\sqrt{\pi/2}/\tau_c$ for Gaussian correlations). (d)
$v_h = (1 - \rho)/(1 + \rho)\cdot v\to hv/(2\tau_c)$. Heun: in (vi)'s scalar example one step multiplies $Q$ by exactly
$1 - (h^2/4)(c - \tilde c)^2 + (h^4/4)c^2\tilde c^2$ ($c$, $\tilde c$ the error at the step's start and at the
predictor, the same draw), and $c - \tilde c = h\lambda c + O(h^2)$, $\lambda = u^2 + v^2$: the mean per step is
$(h\lambda)^2/4$ times the Euler step's $h^2\E[c^2]$ at leading order. (e) As $\rho\to1$ at fixed $h$, with
$\varepsilon = 1 - \rho$: $h\lambda R(a) = 1 - \varepsilon/(h\lambda) + O(\varepsilon^2)$ and $G(a) = aR(a)$, so
$f_{il} = 2\varepsilon/(x_i + x_l - x_ix_l) + O(\varepsilon^2)$ ($x = h\lambda$) and
$\tau_cf_{il}\to2/(\lambda_i + \lambda_l - h\lambda_i\lambda_l)$, which is $X_h - BX_hB^\T = 2hS$ in $A$'s eigenbasis;
as $h\to0$, $2/(\lambda_i + \lambda_l)$, the Lyapunov solution of $AX + XA^\T = -2S$, which for $S = -mA\mathsf M$ is
$m\cdot Q\mathsf MQ^\T$. On $M$, $\nabla^2J = m\langle\nabla\fhat\nabla\fhat^\T\rangle_\rho$ (the cohesion's Hessian
vanishing there), so
$\Sigma = m^2\mathsf M\langle\nabla\fhat\nabla\fhat^\T\rangle_\rho\mathsf M = m\mathsf M\nabla^2J\,\mathsf M
= -m\cdot A\mathsf M$, diagonal in a $g$-orthonormal eigenbasis $V$ of $A$ ($\Sigma = -mV\Lambda V^\T$,
$\mathsf M = VV^\T$); $\Sigma_f = -mV\varphi(\Lambda)\Lambda V^\T$, and $\varphi(\lambda)\lambda\to1/\tau_c$ on the normal
modes: $\Sigma_f\to(m/\tau_c)V_NV_N^\T = (m/\tau_c)Q\mathsf MQ^\T$. In the slow-replicator limit $\mathsf M$'s
log-mass block $\gamma/\beta$ vanishes, so
$v_h\Sigma_f\to(mv_h/\tau_c)Q\cdot\operatorname{diag}(G,0)\cdot Q^\T$, the normal part of the dither
$2\tau_{\mathrm{eff}}G$ with $2\tau_{\mathrm{eff}} = mv_h/\tau_c$, whose drift is the dither's there (the tangent parts'
contribution vanishing at $\gamma\to0$). (v) Linearity of $\tfrac12\partial^2\Phi[\Sigma]$ in $\Sigma$; the Wong--Zakai
term is a sum over the independent noises' own fields. (vi) $\nabla Q\cdot(v,u) = 2uv - 2vu = 0$;
$(u + hcv)^2 - (v + hcu)^2 = (u^2 - v^2)(1 - h^2c^2)$ with $c = \xi - r$, and $c$'s residual part obeys
$r_{n+1} = (1 - h\lambda)r_n + h\lambda\xi_n$; likewise $(u_i - 2hgu_i)(v_i + 2hgv_i) = u_iv_i(1 - 4h^2g^2)$ with
$g = g_i - \tilde\xi_i = X_i^\T e/n$, the residual obeying $r_{n+1} = (I - hK)r_n + hK\xi_n$; the noise fields
$(2x\odot u,-2x\odot v)$ conserve every $u_iv_i$. The generic network: (iv)(b) with $T$'s gradient.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved; (ii) proved at order $v$ (given Katzenberger's theorem and, for white noise, the Wong--Zakai
theorem; the smooth-noise case by linear response), the residual's $O(\gamma)$ relative order beyond the hypothesis
proved at leading order in $\gamma$ (one particle per class; the asymptotic range numerical), its size numerical; (iii)
proved at leading order (the slow-replicator limit for the closed forms); (iv) proved at order $v$ and at linear
response, for any step $h < 2/\lambda_{\max}$ and any stationary correlation (the $O(h\lambda)$ terms included;
Gaussian draws --- the draws' third moments enter at $O(v^{3/2})$), (a)--(e) corollaries ((c)'s concavity for any
colour proved in the continuum limit; (e)'s finite-step limit proved, its identity with the dither's direction proved
in the slow-replicator limit away from the saturations and numerical at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$; the zero set at finite
$\gamma$ and $\tau_c$ numerical); (v) a corollary; (vi) the examples' identities exact per step, their rates by (iv),
the generic network's law at leading order. Classical: Katzenberger's theorem and its use for the noise-driven drift
along a manifold of minimisers (Li--Wang--Arora, \cite{S64}); label-noise SGD's selection of flat minima ---
Blanc--Gupta--Valiant--Valiant, Damian--Ma--Lee (with its finite-step regulariser
$-(1/2\eta)\tr\log(I - \eta\nabla^2L/2)$), Li--Wang--Arora, HaoChen--Wei--Lee--Ma, Shalova--Schlichting--Peletier ---,
all with noise drawn independently at each step, the regulariser's scaling with step $\times$ variance among them; the
It\^o--Stratonovich distinction, the Wong--Zakai theorem and noise-induced drifts (Wong--Zakai, van Kampen,
Horsthemke--Lefever), and the crossover between It\^o's and Stratonovich's limits set by the ratio of the noise's
correlation time to the relaxation time (Kupferman--Pavliotis--Stuart, Pavliotis--Stuart); SDE models of SGD in It\^o's
form (Li--Tai--E, Li--Malladi--Arora); anti-correlated parameter noise as a regulariser of the Hessian's trace (Orvieto
et al.; perturbed gradients, a different mechanism), correlated parameter noise more generally
(Lucchi--Proske--Orvieto--Bach--Kersting), the anti-correlation of epoch-based minibatch noise, which lowers the weight
variance along flat directions (K\"uhn--Rosenow), SGD without replacement's weighted-trace regulariser with a
Hessian-function weight (Beneventano), and a finite-learning-rate Hessian-dependent regulariser of anti-correlated noise
with a trade-off set by the correlation time (Kim et al., an abstract, flagged); the continuum filter itself is the
active Ornstein--Uhlenbeck particle's effective temperature $T/(1 + k\tau)$ in a trap of stiffness $k$, and the factor
$\det(I + \tau\nabla^2U)$ of the unified coloured-noise approximation's stationary law (Jung--H\"anggi, H\"anggi--Jung,
Maggi et al.); the isotropic noise's log-determinant drift along a manifold of minima (Li--Wang--Arora) is (e)'s target;
the law of balance of rescaling symmetries under SGD (Ziyin--Li--Ueda) and the noise equilibria of parameter symmetries
(Ziyin--Wang--Li--Wu), of which (vi)'s Euler identities are exact discrete cases, the Stratonovich conservation following
from their It\^o derivation; the diagonal network's label-noise Lasso (Pillaud-Vivien--Reygner--Flammarion, in
continuous time and It\^o's reading) and its effective initialisation scale (Woodworth et al.) (\cite{S69}). New (we did
not find it stated): the exact cancellation for noise entering through the error, for any temporal spectrum, by the
antisymmetry the limit map's invariance imposes --- a loop adapting in continuous time does not select through its
measurement noise ---; for correlated draws the coloured filter of label noise's drift along $M$ --- $f_{il}$, the cross
term's mean vanishing by the same antisymmetry, Damian--Ma--Lee's regulariser its white case --- and its consequences:
the passage from the trace to $(1/\tau_c)\log\det(I + \tau_c\nabla^2J)$ for a slow Markov noise, a concave selected
potential for every colour, a slow Markov measurement noise selecting as a dither of the flow's own mobility does; the
closed forms on the replicator's masses, with their dependence on the update's coordinates, and the selection's
multistability on the gauge at finite rates.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.41 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/gauge_label.md, the gamma = 10^-5 sentence of (G) from the round-31 rerun of the zero search).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_gauge_label.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/gauge_label.md}). (A) At three points of $M$
and $\gamma = 10^{-2}$, $10^{-5}$ the It\^o drift and the Wong--Zakai term cancel to $4.6\cdot10^{-9}$--$1.1\cdot10^{-8}$
of the It\^o drift (the finite differences' floor), the diffusion along $M$ is at most $2.1\cdot10^{-20}$, and the
closed form of (iii) matches the It\^o drift to $2$--$4\cdot10^{-4}$ at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$ (23--35\% at
$\gamma = 10^{-2}$: its $O(\gamma)$ correction). (A2) Beyond the hypothesis the Stratonovich drift over its own It\^o
drift is $O(\gamma)$ from $\gamma\approx10^{-3}$ on (an offset: $2.4\cdot10^{-3}$, $2.4\cdot10^{-4}$, $2.4\cdot10^{-5}$
at $\gamma = 10^{-3}$, $10^{-4}$, $10^{-5}$ at $W_0$; a noise correlated along the orbit, $\tau_n/T = 1.6$:
$2.4\cdot10^{-3}$ \dots\ $2.4\cdot10^{-5}$; the profile $1 + 0.8\sin t$: $7.2\cdot10^{-7}$ at $\gamma = 10^{-4}$,
$6\cdot10^{-8}$ at $10^{-5}$) and 0.2--12\% at $\gamma = 10^{-1}$, where the law is not yet asymptotic; for white
homoscedastic noise it is $3\cdot10^{-9}$--$1.3\cdot10^{-8}$ at every $\gamma$; the coloured-noise identity holds to
$1.3$--$2.5\cdot10^{-8}$ and the one-sided kernel's L\'evy-area part is below $4\cdot10^{-13}$. (B)
$W_\ell = (0.3163, 0.4115, 0.2722)$, interior ($\abs{a_k}/a_{\max}$ 0.790, 0.425, 0.735), far from
$W^\sharp = (0.3765, 0.2988, 0.3247)$; at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ the It\^o drift vanishes at $(0.3221, 0.3999, 0.2780)$; the
update in $a$: Katzenberger's drift within $1.9$--$3.4\cdot10^{-4}$ of the closed form with $(1 - q)(1 + 3q)$, the
target $W_\ell^a = (0.2967, 0.4513, 0.2520)$. (C) The full loop at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$, $v = 5\cdot10^{-5}$ (4 seeds
$\times$ $2\cdot10^5$ slow units): the gauge coordinate's slope over the linear-response prediction averaged along the
seeds' mean path is 0.996--1.008 for noise drawn afresh (steps 0.25 and 0.125) and for AR(1) draws at $\rho = -1/2$,
$-1/3$, $1/3$, 0.6 (standard errors 0.001--0.005), 0.996--1.014 at $\rho = 0.8$ ($\pm$ 0.005--0.012) and 0.997--1.022 at
$\rho = 0.9$ ($\pm$ 0.012--0.030), 1.004--1.027 at $\rho = 0.95$ ($\pm$ 0.02--0.07); the prediction at $W_0$ alone is off
by up to 2.2\% on $W_3$ where the errors are small (2.7\% at $\rho = 0.95$, $\pm$ 6.6\%; the path moves it); the per-step
law is rejected at $\rho = 0.9$ (the ratio to the It\^o drift $0.0462\pm0.0006$ against
$(1 - \rho)/(1 + \rho) = 0.0526$) and 0.95 ($0.0197\pm0.0005$ against 0.0256); Heun's scheme moves the gauge by
$(2\pm7)\cdot10^{-4}$ of the It\^o drift, with fresh or AR(1) draws. (C2) At the step 8 ($h\lambda_{\max}\approx1.6$; 8
seeds $\times$ $3.2\cdot10^5$): noise drawn afresh $0.988\pm0.007$, $0.996\pm0.004$, $1.020\pm0.032$ of the
path-averaged prediction, $\rho = 1/2$ $0.988\pm0.009$, $0.998\pm0.004$, $1.020\pm0.027$, $\rho = -1/2$
$0.979\pm0.010$, $0.992\pm0.004$, $1.049\pm0.053$ --- where the step-free It\^o drift is off by 42\% (the ratio
$1.417\pm0.005$). (D) $f = uv$: $\E[Q]$'s decay rate is within 0.3\% of the linear-response prediction for every scheme
(1.002 against 1.002 for fresh draws, $9.586\cdot10^{-5}$ against $9.557\cdot10^{-5}$ at $\rho = 0.999$, where it is
0.19 of the per-step law), and Heun's scheme keeps every path's $Q$ to $1.2\cdot10^{-5}$. (E) The diagonal network: the
products' decay rates over the prediction 0.9988--0.9998 on average (0.977--1.024 per coordinate at $\rho = 0.999$),
the prediction's spread across coordinates growing with the colour (1.002 for fresh draws, 1.052 at $\rho = 0.9$, 1.434
at 0.99, 2.169 at 0.999); Heun's scheme with fresh draws decays them at $2.3\cdot10^{-5}$ of the Euler step's rate, its
$O((h\lambda)^2)$ residue. (F) The tanh network: the sharpness's slope over the linear-response prediction 1.000
(fresh), 0.990 ($\rho = -1/3$), 1.006 (1/3), 1.014 (0.6), 0.996 (kept 4 steps), 1.060 (0.9); at $\rho = 0.99$ and
0.999 the predictions lie below the measurement's floor (Heun's slopes, $1.9\cdot10^{-6}$). (G) The zero set of the
per-update selection at the step 0.25: at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$, for fresh draws and $\tau_c = 10$ one interior stable zero
($(0.3222, 0.3997, 0.2781)$, $(0.3134, 0.4201, 0.2665)$); at $\tau_c = 100$ three stable zeros, each with one class's
mass large ($(0.2779, 0.4949, 0.2272)$, $(0.2821, 0.2260, 0.4919)$, $(0.5419, 0.2266, 0.2315)$) and two saddles; from
$\tau_c = 10^3$ on three stable zeros, three saddles and an unstable node at $(0.3773, 0.2955, 0.3271)$, next to
$W^\sharp$; $W_0$'s flow ends on the continued branch at $\tau_c = 100$ and from $\tau_c = 10^3$ on at the zero with
class 1's mass large ($(0.5905, 0.1927, 0.2169)$ at $10^3$). The continued branch stays interior to $\tau_c = 10^5$ at
$\gamma = 10^{-2}$ ($(0.2647, 0.5209, 0.2144)$, $W/W_{\min} - 1 = (0.059, 1.98, 0.072)$, where
$\lambda_{\mathrm{soft}}\cdot\tau_c = 30$), and at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$ approaches the saturation
($W/W_{\min} - 1 = 1.3\cdot10^{-4}$ and $1.6\cdot10^{-4}$ at $\tau_c = 10^5$); at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$ the three stable
zeros, from $\tau_c = 100$ on, each hold two classes near their minimal masses (within $2\cdot10^{-4}$, relatively, at
$\tau_c = 10^5$), an unstable node joins them from $\tau_c = 10^3$ on, and $W_0$'s flow ends on the one with class 1's
mass large (at $\tau_c = 10^5$ it stops on its step budget short of it, that zero's exponents $-2.2$, $-1.9$ --- the
round-31 rerun of the zero search); along the branch $\lambda_{\mathrm{soft}}\cdot\tau_c$ stays below 31 (below 0.22 at
$\gamma = 10^{-5}$): the targets sit where the slow limit has not set in. The slow limit: $\tau_c\Sigma_f$ at $\tau_c = 10^6$ within
$6.9$--$7.4\cdot10^{-4}$ of $X_h$ ($5.3$--$6.3\cdot10^{-3}$ of the continuum's $m\cdot Q\mathsf MQ^\T$, the factor
$2/(2 - h\lambda)$); the slow noise's direction against the dither's cos 1.00000 at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$ and
0.9988--0.9999 at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$, its magnitude there 1.8--3.3 times the dither's normal part's at the effective
temperature ($-6.9$ on one component at $W = 1/3$), the dither's own drift 2.8--4.1 times its normal part's ($-14$ on
that component) --- the masses' block of the mobility ---, both 1.00 at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$; a smooth noise at
$\tau_c = 10^4$ selects 2--5\% as much as the AR(1) noise, its direction against the dither's cos 0.950--1.000.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.42 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d as committed at da1a80e; the numbers
% are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/gauge_classes.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:42 is a \hyperref target, as
% res:thmDppp:21 is. The mobility of Proposition D'''.41, PARADIGM's M in the proof's "whose trace against M", is written
% \mathsf M, as there. Proposition D'''.40, which the text cites, is not stated in PARADIGM.md (in progress), so the
% citation is plain text.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.42}[several particles per class: under isotropic dither the class masses carry the
gauge's laws and the split within a class is frozen; anisotropic dither and measurement noise feel the
split]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:42}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36} let each class $k$ hold $n\ge1$ particles
($m = Kn$, $K = 3$ classes), together at the class's exact-fit position --- the amplitude $A_k/W_k$ with the class mass
$W_k = B_k/m$, $B_k = \sum_{i\in k}\beta_i$ ---, the classes beyond the cohesion's range of each other. The manifold
$M_m$ of these exact fits is parametrised by the class masses and the fractions $s_i = \beta_i/B_k$ within the classes;
$\hat W_k = \abs{\hat A_k}/a_{\max}$ is the class's saturation mass ($\hat A_k$ its contribution).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the determinant)} On $M_m$, $\log\det\nabla^2_\Theta J = \ell_1(W) + c_m$, where $\ell_1$ is the
    one-particle-per-class log-determinant of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37} at the class masses and
    $c_m = pK\log(m/K) + \log\det$ of the cohesion's Hessian on the within-class differences ($p = 3$ coordinates per
    particle): independent of the masses and of the fractions.
  \item \emph{(the isotropic dither)} For the dither $\Sigma = 2\tau G$ on every particle, in the slow-replicator limit
    the class masses follow \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}'s law with $m$ the number of particles ---
    the replicator flow of the entropic free energy $(\tau/2)\ell_1$, the fitness $-(\tau/(2m))\partial_{W_k}\ell_1$ for
    every particle of the class: the one-particle-per-class dynamics slowed by $K/m$ ---, and the fractions do not move:
    their drift vanishes at order $\gamma\tau$ (every particle of a class has the class's averaged fitness), and their
    diffusion vanishes identically at every $\gamma$, for any position noise (the fractions' tangent has no position
    component and is orthogonal to the class masses' tangent in the flow's metric). At finite $\gamma$ an
    $O(\gamma^2\tau)$ drift moves the fractions toward the equal split. For an anisotropic position noise ---
    Proposition D$'''$.40's masks --- the frozen split and the per-class law hold at the equal split (the per-class law
    to the resolution of the computation, after extrapolating its second derivatives' step) but not at unequal ones:
    there the fractions drift at $O(\gamma\tau)$ and the class masses' drift departs from the per-class law at
    $O(\gamma\tau)$; a noise on the centres and widths only, which freezes the gauge with one particle per class
    (Proposition D$'''$.40), moves the class masses at $O(\gamma\tau)$.
  \item \emph{(the corrections)} \hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition D$'''$.38}'s correction evaluated at the class
    masses and the classes' contributions, $\kappa_F(\tau/m)(1 + \hat W_k^2/W_k^2)/(W_k - \hat W_k^2/W_k)$ given to each
    particle of class $k$, is $\kappa_F(\tau/2)\partial_{\beta_i}\ell_1$: under the isotropic dither it acts on the class
    masses exactly as with one particle per class and leaves the fractions alone. Proposition D$'''$.40's statements do
    not carry over per class at unequal splits ((ii)).
  \item \emph{(measurement noise)} At the equal split the $m$-particle loop with measurement noise is
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}'s $m = K$ loop exactly: co-located particles of equal mass get
    identical drifts and identical noise fields, an invariant subspace --- the class masses' It\^o drift is the
    one-per-class one ($m$-independent, not $K/m$ of it: the noise is shared, where the dither's is independent per
    particle) and \hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}'s cancellation holds. At unequal splits the
    cohesion's force, which vanishes on $M_m$ but is not the error's combination $\langle rU\rangle_\rho$, breaks the
    cancellation: a Stratonovich drift along $M_m$ of up to 10\% (9.6\% on $W_1$ at the fractions $(0.2, 0.7, 0.5)$) of
    the It\^o drift, on the class masses and on the fractions, that does not vanish as $\gamma\to0$. With noise drawn
    afresh at each update the selection equalises the fractions: along them it is the replicator flow of
    $(v/4)\sum_k\langle\norm{\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi_k}^2_G\rangle_\rho\sum_{i\in k}\beta_i^2$, the full trace's part
    --- the fractions' tangent moves no position, so no amplitude correction enters ---, minimal at the equal split for
    a fixed class mass.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) In each class change the displacements $(\delta\vartheta_i)_{i\in k}$ to the $\beta$-weighted mean
$\bar\delta_k = \sum_{i\in k}s_i\delta\vartheta_i$ and the differences
$\eta_i = \delta\vartheta_i - \delta\vartheta_{\mathrm{last}}$: a linear map of determinant $\pm\sum_is_i = \pm1$. At an
exact fit the Hessian of $J$ is the Gauss--Newton form $m\langle\nabla\fhat\nabla\fhat^\T\rangle_\rho$, and
$\delta\fhat = \sum_kW_k\nabla\tilde\Phi_k\cdot\bar\delta_k$, the one-per-class $\delta\fhat$ with $\bar\delta_k$ for
$\delta\vartheta_k$: the fit's block depends on the $\bar\delta_k$ alone and is $(m/K)$ times the one-per-class Hessian
at the class masses $W$; the cohesion energy, that of the unweighted cloud, depends on the differences alone and not on
the masses (translation invariance), and the classes do not interact. So $\nabla^2_\Theta J$ is block-diagonal in
$(\bar\delta,\eta)$, and
$\log\det\nabla^2_\Theta J = \ell_1(W) + pK\log(m/K) + \log\det\nabla^2_\eta J_{\mathrm{coh}}$. (ii) At fixed masses
the positions' law under $\Sigma = 2\tau G$ is Gibbs, $\propto e^{-J/\tau}$, so in the slow-replicator limit the masses'
averaged fitness is $-\partial_{\beta_i}$ of the free energy $(\tau/2)\log\det\nabla^2_\Theta J$ to Laplace's order
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}(ii)), $= -(\tau/(2m))\partial_{W_k}\ell_1$ for $i\in k$ by (i); the
replicator then moves each class mass as one particle of mass $B_k$ with that fitness and leaves the ratios
$\beta_i/\beta_j$ within a class fixed. The diffusion: Katzenberger's $P$ is the projection onto $TM_m$ orthogonal in the
flow's metric $g$ (the positions' $\Ginv$, the log-masses' $\beta_i/\gamma$); the fractions' tangent $t_s$ changes the
masses within one class at a fixed class mass and the positions not at all, so
$\langle t_s,t_c\rangle_g = \sum_{i\in k}(\beta_i/\gamma)\delta\log\beta_i^{(s)}\delta\log\beta_i^{(c)}
= (\delta W/(\gamma W_k))\sum_{i\in k}\delta\beta_i^{(s)} = 0$ for the class masses' tangent $t_c$ (which scales the
class's masses together), and a position noise has no component along $t_s$: the fractions' rows of $P\Sigma P^\T$
vanish. The anisotropic case: at unequal masses the barycentre and the differences are coupled --- the fit's force on
particle $i$ is $\propto\beta_i$ (it drives $\eta$ through $\beta_i - \beta_j$), the cohesion's forces $F_i$ sum to zero
but $\sum_is_iF_i$ does not (it drives $\bar\delta$), and per-particle noises make $\dd\bar\delta$ and $\dd\eta$
correlated ($\propto s_i - s_j$) ---; for $\Sigma = 2\tau G$ the joint law is nonetheless the product
$e^{-(J_{\mathrm{fit}}(\bar\delta) + J_{\mathrm{coh}}(\eta))/\tau}$, for another $\Sigma$ it is the Lyapunov solution,
not a product, and the particles' averaged fitnesses depend on the split. At the equal split the three couplings vanish
and the exchange of a class's particles fixes the split, so the fractions do not drift; the barycentre's drift is the
one-per-class particle's ($-G\beta_k^{(K)}\langle r\nabla\tilde\Phi_k\rangle_\rho$, $\sum s_i^2 = 1/n$ against
$B_k = n\beta_k^{(K)}$) and its noise $1/n$ of that particle's, so its fluctuations give each particle $1/n = K/m$ of
the one-per-class fitness; the internal differences' block does not depend on the masses, and numerically they add
nothing (a proof is open: their law is not the barycentre's). (iii)
$\partial_{\beta_i}\ell_1(B/m) = m^{-1}\partial_{W_k}\ell_1$ for $i\in k$;
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:38]{Proposition D$'''$.38}'s closed form at the class mass. (iv) On the subspace
$\{\vartheta_i = \vartheta_k,\ \beta_i = B_k/n\ \text{for}\ i\in k\}$: $\fhat = (1/m)\sum_kB_k\tilde\Phi(\vartheta_k)$ is
the $m = K$ loop's at $\beta_k^{(K)} = KB_k/m = KW_k$; particle $i$'s position drift
$-G\beta_i\langle r\nabla\tilde\Phi_k\rangle_\rho$ has $\beta_i = mW_k/n = KW_k$; the cohesion's force vanishes at
coincidence; the log-mass drift and the noise fields $U_x$ are the class's: the subspace is invariant under every noise
path, and the loop on it is the $m = K$ loop. Off it, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}'s proof with
$\langle r\psi\rangle_\rho = -\partial\Phi\cdot F_{\mathrm{coh}}$: $F_{\mathrm{coh}}$ vanishes on $M_m$ and its
derivative maps into the normal space, so $\partial\Phi\cdot F_{\mathrm{coh}}$ is second order and its Hessian adds a
symmetric part to $B$, whose trace against $\mathsf M$ need not vanish. The It\^o selection along the fractions:
$(U\cdot\nabla)U$'s log-mass component for particle $i$ is
$\gamma[\beta_i\langle\norm{\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi_i}^2_G\rangle_\rho
- m^{-1}\sum_j\beta_j^2\langle\norm{\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi_j}^2_G\rangle_\rho] + O(\gamma^2)$, the second term common
to all particles, and $\partial\Phi$ reads the fractions from the log-masses alone ($t_s$ has no position component): the
fitness $-(v/2)\beta_i\langle\norm{\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi_k}^2_G\rangle_\rho$,
$\dd s_i/\dd s = -\gamma(v/2)\langle\norm{\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi_k}^2_G\rangle_\rho B_ks_i(s_i - \sum_{j\in k}s_j^2)$
--- minus the Wong--Zakai term's fractions; the It\^o drift adds the cohesion's residual.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved; (ii) for the isotropic dither the slow-limit statements proved (given
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}(ii)), the diffusion's vanishing proved at every $\gamma$ for any
position noise, the finite-$\gamma$ drift numerical; the anisotropic case: the mechanism (the couplings, the non-product
law, the equal split's barycentre law) proved, the $O(\gamma\tau)$ sizes and the internal differences' null
contribution at the equal split numerical; (iii) proved under the isotropic dither; (iv) the invariant subspace proved,
the residual's mechanism proved and its size numerical, the fractions' It\^o selection proved at leading order.
Classical: the unit-Jacobian change to the barycentre and the internal coordinates (Jacobi coordinates) and the block
structure of a translation-invariant interaction; in population genetics' terms the fractions are neutral alleles'
frequencies, to which the isotropic dither gives neither selection nor drift at Katzenberger's order; that the
stationary law of a non-isotropically driven linear system is not its Gibbs measure. New: that the dither's selection,
its correction and the frozen split act on the class masses as with one particle per class (with $m$ the number of
particles) exactly when the dither is isotropic; that an anisotropic dither, or measurement noise at unequal splits,
makes the split felt at Katzenberger's order; the invariant subspace at the equal split, where measurement noise's laws
are $m$-independent.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.42 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/gauge_classes.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_gauge_classes.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/gauge_classes.md};
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:36]{Proposition D$'''$.36}'s instance with $n = 2$ and 3 particles per class, together at the
class's exact-fit position). (A) $\log\det\nabla^2_\Theta J$ minus its one-per-class value at the same class masses is
$-46.131219$ ($m = 6$) and $-92.262438$ ($m = 9$) at three class-mass points and three splits each (ranges
$2.8\cdot10^{-7}$ and $4.2\cdot10^{-7}$). (B) In Katzenberger's limit under the isotropic dither the class masses'
drift and diffusion are 0.5000 and 0.3333 of the one-per-class ones at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$ ($n = 3$'s degenerate equal
split included, by the Schur method), the diffusion's ratio exactly so at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ too; the fractions'
diffusion is at most $1.8\cdot10^{-19}$ ($8.7\cdot10^{-17}$ at one point) per $\gamma^2\tau$, their drift at most
$4.7\cdot10^{-5}$ per $\gamma\tau$ at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$ against class drifts of 0.12--0.46, and
$1.1$--$1.7\cdot10^{-2}$ ($n = 2$) and $1.7$--$7.2\cdot10^{-3}$ ($n = 3$) per $\gamma\tau$ at $\gamma = 10^{-2}$ at
unequal splits, toward the equal split. (C) The correction per class at $\kappa_F = 1$ brings the class drift from
0.18--0.46 to $1.3$--$8.8\cdot10^{-5}$ per $\gamma\tau$, as with one particle per class, the fractions' drift
unchanged. (D) Measurement noise at $m = 6$: at the equal split the It\^o drift and the Wong--Zakai term cancel to
$4.5\cdot10^{-9}$ and $5.7\cdot10^{-9}$ of the It\^o drift and the class It\^o drift is $m = 3$'s (1.0000); at unequal
splits the Stratonovich residual is 2.2--2.6\% of the It\^o drift, up to 9.6\% ($W_1$ at the fractions
$(0.2, 0.7, 0.5)$) per component, at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$ as at $10^{-2}$; the fractions' It\^o drift is the full trace's
flow less that residual ($5.390\cdot10^{-7}$ against $5.515\cdot10^{-7}$), the amplitudes' part alone an order of
magnitude smaller. (E) The full loop at $m = 6$ ($\gamma = 10^{-2}$, $\tau = 10^{-4}$, 8 seeds $\times$ $4\cdot10^5$
slow units): the class masses end at $(0.330, 0.349, 0.321)$ (std 0.04--0.07) from the equal split and
$(0.334, 0.315, 0.351)$ (std 0.02--0.04) from $(0.2, 0.7, 0.5)$, against $m = 3$ at half the time
($0.340, 0.346, 0.314$; std 0.06--0.07); from the equal split the fractions do not move (changes $-0.002\pm0.062$,
$+0.043\pm0.058$, $-0.018\pm0.055$); from $(0.2, 0.7, 0.5)$ $s_1$ moves by $-0.035\pm0.012$, away from the equal split,
against the interior drift's $+0.005$ over the run --- the runs reach $\abs\alpha = 26$--28, deep in the saturation,
where the local drift does not hold. (F) Anisotropic dither, the second derivatives Richardson-extrapolated in their
step: at the equal split the per-class law holds for every mask (ratios 1.0000 for the masks $(1, 0, 0)$ and
$(1, 1, 1)$; 1.0000--1.0004 for $(0, 1, 1)$ at $\gamma\ge10^{-5}$, 1.0033 and 1.0075 at $\gamma = 10^{-6}$, where the
drift reaches the computation's limit), the fractions' drift there at most $1.2\cdot10^{-10}$ per $\gamma\tau$ (above
the step-resolution in places: the computation's floor, against $1$--$6\cdot10^{-4}$ at the unequal split); at the
unequal split the mask $(1, 0, 0)$ moves the class masses 1.4--3.5\% off the per-class law at every $\gamma$, the
isotropic dither by $1.3\cdot10^{-2}\to0$ as $\gamma\to0$ (1.0134 at $10^{-3}$, 1.0000 at $10^{-6}$), and the mask
$(0, 1, 1)$, which freezes the gauge with one particle per class, moves them at $4.1\cdot10^{-3}$ per $\gamma\tau$ ---
$5.9\cdot10^4$ times the per-class law at $\gamma = 10^{-5}$; the fractions drift there at $1$--$6\cdot10^{-4}$ per
$\gamma\tau$ under either mask as $\gamma\to0$, and at $1.8\cdot10^{-6}$ per $\gamma\tau$ at $\gamma = 10^{-6}$ under
the isotropic dither (its $O(\gamma)$).
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.43 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d as committed at da1a80e; the numbers
% are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/colour_selection.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:43 is a \hyperref target, as
% res:thmDppp:21 is. Here Phi is the N x d design and phi_i its columns, as in PARADIGM, and M the affine set of exact
% fits. Proposition D'''.44, which the Status cites, is not stated in PARADIGM.md (in progress), so the citation is
% plain text. The Status's references [S69], [S70], [S72] (added to PARADIGM's Status after da1a80e, not yet committed
% when typeset) are the bibitems S69, S70 and S72 of refs.tex; the same holds for [S72]--[S75] in the Status
% paragraphs of Propositions D'''.45--D'''.48 below.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.43}[the colour of the measurement noise selects the kernels: from the trace --- a
weighted $\ell^1$ norm --- to the log-determinant --- a support count]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:43}%
Let $\varphi_1,\dots,\varphi_d$ be a kernel dictionary (fixed centres and widths) sampled at $N < d$ points, $\Phi$ the
$N\times d$ design, $y$ a target with $M = \{w : \Phi w = y\}$ non-empty, and let each atom carry two nonnegative masses
$u_i^2$, $v_i^2$ with coefficient $w_i = u_i^2 - v_i^2$ --- the signed kernel measure $\sum_iw_i\delta_{c_i}$ split into
its two parts, the gauge of the replicator's mass and amplitude in flat coordinates ---, adapted by gradient descent on
$L = (1/2N)\abs{\Phi w - y}^2$ in $(u,v)$: the unbalanced Fisher--Rao (Hellinger) law of the two parts, the masses' half
of the paradigm's laws on a fixed dictionary. Let the loop see the measured error $r + \xi$ (label noise), $\xi$
independent over the samples, of per-step variance $\sigma^2$, stationary in time with step correlations $\rho_d$, and
the update an explicit (Euler) step of length $h < 2/\lambda_{\max}$ --- sampled-data adaptation: the selection below is
that step's It\^o drift, at a rate $\propto h\sigma^2$, absent in continuous time and for Stratonovich-consistent updates
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}(ii); Heun's scheme with the same draw in both stages selects
$O((h\lambda)^2)$ of it) ---, and write $v_h = h\sigma^2/N$, $s = 4v_ht/N$, $P_i = u_iv_i$ (the cancellation between the
measure's two parts at atom $i$), $D_i = u_i^2 + v_i^2 = \sqrt{w_i^2 + 4P_i^2}$ (the atom's relevance, the total mass
there), $K = (4/N)\Phi\operatorname{diag}(D)\Phi^\T$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the selection flow)} At order $v$ and linear response the parameters drift along $M$ by
    \begin{gather*}
      \frac{\dd P}{\dd s} = -\omega\odot P,\qquad \frac{\dd w}{\dd s} = -\Pi_D(\omega\odot w),\\
      \omega_i = \varphi_i^\T f(K)\varphi_i,\qquad \Pi_D = I - D\Phi^\T(\Phi D\Phi^\T)^{-1}\Phi,
    \end{gather*}
    the gradient flow on $M$, in the flat metric ($\operatorname{diag}(1/(4D),1/D)$ in $(w,P)$), of
    $R = \tr\Psi(K)$, $\Psi' = f$ the filter of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}(iv): the cancellations
    decay at the rates $\omega_i$ (the measure's decomposition becoming minimal, $D\to\abs w$) while $w$ moves along
    $M$, reweighted by $\omega$ --- the gradient of $R$ in the relevances, $\partial R/\partial D_i = (4/N)\omega_i$.
    The flow is the small-noise limit of the descent ($\sigma\ll\min\abs{w^*_S}\sigma_{\min}(\Phi_S)$ in practice; at
    larger $\sigma$ the descent kills atoms on its own, Lasso-like), and its time unit $s = 4v_ht/N$ costs about $10^4$
    updates here ($3\cdot10^6$ for $s = 300$).
  \item \emph{(white noise: the trace)} For noise drawn afresh in the limit $h\lambda_{\max}\to0$, $f\equiv1$:
    $\omega_i = \abs{\varphi_i}^2$, $R = (4/N)\sum_i\abs{\varphi_i}^2D_i$, which on $M$ is minimal at $P = 0$ and there
    the weighted $\ell^1$ norm $(4/N)\sum_i\abs{\varphi_i}^2\abs{w_i}$: $R$ is convex, and the selection ends at weighted
    basis pursuit's solution when it is unique and the start's support contains it. At a finite step fresh draws select
    Damian--Ma--Lee's strictly convex $\Psi = -(2/h)\log(1 - h\lambda/2)$, whose minimiser on $M$ is not a vertex ---
    not basis pursuit's solution ---, and at finite $\sigma$ the descent's own pruning adds to it: the white comparator of
    a finite-step experiment is not basis pursuit.
  \item \emph{(slow Markov noise: the log-determinant)} For AR(1) draws of correlation time $\tau_c$ sampled finely,
    $f = (I + \tau_cK)^{-1}$ and $R = (1/\tau_c)\log\det(I + \tau_cK)$, with (Cauchy--Binet)
    \[
      \det(I + \tau_cK) = \sum_{S\subseteq[d],\,\abs S\le N}(4\tau_c/N)^{\abs S}\det(\Phi_S^\T\Phi_S)\prod_{i\in S}D_i,
    \]
    a polynomial in $D$ with nonnegative coefficients whose logarithm is concave. On a $k$-sparse exact fit ($P = 0$,
