    $D = \abs w$, $S = \supp w$ with $\Phi_S$ of full column rank, $k\le N$)
    \[
      \tau_cR = k\log(4\tau_c/N) + \log\Big(\det(\Phi_S^\T\Phi_S)\prod_{i\in S}\abs{w_i}\Big) + O(1/\tau_c):
    \]
    the support's size times $\log(\tau_c)/\tau_c$, so as $\tau_c\to\infty$ the selection's global minimisers on $M$ are
    the sparsest exact fits, ties broken by $\det(\Phi_S^\T\Phi_S)\prod_{i\in S}\abs{w_i}$ --- asymptotically: at a
    finite $\tau_c$ an exact fit on more atoms can have the lower $R$ (numerics C). $R$ is Fazel's log-det heuristic on
    $\Phi\operatorname{diag}\abs w\Phi^\T$ --- sparse Bayesian learning's $\log\abs{\Sigma_y}$ term at
    $\gamma\propto\abs w$ and $\lambda = 1/\tau_c$, not its penalty (which minimises over $\gamma$ and adds
    $w^\T\Gamma^{-1}w$), so Wipf--Rao's results on its local minima do not transfer. The weights
    $\omega_i = \varphi_i^\T(I + \tau_cK)^{-1}\varphi_i$ are $O(1/\tau_c)$ for an atom in the span of the relevant ones
    and $\abs{\Pi^\perp\varphi_i}^2$ for an atom outside it ($\Pi^\perp$ the projection onto the complement of that
    span): the relevant atoms are spared and the others decay.
  \item \emph{(any colour in the continuum: a concave selection)} For any finely sampled stationary noise, as
    $h\lambda_{\max}\to0$, $f$ is decreasing (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}(iv)(c)), so $\Psi$ is
    concave and $R$ is a concave function of $D$ (trace functions of concave functions are concave on symmetric
    matrices, and $K$ is linear in $D$); on $M$ at $P = 0$, $R$ is then concave in $\abs w$ on each orthant, so its
    minimisers on $M$ are basic exact fits (at most $N$ atoms) and from a dense start the selection ends at one of
    several local minima --- the multistability of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}(iv)(e) on the gauge
    ---, which one depending on where it starts. A smooth noise (finite $\E[\dot\xi^2]$) has
    $f\approx(\lambda\tau_c)^{-2}$ for $\lambda\tau_c\gg1$ and selects correspondingly slowly. At a finite step the
    concavity fails: the filter's slope at $\lambda = 0$ is $(1 - 3\rho)/(2(1 - \rho))$ per unit $h\lambda$ for AR(1)
    draws, so for $\rho < 1/3$ (anti- or weakly correlated draws) $R$ is convex near $K = 0$, and for every colour $f$
    rises again beyond $h\lambda\approx0.7$--1; white noise is the continuum's only convex selection, linear in $D$.
  \item \emph{(which exact fits the selection holds)} At a $k$-sparse exact fit ($P = 0$, support $S$, $\Phi_S$ of full
    column rank, $k < N$) the selection is at rest, and its inactive atoms decay --- the fit is a stable point of the
    selection and a strict local minimum of $R$ on $M$ --- when the weighted dual certificate
    \[
      \abs{c_i} < \omega_i\ \text{for every}\ i\notin S,\qquad
      c = \Phi_I^\T(\Phi_S^+)^\T(\omega_S\odot\operatorname{sign}w_S)
    \]
    holds (sufficient, not necessary; $I$ the atoms off $S$, $\omega$ at $D = \abs w$). For white noise the weights are
    $\abs{\varphi_i}^2$ and this is Fuchs's (least-squares) sufficient certificate for weighted basis pursuit,
    conservative. For a slow Markov noise $\omega_S = O(1/\tau_c)$ while $\omega_i\to\abs{\Pi^\perp\varphi_i}^2 > 0$ off
    $S$ (no inactive column in $\operatorname{span}\Phi_S$), so $\abs{c_i}/\omega_i = O(1/\tau_c)$ once
    $\tau_c\gg1/\lambda_{\min}((4/N)\Phi_S\operatorname{diag}\abs{w_S}\Phi_S^\T)$: every exact fit on fewer than $N$
    atoms is held for $\tau_c$ beyond a threshold that grows without bound with the dictionary's coherence (numerics H)
    --- while at a fit on $N$ atoms $\operatorname{span}\Phi_S$ is everything and both sides are $O(1/\tau_c)$. When,
    for generic coefficients on $S$, the truth is the only exact fit on fewer than $N$ atoms, it is the only one the slow
    selection holds robustly; where the flow ends is decided by the basins. An atom whose masses have died
    ($u_i = v_i = 0$) does not revive under the flow: started from basis pursuit's solution, the slow selection stays
    there.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) \hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}(iv)(b): for label noise on a flat gradient flow the drift along
$M$ at order $v$ is $-(v_h/4)P_T\nabla\tr\Psi(H)$, $H = J^\T J/N$ the Gauss--Newton matrix ($\nabla^2L$ on $M$), and
here $J = [2\Phi\operatorname{diag}(u),-2\Phi\operatorname{diag}(v)]$, so $JJ^\T/N = K$ and
$\tr\Psi(H) = \tr\Psi(K)$ ($\Psi(0) = 0$). In the coordinates $(w_i,P_i) = (u_i^2 - v_i^2,u_iv_i)$, whose Jacobian
$\left[\begin{smallmatrix}2u_i & -2v_i\\ v_i & u_i\end{smallmatrix}\right]$ has determinant $2D_i$, the flat metric
reads $\abs{\dd u}^2 + \abs{\dd v}^2 = \sum_i(\dd w_i^2/(4D_i) + \dd P_i^2/D_i)$; $M$ constrains $w$ only. With
$\dd\tr\Psi(K) = \tr(f(K)\dd K)$, $\partial R/\partial D_i = (4/N)\varphi_i^\T f(K)\varphi_i = (4/N)\omega_i$,
$\partial D_i/\partial P_i = 4P_i/D_i$ and $\partial D_i/\partial w_i = w_i/D_i$, the gradient flow
$-(v_h/4)\cdot(\text{metric})^{-1}\nabla R$ projected on $\Phi\dd w = 0$ is
$\dd P_i/\dd t = -(v_h/4)D_i(4/N)\omega_i(4P_i/D_i) = -(4v_h/N)\omega_iP_i$ and
$\dd w/\dd t = -(v_h/4)[4D - 4D\Phi^\T(\Phi4D\Phi^\T)^{-1}\Phi4D](4/N)(\omega\odot w/D)
= -(4v_h/N)\Pi_D(\omega\odot w)$. (ii) $f\equiv1$ gives
$R = (4/N)\tr(\Phi\operatorname{diag}(D)\Phi^\T) = (4/N)\sum_i\abs{\varphi_i}^2D_i$, increasing in each $P_i^2$, and
$D_i\ge\abs{w_i}$ with equality at $P_i = 0$; $R$ is a sum of norms $\abs{(w_i,2P_i)}$, convex, so its gradient flow on
the affine $M$ decreases it to its infimum, the weighted $\ell^1$ minimum; with a unique minimiser and the flow's
multiplicative form (an atom with $w_i = P_i = 0$ never revives) the end is that minimiser when the start's support
contains it. (iii) $\det(I + \Phi_*^\T\Phi_*)$ for $\Phi_* = \Phi\operatorname{diag}(\sqrt{4\tau_cD/N})$ is the sum of
the principal minors of $\Phi_*^\T\Phi_*$, which vanish beyond size $N$; $\log\det$ is concave on positive matrices and
$I + \tau_cK$ is affine in $D$. On the $k$-sparse fit only $S\subseteq\supp w$ contribute, the top term being
$S = \supp w$. The weights: with $K$'s range spanned by the relevant atoms' columns,
$(I + \tau_cK)^{-1} = \Pi^\perp + O(1/\tau_c)$ on it. (iv)
$f(\lambda) = \int\hat s(\omega)\omega^2/(\lambda^2 + \omega^2)\dd\omega/2\pi$ is decreasing
(\hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}(iv)(c)); $K\mapsto\tr\Psi(K)$ is concave because $\Psi$ is;
$D\mapsto K$ is linear; on an orthant of $w$ at $P = 0$, $D = \abs w$ is linear in $w$, and a concave function on the
polytope $M\cap{}$orthant attains its minimum at a vertex --- a basic solution, at most $N$ atoms. At a finite step,
$f = 1 - 2h\lambda R(a) + (h\lambda)^2(1 + 2G(a))/(1 - a^2)$, $a = 1 - h\lambda$, with $G(a) = \rho a/(1 - \rho a)$,
$R = G/a$ for AR(1) draws, expands as $1 + h\lambda(1 - 3\rho)/(2(1 - \rho)) + O((h\lambda)^2)$. (v) At $P = 0$ the
feasible directions keep $\Phi\delta w = 0$; off $\operatorname{span}\Phi_S$ only the inactive atoms can act, so
$\delta w_S = -\Phi_S^+\Phi_I\delta w_I$ and, to first order,
$\delta R = (4/N)[\sum_{i\notin S}\omega_i\abs{\delta w_i} - c^\T\delta w_I]$ ($R$ linear in $D$ to first order,
$D_i = \abs{w_i}$), positive for every $\delta w_I\ne0$ when $\abs{c_i} < \omega_i$; the inactive coordinates' linearised
flow is the gradient flow of that convex form, so they decay. For a slow Markov noise,
$(I + \tau_cK)^{-1} = \Pi^\perp + O(1/\tau_c)$ with $K$'s range $\operatorname{span}\Phi_S$ ($D_I\to0$), giving the
weights; an atom with $D_i = 0$ has zero mobility $(4D_i,D_i)$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved at order $v$ (given \hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}(iv)) where
$\operatorname{rank}K = N$ and $\lambda_{\min}(K)\gg v_h$ --- its spectral gap ---; the pruning phase, in which atoms
die, $\lambda_{\min}(K)\to0$ and the sparse fits are singular points of $M$, and so (v)'s stability, are statements
about the reduced flow (numerical for the descent: numerics B, D, J); (ii) the potential proved, the end at basis
pursuit's solution sketched (the flow's convergence on the degenerate metric at $P = 0$ asymptotic), in the limits
$h\lambda_{\max},\sigma\to0$; (iii), (iv) proved (the identities exact, the asymptotics in $\tau_c$ with an
$O(1/\tau_c)$ remainder; (iv) in the continuum, its failure at a finite step from the filter's expansion); (v) proved
for the reduced flow (the certificate sufficient, from the expansion of $R$ along the feasible directions at the fit);
which local minimum a slow flow reaches, and how often it is the truth, numerical --- from a small start
($u = v = \alpha\lesssim0.5$, the rich regime) and, run to convergence, also from $\alpha = 2$ (numerics F). The
paradigm's own mass law --- the replicator's masses with adaptive amplitudes --- is Proposition D$'''$.44's. Classical:
label-noise gradient descent selecting a weighted $\ell^1$ norm on the quadratic parametrisation, and at a finite
$\sigma$ a Lasso (HaoChen--Wei--Lee--Ma, Pillaud-Vivien--Reygner--Flammarion), the finite-step regulariser
(Damian--Ma--Lee), the hyperbolic-entropy interpolant of the noiseless flow and the kernel and rich regimes (Woodworth et
al.), sparse Bayesian learning and its log-determinant (Tipping; Wipf--Rao) and the theorem that its non-separable
reweighting recovers sparse solutions where $\ell^1$ fails on a set of positive measure (Wipf--Nagarajan), the log-det
heuristic (Fazel--Hindi--Boyd), reweighted $\ell^1$ (Cand\`es--Wakin--Boyd), gradient noise attracting to sparse
invariant sets (Chen--Kunin--Yamamura--Ganguli), sparse optimisation on measures by Fisher--Rao and
Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao flows (Chizat; Chizat--Bach), Fuchs's certificate (\cite{S69}, \cite{S70}, \cite{S72}). New
(not found stated): that the temporal
correlation of the label noise sets the sparsity prior of the explicit update's selection along the exact fits --- the
trace (weighted $\ell^1$) for white noise, the log-determinant of Fazel's heuristic (a support count) for a slow Markov
noise, a concave penalty for every colour in the continuum ---; that this selection recovers sparse expansions where
weighted basis pursuit fails --- at the level of the reduced flow, and by label-noise descent at small $\sigma$ ---, as
Wipf--Nagarajan's theorem for non-separable reweighting leads one to expect, at rates sparse Bayesian learning itself
matches or beats (numerics G): the contribution is the mechanism, not a better sparse solver.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.43 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/colour_selection.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_colour_selection.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/colour_selection.md}; success: within
$10^{-4}\cdot\max\abs{w^*}$ of the truth; the start: the noiseless flow's interpolant from $u = v = 0.5$). (A)
$\det(I + \tau K)$ equals its subset expansion to $2.6\cdot10^{-15}$, $\partial R/\partial D_i$ equals $(4/N)\omega_i$ to
$4\cdot10^{-9}$ (central differences), $R$'s second differences along random segments are negative for every $\tau$
($-8\cdot10^{-5}$ to $-0.39$) and zero for white draws ($\le3.6\cdot10^{-15}$); on $k$-sparse fits
$\tau_cR - k\log(4\tau_c/N)$ tends to $\log(\det(\Phi_S^\T\Phi_S)\prod\abs{w_i})$ ($-0.065892$ against $-0.065900$ at
$\tau_c = 10^6$, $k = 3$; $-1.168220$ against $-1.168243$, $k = 6$). (B) Label-noise descent ($10\times40$ Gaussian
designs, a 4-sparse truth where basis pursuit fails, step 0.5, $\sigma = 0.1$, eight seeds $\times$ 16 paths) follows
the reduced flow --- the paths' mean within 0.011--0.035 of it for fresh draws, within 0.11--0.35 for AR(1) draws
($\tau_c = 100$), whose fluctuations are larger ---: fresh draws end on dense fits (supports 10--13, errors 0.80--2.4,
basis pursuit's 0.80--2.98); AR(1) draws recover the truth on all 16 paths of the four seeds whose reduced flow recovers
it (the end interpolants' errors $10^{-13}$--$7\cdot10^{-11}$, supports 4) and on 0--2 of 16 on the four whose flow does
not; at $\sigma = 0.3$ the four recover on 16, 0, 16 and 13 paths (and one seed whose flow fails on 7), at
$\sigma = 0.5$ one seed still recovers --- beyond the small-$\sigma$ limit the descent prunes on its own. (C) Recovery
rates of the reduced flows (40 instances per row at $10\times40$, 20 at $20\times80$): at $10\times40$, $k = 3$, basis
pursuit 0.38, the white flow 0.38, the slow flows 0.62, 0.75, 0.78 for $\tau_c = 10$, 100, 1000; $k = 4$: 0.17, 0.17,
0.42, 0.60, 0.62; $k = 5$: 0, 0, 0.07, 0.15, 0.17; $k = 6$: none; at $20\times80$, $k = 6$: 0.35, 0.35, 0.65, 0.75,
0.75; $k = 8$: 0.10, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.30; the white flow run to convergence ends at basis pursuit's solution (336 of
340 converged, to $9.4\cdot10^{-9}$); the slow flow's supports are smaller than basis pursuit's ($k = 3$: 4.8 against
7.3); started from the white flow's end it does no better than basis pursuit (the atoms the white flow has dropped do
not revive). Where the slow flow fails at $\tau_c = 100$ its end has a larger $R$ than the truth in every instance up to
$k = 4$ (a local minimum; 0.09 and 0.35 of the failures end below the truth's $R$ at $k = 5$, 6); at $\tau_c = 10$,
0.39, 0.68 and 0.85 of them ($k = 4$, 5, 6) end below it --- there the truth is not the finite-$\tau_c$ minimiser. (D)
Gaussian kernel dictionaries on $[0,1]$ (40 centres, 20 samples, widths 0.03 and 0.05, largest coherence 0.90 and
0.96): the slow flow ($\tau_c = 100$) against basis pursuit 0.95 against 0.80 (width 0.03, $k = 4$), 0.68 against 0.45
($k = 6$), 1.00 against 0.85 (width 0.05, $k = 4$), 0.78 against 0.65 ($k = 6$), the white flow at basis pursuit's rate
in every row; label-noise descent with AR(1) draws ($\tau_c = 100$) recovers the truth on all 16 paths on two of the
three instances where basis pursuit fails (errors $2\cdot10^{-13}$--$7\cdot10^{-8}$); on the third it is still on
12--13 atoms at the end, where its reduced flow is at $8.8\cdot10^{-3}$ --- and recovers the truth when run on (to
$3\cdot10^{-13}$ at $s = 1600$); fresh draws end within 0.08--0.11 of basis pursuit's solution, on 11--15 alive atoms.
(E) At the step 0.5 the other colours select as well ($10\times40$, 20 instances): $k = 3$, fresh 0.30, AR(1) 0.65, a
smooth (Gaussian-correlated) noise of $\tau_c = 10$ 0.70, a draw kept 40 steps 0.70 (basis pursuit 0.45); $k = 4$:
0.20, 0.40, 0.55, 0.40 (0.25); a smooth noise of $\tau_c = 100$ has not begun to select at the slow horizon (0.00) and
recovers 0.85 and 0.65 at ten times it. (F) The start, every flow run to convergence: from $u = v = \alpha$ with
$\alpha = 0.1$, 0.5 the slow flow recovers 0.70, 0.75 ($k = 3$) and 0.40, 0.50 ($k = 4$), from $\alpha = 2$ --- toward
the kernel regime --- 0.35 and 0.30, at basis pursuit's rate (0.45, 0.25), which the white flow matches from every
$\alpha$. (G) On the same instances sparse Bayesian learning (Tipping's EM) recovers 0.97, 0.88, 0.55, 0.12, 0.05 for
$k = 2$--6 against the slow flow's 0.97, 0.75, 0.60, 0.15, 0.00 (mean supports at $k = 4$: 6.6, 6.4; basis pursuit
8.9); the log-determinant's majorisation--minimisation reaches 0.70, 0.45 ($k = 3$, 4) from the flows' start and 0.53,
0.33 from basis pursuit; reweighted $\ell^1$ 0.38--0.53 and 0.17--0.25 at $\varepsilon = 0.01$, 0.1, 1 from either
start. (H) The dual certificate at the truth: white noise certifies 0.38 of the 2-sparse truths and 0--0.03 from $k = 3$
on; $\tau_c = 100$ certifies all for $k = 2$--5 and 0.93 at $k = 6$, $\tau_c = 1000$ all; the median ratio
$\max\abs{c_i}/\omega_i$ falls like $1/\tau_c$ ($k = 2$: 0.439, 0.0814, 0.0087 at $\tau_c = 10$, 100, 1000); basis
pursuit's own solutions where it fails (on $N = 10$ atoms) are certified for 0--30\% of the instances. On the kernel
dictionaries (widths 0.03--0.08, $k = 4$, 6) the certified fraction rises with $\tau_c$ to 0.85--1.00 at
$\tau_c = 10^5$ (white: 0--0.60), and the slow flow returns to the truth from $P = 10^{-3}$ in 0.90--1.00
($\tau_c = 100$) and all ($\tau_c = 1000$) of the instances. (I) Under a fit floor ($y = \Phi w^* + \varepsilon e$,
$\abs e = 1$) the slow flow's $k$ largest atoms are the truth's support in 0.70--0.75 ($k = 3$) and 0.50 ($k = 4$) of the
instances for $\varepsilon = 10^{-3}$--0.05, basis pursuit's in 0.40--0.45 and 0.25. (J) At the same physical noise
(AR(1) of correlation time 50, per-step $\sigma = 0.1$) the cancellations off the truth's support decay by
$\Delta\log P = -2.39$ under Euler's step 0.5, $-0.278$ under Euler's step 0.125 and $-0.097$ under Heun's step 0.5 (25
times less): the selection is the explicit scheme's.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.45 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d as committed at 1269272; the numbers
% are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/mirror_free.md; the numerics' parts in PARADIGM's order, (F) after (B)). The
% anchor res:thmDppp:45 is a \hyperref target, as res:thmDppp:21 is. PARADIGM's beta^0 (the mass at alpha = 0) is
% written \beta^0, its powers (\beta^0)^p. Proposition D'''.44, which the text cites, is not stated in
% PARADIGM.md (in progress), so the citation is plain text.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.45}[the free mass--amplitude law is a mirror flow: a conservation law per particle, the
implicit bias in closed form, the invariance under noise with smooth paths, the real loop's Lyapunov
function]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:45}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34} let the masses be free --- the replicator without its
centring ($\lambda\equiv0$), in the real loop the unnormalised exponentiated-gradient law
$\dot\beta_i = \gamma\beta_ie\tilde\Phi_i(x)$ ---, one particle per class beyond the cohesion's range (the cohesion's
$\alpha$-component vanishing on it), and let each particle be driven by one scalar signal $g_i$:
$\langle(r + \xi)\varphi_i\rangle$ at fixed positions on a dictionary (Proposition D$'''$.44's setting: $\Phi$ the
$N\times d$ design of full row rank, $w = \beta\odot a/m$, $J = (m/2N)\abs{\Phi w - y}^2$), $e(t)\varphi_i(x(t))$ in the
real loop --- a measurement noise read in Stratonovich's sense, as the limit of noise with smooth paths:
\[
  \dd\alpha_i = G\beta_ia'_ig_i\dd t,\qquad \dd\beta_i = \gamma\beta_ia_ig_i\dd t,\qquad
  a_i = a_{\max}\tanh(\alpha_i/a_{\max}),\qquad c := \gamma a_{\max}^2/(4G).
\]
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the conservation law)} $Q_i := \beta_i - c\cosh(2\alpha_i/a_{\max})$ is constant along every
    trajectory, for every drive --- also when the centres and widths move by any law of their own, as long as the
    cohesion's $\alpha$-component vanishes on the particle: each particle moves on the fixed curve
    $\beta = Q_i + c\cosh(2\alpha/a_{\max})$ of its $(\alpha,\beta)$ plane, and the free replicator adds no degree of
    freedom to the amplitude, it reparametrises it. Along the curve $w_i = \beta_ia_i/m$ increases strictly with
    $\alpha_i$ and is unbounded --- past the saturation the mass grows like $e^{2\abs{\alpha_i}/a_{\max}}$ and carries
    the coefficient; the bound $\abs{w_i} < \beta_ia_{\max}/m$ holds only at frozen masses ---; $\beta$ never reaches 0 in
    finite time; for $Q_i > -c$ the curve is a graph over all $\alpha\in\R$ and $w_i$ ranges over $\R$; for
    $Q_i\le-c$ it has two branches $\abs{\alpha_i} > \alpha_0$ ($\cosh(2\alpha_0/a_{\max}) = -Q_i/c$), $\beta\to0$ at
    $\alpha_0$, and on each the sign of $w_i$ is frozen (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{Proposition D$'''$.47}).
  \item \emph{(the mirror flow)} On a level set the mirror coordinate
    \[
      \eta_i := \int\frac{\dd\alpha_i}{G\beta_ia'_i}
      = \frac{\alpha_i - a_{\max}\kappa_i\operatorname{artanh}(\kappa_it_i)}{2Gc},\qquad
      t_i = a_i/a_{\max},\quad \kappa_i^2 = (Q_i - c)/(Q_i + c)
    \]
    ($\kappa\operatorname{artanh}(\kappa t)$ read as $-\kappa'\arctan(\kappa't)$ when $\kappa^2 = -\kappa'^2 < 0$, as
    $a_{\max}\coth(\alpha/a_{\max})$ on the critical curve $Q = -c$, and as
    $\kappa\cdot\frac12\log((\kappa t + 1)/(\kappa t - 1))$ on a branch) obeys $\dd\eta_i = g_i\dd t$ exactly, and
    $\dd w_i/\dd\eta_i = mD_i$, $D_i = (\beta_i/m^2)(G\beta_ia_i'^2 + \gamma a_i^2)$ --- Proposition D$'''$.44's
    relevance: $w$ is the mirror flow of the separable, strictly convex potential
    $\Psi_Q(w) = \sum_i\psi_{Q_i}(w_i)$, $\psi'_Q = \eta$, $\psi''_Q = 1/(mD)$, whose conjugate is the mass gained,
    \begin{gather*}
      \psi^*_Q(\eta) = (\beta - \beta^0)/(\gamma m) = 2c\sinh^2(\alpha/a_{\max})/(\gamma m),\qquad
      \psi_Q(w) = \eta w - \psi^*_Q,\\
      \beta^0 := Q + c\ \text{(the mass at}\ \alpha = 0).
    \end{gather*}
    Its shape: near $w = 0$ a mass-weighted $\ell^2$ core, $\psi\approx mw^2/(2G(\beta^0)^2)$
    ($\eta\approx mw/(G(\beta^0)^2)$); at $\abs w\approx w_\sqcap := \beta^0a_{\max}/m$ the amplitude saturates and the
    mass takes over, and beyond it $\eta\approx(1/(\gamma a_{\max}))\log(\abs w/w_\sqcap)$ (for $\beta^0\gg c$): the
    entropy, $\psi\approx(1/(\gamma a_{\max}))\abs w(\log(\abs w/w_\sqcap) - 1)$ --- the marginal cost rising across the
    saturation from $\approx a_{\max}/(G\beta^0)$ to $O(1/(\gamma a_{\max}))$, by the factor $\beta^0/(4c)$, a soft wall
    at $\abs w = w_\sqcap$; for $\beta^0\ll c$ the core is crossed with a jump of $\eta$ of $2\lambda_1$,
    $\lambda_1 = \pi a_{\max}\kappa'/(4Gc)$, $\kappa' = ((2c - \beta^0)/\beta^0)^{1/2}$
    ($\lambda_1 = (\pi a_{\max}/(4G))(2/(c\beta^0))^{1/2}(1 + O(\beta^0/c))$), and beyond the core the curve follows
    the critical one, $\eta\approx\operatorname{sign}(w)\lambda_1 - K\operatorname{sign}(w)\abs w^{-1/3}$:
    \begin{gather*}
      \psi = \lambda_1\abs w - (3/2)K\abs w^{2/3}(1 + o(1))\qquad((\beta^0)^{3/2}\ll\abs w\ll1),\\
      K = (a_{\max}^2/(2Gc))(2c/(a_{\max}^2m))^{1/3},
    \end{gather*}
    an $\ell^1$ norm of weight $\lambda_1$ with a concave correction that under-penalises small coefficients.
  \item \emph{(the implicit bias)} On the dictionary, if every $Q_i > -c$, the noiseless loop converges, from every
    start, to the Bregman projection of its start onto the exact fits,
    \[
      w_\infty = \operatorname{argmin}\{\Psi_Q(w) - \langle\eta(0),w\rangle : \Phi w = y\},
    \]
    which depends on the start only through $(Q,\eta(0))$ --- no persistent excitation is needed. From $\alpha = 0$ and
    masses $\beta^0_i$ ($\eta(0) = 0$) the masses set the regime: as $\beta^0\to\infty$ (a common scale) $w_\infty$ tends
    to the minimum of $\sum_iw_i^2/(\beta^0_i)^2$ on the fits --- the lazy regime; the minimum-norm fit for equal masses
    ---, as $\beta^0\to0$ to the minimum of $\sum_i\abs{w_i}/\sqrt{\beta^0_i}$ --- the rich regime: weighted basis
    pursuit, the weights the starting masses' inverse square roots (when it is unique; otherwise the minimiser selected
    at the next order). The approach is a power, set by the core's barrier: at a vertex $w_{\mathrm{bp}}$ with $N$ atoms
    ($\Phi_S$ invertible) whose certificate $\xi$ ($\Phi_S^\T\xi = \rho_S\cdot\operatorname{sign}w_{\mathrm{bp},S}$,
    $\rho_i = (\beta^0_i)^{-1/2}$) has the relative slacks $\mu_j = 1 - \abs{(\Phi^\T\xi)_j}/\rho_j > 0$ off the support,
    every off-support coefficient sits on the core's tangent profile at $\eta_j = (1 - \mu_j)\lambda_{1,j}$,
    \[
      w_j = \operatorname{sign}((\Phi^\T\xi)_j)\cdot\frac{a_{\max}(\beta^0_j)^{3/2}}{m(2c)^{1/2}}\cdot
      \frac{\cos(\pi\mu_j/2)}{\sin^3(\pi\mu_j/2)}\cdot\big(1 + O((\beta^0)^{1/2}/\mu_j)\big),
    \]
    and the support refits ($\Phi_Sw_S = y - \Phi_{S^c}w_{S^c}$):
    $\abs{w_\infty - w_{\mathrm{bp}}} = O((\beta^0)^{3/2}\mu_{\min}^{-3})$, the next order computed exactly from the
    support's mirror coordinates at the vertex; a small slack --- a near-optimal second vertex --- delays this regime
    until $(\beta^0)^{1/2}\ll\mu_{\min}$, and the closed form's relative error is then of order
    $(\beta^0)^{1/2}/\mu_j$ with a large constant. A free start must sit at $\alpha(0) = 0$ to within
    $\theta = \abs{\alpha(0)}/(\beta^0/(2c))^{1/2}\to0$: otherwise the weights are
    $\lambda_1(1\mp(2/\pi)\arcsin\theta)$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{Proposition D$'''$.47}). (On a branch the barrier is
    an entropy and the approach $\varepsilon^{\mu_j}$, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{Proposition D$'''$.47}(iii).) Its cost
    is time: the transit to the support takes a time $\propto(\beta^0)^{-1/2}$
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:48]{Proposition D$'''$.48}), the last digits a time $\propto1/(\beta^0)^2$ when the support has
    fewer than $N$ atoms (the off-support curvature $\dd w/\dd\eta\propto(\beta^0)^2$). From nonzero amplitudes at tiny
    masses the particles sit on branches ($Q_i < -c$, their signs frozen): their limit is the Bregman projection onto
    the sign-constrained least-squares fits --- \hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{Proposition D$'''$.47}; an exact fit may cease
    to exist, and the bias is basis pursuit only where the frozen signs allow it.
  \item \emph{(noise)} (a) In continuous time a noise with smooth paths --- white noise read in Stratonovich's sense
    ---, of any colour, any covariance over the nodes and any amplitude, leaves $Q$ fixed and moves $\eta$ only within
    $\eta(0) + \operatorname{range}(\Phi^\T)$: the limit map (the Bregman projection of the current state) is invariant
    pathwise --- such a noise never selects among the exact fits; it moves the state only across them, on the fixed
    $N$-dimensional manifold $\nabla\Psi^*_Q(\eta(0) + \operatorname{range}\Phi^\T)$; under persistent noise the
    stationary mean is an exact fit displaced from the projection at $O(v)$. Read in It\^o's sense (Pesme et al.'s
    stochastic gradient flow) white noise adds (b)'s drift per unit time;
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:41]{Proposition D$'''$.41}(ii)'s requirement $C\propto I$ is the conserved law's --- its
    centring (vi) ---, not the free law's. (b) An Euler step of length $h$ on $(\alpha,\log\beta)$ --- sampled-data
    adaptation, the paradigm's update --- changes $Q_i$ by
    \[
      \Delta Q_i = (h^2g_i^2\gamma\beta_i/2)\big(\gamma a_i^2 - G\beta_ia'_i(1 + a_i^2/a_{\max}^2)\big) + O(h^3),
    \]
    negative below the saturation's layer ($G\beta a'(1 + a^2/a_{\max}^2) > \gamma a^2$), zero where
    $\gamma a_{\max}^3t^3 = Gm\abs w(1 - t^4)$ ($t = a/a_{\max}$): under white label noise near $M$, per unit
    $s = (mv/4)t$,
    $\dd Q_i/\dd s = (2\abs{\varphi_i}^2/(mN))\gamma\beta_i(\gamma a_i^2 - G\beta_ia'_i(1 + a_i^2/a_{\max}^2))$ --- the
    conserved quantities' share of Proposition D$'''$.44's selection with the paradigm's update, which is, in these
    coordinates, the drift of $(Q,\eta\bmod\operatorname{range}\Phi^\T)$. Along it the state first moves away from basis
    pursuit ($\norm w_1$ rising), then atoms die ($Q\to-c$) and it settles on a sparse exact fit --- basis pursuit's where
    basis pursuit is the truth; where it is not, basis pursuit's support or another sparse fit of larger $\ell^1$ norm
    (numerical). The mirror update --- $\eta^+ = \eta + hg$, the particle placed on its own curve, an exact
    zero-order-hold integration of each particle --- keeps $Q$ and the projection exactly at every step, whatever the
    noise: which update the loop runs decides whether its measurement noise selects. (c) Dither on the amplitudes
    ($\dd\alpha$ gains $\sqrt{2\tau G_\alpha}\dd B$) drifts $Q$:
    $\dd Q_i = -\gamma\tau\cosh(2\alpha_i/a_{\max})\dd t + \dd M_i$ ($M$ a martingale), so $Q$ falls without bound in
    expectation while the amplitudes diffuse into their saturation, where the masses carry the coefficients with frozen
    signs --- the free law's form of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:37]{Proposition D$'''$.37}'s drive into the saturation.
  \item \emph{(the real loop)} With $f = \sum_iw^*_i\varphi_i$ on the orbit, $\dot e = -k_ee + f(x) - \fhat(x)$ and
    $\fhat = \sum_iw_i\varphi_i$,
    \begin{gather*}
      V := \tfrac12e^2 + \sum_iB_{Q_i}(w^*_i,w_i),\qquad
      B_Q(w^*,w) = w^*(\eta^* - \eta) - (\beta^* - \beta)/(\gamma m)\\
      (\eta^*,\beta^*\ \text{at}\ w^*\ \text{on the particle's own curve}),
    \end{gather*}
    has $\dot V = -k_ee^2$ for every start with $w^*_i$ in the range of its particle's curve --- Boffi--Slotine's Bregman
    Lyapunov function for this law's potential (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34}(ii) has the masses'
    alone); $e\to0$ and the parameters stay bounded, their convergence needing excitation. Two particles on one atom
    without cohesion between them receive the same mirror increment --- $\eta_i - \eta_i(0) = \eta_j - \eta_j(0)$ at
    every time, under any common drive, a measurement noise in continuous time included ---, so the start fixes how they
    split the atom's coefficient: the split is the Bregman split of the current total. The free law loses the conserved
    law's a-priori bound $\abs\fhat\le a_{\max}$: under an approximation error or a disturbance nothing bounds its
    coefficients a priori --- \hyperref[res:thmDppp:46]{Proposition D$'''$.46}'s leak does.
  \item \emph{(the conserved law)} With $\sum\beta = m$ the replicator's centring shifts every level,
    $\dd Q_i = -\gamma\beta_i\lambda\dd t$, $\lambda = \sum_jw_jg_j$: noiselessly $\lambda$ vanishes on the exact fits,
    so the shift is the transient's, and the conserved law's limit differs from the free law's projection (numerical);
    with noise $\lambda\ne0$ at first order.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) $\dd Q_i = \gamma\beta_ia_ig_i\dd t - (2c/a_{\max})\sinh(2\alpha_i/a_{\max})G\beta_ia'_ig_i\dd t$ and
$\sinh(2x)\operatorname{sech}^2x = 2\tanh x$, $4Gc/a_{\max}^2 = \gamma$: zero, for any $g_i$; Stratonovich's calculus
obeys the chain rule. $\dd w/\dd\alpha = (\beta'a + \beta a')/m > 0$ where $\beta > 0$,
$\beta' = (2c/a_{\max})\sinh(2\alpha/a_{\max})$ having $\alpha$'s sign, as $a$ does;
$\beta(t) = \beta(0)\exp(\gamma\int ag\dd t) > 0$. (ii) $\dd\eta = \dd\alpha/(G\beta a') = g\dd t$ by the law; with
$\cosh^2x = (1 + \cosh2x)/2$, $1/(G\beta a') = (1/(2Gc))[1 + (c - Q)/(Q + c\cosh(2\alpha/a_{\max}))]$, and
$t = \tanh(\alpha/a_{\max})$ turns the second term's integral into
$(c - Q)\int a_{\max}\dd t/((Q + c) + (c - Q)t^2)$; at $Q = -c$, $1/(G\beta a') = \coth^2(\alpha/a_{\max})/(2Gc)$.
$\dd w/\dd\eta = (\dd w/\dd t)/(\dd\eta/\dd t) = mD$ by the two laws; $\psi^*$:
$\dd(\beta - \beta^0)/(\gamma m\dd\eta) = \gamma\beta a/(\gamma m) = w = (\psi^*)'$, and $\psi = \eta w - \psi^*$. The
shape: near 0, $\eta\approx a/(G\beta^0)$ and $w\approx\beta^0a/m$; for $\beta^0\gg c$ the artanh term saturates at
$a_{\max}\kappa\operatorname{artanh}\kappa\approx(a_{\max}/2)\log(2\beta^0/c)$ while $\alpha/(2Gc)$ keeps growing, and
$w\approx(Q + (c/2)e^{2\alpha/a_{\max}})a_{\max}/m$, so
$\eta\approx(a_{\max}/(4Gc))\log(mw/(\beta^0a_{\max})) = (1/(\gamma a_{\max}))\log(\abs w/w_\sqcap)$; for
$\beta^0\ll c$ the arctan jumps by $\pi\operatorname{sign}(t)$ over $\abs t\lesssim1/\kappa'$, a range in which
$\abs w = O((\beta^0)^{3/2})$, and beyond it $\kappa'\arctan(\kappa't) = \pi/2 - 1/t + O(1/(\kappa'^2t^3))$, so
$\eta = \lambda_1 + (\alpha - a_{\max}/t)/(2Gc) + \dots$ --- the critical curve's $\eta\cdot w^{1/3}\to-K$ at small
$\alpha$ ($w\approx2c\alpha^3/(ma_{\max}^2)$) ---, and $\psi = \int\eta\dd w$. (iii) $\dd\eta = \Phi^\T r\dd t/N$ keeps
$\eta(t)\in\eta(0) + \operatorname{range}(\Phi^\T)$; writing $\eta = \eta(0) + \Phi^\T z$,
$\dot z = -\nabla\mathcal D(z)/N$ for the dual function
$\mathcal D(z) = \sum_i\psi^*_{Q_i}(\eta_i(0) + (\Phi^\T z)_i) - y^\T z$, convex with Hessian
$\Phi\operatorname{diag}(mD)\Phi^\T\succ0$ and coercive ($\psi^*$ grows exponentially in both directions when
$Q > -c$, $\Phi^\T$ injective), so $z(t)$ converges to $\mathcal D$'s unique minimiser (the gradient flow of a strictly
convex coercive function), and $w = (\psi^*)'(\eta(0) + \Phi^\T z)$ to the point where $\Phi w = y$ and
$\nabla\Psi_Q(w) - \eta(0)\in\operatorname{range}(\Phi^\T)$: the KKT conditions of the strictly convex problem. The
limits: on compacts $\psi_Q(w)\cdot(2G(\beta^0)^2/m)\to w^2$ as $\beta^0\to\infty$ and $\psi_Q(w)/\lambda_1\to\abs w$
as $\beta^0\to0$ (the remainder $O(\abs w^{2/3}/\lambda_1)$), with the minimisers bounded; the minimisers of uniformly
converging strictly convex (resp.\ convex, with a unique limit minimiser) objectives on a fixed affine set converge. The
rate: the support's coefficients sit beyond the core,
$\eta_i = \lambda_{1,i} - K\abs{w_i}^{-1/3} + \dots = \Lambda\rho_i + O(1)$
($\Lambda := (\pi a_{\max}/(4G))(2/(c\beta^0))^{1/2}$ for equal scales), so $\Phi_S^\T z = \eta_S - \eta_S(0)$ gives
$z = \Lambda\xi + O(1)$ and $\eta_j = (\Phi^\T z)_j = \pm(1 - \mu_j)\lambda_{1,j} + O(1)$ off the support, inside the
core, where $\alpha = a_{\max}(\beta^0/(2c))^{1/2}\tan(\pi\eta/(2\lambda_1))(1 + O(\beta^0))$ and
$w = (\beta^0 + 2c\sinh^2(\alpha/a_{\max}))a/m$: at $\tan = \cot(\pi\mu_j/2)$, the formula; the $O(1)$ in $\eta$ moves
the tangent's argument by $O(1/\lambda_1)$, the relative $O((\beta^0)^{1/2}/\mu_j)$. (iv) (a) The computation of (i)
and (ii) holds for any $g_i$, and $r + \xi$ enters only through $g = \Phi^\T(r + \xi)/N$; the projection depends on
$\eta$ modulo $\operatorname{range}(\Phi^\T)$ ($\langle\Phi^\T z,w\rangle = \langle z,y\rangle$ on the fits). (b)
$Q(\alpha + hG\beta a'g,\beta e^{h\gamma ag})$ to second order in $h$: the first order vanishes by (i), the second is
$(h^2g^2/2)(\gamma^2\beta a^2 - (4c/a_{\max}^2)\cosh(2\alpha/a_{\max})G^2\beta^2a'^2)$ and
$a'^2\cosh(2\alpha/a_{\max}) = a'(1 + a^2/a_{\max}^2)$; in It\^o's reading this is $\tfrac12F^\T\nabla^2QF$ per unit of
$\langle\eta\rangle$, $F = (G\beta a',\gamma a)$ on $(\alpha,\log\beta)$; near $M$,
$\E[g_i^2] = \sigma^2\abs{\varphi_i}^2/N^2 + O(h\lambda)$, and $s = (mv/4)t$ with $v = h\sigma^2/N$. (c) It\^o's formula
for $Q$ with the additive noise $\sqrt{2\tau G_\alpha}\dd B$ on $\alpha$:
$\tfrac12\cdot(2\tau G_\alpha)\cdot\partial^2_\alpha Q = -(4c\tau G_\alpha/a_{\max}^2)\cosh(2\alpha/a_{\max})
= -\gamma\tau\cosh(2\alpha/a_{\max})$. (v)
$\frac{\dd}{\dd t}B_Q(w^*,w) = -\psi''(w)\dot w(w^* - w) = -e\varphi_i(x)(w^*_i - w_i)$ since
$\dot w_i = mD_ie\varphi_i(x)$ (the two laws); summed, $-e(f - \fhat)$, and
$\dd(\tfrac12e^2)/\dd t = -k_ee^2 + e(f - \fhat)$. The pair: the same drive gives the same $\dd\eta$. (vi) The centring
adds $-\gamma\beta_i\lambda$ to $\dd\beta_i$ and nothing to $\dd\alpha_i$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i), (ii), (iv)(a), (v) and (vi)'s identity proved (exact identities); (iii) the convergence and the projection
proved for $Q > -c$, the lazy limit proved, the rich limit proved when weighted basis pursuit is unique, its leading
order at an $N$-atom vertex derived (the core's closed form; the next order from the certificate computed and checked),
the time costs' scalings derived and numerical; (iv)(b) the formula proved, the selection's end numerical; (iv)(c) in
expectation; the branches: \hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{Proposition D$'''$.47}. Classical: the law is, in Proposition
D$'''$.44's flat coordinates $(\alpha/\sqrt G,2\sqrt\beta/\sqrt\gamma)$, the Euclidean gradient flow of a separable
$2\to1$ reparametrisation, so it is a mirror flow with a start-dependent potential by Li--Wang--Lee--Arora's
commuting-reparametrisation theorem, with exactly one conserved function per unit, $Q$ (Marcotte--Gribonval--Peyr\'e;
the $u\odot v$ case with $\delta = u^2 - v^2$: Azulay et al.); the kernel and rich regimes of quadratic
parametrisations and the hyperbolic entropy's $\ell^2$-to-entropy shape (Woodworth et al.; Ghai--Hazan--Singer);
exponentiated gradient and its reparametrisation as gradient descent (Kivinen--Warmuth; Amid--Warmuth); the invariance
of stochastic mirror descent's limit under noise in the dual span (Gunasekar et al.; Azizan--Hassibi); the selection by
SGD's It\^o noise and the shrinking effective initialisation of diagonal networks (Pesme--Pillaud-Vivien--Flammarion;
Even--Pesme--Gunasekar--Flammarion), and that isotropic label noise does not select (HaoChen--Wei--Lee--Ma); Bregman
Lyapunov functions for mirror-descent adaptation laws (Boffi--Slotine) (\cite{S72}). New: the paradigm's free
mass--amplitude law
made explicit --- the conserved $Q = \beta - c\cosh(2\alpha/a_{\max})$, the mirror coordinate's closed form in every
regime, the conjugate as the mass gained, the potential's $\ell^2$ core, the saturation's soft wall and its entropy
tail, the $\ell^1$ weights $1/\sqrt{\beta^0}$ of the rich regime (a power law in the starting mass, to be compared with
depth-$D$ diagonal networks' polynomial rich limits) with their $-(3/2)K\abs w^{2/3}$ correction, the branches ---; the
identification of the Euler update's change of $Q$ with Proposition D$'''$.44's selection; and the mirror update as a
sampled-data law whose noise never selects.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.45 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/mirror_free.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_mirror_free.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/mirror_free.md}; $5\times12$ Gaussian designs
with unit columns and 2- or 3-sparse truths of magnitudes in $[0.15,0.3]$, $m = 12$, $G = 1$, $\gamma = 0.1$,
$a_{\max} = 1$, so $c = 0.025$, unless stated; a projection whose residual exceeds $10^{-9}(1 + \abs y)$ would count as a
failure --- there were none). (A) $\eta$'s closed form agrees with the quadrature of $\dd\alpha/(G\beta a')$ to
$1.5\cdot10^{-14}$ (relative; $q = 100c$, 3, 0.5, $c$, $c/2$, 0, $-c/2$ and the branch $q = -2c$), $(\psi^*)' = w$ and
$\dd w/\dd\eta = mD$ to $7.2\cdot10^{-11}$ and $4.3\cdot10^{-10}$ (finite differences). Along the loop $Q$ moves by at
most $1.4\cdot10^{-11}$ noiseless ($t\le10^4$) and $3.6\cdot10^{-10}$ under a smooth noise of 30 sinusoids (frequencies
$10^{-3}$--3, mixed over the nodes, amplitude 0.3; $t\le3\cdot10^4$) --- the integrator's tolerance ---; the conserved
law's shift, integrated along its own trajectory under that noise ($t\le200$), equals the quadrature of
$-\gamma\beta_i\lambda$ to $1.5$--$4.4\cdot10^{-7}$ (relative); along Proposition D$'''$.44's reduced flow (free
masses, white noise, the paradigm's update) $\dd Q/\dd s$ equals (iv)(b)'s closed form to $9.1\cdot10^{-16}$. (B) From
$\alpha = 0$ and equal masses $s\cdot m/d$ the noiseless loop's end at $t = 10^7$ is the Bregman projection to
$4.7\cdot10^{-11}$ for $s = 10$, 1, 0.1, 0.01 (residuals $\le5.7\cdot10^{-15}$; 20 instances per $k$). The projection
moves from the lazy to the rich regime with $s$: for $k = 2$ its distance to the minimum-norm fit is 0.5\% of it at
$s = 10$ and its $\norm w_1$ is 1.37 times basis pursuit's on 11.8 atoms, then 1.52 ($s = 1$), 1.17 (0.1), 1.027
(0.01), 1.0022 ($10^{-3}$) and 1.0004 ($10^{-4}$), the median distance to basis pursuit 0.14, 0.16, 0.097, 0.0235,
$9.0\cdot10^{-4}$, $1.4\cdot10^{-5}$ (falling from $s = 1$ on), the atoms used to 3.0, the truth recovered nearly as
often as basis pursuit recovers it (0.75 at $s = 10^{-4}$ against 0.80; for $k = 3$, 0.30 and 0.30, the median distance
$1.3\cdot10^{-4}$). At $s = 10^{-3}$ the loop is 0.277 from its projection up to $t = 10^3$, then $6.4\cdot10^{-3}$
($t = 2\cdot10^4$), $7.1\cdot10^{-4}$ ($2\cdot10^5$), $1.5\cdot10^{-5}$ ($2\cdot10^6$), below $10^{-8}$ from
$t\approx8.5\cdot10^6$ --- the last digits at the time scale $1/(\beta^0)^2 = 10^6$ of a support with fewer than $N$
atoms. With unequal masses $s\cdot r_i$ ($r_i$ log-uniform in $[1/4,4]$) the projection's median distance to the
weighted basis pursuit $\min\sum\abs{w_i}/\sqrt{r_i}$ is $6.2\cdot10^{-3}$, $2.3\cdot10^{-6}$, $1.7\cdot10^{-9}$ at
$s = 10^{-3}$, $10^{-5}$, $10^{-7}$ ($k = 2$; $1.3\cdot10^{-2}$, $3.0\cdot10^{-5}$, $2.1\cdot10^{-8}$ for $k = 3$),
against a median 0.084 from the unweighted one for $k = 3$ (for $k = 2$ the two coincide where both recover the
2-sparse truth), and to the weighted minimum norm $\min\sum w_i^2/r_i^2$ $2.4$--$2.5\cdot10^{-5}$ and
$2.4$--$2.5\cdot10^{-9}$ (relative) at $s = 10^2$, $10^4$. From $\alpha(0)\sim N(0,0.05^2)$ at tiny masses the particles
land on branches --- 4.2 of 12 at $s = 10^{-4}$, 11.1 and 11.4 at $10^{-6}$ ---; at $10^{-4}$ the frozen signs keep an
exact fit in every instance and the projection's median distance to basis pursuit is $6.6\cdot10^{-4}$ ($k = 2$;
bimodal --- 10 of 20 within $10^{-3}$, the largest 0.37) and 0.15 ($k = 3$); at $10^{-6}$ a truth atom sits on a
wrong-signed branch in 13 and 18 of 20 instances, the frozen signs still fit $y$ exactly in 18 and 19, and the median
distance to basis pursuit is 0.25 and 0.36 (largest 16.3): the bias is the sign-constrained one of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{Proposition D$'''$.47}. (F) The rate (unequal masses $\varepsilon r_i$, the instances whose
weighted basis pursuit is a vertex with $N$ atoms: 10 of 20 for $k = 2$, 17 of 20 for $k = 3$): the leading order
computed from the certificate agrees with the projection to the rounding floor --- $\abs{w - w_1}_\infty$ at most
$1.8\cdot10^{-13}$ ($k = 2$) and $2.6\cdot10^{-11}$ ($k = 3$) at $\varepsilon = 10^{-7}$, where the distances to basis
pursuit are $1.6\cdot10^{-8}$ and $1.4\cdot10^{-8}$ (medians, the instances with $\mu_{\min}\ge0.01$; the largest
$1.1\cdot10^{-7}$ and $1.5\cdot10^{-6}$) ---; the closed form off the support is within 7.6\% and 6.7\% (medians; the
largest errors 113\% and 138\%) at $10^{-7}$ and 0.73\% and 0.69\% (the largest 11\% and 8\%) at $10^{-9}$ --- an
$O(\varepsilon^{1/2}/\mu)$ correction with a large constant, the closed form's $\mu^{-3}$ making the small slacks'
coefficients large ---; and the distance falls between $10^{-7}$ and $10^{-9}$ by the factors 1068 (median; range
609--1252) and 984 (185--2200) ($\varepsilon^{3/2}$: 1000). The instances with a small slack approach late:
$\mu_{\min} = 2.7\cdot10^{-4}$ ($k = 3$, instance 13) keeps a sixth atom and stays 0.17 from basis pursuit at
$\varepsilon = 10^{-5}$ and $10^{-7}$ --- the leading order meaningless there (114 at $10^{-7}$) ---, is
$4.3\cdot10^{-3}$ from it at $10^{-9}$ (leading order within $3.5\cdot10^{-4}$) and $3.2\cdot10^{-8}$ at $10^{-11}$
(within $4.4\cdot10^{-12}$); $\mu_{\min} = 3.3\cdot10^{-3}$ (instance 1) is $3.3\cdot10^{-3}$ away at $10^{-7}$ and
agrees with the leading order to $6.2\cdot10^{-12}$ from $10^{-9}$ on --- the asymptotic regime begins once
$\varepsilon^{1/2}\ll\mu_{\min}$. (C) (1) White label noise at $\sigma = 0.3$ (64 paths to $t = 200$ from random
states): Euler--Maruyama on $(\alpha,\log\beta)$ changes $Q$ on average by its It\^o correction's time integral (ratios
1.0106, 1.0037, 1.0011 at $h = 0.1$, 0.03, 0.01; three instances alike), Heun's (Stratonovich) mean change is
$1.4\cdot10^{-5}$, $4.1\cdot10^{-6}$, $1.4\cdot10^{-6}$ --- vanishing like $h$ ---, and the mirror update keeps $Q$ to
$2.2\cdot10^{-16}$ (by construction). (2) The Euler step's exact mean change of $Q$ (Gauss--Hermite, 80 nodes) agrees
with the closed form to 3.2--3.6\% at $h = 0.5$, 0.29--0.35\% at 0.05 and 0.029--0.036\% at 0.005 --- the $O(h^3)$
term. (3) Along Proposition D$'''$.44's reduced flow from the lazy projection ($s = 1$; ten instances) $\norm w_1$ first
rises ($0.706\to0.781$ by $s = 10^3$ on instance 0), then masses fall toward extinction --- $Q$'s mean falls from 0.975
to $-0.021$ by $s = 10^8$, its minimum $-c$, no atom yet extinct ---; where basis pursuit is the truth (6 instances) the
state reaches it on its two atoms, within $1.0$--$1.6\cdot10^{-6}$ at $s = 10^6$ and $2.7$--$11.6\cdot10^{-9}$ at
$10^8$; of the four where it is not, two end on basis pursuit's support (within $2.6\cdot10^{-5}$ and
$6.5\cdot10^{-4}$) and two on other sparse fits of larger $\ell^1$ norm (0.4228 against 0.4156 on 5 atoms, 0.4714
against 0.4516 on 2). (4) At $h = 0.5$ with $\sigma = 0.3$ over $2\cdot10^4$ steps (16 paths) the paradigm's Euler
update lowers $Q$ by 0.34--0.36 on average (at most 0.41--0.48) and moves the projection by 0.030--0.043, its
$\norm w_1$ rising $0.706\to0.778$ and $0.569\to0.714$ --- as Proposition D$'''$.44's reduced flow to the same
$s = 270$ does ($\norm w_1$ 0.779 and 0.723, $Q$'s mean change $-0.338$ and $-0.355$; the paths' mean projection within
$7.4\cdot10^{-4}$ and $5.7\cdot10^{-3}$ of its state) ---; the mirror update keeps $Q$ to $1.1\cdot10^{-16}$ (by
construction) and the projection to $9.0\cdot10^{-12}$. (D) The real loop ($k_e = 5$, $\gamma = 0.5$, $m = 6$, six
Gaussian atoms of width 0.4 on $[-1.2,1.2]$ and a second particle on the third, $x_m = \sin t + 0.4\sin2.7t$, 2000 time
units): noiseless, $V$ falls monotonically from 0.175 to 0.022 with $\dot V = -k_ee^2$ to $3.0\cdot10^{-9}$
(relative), $Q$ is kept to $7.6\cdot10^{-15}$ and the pair's mirror coordinates move together to $2.4\cdot10^{-15}$;
with a smooth noise in the adaptation only, or a sensor noise in the feedback, the regressor and the adaptation, $V$ is
no longer monotone ($0.175\to0.136$, 0.377) while $Q$ is kept to $6.8\cdot10^{-12}$ and $4.9\cdot10^{-13}$ and the
pair's coordinates move together to $6.3\cdot10^{-13}$ and $9.2\cdot10^{-14}$; at $t = 2000$ the noiseless parameters
are still 0.048 (the distinct atoms) and 0.027 (the pair's sum) from $w^*$. (E) The conserved law ($\sum\beta = m$
kept) from the same starts ($\alpha = 0$, $\beta = m/d$) reaches exact fits (residuals $\le7.5\cdot10^{-17}$) at
distances $1.6$--$4.1\cdot10^{-3}$ from the free law's projections, its $Q$ moving by 0.058--0.22.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.46 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d as committed at 1269272; the numbers
% are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/mirror_leak.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:46 is a \hyperref target, as
% res:thmDppp:21 is. Here epsilon is the approximation error and lambda = min(k_e, sigma), as in PARADIGM.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.46}[a leak in the mirror coordinates: the free mass--amplitude law made robust, its bias
a Bregman-regularised fit --- a ridge from large starting masses, a LASSO from small
ones]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:46}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45} (free masses, one particle per class beyond the
cohesion's range, each particle on its curve $Q_i$ with mirror coordinate $\eta_i$ and potential $\psi_{Q_i}$), add to
each particle's scalar drive the leak $-\sigma(\eta_i - \eta_{p,i})$ toward a point
$\eta_{p,i} = \eta(\alpha_{p,i};Q_i)$ of its own curve (the coefficient $w_{p,i} = w(\alpha_{p,i};Q_i)$; the start,
say):
\[
  \dd\alpha_i = G\beta_ia'_i[g_i - \sigma(\eta_i - \eta_{p,i})]\dd t,\qquad
  \dd\beta_i = \gamma\beta_ia_i[g_i - \sigma(\eta_i - \eta_{p,i})]\dd t,\qquad \sigma > 0,
\]
so that $\dd\eta_i = [g_i - \sigma(\eta_i - \eta_{p,i})]\dd t$ and $Q_i$ stays conserved --- the leak is a law on
$(\alpha,\beta)$, $\eta$ explicit and $Q$ read off the state.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the real loop: robustness)} Let $\dot e = -k_ee + f(x) - \fhat(x) + d(t)$ along the loop's own
    trajectory, with $f = \sum_iw^*_i\varphi_i + \varepsilon$ (any split of $f$ into a representable part with each
    $w^*_i$ in its particle's range and a remainder $\varepsilon$ counted in the disturbance),
    $\abs{\varepsilon + d}\le\bar d$, and $V = \tfrac12e^2 + \sum_iB_i(w^*_i,w_i)$
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45}(v); proper on each curve: $B_i(w^*_i,\cdot)\to\infty$ at both ends
    when $Q_i > -c$). With a measurement noise $n$ in the adaptation (the drive $(e + n)\varphi_i$),
    \begin{align*}
      \dot V &= -k_ee^2 + e(\varepsilon + d) - n(f - \fhat - \varepsilon)
      - \sigma\sum_i[B_i(w^*_i,w_i) + B_i(w_i,w_{p,i}) - B_i(w^*_i,w_{p,i})]\\
      &\le -(k_e/2)e^2 - \sigma\sum_iB_i(w^*_i,w_i) + \bar d^2/(2k_e) + \sigma B(w^*,w_p)\qquad(n = 0),
    \end{align*}
    so $V(t)\le V(0)e^{-\lambda t} + (C/\lambda)(1 - e^{-\lambda t})$, $\lambda = \min(k_e,\sigma)$,
    $C = \bar d^2/(2k_e) + \sigma B(w^*,w_p)$: $e$ and the parameters are ultimately bounded, whatever the disturbance's
    form (the bound minimised over the split). Without the leak nothing bounds the free masses' coefficients a priori
    (the conserved law's $\abs\fhat\le a_{\max}$ is lost with the constraint): a decaying excitation and a bounded
    disturbance can drive them to infinity --- $\varphi(t) = (1 + t)^{-1/2}$ and a $d$ built for it give
    $w\propto(1 + t)^{1/4}$ with $e\to0$ ---, while on a persistently exciting orbit they converge, slowly, to the biased
    fit $w^* + H_L^{-1}\bar d_L$ of (ii). With $d = 0$ the leak's price is the bias below.
  \item \emph{(the averaged loop: the leak's bias)} Let the regressor $\varphi(t) = \varphi(x_m(t))$ be periodic and the
    adaptation slow, the error slaved to its frozen-parameter periodic steady state
    $e = L[(w^* - w)^\T\varphi + \varepsilon + d]$, $L = 1/(s + k_e)$. Then
    \[
      \dot\eta = (H_L(w^* - w) + \bar d_L)/k_e - \sigma(\eta - \eta_p),\qquad
      H_L = k_e\langle\varphi L[\varphi]^\T\rangle,\qquad \bar d_L = k_e\langle\varphi L[\varepsilon + d]\rangle,
    \]
    where $H_L + H_L^\T = 2k_e^2\langle L[\varphi]L[\varphi]^\T\rangle\succeq0$ (the error filter's Gram --- the
    classical averaged adaptive system); its equilibrium $w_\sigma$ is the unique solution of the monotone equation
    $(H_L(w - w^*) - \bar d_L)/k_e + \sigma(\eta(w) - \eta_p) = 0$ and attracts every start ($Q_i > -c$), with the
    Lyapunov function $B(w_\sigma,w)$. On a scalar periodic orbit the first-order lag vanishes
    ($\langle\varphi\dot\varphi^\T\rangle = 0$, an exact differential):
    $H_L = H - \langle\dot\varphi\dot\varphi^\T\rangle/k_e^2 + O(k_e^{-3})$, symmetric to that order, and to that order
    the averaged loop is the mirror flow of the strictly convex
    \[
      F_\sigma(w) = (1/(2k_e))(w - w^*)^\T H_L(w - w^*) - \langle\bar d_L,w\rangle/k_e
      + \sigma\sum_iB_i(w_i,w_{p,i}),
    \]
    $H_L\to H = \langle\varphi\varphi^\T\rangle$ and $\bar d_L\to\langle\varphi(\varepsilon + d)\rangle$ as
    $k_e\to\infty$. As $\sigma\to0$, $w_\sigma\to w^* + H_L^{-1}\bar d_L$ (the excitation's consistent fit nearest $w_p$
    in $\Psi$'s divergence when $H_L$ is singular) --- $w^*$ only without a bias. The real loop's mean follows
    $w_\sigma$ to $O(\text{the adaptation gains})$ (the averaging theorem for adaptive systems, an exponentially stable
    averaged equilibrium).
  \item \emph{(the regimes)} From $\alpha = 0$ and masses $\beta^0_i$ with $w_p = 0$: for $\beta^0\gg c$ the regulariser
    is the ridge $\sum_i(m/(2G(\beta^0_i)^2))w_i^2$ up to the saturation's wall $w_\sqcap = \beta^0a_{\max}/m$ --- the
    leak a generalised Tikhonov regularisation ---; for $\beta^0\to0$ it is the weighted $\ell^1$ norm
    $\sum_i\lambda_1(\beta^0_i)\abs{w_i}$, $\lambda_1(\beta^0) = \pi a_{\max}\kappa'/(4Gc)$,
    $\kappa' = ((2c - \beta^0)/\beta^0)^{1/2}$ ($= (\pi a_{\max}/(4G))(2/(c\beta^0))^{1/2}(1 + O(\beta^0/c))$), minus
    the remainder $(3/2)K\abs w^{2/3}$, $K = (a_{\max}^2/(2Gc))(2c/(a_{\max}^2m))^{1/3}$
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45}(ii)), which vanishes against $\sigma\lambda_1$ in the limit: the
    averaged loop solves the LASSO
    $\min\,(1/(2k_e))\allowbreak(w - w^*)^\T H(w - w^*) + \sigma\sum_i\lambda_1(\beta^0_i)\abs{w_i}$ at a fixed penalty
    $\sigma\lambda_1$ ($\sigma\propto\sqrt{\beta^0}$), its regularisation path parametrised by $\sigma$. Its inactive
    coordinates obey the KKT condition $\abs{(H(w^* - w))_i}/k_e\le\sigma\lambda_{1,i}$; it recovers the target's
    support exactly --- for a small penalty --- when the weighted irrepresentable condition
    $\max_{j\notin S}\abs{(H_{S^cS}H_{SS}^{-1}(\lambda_{1,S}\odot\operatorname{sign}w^*_S))_j}/\lambda_{1,j} < 1$ holds
    (and no sign flips; $\le1$ is necessary; with equal masses the weights cancel and it is the classical
    $\norm{H_{S^cS}H_{SS}^{-1}\operatorname{sign}(w^*_S)}_\infty < 1$): the leak then prunes to the target's kernels,
    otherwise it keeps spurious atoms at every penalty. The rich equilibria are reached slowly: the averaged loop's
    escape from a rich start takes a time growing like $k_e\lambda_1\propto k_e(\beta^0)^{-1/2}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(v)'s computation with the drive's leak term and the noise:
$\frac{\dd}{\dd t}B_i(w^*_i,w_i) = -(\dd\eta_i/\dd t)(w^*_i - w_i)$,
$\dd\eta_i/\dd t = (e + n)\varphi_i - \sigma(\eta_i - \eta_{p,i})$, and the three-point identity
$(\eta(w) - \eta(w_p))(w^* - w) = B(w^*,w_p) - B(w^*,w) - B(w,w_p)$;
$e(\varepsilon + d)\le(k_e/2)e^2 + \bar d^2/(2k_e)$; then $\dot V\le-\lambda V + C$ and the comparison lemma. The drift
example: with $\eta = \eta(0) + \kappa\log(1 + t)$, $\kappa = 1/(4\gamma a_{\max})$,
$e = \dot\eta/\varphi = \kappa(1 + t)^{-1/2}$ and $d = \dot e + k_ee + w(\eta(t))\varphi(t)$ ($w^* = 0$) is bounded,
$w(\eta)$ growing like $e^{\gamma a_{\max}\eta}$ in the entropy tail. (ii) Averaging the slow law over the period with
$e$ at its steady state gives $\dot\eta$; $H_L + H_L^\T$: with $\psi = L[\varphi]$, $\dot\psi + k_e\psi = \varphi$,
$\langle\varphi\psi^\T + \psi\varphi^\T\rangle = \dd\langle\psi\psi^\T\rangle/\dd t + 2k_e\langle\psi\psi^\T\rangle$ and
the first term vanishes over a period. Monotonicity: for two points,
$(R(w_1) - R(w_2))\cdot(w_1 - w_2)\ge\sigma(\eta(w_1) - \eta(w_2))(w_1 - w_2) > 0$, so the root is unique (it exists:
$F$'s coercivity, or a degree argument on the coercive monotone map);
$\frac{\dd}{\dd t}B(w_\sigma,w) = -\dot\eta\cdot(w_\sigma - w)
= -(w - w_\sigma)^\T H_L(w - w_\sigma)/k_e - \sigma(\eta - \eta_\sigma)(w - w_\sigma) < 0$. The expansion:
$L = (1/k_e)\sum_j(-s/k_e)^j$, $\langle\varphi\dot\varphi^\T\rangle = (1/T)\oint\varphi(x)\varphi'(x)^\T\dd x = 0$,
$\langle\varphi\ddot\varphi^\T\rangle = -\langle\dot\varphi\dot\varphi^\T\rangle$; the odd orders' antisymmetric parts
survive from $k_e^{-3}$ on
($\langle\dot\varphi_i\ddot\varphi_j - \dot\varphi_j\ddot\varphi_i\rangle
= \langle\dot x^3(\varphi_i'\varphi_j'' - \varphi_j'\varphi_i'')\rangle$, not an exact differential). As $\sigma\to0$ the
monotone equation's root tends to the root of $H_L(w - w^*) = \bar d_L$. (iii)
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(ii)--(iii)'s limits of $\psi_Q$ with the corrected remainder; with $w_p = 0$,
$B(w,0) = \psi(w) - \psi(0) - \eta(0)w = \psi(w)$; the LASSO's support theory (Zhao--Yu; Wainwright); the escape time:
from $w\approx0$ the coordinate's $\eta$ must reach $\sigma\lambda_1$'s level against the drive
$(H(w^* - w))_i/k_e$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved (the identity, the comparison bound; the drift example by construction); (ii) proved for the averaged
loop (existence, uniqueness, global attraction, the expansion to $O(k_e^{-2})$), the real loop's mean by the classical
averaging theorem (cited, not re-proved), numerical: the agreement; (iii) the two limits proved, the support recovery by
the LASSO's theory and computed over every 3-sparse pattern of an orbit's Gram, the escape time's scaling numerical.
Classical: the $\sigma$-modification and its ultimate bound (Ioannou--Kokotovi\'c; Ioannou--Sun), averaging for
adaptive systems with the filtered regressor's correlation (Anderson et al.;
Bodson--Sastry--Anderson--Mareels--Bitmead; Sastry--Bodson), continuous-time discounted mirror descent and its
regulariser-perturbed rest points with Bregman (Fenchel-coupling) Lyapunov functions (Gao--Pavel; Romano--Pavel), its
entropic case for the replicator --- exponentially discounted payoffs with a smooth best response
(Coucheney--Gaujal--Mertikopoulos) ---, regularisation toward a reference with a Lyapunov function (P\'erolat et al.;
Abe et al.), explicit regularisation through mirror descent (Azizan--Lale--Hassibi), the vanishing-leak (Tikhonov,
viscosity) selection (Alvarez--Bolte--Brahic; Attouch), the LASSO and its irrepresentable condition (Tibshirani;
Zhao--Yu; Wainwright), mirror-descent adaptation laws (Boffi--Slotine) (\cite{S73}). New: the dual-coordinate leak
implemented on the
paradigm's $(\alpha,\beta)$ with $Q$ kept; its ultimate bound in the adaptive loop with this law; the starting masses
setting the regime and the LASSO weights $\lambda_1\propto(\beta^0)^{-1/2}$; the averaged equilibrium with the error
filter's Gram written as a monotone equation for this law.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.46 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/mirror_leak.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_mirror_leak.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/mirror_leak.md}). (A) The identity of (i),
$\dot V = -k_ee^2 + ed - n(f - \fhat) - \sigma\sum[B(w^*,w) + B(w,w_p) - B(w^*,w_p)]$, holds to $9.5\cdot10^{-11}$
(relative) at twelve random states of the real loop with a disturbance and a measurement noise in the adaptation
($\sigma = 0$ and 0.3). On random positive definite $H$ ($10\times10$, $k_e = 2$, four draws) the averaged equilibrium
is the ridge $(H + \rho I)^{-1}(Hw^* + \bar d)$, $\rho = k_e\sigma m/(G(\beta^0)^2) = 0.5$, to $1.3\cdot10^{-8}$
($\beta^0 = 10^4$) and $1.3\cdot10^{-4}$ ($10^2$) --- a ridge that moves $w$ by 31--55\% ---, while at $\beta^0 = 3$,
where the saturation's wall $w_\sqcap = 0.25$ lies inside the coefficients' range ($\max\abs{w^*} = 0.295$), it departs
from the ridge by 0.15 (relative): the regulariser is a ridge only below the wall; and it is the LASSO of penalty
$\sigma\lambda_1 = 0.02$ to 0.015, $1.5\cdot10^{-3}$, $1.5\cdot10^{-4}$ (relative; $\beta^0 = 10^{-4}$, $10^{-6}$,
$10^{-8}$ --- the remainder falls like $(\beta^0)^{1/2}$), with the LASSO's support in every draw ($\lambda_1$'s leading
term exact to $1.0\cdot10^{-7}$ at $10^{-8}$). (B) (1) On a persistently exciting orbit ($k_e = 5$, $m = 6$, six atoms of
width 0.4, $x_m$ of period $20\pi$; the filtered Gram's eigenvalues $2.7\cdot10^{-3}$ to 0.68) under an approximation
error ($0.1\sin4x$) and a disturbance with a bias ($0.2\sin3.1t + 0.1$), the free law without the leak creeps toward the
biased fit $w^* + H_L^{-1}\bar d_L$ ($\max\abs w$ 0.337, $\sum B(w^*,w) = 0.465$, the averaged loop's slowest time
constant $1.5\cdot10^4$): its largest $\abs w$ by quarters of $2\cdot10^4$ time units 0.309, 0.330, 0.337, 0.341 (each
quarter's largest value includes the parameters' oscillation along the orbit about the averaged state) and $V$ rising
from 0.40 to 0.45; $\sigma = 0.01$ and 0.1 hold the coefficients at 0.081 and 0.040 with $V$ at 0.085 and 0.13, below
the ultimate bounds $C/\lambda = 1.74$ and 0.30 and below the comparison bound
$V(0)e^{-\lambda t} + (C/\lambda)(1 - e^{-\lambda t})$ at every sample, the late means within $2.5\cdot10^{-3}$ and
$6.5\cdot10^{-4}$ of the averaged equilibrium $w_\sigma^L$; the rms error rises from 0.024 to 0.031 and 0.037 (the
leak's price); $Q$ is kept to $6\cdot10^{-11}$. (2) Without persistent excitation ($\varphi(t) = (1 + t)^{-1/2}$, one
particle, a bounded disturbance $\abs d\le0.77$ built for it) the free law's coefficient grows like $(1 + t)^{1/4}$ as
constructed --- 0.70, 2.31, 7.37, 23.3 at $t = 1$, $10^2$, $10^4$, $10^6$ ---, while the leak bounds it: $\sigma = 0.01$
gives 0.70, 1.52, 0.52, 0.46 and $\sigma = 0.1$ gives 0.69, 0.63, 0.47, 0.46, below the comparison bound throughout
($C/\lambda = 29.5$ and 3.07). (C) In slow adaptation ($k_e = 20$, $G = 0.5$, $\gamma = 0.05$, the leak toward the
start) the error filter's Gram differs from the idealised $\langle\varphi\varphi^\T\rangle$ by 0.89\% (max, relative)
and is symmetric to $3\cdot10^{-14}$ on this scalar orbit; started at the averaged equilibrium $w_\sigma^L$, the real
loop's period means (period $20\pi$) stay near it --- the last within $1.6\cdot10^{-4}$ (lazy start, $\beta^0 = 3$,
$\sigma = 5\cdot10^{-4}$, 318 periods) and $1.7\cdot10^{-4}$ (intermediate, $\beta^0 = 0.3$, $\sigma = 10^{-4}$, 954
periods), 4.4 and 2.6 times closer than to the idealised equilibrium (lag
$\abs{w_\sigma^L - w_\sigma} = 8.2\cdot10^{-4}$ and $5.1\cdot10^{-4}$), against the leak's bias
$\abs{w_\sigma^L - w^*} = 0.038$ and 0.12 ---, the last periods' means changing by $1.6\cdot10^{-10}$ and
$1.1\cdot10^{-9}$. (D) On the Gram of an orbit (twelve atoms of width 0.25 on $[-1.4,1.4]$, eigenvalues
$1.4\cdot10^{-4}$ to 0.55): (1) for a 3-sparse truth (irrepresentable quantity 0.771) the equilibrium from a rich start
($\beta^0 = 10^{-6}$) is the LASSO of penalty $\mu = \sigma\lambda_1$ to $3.7\cdot10^{-6}$--$6.5\cdot10^{-4}$
(absolute) and keeps exactly the truth's three atoms for $\mu\le10^{-2}$ (at $3\cdot10^{-2}$ it drops one, the LASSO's
own shrinkage), while from a lazy start ($\beta^0 = 30$) it is the ridge to $3.9\cdot10^{-6}$--$2.5\cdot10^{-4}$, dense
(no coefficient vanishes; 6--12 above the reporting threshold); (2) over every 3-sparse support and sign pattern (1760)
at $\mu = 10^{-3}$ the irrepresentable condition holds for 416, the LASSO's prediction (the condition and no sign flip)
for 416, and the equilibrium's support is exactly the truth's for 415 --- prediction and equilibrium agree on 1745 of
1760; elsewhere it carries 1.76 extra atoms on average; (3) with a disturbance bias correlated with the regressors
($\bar d = Hv$, $\abs v = 0.05$) the equilibrium tends to $w^* + H^{-1}\bar d$ as $\sigma\to0$ (distance
$2.5\cdot10^{-2}$, $3.7\cdot10^{-4}$, $3.7\cdot10^{-6}$, $3.7\cdot10^{-8}$ at $\sigma = 10^{-2}$, $10^{-4}$, $10^{-6}$,
$10^{-8}$, linear in $\sigma$), 0.043 from $w^*$; (4) the averaged loop from the rich start reaches its equilibrium (to
$10^{-3}$ relative) at $t\approx3.2\cdot10^5$ for the truth above and $2\cdot10^6$ for a support violating the
irrepresentable condition (quantity 1.22), whose equilibrium keeps five atoms.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.47 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d as committed at 1269272; the numbers
% are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/mirror_branch.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:47 is a \hyperref target, as
% res:thmDppp:21 is. As in PARADIGM, K in (ii)--(v) is the cone of the frozen signs, while the numerics' K = 3.218 is
% the constant of Proposition D'''.45(ii).
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.47}[the free law's branches: an exponentiated gradient with frozen signs --- small starts
land on them; the limit is the Bregman projection onto the sign-constrained least-squares fits, the wrong-signed
particles' masses going extinct; on positive kernel dictionaries the target's kernels at every
scale]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:47}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45} (free masses, one particle per class beyond the
cohesion's range, each driven by its scalar $g_i$; $c = \gamma a_{\max}^2/(4G)$), call particle $i$ \emph{on a branch}
when $Q_i < -c$, i.e.\ when it starts with
\[
  \beta_i(0) < 2c\sinh^2(\alpha_i(0)/a_{\max})
\]
--- a mass small against its amplitude's square: a start at small masses and nonzero amplitudes puts every particle on
a branch, and only $\abs{\alpha_i(0)} < a_{\max}\operatorname{asinh}((\beta_i(0)/(2c))^{1/2})$ keeps a free sign. A free
sign is not enough for \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45}(iii)'s symmetric rich limit: with
$\theta_i = \abs{\alpha_i(0)}/(\beta_i(0)/(2c))^{1/2} < 1$ the curve's mass is $\beta^0_i = (1 - \theta_i^2)\beta_i(0)$
and the start sits at $\eta_i(0) = s_i(2/\pi)\arcsin\theta_i\cdot\lambda_{1,i}$, so its weights become
$\lambda_{1,i}(1\mp(2/\pi)\arcsin\theta_i)$ --- the limit needs $\theta_i\to0$. On a branch let
$s_i = \operatorname{sign}\alpha_i$, $\alpha_{0,i}$ its branch point ($\cosh(2\alpha_{0,i}/a_{\max}) = -Q_i/c$,
$\beta = 0$ there) and $a_{0,i} = a_{\max}\tanh(\alpha_{0,i}/a_{\max})$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the potential on a branch: an entropy with a temperature)} Along every curve the mirror coordinate's
    slope in $\log\abs w$ is
    \[
      \frac{\dd\eta_i}{\dd\log\abs{w_i}} = \frac{a_i}{\gamma a_i^2 + G\beta_ia_i'^2},
    \]
    so on a branch, where $\beta\to0$ at $\alpha_0$ with $a\to a_0$, the potential is an entropy near $w = 0$ with the
    temperature $1/(\gamma a_{0,i})$ ---
    $\eta_i = s_i(1/(\gamma a_{0,i}))\log\abs{w_i} + C_i + O(\abs{w_i})$,
    $\psi_i(w) = (1/(\gamma a_{0,i}))(\abs w\log\abs w - \abs w) + C_iw + \psi_i(0) + O(w^2)$ --- and at
    $\abs w\to\infty$ an entropy with the temperature $1/(\gamma a_{\max})$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(ii)'s
    tail). $\psi_i$ is finite at 0, $\psi_i(0) = (Q_i + c)/(\gamma m)$, with an infinite slope there (a barrier: the
    sign is frozen), and the Bregman divergence from 0 is the particle's mass,
    \[
      B_i(0,w_i) = \beta_i/(\gamma m).
    \]
    Near its branch point the particle runs the unnormalised exponentiated gradient: its amplitude frozen near $a_0$,
    $\beta_i\approx\beta_i(0)\exp(\gamma a_{0,i}\int g_i\dd t)$. (On the critical curve $Q_i = -c$ the two branches meet
    at $\alpha = 0$, $a_0 = 0$, and the barrier is a power, $\eta\propto-\abs w^{-1/3}$.)
  \item \emph{(the limit: the Bregman projection onto the sign-constrained least-squares fits)} On a dictionary ($\Phi$
    the $N\times d$ design, labels $y$, $J(w) = (m/2N)\abs{\Phi w - y}^2$, the noiseless loop
    $\dd\eta = -\nabla J(w)\dd t/m$) let $K = \{w : s_iw_i\ge0\ \text{for every particle on a branch}\}$ and
    $S = \operatorname{argmin}_KJ$, the sign-constrained least-squares fits --- nonempty (a convex quadratic on a
    polyhedron), $S = K\cap\{\Phi w = \hat y\}$ with $\hat y$ the common value of $\Phi w$ on it. From every start (any
    mix of free particles and branches):
    \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
      \item $B(\bar w,w(t))$ is nonincreasing for every $\bar w\in S$, and
        \[
          J(w(t)) - \min_KJ\le m\cdot\min_{\bar w\in S}B(\bar w,w(0))/t;
        \]
      \item $w(t)$ converges, and its limit is the Bregman projection of the start onto $S$,
        \[
          w_\infty = \operatorname{argmin}\{\Psi_Q(w) - \langle\eta(0),w\rangle : w\in S\};
        \]
      \item the particles on branches whose coefficient vanishes on all of $S$ go extinct --- $\beta_i\to0$,
        $s_i\eta_i\to-\infty$ ---, exponentially at the rate $\gamma a_{0,i}\abs{g_i(\infty)}$ when
        $g_i(\infty) = -(\nabla J(w_\infty))_i/m\ne0$ (strict complementarity; on the critical curve $Q_i = -c$,
        $a_0 = 0$, the decay is algebraic, about $t^{-3}$); every other particle's $\eta$ converges, and no other branch
        coefficient vanishes in the limit (a free particle's may, e.g.\ on a column uncorrelated with everything,
        started at $\alpha = 0$).
    \end{enumerate}
    When $y$ has a fit in $K$ with every branch coefficient strictly of its particle's sign, $S$ is the set of exact
    fits in $K$, no coefficient vanishes on all of it and nothing goes extinct ---
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45}(iii) with branches ---; otherwise the wrong-signed particles die and
    the loop fits what the frozen signs allow, in the least-squares sense.
  \item \emph{(the rich limit on branches: sign-constrained basis pursuit at a logarithmic temperature)} Let the masses
    shrink at fixed amplitudes, $\beta_i(0) = \varepsilon b_i$, $\alpha_i(0)\ne0$. As $\varepsilon\to0$ every particle is
    on a branch, $a_{0,i}\to\abs{a_i(0)}$, the objective of (ii)(b) is
    $\log(1/\varepsilon)\sum_i\abs{w_i}/(\gamma\abs{a_i(0)}) + O(1)$ on $S$, and
    \[
      w_\infty(\varepsilon)\to\operatorname{argmin}\Big\{\sum_i\abs{w_i}/\abs{a_i(0)} : w\in S\Big\}\qquad
      \text{(when unique)},
    \]
    the sign-constrained basis pursuit weighted by the inverse starting amplitudes. The linear program is perturbed by
    an entropy at the temperature $1/\log(1/\varepsilon)$, and an entropic penalty's minimiser approaches a
    nondegenerate vertex exponentially in the inverse temperature (Cominetti--San Mart\'in; Weed): at a vertex with $N$
    atoms whose certificate $\xi$ ($s_j(\Phi^\T\xi)_j\le\rho_j := 1/(\gamma\abs{a_j(0)})$, with equality on the support)
    has the relative slacks $\mu_j = 1 - s_j(\Phi^\T\xi)_j/\rho_j > 0$, the off-support coefficients are
    \[
      \abs{w_j} = e^{O(1)}\varepsilon^{\mu_j},
    \]
    a power of $\varepsilon$ with the slack for its exponent (the $O(1)$ computed from the support's mirror coordinates
    at the vertex), and the support refits --- against $\varepsilon^{3/2}$ from
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(iii)'s start $\alpha(0) = 0$, whose barrier is the core's tangent, not an
    entropy: with $\mu_{\min}$ well below 3/2 the branches' rich limit is reached far more slowly. At a vertex with fewer
    than $N$ atoms the approach is still a power, $\varepsilon^{\mu^*}$, $\mu^*$ the slack of the dual direction
    $z/\log(1/\varepsilon)$ the entropy selects (not computed here). In a mixed start the free particles' weight
    $\varepsilon^{-1/2}$ dominates the branches' $\log(1/\varepsilon)$: when the branch atoms' frozen signs admit an
    exact fit, the branches carry it and the free particles keep $O(\varepsilon^2\log(1/\varepsilon))$; when they do
    not, the free particles reach their walls on the power scale and the limit is basis pursuit over the free atoms
    (weights $\rho_i$) with the branch atoms sign-constrained and, to leading order, free of charge --- their weights
    $1/(\gamma a_0)$ breaking ties ---, the free coefficients $O(1)$.
  \item \emph{(a pair per atom: the infimal convolution, the hyperbolic entropy)} Particles on one atom without cohesion
    between them receive the same mirror increment $\Delta$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(v)), so the atom's
    coefficient $W(\Delta) = \sum_jw_j(\eta_j(0) + \Delta)$ has the conjugate $\sum_j\psi^*_j(\eta_j(0) + \Delta)$: the
    atom's potential is the infimal convolution of its particles' tilted potentials. For a pair started at
    $\pm\alpha(0)$ with equal masses ($Q_+ = Q_-$), near the branch points $W = 2u\sinh(\gamma a_0\Delta)$
    ($u = w_+(0) = -w_-(0)$) and the atom's potential is the hyperbolic entropy
    \[
      \psi_{\mathrm{pair}}(W) = \frac1{\gamma a_0}\big[W\operatorname{asinh}(W/(2u)) - (W^2 + 4u^2)^{1/2}\big],
    \]
    the $u^2 - v^2$ parametrisation's: $\ell^2$ for $\abs W\ll u$ (curvature $1/(2\gamma a_0u)$), an entropy for
    $\abs W\gg u$. A pair per atom gives the branches a free sign; its rich limit ($u\to0$) is basis pursuit weighted by
    $1/a_0$, at the temperature $1/\log(1/u)$ of (iii).
  \item \emph{(positive kernel dictionaries: the target at every scale)} Let $\Phi\ge0$ have a strictly positive vector
    in its row space (positive kernels: every row of a Gaussian design is one) and every particle start on a positive
    branch. Then $K\cap\{\Phi w = y\}$ is a bounded polytope; if the target $w^*\ge0$ is its only point --- certified by
    a $v$ with $h = \Phi^\T v$ vanishing on the target's atoms and $h_i\ge\mu > 0$ on the others (for Gaussian atoms of
    one width and a $k$-sparse nonnegative target such a certificate exists once $N\ge2k + 1$ exact samples, whatever
    the atoms' separation: Schiebinger--Robeva--Recht; Eftekhari--Tanner--Thompson--Toader--Tyagi) ---, then
    $S = \{w^*\}$ and the loop converges to $w^*$ from every positive-branch start, at every mass scale, where the free
    law's own limits from $\alpha = 0$ depend on the scale (the weighted minimum-norm fit of a lazy start, the
    equal-weight basis pursuit of a rich one, which need not be the target). The convergence is algebraic, not
    exponential --- the residual vanishes in the limit, so $g(\infty) = 0$ ---: the other atoms' total mass obeys
    \[
      \sum_{i\notin\supp w^*}w_i(t)\le(\abs v/\mu)\big(2N\cdot B(w^*,w(0))/t\big)^{1/2},
    \]
    so the selection's speed is the certificate's margin $\mu$, and on Gaussian grids sampled at few points $\mu$ is
    typically tiny: over all the three-atom supports of the instances the median margin is far below the showcase
    targets', a large fraction has no certificate in double precision (exact margins of order $10^{-32}$), and there
    nonnegative fits carrying $O(1)$ mass off the support exist to rounding --- the selection then is invisible on any
    reachable horizon, the masses lingering on the target's neighbouring atoms (numerics E). Uniqueness alone does not
    make the target reachable; stability constants (Eftekhari et al.) govern it.
  \item \emph{(the real loop)} \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45}(v)'s
    $V = \tfrac12e^2 + \sum_iB_i(w^*_i,w_i)$ has $\dot V = -k_ee^2$ also with particles on branches when each $w^*_i$
    has its particle's sign or is 0, the atoms the target does not use contributing their masses $\beta_i/(\gamma m)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) By the two laws $\dd w/\dd\eta = mD = (\beta/m)(G\beta a'^2 + \gamma a^2)$ and $w = \beta a/m$, so
$\dd\eta/\dd\log\abs w = w/(mD) = a/(G\beta a'^2 + \gamma a^2)$. On a branch $\beta\to0$ at $\alpha_0$ with
$a\to a_0\ne0$: the slope tends to $1/(\gamma a_0)$ with an $O(\beta) = O(\abs w)$ correction; integrate twice.
$\psi = \eta w - (\beta - \beta^0)/(\gamma m)$ ($\beta^0 := Q + c$) with $\eta w\to0$ and $\beta\to0$ gives
$\psi(0) = (Q + c)/(\gamma m)$, and $B(0,w) = \psi(0) - \psi(w) + \eta(w)w = \beta/(\gamma m)$. At $Q = -c$:
$\beta = 2c\sinh^2(\alpha/a_{\max})$, $1/(G\beta a')\approx a_{\max}^2/(2Gc\alpha^2)$,
$w\approx2c\alpha^3/(ma_{\max}^2)$. (ii)(a)
$\frac{\dd}{\dd t}B(\bar w,w) = -\dot\eta\cdot(\bar w - w) = \nabla J(w)\cdot(\bar w - w)/m\le(J(\bar w) - J(w))/m\le0$
by convexity, also for $\bar w$ on $K$'s boundary (the term $B_i(0,\cdot) = \beta_i/(\gamma m)$ has
$\dd(\beta_i/(\gamma m))/\dd t = w_ig_i$, the same formula); $J$ decreases
($\dd J/\dd t = -\nabla J^\T\operatorname{diag}(mD)\nabla J/m$), and integrating gives the rate. (b) The sublevel sets
of $B(\bar w,\cdot)$ are bounded ($\psi$ superlinear), the cluster points lie in $S$ by (a); at a cluster point $w^1$,
$B(w^1,w(t_n))\to0$ ($B_i(w^1_i,\cdot)$ is continuous at $w^1_i$, also at $w^1_i = 0$ on a branch by (i)), and
$B(w^1,w(t))$ is nonincreasing, so $w(t)\to w^1 =: w_\infty$. Let $\bar w$ be the projection of the start onto $S$, $Z'$
the branch particles with $w_{\infty,i} = 0$; the others' $\eta$ converge. Since $\eta(t) - \eta(0) = \Phi^\T z(t)$ and
$\Phi\bar w = \Phi w_\infty = \hat y$, $\sum_i(\eta_i(t) - \eta_i(0))(\bar w_i - w_{\infty,i}) = 0$ at every $t$; a
term with $i\in Z'$ and $\bar w_i\ne0$ (so $s_i\bar w_i > 0$) would tend to $-\infty$ while the others converge: so
$\bar w$ vanishes on $Z'$ too. On the remaining coordinates $\eta_\infty - \eta(0)$ lies in the closed range of their
transposed design and $\Phi w_\infty = \hat y$: the KKT conditions of the strictly convex projection onto the face
$S\cap\{w_{Z'} = 0\}$, which contains $\bar w$; so $w_\infty = \bar w$. (c) The projection puts no branch coefficient at
0 unless all of $S$ does ($\psi_i'\to-s_i\infty$ at 0: moving toward a point of $S$ with $s_iw_i > 0$ lowers the
objective at an infinite rate), so $Z'$ is the set $Z$ of coordinates vanishing on $S$. For $i\in Z$,
$s_ig_i(\infty)\le0$ (the KKT conditions of $\min_KJ$); if it is $< 0$, $\eta_i = g_i(\infty)t + o(t)$ and by (i)
$\abs{w_i}\propto\beta_i$ decays at the rate $\gamma a_{0,i}\abs{g_i(\infty)}$. (iii) With
$\beta_i(0) = \varepsilon b_i$ the start approaches the branch point and $Q_i$ converges, so $\psi_i$ converges uniformly
on compacts of $K$ and $\eta_i(0) = -s_i\log(1/\varepsilon)/(\gamma a_{0,i}) + O(1)$: the term $-\eta_i(0)w_i$ is
$\log(1/\varepsilon)\abs{w_i}/(\gamma a_{0,i}) + O(\abs{w_i})$; the leading term is coercive, so the minimisers stay
bounded and converge to its minimiser on $S$ ($\Gamma$-convergence); divided by $\log(1/\varepsilon)$ the objective is
the linear program plus $1/\log(1/\varepsilon)$ times a term that is an entropy at 0 (by (i)). The rate:
$\eta_j(w) = s_j(1/(\gamma a_{0,j}))\log\abs{w_j} + C_j + O(\abs{w_j})$ near the branch point and
$\eta_j(0) = -s_j\log(1/\varepsilon)/(\gamma a_{0,j}) + O(1)$; the support's equations
$\Phi_S^\T z = \eta_S(w_\infty) - \eta_S(0)$, with $\eta_S(w_\infty) = O(1)$ at the vertex, give
$z = \log(1/\varepsilon)\xi + O(1)$, so off the support
$\log\abs{w_j} = \log\abs{w_j(0)} + \gamma a_{0,j}s_j(\Phi^\T z)_j + O(1) = -\mu_j\log(1/\varepsilon) + O(1)$
($\gamma a_{0,j}\rho_j = 1 + O(\varepsilon)$). The free particles: \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(ii), their
$\eta = O(\log(1/\varepsilon))$ deep in the core where $w\approx G(\beta^0)^2\eta/m$. (iv) The common increment is
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(v); the conjugate of an atom's $W$ as a function of $\Delta$ is the sum of its
particles' ($\dd W/\dd\Delta = \sum_jmD_j$). With $w_+ = ue^{\gamma a_0\Delta}$, $w_- = -ue^{-\gamma a_0\Delta}$ near the
branch points (by (i)), $W = 2u\sinh(\gamma a_0\Delta)$,
$\psi'_{\mathrm{pair}}(W) = \Delta = \operatorname{asinh}(W/(2u))/(\gamma a_0)$, and one integration gives
$\psi_{\mathrm{pair}}$. (v) If $h = \Phi^\T v > 0$, every point of $K\cap\{\Phi w = y\}$ has $h^\T w = v^\T y$, so
$\abs w_1\le v^\T y/\min_ih_i$; when $w^*$ is its only point, $\min_KJ = 0$ is attained only there, $S = \{w^*\}$, and
(ii) applies ($B(w^*,w(0)) < \infty$ by (i)). With the certificate, $v^\T y = h^\T w^* = 0$, so
$\sum_{i\notin\supp}h_iw_i = v^\T(\Phi w - y)\le\abs v\abs{\Phi w - y}$, and (ii)(a) gives
$\abs{\Phi w - y}^2 = 2NJ/m\le2NB(w^*,w(0))/t$. The certificate for Gaussian translates is the Chebyshev-system argument
(after Karlin): a nonnegative $\sum_jv_j\varphi(x_j - \cdot)$ vanishing exactly at the target's $k$ atoms exists when
$N\ge2k + 1$. (vi) \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(v)'s computation with $B_i(0,\cdot) = \beta_i/(\gamma m)$:
$\dd(\beta_i/(\gamma m))/\dd t = w_ie\varphi_i(x) = -\dot\eta_i(0 - w_i)$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i), (iv)'s conjugate and (vi) exact identities; (ii) proved for every start; (iii) the limit and the
off-support rate $\varepsilon^{\mu_j}$ at an $N$-atom vertex proved, the $O(1)$ computed and checked; (iv)'s hyperbolic
entropy at leading order near the branch points; (v) proved, the uniqueness for Gaussian translates cited and its
certificate computed by linear programming on the instances, the observed rate ($t^{-0.9}$--$t^{-1}$) and the margins'
distribution numerical. Classical: exponentiated gradient and the implicit bias of mirror descent (Kivinen--Warmuth;
Gunasekar--Lee--Soudry--Srebro), Hessian--Riemannian gradient flows on constrained domains and their limits
(Alvarez--Bolte--Brahic), nonnegative least squares by overparametrised gradient flow (Chou--Maly--Verdun et al.), the
$u^2 - v^2$ parametrisation and the hyperbolic entropy (Woodworth et al.; Ghai--Hazan--Singer;
Va\v{s}kevi\v{c}ius--Kanade--Rebeschini), the uniqueness of sparse nonnegative solutions (Donoho--Tanner;
Bruckstein--Elad--Zibulevsky), nonnegative super-resolution without separation (Schiebinger--Robeva--Recht; Eftekhari et
al.), the exponential penalty in linear programming (Cominetti--San Mart\'in; Weed), the implicit bias of reparametrised
linear models as a Bregman divergence (Chou--Maly--St\"oger). (ii) is Alvarez--Bolte--Brahic's convergence with the range
argument; the inconsistent case with extinction a modest corollary of it; (iv) is the $u^2 - v^2$ parametrisation's;
(v)'s identifiability is Eftekhari et al.'s (\cite{S74}). New: the paradigm's own law is an exponentiated gradient on
its branches,
with the temperature $1/(\gamma a_0)$ read off the conservation law, so that a small start freezes the signs ---
completing \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45}(iii), whose branches were numerical ---;
$B(0,w) = \beta/(\gamma m)$; the two rich limits' temperatures and rates ($\varepsilon^{\mu_j}$ on branches against
$\varepsilon^{3/2}$ from $\alpha = 0$) and the mixed start's two scales; and, on positive kernel dictionaries, a
