scale-independent selection whose speed is the certificate's margin.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.47 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/mirror_branch.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_mirror_branch.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/mirror_branch.md}; particles on branches
represented without cancellation near their branch points, \code{mirror_free.ParticleSet}). (A)
$\dd\eta/\dd\log\abs w = a/(\gamma a^2 + G\beta a'^2)$ holds to $2.3\cdot10^{-9}$ (finite differences, 40 random states
free and on branches); at the branch point the temperature equals $1/(\gamma a_0)$ to ten digits ($\alpha_0 = 0.05$,
0.3, 1, 2); $B(\bar w,w)\to\beta/(\gamma m)$ as $\bar w\to0$ ($2\cdot10^{-4}$ relative at $\bar\delta = 10^{-6}$,
$5\cdot10^{-10}$ at $10^{-12}$, all digits shown at $10^{-30}$ and $10^{-100}$); with $\alpha(0)$ uniform on
$[-0.3,0.3]$ the fraction of starts on branches is 0.52, 0.95, 0.995 and 0.9996 at $\beta(0) = 10^{-3}$, $10^{-5}$,
$10^{-7}$, $10^{-9}$; a $\pm$ pair's coefficient follows $2u\sinh(\gamma a_0\Delta)$ to $2\cdot10^{-5}$ for
$\Delta\le10$ ($6\cdot10^{-4}$ at $\Delta = 120$, where the members leave their branch points); and on the critical
curve $\eta\cdot w^{1/3}\to-K = -3.218$ ($-3.2182$ at $\alpha = 10^{-4}$). (B) On random $6\times12$ Gaussian designs
(the target on four atoms with signs $+$, $-$, $+$, $-$; masses $10^{-3}$, amplitudes 0.2--0.4; 8 seeds for each of
three sign patterns) the flow at $t = 3\cdot10^7$ is the Bregman projection onto $S$ computed independently, to
$5.0\cdot10^{-10}$ at most; where the frozen signs cannot fit $y$ (5 of 8 positive starts, 2 of 8 random ones;
$\abs{y - \hat y}$ up to 2.25, 7--8 coordinates vanishing on $S$) $J$ reaches its sign-constrained minimum (to rounding)
and the wrong-signed particles go extinct --- their masses below $10^{-103}$ ---, $B(\bar w,w(t))$ never increases (by
more than $2.2\cdot10^{-16}$), and the extinction rates fitted where $10^{-250} < \beta < 10^{-12}$ match
$\gamma a_0\abs{g(\infty)}$ to 0.5--10\% (a fit over a finite window of an exponential with a slowly converging
prefactor); the mixed starts (half the particles free) fit exactly. Where the least-squares set is not a point ---
$5\times4$ designs with every atom carried by two particles, $y$ random, one atom's frozen sign against its
unconstrained least-squares coefficient (8 seeds; 2--6 coordinates vanishing on $S$ on 7 of them, negative coefficients
alive on 6) --- the flow at $t = 3\cdot10^7$ is the Bregman projection to $6.4\cdot10^{-10}$ at most, while the
projection lies $1.6\cdot10^{-4}$--0.20 from the equal split of the duplicates (another point of $S$): the selection
with extinction is the Bregman one, not the symmetric one. (C) The rich limits on one $8\times20$ design: from branches
(amplitudes 0.2--0.6, masses $\varepsilon$) the projection tends to the sign-constrained basis pursuit weighted by
$1/\abs{a(0)}$ --- distances 0.23, 0.030, $1.3\cdot10^{-3}$, $5.1\cdot10^{-6}$, $1.1\cdot10^{-9}$ at
$\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$, $10^{-8}$, $10^{-16}$, $10^{-32}$, $10^{-64}$ ($6\cdot10^{-13}$ at $10^{-128}$, the solver's
floor) ---, the leading order from the certificate (slacks $\mu_{\min} = 0.104$, then 0.130, 0.187) agreeing with it to
$4.1\cdot10^{-4}$, $2.4\cdot10^{-9}$, $3.4\cdot10^{-14}$ from $\varepsilon = 10^{-16}$ on: the approach
$\varepsilon^{\mu_j}$; from $\alpha(0) = 0$ to (unweighted) basis pursuit --- 0.29, 0.14, $4.0\cdot10^{-4}$,
$5.2\cdot10^{-8}$, $4.3\cdot10^{-11}$ at $\varepsilon = 10^{-2}$, \dots, $10^{-10}$ ---, the leading order agreeing to
$3.5\cdot10^{-6}$ at $10^{-6}$ and $8.4\cdot10^{-13}$ at $10^{-8}$, the closed form off the support to 0.19 and 0.017 at
$10^{-8}$ and $10^{-10}$ (its certificate's slack 0.016 is small): the approach $\varepsilon^{3/2}$. With the even atoms
free and the odd ones on branches the free particles carry $2.5\cdot10^{-6}$, $7.8\cdot10^{-14}$, $7.8\cdot10^{-22}$
($\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$, $10^{-8}$, $10^{-12}$: $\approx\varepsilon^2$) and the projection is the branch atoms' own
sign-constrained basis pursuit to $2\cdot10^{-6}$, $1\cdot10^{-12}$, $2\cdot10^{-14}$; with the odd atoms' frozen signs
against the truth's (no sign-constrained fit of their own) the free particles enter on the power scale and carry 2.27 at
$\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$ and $10^{-8}$, the limit's own (basis pursuit over the free atoms, the branch atoms
sign-constrained and free of charge), at the distances $4.1\cdot10^{-4}$ and $2.5\cdot10^{-11}$ --- at $10^{-12}$ the
projection fails (residual 1.95; reported as failed, C's one projection with a residual above the tolerance). Below
$\varepsilon\approx10^{-32}$ the float Newton step stalls --- the curvatures of particles near extinction fall below the
float Hessian's rounding --- and the decimal-arithmetic fallback converges in 37--55 steps. (D) A $\pm$ pair per atom
(amplitudes $\pm0.4$, equal masses, $u = w(0)$): the atom's coefficient tends to basis pursuit weighted by $1/a_0$ ---
distances 0.29, 0.21, 0.12, 0.020, 0.011, $2.6\cdot10^{-4}$, $1.9\cdot10^{-6}$ at $u = 10^{-4}$, $10^{-8}$,
$10^{-16}$, $10^{-32}$, $10^{-64}$, $10^{-128}$, $10^{-256}$ ---, slowly, its certificate's slacks being small (0.016,
0.020), the leading order agreeing to $1.4\cdot10^{-6}$ at $10^{-128}$ and $5.7\cdot10^{-11}$ at $10^{-256}$; never are
both members of an atom active. (E) Positive kernel dictionaries (Gaussian atoms of one width on a grid of $[-1,1]$,
samples on $[-1.1,1.1]$, the target 1.0, 0.7, 0.5 on two adjacent atoms and one more, positive branches with
$\alpha(0) = 1$ or 2; the showcase targets' margins above 89--94\% of all three-atom supports'): with 21 atoms of width
0.15 and 7 samples (margin $\mu = 0.015$) the loop reaches the target from every start scale, $\beta(0) = 10^{-6}$ to
10 --- within $6.0\cdot10^{-5}$ and on its three atoms at $t = 10^9$, the other atoms' mass at most $5.6\cdot10^{-5}$
(the certificate's bound 0.03--0.09) ---, while from $\alpha(0) = 0$ the rich start ($\beta(0) = 10^{-6}$) ends on a
seven-atom exact fit 1 away from the target (as is equal-weight basis pursuit) and the lazy one ($\beta(0) = 100$)
spreads over all 21 atoms; with 41 atoms and 7 samples ($\mu = 0.003$) the selection is slow ---
$1.0$--$1.3\cdot10^{-3}$ away at $t = 10^9$ on $[10, 11, 29, 30, 31]$, the target's third atom shared with its
neighbours ---; with 41 atoms of width 0.1 and 11 samples ($\mu = 0.027$) every positive start ends on the target's
atoms ($\le2.3\cdot10^{-5}$ at $10^9$), as does the rich free start here, where basis pursuit recovers the target. The
decay is algebraic ($t^{-0.9}$--$t^{-1}$ between $10^7$ and $10^9$). Typical targets are another matter: over all
three-atom supports the margins' 10, 25, 50, 75, 90\% quantiles are 0, $8.4\cdot10^{-10}$, $4.0\cdot10^{-6}$,
$2.0\cdot10^{-3}$, 0.015 (21 atoms, width 0.15), 0, 0, $3.8\cdot10^{-9}$, $7.0\cdot10^{-6}$, $8.1\cdot10^{-4}$ (41
atoms, width 0.15) and $1.9\cdot10^{-7}$, $2.8\cdot10^{-5}$, $1.2\cdot10^{-3}$, 0.014, 0.026 (41 atoms, width 0.1) ---
25\%, 45\% and 4\% at most $10^{-9}$, numerically without a certificate ---, and from a positive start
($\alpha(0) = 1$, $\beta(0) = 10^{-2}$) the loop reaches the supports at the quantiles whose margins exceed $10^{-3}$ by
$t = 10^9$ (to $2.3\cdot10^{-3}$ and $5.3\cdot10^{-5}$; $6.0\cdot10^{-3}$, $1.2\cdot10^{-4}$ and $2.1\cdot10^{-5}$; at
the margin $8.1\cdot10^{-4}$ it is $3.6\cdot10^{-2}$ away, on its way), while on those below $10^{-4}$ it is still
0.37--0.83 away at $10^9$, hardly moving from $10^5$ on, with 0.45--1.33 of mass on other atoms: the selection's speed
is the margin. (F) The real loop on positive branches ($k_e = 5$, $G = \gamma = 1$, twelve atoms of width 0.25, the
target on atoms $[2, 6, 9]$, $\alpha(0) = 0.5$, $\beta(0) = 10^{-3}$): $\dot V = -k_ee^2$ holds to $1.7\cdot10^{-8}$
at random states, $V$ falls monotonically from 3.64 (largest by quarters 3.64, 2.67, 1.72, 0.87), the rms error from
0.014 to 0.009 --- while the masses on the unused atoms rise (largest 0.0055, 0.031, 0.20, 0.78 by quarters): over 4000
time units the coefficients are still 0.080 from $w^*$ on this weakly exciting orbit, the unused atoms helping the fit
along it, their terms $\beta/(\gamma m)$ in $V$ paid for by the others.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.48 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d as committed at 1269272; the numbers
% are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/mirror_transit.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:48 is a \hyperref target, as
% res:thmDppp:21 is. PARADIGM's first item is labelled (o), here by an explicit item label; tau is the rescaled time,
% as in PARADIGM. Proposition D'''.51, which the text cites, is not stated in PARADIGM.md (in progress), so the citation
% is plain text.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.48}[the rich transit: from small masses the kernels enter one at a time --- the inverse
scale space flow, on the power scale $(\beta^0)^{-1/2}$ from zero amplitudes and on the logarithmic scale on branches;
with the paradigm's moving centres the kernels that have not entered stay where they started and the entered ones slide;
under a Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao metric the massless kernels condense at the correlation's peaks instead, and the count
reaches one kernel per feature only at much smaller masses]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:48}%
In the setting of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45} on a dictionary ($\Phi$ the $N\times d$ design of
full row rank, $J = (m/2N)\abs{\Phi w - y}^2$, the noiseless loop $\dd\eta = \Phi^\T(y - \Phi w)\dd t/N$):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item[\textup{(o)}] \emph{(the unit at small mass)} On the critical curve $Q = -c$ the unit is
    $w = (2c/(ma_{\max}^2))\alpha^3(1 + O(\alpha^2))$ with $\dot\alpha = (2Gc/a_{\max}^2)\alpha^2g(1 + O(\alpha^2))$ --- to
    leading order the gradient flow of a depth-3 diagonal unit $u^3$ ($u\propto\alpha$). A start at $\alpha = 0$ with
    the mass $\beta^0$ lies on $Q = \beta^0 - c$, whose core $\abs\alpha\lesssim a_{\max}(\beta^0/(2c))^{1/2}$ the loop
    crosses with the jump $2\lambda_1$ of $\eta$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{Proposition D$'''$.45}(ii)):
    $\lambda_1\propto(\beta^0)^{-1/2}$ plays the depth-3 unit's $\alpha_0^{-(D-2)}$, and the remainder
    $-(3/2)K\abs w^{2/3}$ is the depth-3 potential's. On a branch ($Q < -c$) the amplitude is frozen near $a_0$ and the
    unit is the exponentiated gradient at the temperature $1/(\gamma a_0)$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{Proposition
    D$'''$.47}(i)) --- the depth-2 behaviour. The scale is set by the start's amplitude against its mass,
    $\theta = \abs{\alpha(0)}/(\beta^0/(2c))^{1/2}$: $\theta\to0$ (in particular $\alpha(0) = 0$) gives the power scale
    $(\beta^0)^{-1/2}$, a fixed amplitude the logarithmic scale $\log(1/\beta^0)/(\gamma a(0))$, and in between there
    is a continuum --- $\alpha(0)\propto\varepsilon^p$ with $0 < p < \frac12$ and $\beta^0\propto\varepsilon$ give
    $\varepsilon^{-p}\log(1/\varepsilon)$, and a branch start with $\alpha(0)\lesssim\varepsilon^{1/2}$ is still on the
    power scale.
  \item \emph{(from $\alpha = 0$: the inverse scale space flow on the power scale)} Let $\alpha_i(0) = 0$,
    $\beta^0_i = \varepsilon b_i$, $\Lambda(\varepsilon) = (\pi a_{\max}/(4G))(2/(c\varepsilon))^{1/2}$,
    $\rho_i = b_i^{-1/2}$ (so $\lambda_{1,i} = \Lambda\rho_i(1 + O(\varepsilon))$), $\tau = t/\Lambda$ and
    $u_i(\tau) = \eta_i(\Lambda\tau)/\Lambda$. As $\varepsilon\to0$, $u^\varepsilon\to u$ uniformly on compacts of
    $[0,\infty)$ and $\Phi w^\varepsilon\to\Phi w$ in $L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}$ ($w^\varepsilon\to w$ in
    $L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}$ on the intervals where the active columns are independent), where $(u,w)$ is the inverse scale
    space flow of $R(w) = \sum_i\rho_i\abs{w_i}$,
    \[
      \frac{\dd u}{\dd\tau} = \Phi^\T(y - \Phi w)/N,\qquad u(0) = 0,\qquad
      w(\tau) = \operatorname{argmin}\{\abs{\Phi v - y} : v\in\mathcal N(u(\tau))\},
    \]
    $\mathcal N(u) = \{v : v_i = 0\ \text{where}\ \abs{u_i} < \rho_i,\ u_iv_i\ge0\ \text{where}\ \abs{u_i} = \rho_i\}$
    the box's normal cone --- the adaptive inverse scale space path (Burger--M\"oller--Benning--Osher): $u$ piecewise
    linear, $w$ piecewise constant; an atom enters when $\abs{u_i}$ reaches $\rho_i$ (the first at
    $\tau_1 = \min_i\rho_iN/\abs{(\Phi^\T y)_i}$) and leaves when its least-squares coefficient vanishes with an inward
    velocity; the path reaches weighted basis pursuit at a finite $\tau^*$. The loop reaches the rich limit's support at
    $t\approx\Lambda\tau^*\propto(\beta^0)^{-1/2}$ (its last digits later, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(iii)).
  \item \emph{(on branches: the logarithmic scale)} Let $\alpha_i(0)\ne0$ with the signs $s_i$ and the masses
    $\varepsilon b_i$ (on branches for small $\varepsilon$, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{D$'''$.47}),
    $\rho_i = 1/(\gamma\abs{a_i(0)})$, $\ell = \log(1/\varepsilon)$, $\tau = t/\ell$ and
    $u_i = (\eta_i - \eta_i(0))/\ell$. As $\varepsilon\to0$ the same convergence holds with the one-sided boxes
    $s_iu_i\le\rho_i$: the inverse scale space flow of $\sum_i\rho_i\abs{w_i} + \chi\{s_iw_i\ge0\ \forall i\}$, ending at
    the sign-constrained weighted basis pursuit of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{D$'''$.47}(iii) when $y$ has a fit with the
    frozen signs; $u$ converges like $1/\log(1/\varepsilon)$. A $\pm$ pair per atom
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{D$'''$.47}(iv)) gives the two-sided box with the weights $1/(\gamma a_0)$ ---
    Pesme--Flammarion's depth-2 limit. The rate $1/\log(1/\varepsilon)$ keeps this limit asymptotic: at
    $\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$ the dual is still 40--80\% of the weights away and the entry times are off by more than the
    first entry.
  \item \emph{(moving centres, the paradigm's metric: the sliding transit)} Let the kernels' centres move by the
    paradigm's law (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{Proposition D$'''$.34}'s positions, Euclidean, the mass in the drift),
    $\dot c_i = G\beta_ia_i\langle r\partial_c\varphi_i\rangle = Gmw_i\langle r\partial_c\varphi_i\rangle$, the free law
    on $(\alpha,\beta)$ unchanged ($Q$ kept for any drive). (a) A kernel whose dual coordinate stays inside its box by
    the margin $\mu$, $\abs{u_i}\le(1 - \mu)\rho_i$ on $[0,\tau]$, has the coefficient of the core's profile,
    $w_i = s_i(a_{\max}(\varepsilon b_i)^{3/2}/(m(2c)^{1/2}))\cos(\pi\mu/2)/\sin^3(\pi\mu/2)
    (1 + O(\varepsilon^{1/2}/\mu))$ ($s_i$ the sign of $u_i$; at most the same with $\mu^{-3}$,
    $\cos(\pi\mu/2)/\sin^3(\pi\mu/2)\le(8/\pi^3)\mu^{-3}(1 + O(\mu^2))$), and its centre creeps at
    \[
      \frac{\dd c_i}{\dd\tau} = \frac{\pi a_{\max}^2}{4c}\varepsilon b_i^{3/2}\cdot
      \frac{\cos(\pi\mu/2)}{\sin^3(\pi\mu/2)}\cdot s_i\langle r\partial_c\varphi_i\rangle\cdot
      \big(1 + O(\varepsilon^{1/2}/\mu)\big),
    \]
    toward a larger correlation: in the limit the kernels that have not entered stay where they are --- at their
    starts, or where they left the active block ---, their dual coordinates integrating the residual's correlation
    there. The limit is not uniform in the margins: a kernel that loses a near-tie lingers inside its wall at a small
    margin $\mu$, so its position stays frozen only while $\varepsilon\ll\mu^3$ --- at a finite $\varepsilon$ it may
    slide toward the residual's peak and enter in place of the predicted kernel. (b) Between entries the active block
    relaxes on the fast time $t = O(1)$ --- on the critical curve, by an $\varepsilon$-independent flow --- to a
    stationary point of $J$ over its coefficients and centres. The limit is the sliding inverse scale space:
    $u_j(\tau) = u_j(\tau_k) + (\tau - \tau_k)\langle r_k\varphi(\cdot;c_j)\rangle$ for the inactive kernels ($c_j$
    where it is), entry at $\abs{u_j} = \rho_j$ --- kernels reaching their walls together (a tie, e.g.\ by symmetry)
    entering together ---, the block's relaxation, an exit where a coefficient vanishes with an inward correlation; no
    kernel enters once the block fits the target exactly. A tie shares a feature: a bump midway between two grid kernels
    is formed by both, at its centre, with half its amplitude each, at every $\varepsilon$. Generically (no ties), a
    target of $k$ separated bumps of the kernels' width is formed by $k$ kernels at the bumps' centres, the others
    keeping their starting positions and negligible masses --- in the limit $\varepsilon\to0$ at fixed margins. At a
    finite $\varepsilon$ the margins the entries leave decide (Proposition D$'''$.51): a runner-up whose margin at an
    entry is $O(\varepsilon^{1/2})$ is pulled across its wall by the entrant's overshoot and shares the feature, and a
    kernel lingering near its wall in an unfitted residual creeps into it unless $\varepsilon\ll\mu^4$; so near a tie (a
    bump 0.001 off the midpoint) the loop forms an extra kernel down to $\varepsilon\approx10^{-6}$, and close bumps'
    next kernel, creeping toward the residual's peak while it waits, enters earlier than on the path. A target that is
    not a few kernels of the dictionary's width (bumps wider than the kernels) is fitted along a staircase: the kernels
    enter one at a time at roughly geometrically growing times (the next entry waits $\propto1/\abs r$), the residual
    falling at each entry.
  \item \emph{(moving centres, a Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao metric: condensation)} With the positions weighted by the
    masses ($\dot c_i = Ga_i\langle r\partial_c\varphi_i\rangle$, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:34]{D$'''$.34}(i)'s alternative)
    an inactive kernel's amplitude is $a\approx a_{\max}(\varepsilon b_i/(2c))^{1/2}\tan(\pi u_i/(2\rho_i))$ and its
    centre moves at $O(1)$ in $\tau$: before the first entry the limit is
    \[
      \frac{\dd u_i}{\dd\tau} = \langle f^*\varphi(\cdot;c_i)\rangle,\qquad
      \frac{\dd c_i}{\dd\tau} = \frac{\pi a_{\max}^2}{4c\rho_i}\tan\Big(\frac{\pi u_i}{2\rho_i}\Big)
      \langle f^*\partial_c\varphi(\cdot;c_i)\rangle,
    \]
    the kernels climbing the correlation's peaks, faster as they near the wall: the massless kernels condense at the
    peaks before entering. In the limit the count is still one kernel per feature --- the condensed kernels reach their
    walls at different times (their dual coordinates integrate the correlation along different paths), so generically
    only one enters per feature ---, but the condensed kernels are near-ties at small margins, so at a finite
    $\varepsilon$ several enter with each feature, and the count falls to one per feature only at much smaller masses
    than under the paradigm's metric.
  \item \emph{(moving widths)} With the widths moving by the same position law
    ($\dot\sigma_i = G\beta_ia_i\langle r\partial_\sigma\varphi_i\rangle$, the paradigm's full kernel law) the sliding
    transit fits the features' widths as well: bumps wider or narrower than the kernels' starting width are formed one
    per bump, at their centres, widths and amplitudes, where fixed widths spread a wider bump over several kernels and
    fit a narrower one with the wrong amplitude.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(o) With $x = \alpha/a_{\max}$, $2c\sinh^2x\cdot a_{\max}\tanh x/m = (2ca_{\max}/m)x^3(1 + O(x^2))$ and
$\beta = 2c\sinh^2x$, $a' = 1 + O(x^2)$; with $u = \kappa\alpha$, $\kappa^3 = 2c/(ma_{\max}^2)$: $w = u^3$ and
$\dot u = (2Gc/(\kappa a_{\max}^2))u^2g$, the depth-3 unit's gradient flow $3u^2\cdot hg$ with the step
$h = 2Gc/(3\kappa a_{\max}^2)$. (i) In the dual variable $\zeta$ ($u = \Phi^\T\zeta$) the rescaled loop is
$\dd\zeta/\dd\tau = -\nabla\mathcal D_\varepsilon(\zeta)/N$,
$\mathcal D_\varepsilon(\zeta) = \sum_ig_{\varepsilon,i}((\Phi^\T\zeta)_i) - y^\T\zeta$,
$g_{\varepsilon,i}(v) = \psi^*_i(\Lambda v)/\Lambda$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(iii)'s dual flow in $\tau$). In
the core $\alpha = a_{\max}(\beta^0/(2c))^{1/2}\tan(\pi\eta/(2\lambda_1))(1 + O(\beta^0))$ and
$\psi^* = 2c\sinh^2(\alpha/a_{\max})/(\gamma m)$, so $g_{\varepsilon,i}(v) = O(\varepsilon^{3/2}\delta^{-2})$ uniformly
on $\abs v\le(1 - \delta)\rho_i$; beyond the wall $\alpha\ge2Gc(\eta - \lambda_1)$ exactly ($\arctan\le\pi/2$), so
$g_{\varepsilon,i}(v)\ge C\Lambda^{-1}e^{(4Gc/a_{\max})\Lambda\delta\rho_i}$ on $\abs v\ge(1 + \delta)\rho_i$. So each
$g_{\varepsilon,i}$ converges to the indicator of $[-\rho_i,\rho_i]$ uniformly on the compacts off $\pm\rho_i$; for the
composition, a compact set of $\R^N\setminus P$ lies at a positive distance from $P$, so
$\max_i\abs{(\Phi^\T\zeta)_i}/\rho_i\ge1 + \delta$ on it and, every $g_{\varepsilon,j}$ being nonnegative,
$\mathcal D_\varepsilon\to+\infty$ uniformly there, while on the compacts of $\operatorname{int}P$
$\mathcal D_\varepsilon\to-y^\T\zeta$ uniformly:
$\mathcal D_\varepsilon\to\mathcal D_\infty(\zeta) = \chi_P(\zeta) - y^\T\zeta$, $P = \{\abs{\Phi^\T\zeta}\le\rho\}$,
epigraphically (convex functions on $\R^N$, $0\in\operatorname{int}\operatorname{dom}\mathcal D_\infty$:
Rockafellar--Wets, Thm.~7.17) --- in finite dimension, Mosco convergence. Attouch's theorem turns it into the graph
convergence of the subdifferentials and Brezis--Pazy's into the convergence of the semigroups, uniformly on compacts,
from $\zeta(0) = 0\in\operatorname{int}P$ --- the route Berthier--Pillaud-Vivien take for mirror flows started near the
boundary of a fixed potential's domain; here the potential itself changes with the start. The energy identity
$\mathcal D_\varepsilon(\zeta(T)) + N\int_0^T\abs{\dot\zeta}^2 = \mathcal D_\varepsilon(0) = 0$
($\dd\zeta/\dd\tau = -\nabla\mathcal D_\varepsilon/N$), with the liminf inequality at $\zeta(T)$, makes
$\dot\zeta^\varepsilon\to\dot\zeta$ in $L^2(0,T)$, i.e.\ $\Phi w^\varepsilon\to\Phi w$; the limit's derivative is the
minimal-norm section, $-N^{-1}(\Phi v - y)$ with $v$ the least-squares element of $\mathcal N(u)$; and
$w^\varepsilon\to w$ on the compact subintervals between events where the active columns are independent and the
inactive atoms' margins are bounded below. Its structure --- piecewise linear, finite termination at weighted basis
pursuit --- is the adaptive inverse scale space method's (Burger et al.). (ii) The same with
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{D$'''$.47}(i)'s entropy at the branch point:
$\psi^*_i(\eta_i(0) + \ell v) - \psi^*_i(\eta_i(0))
= (\abs{w_i(0)}/(\gamma a_{0,i}))(e^{\gamma a_{0,i}\ell s_iv} - 1)(1 + o(1))$ near the branch point, so
$g_{\varepsilon,i}(v)\to0$ for $s_iv < \rho_i$ (uniformly on compacts, like
$e^{-\ell(1 - \gamma a_{0,i}s_iv)}/\ell$) and $\to+\infty$ for $s_iv > \rho_i$; the $O(1)$ in $\eta_i(0)$ gives $u$'s
$1/\ell$; the pair: \hyperref[res:thmDppp:47]{D$'''$.47}(iv)'s infimal convolution. (iii)(a) At
$\eta = (1 - \mu)\lambda_1$ the core's tangent profile gives \hyperref[res:thmDppp:45]{D$'''$.45}(iii)'s off-support
formula, with $\cos(\pi\mu/2)/\sin^3(\pi\mu/2) = (2/(\pi\mu))^3(1 + O(\mu^2))$; then
$\dot c_i = Gmw_i\langle r\partial_c\varphi_i\rangle$ and $\dd c_i/\dd\tau = \Lambda\dot c_i$, with
$\Lambda Gm\cdot a_{\max}(\varepsilon b_i)^{3/2}/(m(2c)^{1/2}) = (\pi a_{\max}^2/(4c))\varepsilon b_i^{3/2}$: the
creep's speed. (b) Formal: the active block's mobility on the critical curve is $mD = 3\abs w^{4/3}/K$ (from
$\eta = \lambda_1 - K\abs w^{-1/3}$), independent of $\varepsilon$, while the inactive kernels' and the entrants' times
are $O(\Lambda)$. (iv) In the core $a\approx\alpha$, and
$\Lambda G\cdot a_{\max}(\varepsilon b/(2c))^{1/2} = \pi a_{\max}^2\sqrt b/(4c)$, $\sqrt b = 1/\rho$.
\end{proof}
\status{(o) exact expansions; (i) and (ii) proved (Mosco convergence of the explicit dual potentials, the convergence of
the semigroups; the path's structure classical), $u$'s rates numerical; (iii)(a) proved (the profile to leading order in
$\varepsilon^{1/2}/\mu$), (iii)(b) formal (two time scales; the block's relaxation to a stationary point assumed; ties
and near ties as stated), the sliding path and the recovery of the bumps numerical; (iv) the inactive kernels' limit
derived to leading order and checked along the trajectory, with unequal masses; the count as $\varepsilon\to0$
numerical; (v) numerical. Classical: the inverse scale space flow and its adaptive algorithm (Burger--Gilboa--Osher--Xu;
Burger--M\"oller--Benning--Osher), linearised Bregman iterations and differential inclusions
(Yin--Osher--Goldfarb--Darbon; Osher--Ruan--Xiong--Yao--Yin), saddle-to-saddle dynamics and incremental learning of
diagonal linear networks at vanishing initialisation and their identification with the adaptive inverse scale space
method (Pesme--Flammarion; Berthier; Gissin--Shalev-Shwartz--Daniely), inverse scale space limits of mirror flows by
Mosco convergence (Berthier--Pillaud-Vivien), depth-$D$ potentials and the polynomial scales of deep units (Woodworth et
al.; Jacot et al.; Chou--Maly--Rauhut's initialisation-weighted $\ell^1$), Mosco convergence and the convergence of
gradient flows (Mosco; Attouch; Brezis--Pazy), sliding Frank--Wolfe and ADCG (Denoyelle--Duval--Peyr\'e--Soubies;
Boyd--Schiebinger--Recht), conic particle gradient descent (Chizat), condensation and early alignment at small
initialisation (Luo et al.; Zhou et al.; Boursier--Pillaud-Vivien--Flammarion; Min--Mallada--Vidal; Kumar--Haupt),
alternating gradient flows and unbalanced initialisations' rapid feature learning (Kunin et al.), deep diagonal networks
as mirror flows (Labarri\`ere et al.), the quantisation of features at small initialisation (Maennel--Bousquet--Gelly).
(i) follows Berthier--Pillaud-Vivien's route for a family of potentials that changes with the start, and (ii) is their
entropic case (\cite{S75}). New: the paradigm's free mass--amplitude unit at small mass as an effective depth-3 unit
with an explicit
potential, its transit scale $\Lambda(\varepsilon)\propto(\beta^0)^{-1/2}$ and the continuum of scales set by
$\alpha(0)/(\beta^0)^{1/2}$; with the paradigm's moving centres (and widths) the sliding transit --- the inactive kernels
frozen by the mass in the position law's drift, with the creep's exact profile and its non-uniformity
$\varepsilon\ll\mu^3$, ties sharing a feature ---, answering which kernels form from a spread cloud (paper 13's open
question for the cohesion leak) at small masses: one per feature, at its centre and width; and what the metric on the
positions decides: not the limit's count (one per feature under both) but where the massless kernels go --- they stay put
under the paradigm's metric, condense at the correlation's peaks under a Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao one --- and how small
the masses must be.}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.48 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/mirror_transit.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_mirror_transit.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/mirror_transit.md}; $m = 12$, $G = 1$,
$\gamma = 0.1$, $a_{\max} = 1$, $c = 0.025$; each kernel integrated on its own curve --- $\alpha$ alone,
$\beta = \varepsilon b_i + 2c\sinh^2(\alpha/a_{\max})$ ---, so that $Q$ is kept exactly). (A) From $\alpha = 0$ on three
$6\times14$ Gaussian designs (3-sparse truths, masses $\varepsilon b_i$, $b_i$ log-uniform in $[1/4,4]$) the adaptive
inverse scale space path of $\sum\abs{w_i}/\sqrt{b_i}$ ends at weighted basis pursuit (to $5\cdot10^{-16}$) after 3, 8
and 10 events (entries and exits); the loop in $\tau = t/\Lambda(\varepsilon)$ follows it:
$\sup_\tau\abs{u - u_{\mathrm{ISS}}}$ falls from 0.54, 0.61, 1.15 at $\varepsilon = 10^{-2}$ to $6.9\cdot10^{-4}$,
$8.9\cdot10^{-4}$, $1.7\cdot10^{-3}$ at $10^{-8}$ and $6.7\cdot10^{-6}$, $8.8\cdot10^{-6}$, $2.0\cdot10^{-5}$ at
$10^{-12}$ --- log-log slopes 0.500 on all three between $10^{-6}$ and $10^{-8}$, 0.503, 0.501, 0.484 between $10^{-8}$
and $10^{-12}$: the rate $1/\Lambda\propto\varepsilon^{1/2}$ ---; the entry times converge at the same rate down to the
grid's step ($1.5\cdot10^{-3}$ on the first design by $\varepsilon = 10^{-8}$; 0.35 and 0.26 at $10^{-8}$ on the
others, whose late entries approach their walls slowly, their steps 0.059 and 0.044 by $10^{-10}$); the residual's
difference $\abs{\Phi(w - w_{\mathrm{ISS}})}$ is $O(1)$ at an event over a $\tau$-width $\propto\varepsilon^{1/2}$ and
$O(\varepsilon^{3/2})$ away from the events --- its median over the samples $9.7\cdot10^{-8}$, $8.8\cdot10^{-11}$,
$8.7\cdot10^{-14}$ at $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$, $10^{-8}$, $10^{-10}$ on the first design, the slopes 1.51, 1.69, 1.46
between $10^{-6}$ and $10^{-10}$, the inactive atoms' coefficients $\propto\varepsilon^{3/2}$ ---, so its rms over $\tau$
falls like $\varepsilon^{1/4}$ (slope 0.26 between $10^{-6}$ and $10^{-8}$ on the first design) until the width drops
below the grid's step, below which the rms counts the few samples that fall within an entry; the end at $1.5\tau^*$ is
weighted basis pursuit to $8.5\cdot10^{-12}$--$6.2\cdot10^{-9}$ at $10^{-8}$ and
$1.1\cdot10^{-16}$--$6.1\cdot10^{-15}$ at $10^{-12}$. (B) On branches (amplitudes 0.2--0.6, the first design) the loop
follows the one-sided path in $\tau = t/\log(1/\varepsilon)$ --- $\sup\abs{u - u_{\mathrm{ISS}}} = 15.7$, 8.0, 4.0,
2.0, 1.0 and the entry times' largest difference 55, 28, 14, 7.0, 3.5 at $\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$, $10^{-8}$,
$10^{-16}$, $10^{-32}$, $10^{-64}$, exactly $\propto1/\log(1/\varepsilon)$ (the path's weights
$1/(\gamma\abs{a(0)})\approx17$--50) ---, ending at the sign-constrained weighted basis pursuit (to $1.3\cdot10^{-5}$ at
$\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$, below $10^{-14}$ from $10^{-16}$ on); $\pm$ pairs follow the two-sided path the same way (21,
7.9, 4.0, 2.0, 0.99 at $u = 10^{-4}$ \dots\ $10^{-64}$). (C) Moving centres (Gaussian kernels of width 0.12 on 61
samples of $[-1,1]$, 21 kernels started on a grid of spacing 0.1, the target three bumps of the same width at $-0.52$,
0.07, 0.61 with amplitudes 0.9, $-0.6$, 0.75): (1) the sliding inverse scale space enters the kernels started at
$-0.5$, 0.6 and 0.1 ($\tau = 10.72$, 12.85, 16.39), which slide onto the bumps and fit them exactly (rms residual
$1.4\cdot10^{-16}$); the loop with the paradigm's metric forms 8 kernels at $\varepsilon = 10^{-3}$ (three clusters on
the bumps), 4 at $10^{-4}$ (a fourth of coefficient $-0.013$), and exactly these three for $\varepsilon\le10^{-5}$ ---
entering at $\tau = 10.75$, 12.88, 16.41 (the grid's step 0.048), at the bumps' centres with their amplitudes (rms
residual $3.8\cdot10^{-7}$, $1.6\cdot10^{-8}$, $4.4\cdot10^{-10}$, $1.3\cdot10^{-11}$ at $\varepsilon = 10^{-5}$ \dots\
$10^{-8}$) ---, the other kernels displaced by at most 0.033, $2.6\cdot10^{-3}$, $2.3\cdot10^{-4}$, $2.3\cdot10^{-5}$
($\propto\varepsilon$, the creep) with coefficients below $2.9\cdot10^{-5}$, $4.3\cdot10^{-7}$, $1.1\cdot10^{-8}$,
$3.4\cdot10^{-10}$ ($\to\varepsilon^{3/2}$, the core's profile); (2) with the Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao metric the
kernels' centres before the first entry follow (iv)'s limit to $1.7\cdot10^{-5}$ at $\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$,
$3.4\cdot10^{-7}$ at $10^{-5}$ and $2\cdot10^{-7}$ below (the integrators' floor at their tolerance $10^{-9}$), all 21
gathered at three positions ($-0.521$, 0.068, 0.613) by $\tau = 10$ --- with unequal masses ($b_i$ log-uniform in
$[0.26,3.8]$, $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$) the limit with $\rho_i = b_i^{-1/2}$ to $1.1\cdot10^{-7}$, the one with the
masses ignored 0.45 away ---, and the loop forms 15, 12, 8, 3, 3 kernels at $\varepsilon = 10^{-4}$, $10^{-5}$,
$10^{-6}$, $10^{-8}$, $10^{-10}$, always in three clusters at the bumps (rms residual $\le6\cdot10^{-10}$): one per
feature from $\varepsilon\approx10^{-8}$; (3) with the centres held on the grid ($\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$, to
$\tau = 64$) it forms 5 kernels and is still 0.019 from the target (the grid's least-squares fit $8.4\cdot10^{-5}$); (4)
on sixteen random three-bump targets (centres in $[-0.8,0.8]$ at least 0.3 apart, amplitudes $\pm U(0.4,1)$) the
sliding path recovers all sixteen exactly with three kernels and no exits; the loop with the paradigm's metric 11, 13
and 16 of them at $\varepsilon = 10^{-5}$, $10^{-6}$, $10^{-8}$ (otherwise one or two extra kernels; the inactive
kernels' largest displacement 0.12, 0.070, 0.0055), its formed kernels those of the path, in its order, on 15 of 16 at
$10^{-8}$ (the sixteenth: (6)); with the Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao metric 4--7 kernels at $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$
(exactly three at the bumps on none), 3--5 at $10^{-8}$ (on 10 of 16) and exactly three at the bumps on all sixteen at
$10^{-10}$; with the centres held on the grid 4--6 kernels, rms residuals 0.011--0.026; (5) ties and near ties: a bump
midway between two kernels enters both together, on the path and in the loop at every $\varepsilon$, each with half its
amplitude at its centre (0.4, 0.4); a bump 0.001 off the midpoint (the runner-up's margin $\mu^* = 3.5\cdot10^{-3}$) is
formed by one kernel on the path, by two at $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$ (the runner-up with 0.062 of 0.8;
$\mu^*/\varepsilon^{1/2} = 3.5$) and by one from $10^{-8}$ on, and three bumps with one at a midpoint (the tie broken
by the other bumps' tails) by four kernels at $10^{-6}$ ($-0.446$ and $-0.154$ on the midpoint's bump) and by the path's
three from $10^{-8}$ on --- the near-tie law of Proposition D$'''$.51; two bumps 0.15 apart (1.25 widths) and 0.25
apart, off the grid, are resolved by two kernels at every $\varepsilon$, the second entering earlier than on the path
($\tau = 16.2$, 29.6, 33.1 at $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$, $10^{-8}$, $10^{-10}$ against 33.5; 12.1, 16.1, 17.0 against
17.1) --- it creeps toward the residual's peak while it waits; and a target of three bumps wider than the kernels (width
0.2), not three kernels of the dictionary, is fitted along a staircase: the path enters the kernels one at a time at
roughly geometrically growing times (21 entries from $\tau = 9.1$ to $1.2\cdot10^{10}$, its rms residual falling from
0.51 to $3.3\cdot10^{-5}$ --- 11 of its 21 relaxations stationary within the budget), and the loop at
$\varepsilon = 10^{-5}$, followed on a geometric $\tau$ grid, enters the same ten kernels first (in another order,
earlier: the creep at this mass) and ends with 16 kernels at the rms residual $2.4\cdot10^{-7}$; (6) the creep (target
10 of (4)): after the second entry an inactive kernel lingers inside its wall at a small margin $\mu$; its instantaneous
speed $\dd c/\dd\tau$ (the loop's own right-hand side) is (iii)(a)'s profile to 0.990--0.996 at
$\varepsilon = 10^{-8}$ ($\mu = 0.0039$--0.0065) and to 0.9999--1.0000 at $10^{-10}$ --- the correction
$O(\varepsilon^{1/2}/\mu)$; 0.55 at $\mu = 4.5\cdot10^{-4}$, $\varepsilon = 10^{-8}$, where
$\varepsilon^{1/2}/\mu = 0.22$ and the kernel is entering --- and a quarter of the bound's $\mu^{-3}$ form
(0.255--0.258: $8/\pi^3 = 0.258$); at $\varepsilon = 10^{-8}$ kernel 15, 0.0065 inside its wall where
$\langle r\partial_c\varphi\rangle = -0.033$, creeps by 0.16 before the third entry and enters in place of the path's
kernel 12 (the formed kernels' centres unchanged), at $10^{-10}$ it moves by $8\cdot10^{-4}$ and the loop enters the
path's kernel; a kernel left at the margin 0.0043 beside a fitted bump, where
$\langle r\partial_c\varphi\rangle\approx-10^{-5}$, moves by $3.8\cdot10^{-3}$ and $3.2\cdot10^{-5}$ in all at
$10^{-8}$ and $10^{-10}$. (D) With moving widths too ($\dot\sigma = G\beta a\langle r\partial_\sigma\varphi\rangle$,
kernels started at width 0.12) the transit forms the three bumps at widths 0.2 and 0.08 with exactly three kernels at
their centres, widths and amplitudes at $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$ (rms residual $2.3\cdot10^{-8}$ and
$1.2\cdot10^{-8}$; at $10^{-5}$ the wide bumps with a fourth kernel of coefficient 0.059), where fixed widths spread the
wide bumps over six kernels (rms residual 0.024) and fit the narrow ones by three kernels with the amplitudes 0.71,
$-0.48$, 0.59 (rms residual 0.094).
\end{sloppypar}

% Numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, third pass; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/learning_table.md).
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_chaos_learning.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/learning_table.md}). The instance:
$n = 1$, saturated features with $a_{\max} = 4$, $c_{\max} = 3$, $s\in[0.3,2]$, prior $\vartheta^0 = 0$,
$G = \operatorname{diag}(1,0.25,0.25)$; a plant of three Gaussian bumps, $(A,C,S) = (1,-1.5,0.5)$, $(-0.7,0,0.4)$,
$(0.8,1.5,0.5)$, which three kernels of weights $3A_k$ represent exactly; reference $x_m = 2\sin t$, $\rho$ on a grid
of 256 times; slow time, midpoint rule with step $0.02$; $m\in\{16,64,256\}$ sharing their particles with an
$M = 2048$ reference (the stand-in for the mean-field flow, as in Theorems D$'$ and D$''$); horizon 900 slow units;
four seeds per start. Proposition K gives $\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}} = 0.04716$ (collapsed atom $a = 0.331$,
$c = -0.057$, $s = 1.218$); the runs use $\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}} = 0.00707$ (leak time 141 slow units).
Whitened constants by search: $L_\vartheta^G = 4.044$ (closed form $4.658$), $L_2^G = 11.11$ (closed form
$\lambda_Ga_{\max}/s_{\min}^2 = 44.44$); $K_\Phi = 4$, $F_\rho = 0.999$.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{The hypothesis at the stationary measure, and the window (table 1).} The critical point of $J_3$
reached from the exact representation, atoms
\[
  (a,c,s) = (2.4504,-1.4161,0.8287),\quad(-2.2879,0.0120,0.7009),\quad(2.0478,1.3427,0.8810),
\]
has $\norm{\nabla J_3}_\infty = 4\cdot10^{-17}$ (a stationary measure $\mu^*$ of the
mean-field flow), fit error $\norm{f - \fhat}/\norm{f} = 0.244$ on the orbit (the leak's bias: amplitudes
$2.0$--$2.5$ against $3A_k = 2.1$--$3$, widths $0.70$--$0.88$ against $0.4$--$0.5$), and a positive whitened Hessian
(eigenvalues $0.0057$--$0.2003$). $\kappa_R$ at the atoms is $0.00004$, $-0.00227$, $0.00411$; over the spheres of
radius $\varrho'$ around them (400 directions, radii $\varrho'$ and $\varrho'/2$) it is $0.00461$
($\varrho' = 0.05$), $0.00512$ ($0.1$), $0.00619$ ($0.2$) and $0.00730$ ($0.3$), so (H) holds at $\mu^*$ with
$\varrho' = 0.1$ and margin $\kappa = \sigma_0 - 0.00512 = 0.00195$ (relaxation time $1/\kappa = 512$) and fails from
$\varrho'\approx0.3$ on. The window of Remark D$'''$.1 is realised:
$\kappa_R[\mu^*](N_{0.1}) = 0.00512 < \sigma_0 = 0.00707 < \sigma_{\mathrm{crit}} = 0.04716$. At
$\sigma_0 = 0.3\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$, by contrast, the three-atom critical point (fit error $0.406$) fails the
splitting condition at its third atom ($\kappa_R = 0.01696 > \sigma_0 = 0.01415$): there the mean-field limit has
more atoms, and the hypothesis has to be checked at that configuration. The theorem's constants for the warm start
($\Lambda_1 = 0$, $\kappa := \frac12\min\{\kappa(\varrho'),\min_sa(s)\} = 0.00070$, the factor $\frac12$ leaving room for the
sampling of the supremum by finitely many directions): $R_\Theta = 2857$,
$\varrho = 1.1\cdot10^{-6}$, $\theta = 4.6\cdot10^{-11}$, $C_1 = 2.2\cdot10^9$, $m_0 = 1.2\cdot10^{23}$ --- the
worst-case envelope of Remark D$'''$.6.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{The hypothesis along the flow (table 2).} On the warm start ($\mu_0$ the mixture of Gaussians of
width $0.02$ around the atoms) the margin $a(s) = \sigma_0 - \kappa_R[\mu^*]($spheres of radius $0.1$ around 256 reference
particles, 24 directions$)$ stays between $+0.00139$ and $+0.00167$ over the whole run, so (H) holds
with $s_1 = 0$; the same statistic with the reference's own residual is larger by at most $0.00013$ (its sampling
fluctuation; the proof's bound (0.2) allows $L_2^G\norm{r_{\mathrm{ref}} - r^*}_{L^1(\rho)}\le0.214$), the
reference's rms drift decays from $6\cdot10^{-3}$ to $3\cdot10^{-6}$ and its fit error stays at $0.245$--$0.248$: the reference is stationary. On the cold start ($a\sim\mathcal{N}(0,0.8^2)$, $c\sim\mathcal{U}(-2,2)$,
$s\sim\mathcal{U}(0.4,1)$, a connected cloud) the margin is negative over the whole run, $-0.196$ at the first record ($-0.179$ at $s = 1$) and $-0.0025$ at
$s = 900$, while the fit error falls from $0.948$ to $0.244$ --- the same fit as the warm limit --- and
the rms drift from $0.15$ to $4.7\cdot10^{-4}$: the cold reference converges to a configuration of the same quality
whose support reaches the ridges (Remark D$'''$.2).

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{Warm start (table 3).} Window maxima of $D_m$ over $(0,112.5]$, $(112.5,225]$, $(225,450]$,
$(450,900]$ (Table~\ref{tab:thmDppp:warm}):

\medskip

The distance saturates: last-to-first window ratios $1.13$, $1.13$, $1.15$ and last-to-previous $1.02$, $1.01$,
$1.02$. Its $m$-law has slope $-1.28\pm0.30$ (per-seed fits; the theorem's mean square: $-1$), the learned function's
$-0.69\pm0.16$ ($m^{-1/2}$: $-0.5$). The per-particle maxima $1.36$, $0.62$, $0.21$ are far above
$3\varrho'/4 = 0.075$: the localisation of part (c) is not in force at these $m$ (Remark D$'''$.6), and the values
lie below the vacuous $C_1/m$ by 8.4--8.8 orders of magnitude.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{Proposition D$'''$.5 (the same table).} The sensitivities of the three atoms to the weights have
whitened norms $\norm{S_k} = 8.38$, $4.66$, $8.32$. The multinomial expectation
$\E D_\infty = (1/m - 1/M)\sum_k\bar w_k\tr(\tilde S_k\tilde C\tilde S_k^\T)$ is $0.823$, $0.201$, $0.0454$ against
the four-seed means $0.446\pm0.28$, $0.138\pm0.051$, $0.0136\pm0.0055$ (ratios $0.54$, $0.69$, $0.30$: a mean over
four seeds of a heavy-tailed quantity, $\E\abs{w^m - w^M}^2$ being carried by the rare large fluctuations). The
per-seed law is the sharp test: $\sum_kw^m_k\norm{\tilde S_k(w^m - w^M)}^2$ computed from the realised cluster
weights predicts the measured $D_m$ at $s = 900$ within 25\% in 9 of the 12 (seed, $m$) pairs (for instance
$0.123\to0.112$, $0.187\to0.186$, $0.206\to0.205$ at $m = 16$; $0.0303\to0.0261$, $0.00087\to0.00085$,
$0.0081\to0.0087$, $0.0187\to0.0190$ at $m = 256$); the largest deviation, a ratio $0.57$ at $m = 16$, is the seed
with the largest weight fluctuation $\abs{w^m - w^M} = 0.275$, where the linearisation's remainder and the saturation
$a\le a_{\max}$ enter. The exact first equality of (ii) --- $\sum_kw^m_k\norm{\vartheta^*_k(w^m) - \vartheta^*_k(w^M)}^2$
with the weighted stationary points themselves --- reproduces the measured $D_m$ within 2.3\% in all 12 pairs
($1.292\to1.292$ for the seed just mentioned): every $m$-system sits at the replicated weighted stationary
configuration. The splitting margin of $\Theta^*(w^m)$ is positive in 11 of the 12 pairs ($+0.0006$ to $+0.0050$);
it is negative, $-0.0022$, for $m = 16$, seed 3, whose weights $(9,4,3)/16$ lie outside the admissible set: there
the replicated configuration is a saddle of $J_{16}$ and the plateau at $s = 900$ is the saddle's --- referee round 9
continued that run to $s = 3600$ and saw $D$ grow from $1.29$ to $5.13$ as two particles changed basins and the
weights re-sorted to $(7,5,4)/16$ (Proposition D$'''$.5(iv); the root-mean-square weight fluctuation is $0.204$,
$0.102$, $0.051$ at $m = 16$, $64$, $256$ against admissible margins of $0.06$--$0.36$ in $\norm{\cdot}_\infty$). Per
cluster (seed 0), the measured mean displacements of the $m$-system's particles point along $S_k(w^m - w^M)$ with
cosines $0.92$--$1.000$ and norms within 0.8--20\% ($m = 16$: $0.434$ against $0.455$, $0.130$ against $0.129$,
$0.338$ against $0.358$): the particles of a cluster move together, by the predicted vector. The ratios $0.54$,
$0.69$, $0.30$ of the four-seed means to the multinomial expectation are the sampling of a heavy-tailed quantity at
$m = 16$ and $64$ and, at $m = 256$, four seeds whose realised fluctuations happen to be small.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{Cold start (table 4).} Window maxima of $D_m$: $1.81\pm0.28\to3.13\to4.50\to5.33\pm0.16$
($m = 16$), $0.505\to0.900\to1.42\to1.79\pm0.93$ ($64$), $0.0744\to0.162\to0.347\to0.583\pm0.19$ ($256$): the
distance grows over the whole horizon (last-to-first $2.95$, $3.54$, $7.84$; last-to-previous $1.19$, $1.26$,
$1.68$), with slope $-0.87\pm0.14$ in $m$ (the learned function $-0.66\pm0.06$, steeper than the $-0.5$ of the warm start). The
per-particle deviations are
bimodal: medians $0.40$, $0.14$, $0.067$ (the $m^{-1/2}$ law of the particles that stayed with their references)
against maxima $6.7$, $6.3$, $6.2$ for every $m$ (the separation between basins), and the fraction of pairs more than
one whitened unit apart is $0.203\pm0.047$, $0.082\pm0.021$, $0.037\pm0.010$ --- a factor $2.5$ and $2.2$ per factor 4 in $m$, a decay like $m^{-0.64\pm0.08}$
(per-seed fits; the medians decay like $m^{-0.62\pm0.07}$) --- steeper than the $m^{-1/2}$ of Remark D$'''$.2's
boundary-layer count, which is a lower-order prediction (the flips contribute $p_m\cdot O(R^2)\approx0.2\cdot38 = 7.8$,
$3.1$, $1.4$ against the measured $5.3$, $1.8$, $0.58$). This is Theorem
D$''$'s negative finding reproduced and diagnosed on the averaged system: a connected cold start that splits between
basins is outside (H), and its coupling distance is dominated by the pairs that chose different basins.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{The real loop (table 5).} The closed loop at $k_e = 25$ ($\sigma = \sigma_0/k_e = 2.8\cdot10^{-4}$,
$\tau = 0$, $\mathrm{d}t = 10^{-3}$, three seeds) from the same warm start: window maxima over $(0,500]$,
$(500,1000]$, $(1000,2000]$ real-time units are $0.328\pm0.11\to0.467\to0.590\pm0.17$ ($m = 16$),
$0.0605\to0.0801\to0.0909\pm0.045$ ($64$), $0.0188\to0.0253\to0.0294\pm0.011$ ($256$), slope $-1.13\pm0.15$ in $m$;
the reference's fit error stays at $0.245$--$0.246$. The horizon is 80 slow units, a sixth of the relaxation time
$1/\kappa = 512$, and the growth (last-to-first $1.80$, $1.50$, $1.56$) is the early transient of the averaged dynamics: the averaged
flow started from the real loop's own initial clouds (reproduced from their seeds) gives
$D_{\mathrm{real}}(t)/D_{\mathrm{averaged}}(t/k_e)$ between $0.993$ and $1.004$ at every window end, for every seed
and every $m$ --- the real loop at $k_e = 25$ follows its averaged slow dynamics to within 0.7\% (across different
clouds the last-window maxima are $1.57$, $0.78$ and $2.58$ times the averaged runs' $0.377$, $0.117$, $0.0114$,
within the seed-to-seed spread). Two of the three $m = 16$ clouds have inadmissible or marginal weights (splitting
margins $-0.0021$, $-0.00001$; the third $+0.0026$), so their eventual configuration is a saddle's; at $m = 64$ and
$256$ all margins are positive ($+0.0029$ to $+0.0049$). The saturation at the orbit distance of Corollary
D$'''$.7 lies beyond the horizon run.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{Reading.} In the window $\kappa_R[\mu^*] < \sigma_0 < \sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$ the network learns
(the collapsed configuration is unstable, the three kernels fit the plant to $0.244$) and propagation of chaos is
uniform in time on a warm start with admissible weights: the coupled distance saturates with the $1/m$ law at the
stationary distance of the replicated weighted configuration, which the exact form of Proposition D$'''$.5(ii) gives
within 2.3\% --- the level is the weight-sensitivity law, not the theorem's constant. At $m = 16$ the multinomial
weights leave the admissible set with appreciable probability; the one such seed sits on a saddle and later splits a
cluster. The cold start of a connected cloud shows what the hypothesis excludes: growth over the horizon and a floor
of pairs in different basins whose fraction decays like $m^{-0.64}$, while the learned function still converges at a
rate between $m^{-1/2}$ and $m^{-2/3}$ in both cases. The real loop at $k_e = 25$ follows its averaged slow dynamics
to within 0.7\% over the horizon run.

three-term identity of Step~1 holds to rounding on random configurations with a mean-field stand-in, the interaction
term equals $-\norm{\fhat_\Theta - \fhat_{\bar\Theta}}^2$ up to a relative $O(D^{1/2})$ correction (ratio
$0.98\to0.9999$ as $\delta$ is scaled by $1\to0.01$) and obeys (3.1) at every scale\newline
(\code{test_coupling_identity_three_terms_and_the_sign_of_the_interaction}); $\kappa_R$ at the atoms of a collapsed
configuration with the collapsed residual equals Proposition K's $\lambda_{\max}(G^{1/2}R_2G^{1/2})$, which equals
$\sigma_0$ at $\sigma_0 = \sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$, the collapsed critical point has zero drift, and the deterministic
neighbourhood search over the sphere of radius $\varrho$ reproduces the first-order expansion
$\lambda_{\max} + \varrho\norm{G^{1/2}\nabla\lambda_{\max}}$ with a relative error $O(\varrho)$
(\code{test_residual_curvature_}\dots); the searched whitened constants reproduce the document's values and lie below
their closed forms, \code{theorem_constants} satisfies the four constraints the bootstrap needs, and $\Lambda_1$
integrates exactly to $s_1$ (\code{test_whitened_constants_}\dots); at $\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$ the
three-atom critical point of $J_3$ has zero drift and (H) holds at it with $\varrho' = 0.1$ inside the learning
regime, the replicated weighted stationary point is a critical point of $J_{16}$ to $10^{-10}$, the linearisation in
the weights has a second-order remainder, and on a coupled run from the jittered mixture ($m = 16$, $64$ against
$M = 512$, 60 slow units) $\kappa_R[\mu^*]$ over the neighbourhood of the reference's support stays below $\sigma_0$,
the two curvature statistics differ by at most the bound ($0.2$), the per-cluster displacements point along
$S_k(w^m - w^M)$ (cosine $\ge0.8$) with $D$ within a factor 2 of $\sum_kw^m_k\norm{S_k(w^m - w^M)}^2$,
$\norm{\fhat_\Theta - \fhat_{\bar\Theta}}\le L_\vartheta^G\cdot\operatorname{mean}\norm{\delta_i}$, $D_{64} < D_{16}$
and the (vacuous) bound $C_1/m$ holds\newline
(\code{test_hypothesis_H_at_the_three_atom_stationary_measure_and_the_weight_sensitivity_law}).}
